## Chapter 1

### Notes

#### Work Notes

Hello, this is my first ever fanfiction! (eep, I'm scared)

I have been a reader for years, more than I'd like to admit, but I have never breached the bar into actually writing. I've just had this idea for a long time now and it has finally driven me insane enough to give it a shot. I was unsure how to start this fic and just decided to go with the scene that has been on repeat in my brain. I may come back and pad the beginning up to this point if wanted, by others or myself.

So, please enjoy and let me know thoughts, comments, or any ideas!

### Text

Sokka growled and punched the wall. Fuck, this was all his doing. He glanced around to ensure he was alone in this prison and was hit with how much he had needed Zuko to be a guard at Boiling Rock or just a person with him. That was ruined now.

Sokka sighed out as he rubbed his shoulder. This day had gone from bad to worse to exponentially shitty in the matter of hours and there was still so much time left. There was no telling what was going to happen next but all Sokka knew was he had fucked up. Horribly. If it wasn’t for his need to prove himself and make up for his horrible blunder during the eclipse, then his dad and other tribesmen wouldn’t have been captured and he would have had no reason to bring Zuko to this cursed place to begin with. What made that even worse was that his dad had turned out to not even be in this blasted place after all!

That brought him to blunder number two, Zuko. Zuko and his surprising need to just stick his scrawny neck into every dangerous thing that didn’t even involve him and to somehow manage and get himself into even worse situations than Sokka usually did. How was that one even feasibly possible? Sokka groaned as he forced himself to start walking forward. Enough time had been spent on these thoughts as it was and he had a new mission.

His dad might not be in this literal volcano but Suki was, and now so was Zuko. He had reasons to push onward. He won’t fail them but right now he needed to find them both and start step one of his new plan. That was to hopefully get them onto friendly terms. This was going to be a fifty-fifty that relied on Suki being forgiving-Sokka sighed and tried that thought again. This was going to rely on Zuko not- erm- being Zuko? Right, Sokka only needed to figure out how to get them into the same location and to get them on working terms. Not so hard. Friends were not needed to break out of a prison.

Sokka strode forward through the courtyard with as much confidence as he could fake as he tried to not swivel his head while scanning for two very distinctively different figures. The courtyard was full of tattered red shirts all hunched and doing some kind of labor. Some were on hands and knees scrubbing the metal floor. Others must have had good behavior jobs because they seemed to be dusting the air. Suppose that was needed with the constant falling of ash from the volcano they built this in. The figures were all mostly adult, which was reason enough to continue forward.

The search felt pointless until finally he rounded a corner to an area mostly empty of red. Sokka let out a huge sigh of relief as he spotted the two of them actually working in the same spot already. Even if they did not realize it they were making Sokka’s job so much easier as he turned towards them. With a convincing enough nod to the guard on duty there he was able to slip into the role of the new guard for that area. The guard who had nodded back at Sokka hardly hesitated to walk away and take a break he probably would not have been given normally.

Sokka watched as the guard turned out of sight before twisting to the two mopping the floor in front of him. He was almost giddy with the sight of both of them together. Smirking, he chuckled and muttered “missed a spot.”

Suki’s head snapped around in the way that begged for a challenge and her storm colored eyes hardened in a steely glare before realization of just who had spoken registered.

“Dammit Sokka, it is like you want me to hurt you. Quit doing this, I was about to risk my food privileges.” She whispered in a huff as she turned back to mopping with a side glance to her mopping partner. She froze seeing that his attention was now also on them and her body tensed.

Sokka knew that as a sign that she would and was about to take out the person who could ruin their chances of escape and that was unfortunately Zuko. He quickly pushed off the wall and shifted to be between the two, back to Zuko, with a hand raised in Suki’s direction.

“Whoa, hold up. Suki. I see you two have met but haven’t, uh, talked?” he glanced back at Zuko’s confused face to confirm “right, uhm. Suki, this is my partner in this prison break. The one who uh… got caught because I was kinda too stupid to wait and talk to you?” Sokka gave a sheepish grin at this as the boy behind him huffed.

Suki relaxed at Sokka’s stuttering and tuned it out mostly as she looked to the boy behind him. Sokka was still rambling about being on “our side” while Suki seemed to be forming her own plan of attack against said member of the team. She had locked eyes with Zuko and they seemed to be in the worlds most intense eye contact battle that Sokka was trying to wiggle between. Knowing no one was listening to him as both Zuko and Suki did not so much as twitch at their own names Sokka finished rather loudly with a rambled out “-so yeah. Team!”

The teen behind him gave a slight smile that looked more like a flash of teeth from one who had never smiled before and choked out a rough “uh, hello. Zuko here.”

Suki looked as if she was about to murder him where he stood if Sokka would just move even an inch. Sokka thought that was fair, but was also realizing just how bad at social introductions this prince was. Like, seriously, this was the same lame line he had used with them those few days ago and it had gone so bad then, what did he think would be the result to it now!? Did the Fire Nation really not teach people how to say hello without a fireball to the face!?

“I know. We’ve met before by the way.”

Zuko’s slight wave died in the air as his face fell. “Oh. Uhm. I’m sorry, I don’t…”

“Remember? I don’t expect you to.” Her voice was as icy as her glare. “You kinda burned down my village.”

Zuko’s full body flinch was visible even out of Sokka’s obscured peripheral vision and he seemed to physically shrink back and into himself slightly. Sokka was learning quickly that, while Zuko was constant force of nature, he was also very quick to shrink in the face of his past actions. He had noticed it first with Toph after the whole burning of her feet moment. Zuko had caved to the girls every demand for well after the time it took for her feet to heal. Sokka still thinks that Zuko would even now bend to any comment made by Toph despite the incident seemingly being behind them both.

Then came Katara, who at first Sokka agreed with having every right to be as angry and aggressive to the firebender as she wanted. Now he wasn’t so sure, but the fact still stood that every barked order from her had the teen crumpling like a kicked polerpuppy. Even Aang had this weird pull over the older teen as Zuko worked hard to prove he was there to stay, which was debatable with the odds stacked against him thanks to past actions.

Sokka could see it happening again, in real time, right before his eyes. He knew the exact moment Zuko registered just who Suki was and that he had just added another person to his weird ‘worth more than me’ list thing he seemed to have going. He watched as the other boys eyes looked anywhere but Suki after that comment and knew that the firebender would most likely bend to Suki’s every demand of him no matter the result.

“I’m… I’m really sorry about that-”

“Look me in the eyes.” Suki’s harsh tone cut Zuko’s rambling short and just like Sokka had guessed Zuko caved to the demand in seconds. The other boys gold eyes, full of nervous energy, met her stormy ones and he clamped his mouth shut.

Sokka really should probably stop this but he had to see where Suki was going. He was unfortunately curious. After all, Suki was a good leader and even better at putting people in their place than Sokka ever was. He knew that personally and easily recalled when this energy was directed at him those many months ago at their first meeting. He knew she wouldn’t stop once she needed something. Sokka decided that he would step in if it got too bad.

“Good. Now I want you to tell me how you really feel about burning my village. I need to know.” Her eyes were like stone cut to kill.

Zuko reacted with his whole body. Jerking forward slightly, and keeping a surprising amount of eye contact that Sokka had to admit was impressive, he stammered before seeming to find his words “I-I mean it. Every word. I am so, so sorry about what I did to your village. I do remember now. You are from Kyoshi, right? I’m sorry- I was… It was wrong and-” he was losing his words again and the eye contact was doing a number on this poor mess of a firebender. Right, intervention time.

Sokka cleared his throat “right, see? Are we at least able to work together to get out of here now?”

Suki, never predictable Suki to Sokka’s ever thinning mental state, started to chuckle and waved him off like this was all some game she had won. This reaction had both boys blinking in confusion.

“Yeah, I forgive you already. I needed to see it in your eyes. See your face to see if you were lying you know. Consider it water under the bridge for now. No one was burned anyway and you really didn’t do that much damage all things considered.” Ouch, she really was good at hitting ones ego. Sokka was just glad that wasn’t his ego she was after this time. “Glad to see you do remember it though and you are in some way sorry. That I do believe. Now, about getting out of here.” She turned to Sokka with a raised eyebrow.

Sokka nodded and was about to state his brilliant plan, because that is what it was, a brilliant plan from a brilliant man, when a voice no one could forget rang over to the small group.

“Yo, Sokka! Not sure how you got here but count me into whatever you got cooked up to get out. I can’t stand being in the same place as so many ashmakers.” A teen in prison red who had somehow managed to keep a damn piece of wheat in his mouth even in this place swaggered up towards them.

Jet.

## Chapter 2

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

I had a few chapters typed so I figured to just put them all up.

Anyway, have fun with Jet. He is a handful even on writing terms.

-Forgive the many edits, I just want it to be right and easy to read. Breaking up some of the chunky paragraphs to help with flow, maybe.-

### Text

Jet, all tan skinned and black eyes. Jet, the man who was left behind in Ba Sing Se.

Jet smirked at the group before him as he walked forward and stared at Sokka. Sokka could feel his blood boil a bit with agitation at the sight of Jet, after all this was a man who was always a wild card and not at all what Sokka and his plan needed. Sokka had factored for Suki and Zuko, no more no less. This was a delicate situation and they only had time to really focus on the few here. The hours were against them and against Zuko even more so than the teen seemed to realize. Sokka knew it was a matter of time before the Fire Nation knew their enemy number one was in their grasp and he wasn’t sure he could act fast enough if they did. Still, Sokka couldn’t leave Jet either. Not after the last time they saw each other.

“Jet, I thought…” Sokka choked on the last word. He couldn’t say it.

He had thought. He had believed with his whole being. Toph had felt it and cried for nights when she thought no one was still up. Sokka had listened to the girl as tough as stone itself sniffle at the reality of what she saw that no one else did. Yet, here that asshole stood. Wheat in mouth, cocky smile, smug posture, alive. He was alive, that bastard. He owed Toph an apology and that was enough to start factoring him into the escape plan. If anything, Sokka could bring Toph some peace or at least bring her someone to beat to a pulp for the emotions she had experienced.

“Dead?” Jet finished just as casually as one does a conversation about the weather.

Sokka just nodded. He knew the others were probably staring at him as he added a whole new person into the group he was going to have to introduce. Great, that was another thing. Jet would probably love to get to know Suki. Earth Kingdom and all. Sokka was going to have to keep himself between Jet and Suki. He didn’t trust that man after the whole thing with Katara. Suki meeting Jet was annoying at most but Sokka had to believe she had more sense and would put Jet in his place as she had just done with Zuko.

Zuko, was once again, Sokka’s tricky spot to work with. Jet, the worlds biggest firebender hater, the one that tops Katara in a shocking turn of events, was now forcing his way into Sokka’s plans yet again. Where Sokka would have to physically be between Suki and this man, he would have to somehow make a moving wall to keep Jet away from Zuko.

“Wait, you know Jet?” Suki asked confused. Great, so she knows him already Sokka groaned internally.

“Yeah, we had a few different run ins with each other. The last one left us believing he died, not cool by the way.” Sokka glared at Jet to prove his point. “How do you-” he was turning to look at Suki who was just giving him her ‘don’t ask stupid questions’ look as he took in her uniform. Right, prison. Jet was a prisoner in the same place Suki had been for a long time. Longer time than Sokka could ever being to apologize for.

Jet smiled and winked at Suki which made Sokka’s mood go from agitated to down right furious but to Sokka’s instant relief Suki turned her glare onto him as a warning. Jet did not seem phased though and shrugged it off before glancing at Sokka’s other companion. Sokka, who was getting ready to defend Zuko the firebender, was not prepared for what happened next. He watched as Jet looked confused for a second before he seemed suddenly shocked as he yelled out “Lee!?”

Sokka and Suki both shared a very confused look at this and looked back at Zuko who’s whole body spoke of a coil wound far too tightly. Sokka had been so focused on keeping Jet from Suki he had neglected to pay attention to Zuko and his reaction. Clearly this was going to be his biggest misstep in this whole mess of a situation.

“Jet.” Zuko nodded, a barely there tilt of the chin given just how tense he stood to Sokka’s left. “It’s not Lee-”

“No shit!” Jet snarled out. All that shock he had seemed to evaporate into hated in an instant. “The fuck are you doing here, no wait, you’re right where you belong arnt ya. A prison of your own fucking people. Sokka, what are you doing with this ashmaker?”

Jet spun his glare onto Sokka instead and Suki tensed. They were going to be caught at this rate if Sokka did not end this right here and now. He was furious at this whole spirits forbid situation and Jet was about to blow everything!

“Look, Jet, you need to keep your voice down. I don’t know how you know Zuko but either you suck it up and work with us to get out or you walk away now. This is not up for negotiation.” Sokka met Jet's glare equally. Growing up with Katara had taught him a few things, and one of which was how to match ones anger. He did not care about whatever weird thing stood between the two boys but it was not going to be what caused them all to be stuck here and for Zuko to be for sure taken back to his family or worse.

Jet huffed and rolled his neck to crack it loudly “fine. Fine. Just know one thing, the second I am out of here you and I have unfinished business. Got that *baby*?”

Jet let the last word, which by Sokka’s understanding was a pet name if he ever heard one, roll off his tongue as nastily as the other words he used for firebenders did. This, was another thing Sokka would have to process later. He glanced to Zuko expecting to see the anger Jet was showing matched by the teen who was so used to being angry first over any other emotion and was shocked even further. Instead of the usual anger the boy was an emotionless husk. Zuko had said nothing during this whole thing yet he looked as if he had turned to stone with just those words. This, was every part, not good. Sokka watched as the boy never broke eye contact with Jet as he nodded once to this arrangement. This was, in Sokka’s brilliant opinion, the worst possible thing to have come from this cursed place. Jet, was about to bring a world of hurt onto Sokka and he was not sure he was ready to face it. Ready or not, here they would go.

The court yard whistle blew signaling for the prisoners to return to their cells for lunch and Sokka was out of time. He cursed as he spun to see a new man who towered over them all had joined in while Jet threw his tantrum as only Jet knew how to do. Tui and La could this situation stop getting shittier any moment now please!?

“Prisoners, back to your cells! You heard the bell!” Sokka barked out in his best guard voice. He thought he had it pretty well mastered and Suki, spirits Sokka had missed her, bowed and moved back as if any other guard had said this. Zuko shifted as well but he never took his eyes from Jet, which was smart in Sokka’s humble opinion. Jet was a king among wild cards. The man, however, laughed a booming laugh.

“Good try kid, almost had me ya did. You lot plannin’ a break-out yeah? Count me in.”

“Oh no, no no. This has already gained too much attention as it is-”

“Either count me in, or count yerselves as good as caught.” The man cut Sokka off mid sentence and Sokka swallowed hard. He already had to factor in Jet so he could apologize to Toph, what was one more massive human. The man seemed to read Sokka as his resolve broke and smiled wide “names Chit Sang. Meet here after lunch and we can get to talkin’ about it all.”

Sokka nodded and groaned. He turned and led them all out of the cleaning area and left them as he headed to the mess hall for the other guards. This had just gone so much better in his head. In his head he had only had to get Zuko and Suki on working together terms, not so hard! Yet here he was, Zuko and Suki were patching things together oddly well, Jet was seemingly back from the dead just to fuck Sokka’s plans up royally, and a random beast of a firebender who was back from the cooler had just dropped himself into Sokka’s plans rather rudely. Sokka was grumbling as he got his mush of food and slumped into his seat. He had to admit it now, he had mere hours to work out a plan, get said plan in action, and get everyone off this cursed volcano. He was the only one who could do it and yet he felt so out of his depth. Why did he ever think this was something he could do in the first place!? No one, Zuko had warned him before they even left the temple, no one had ever escaped and here he was all of 15 years old and thinking himself better than every man or woman in this place. What could he see that they couldn’t? Not a Tui and La blessed thing and he was admitting it now. He was going to be the reason Suki and Jet stayed, and possibly worst of all the reason Zuko was just handed back to the fire nation on a golden platter. Aang was now once again without a firebending teacher and it was all Sokka’s fault.

He poked at his mush, not really hungry after everything that had just happened, and startled when someone nudged his shoulder. A female guard was grinning at him and winked. “Relax newbie, everyone has a rough time adjusting to this place. Some drop a few hours in from the heat and metal combo, so at least yer doin’ better than them.”

A man sat across from Sokka and huffed “You always going to start conversations with that? I passed out from dehydration during role call once and you never seem to just let it go.” He shoveled a spoonful of the mystery lunch into his mouth without hesitation.

“Well yeah, makes all the newbies feel better about whatever shit they ran into. So, I have you to thank for having a conversation starter.” She laughed out as the other man flicked his spoon at her. He missed and splattered that goop of lunch all over to Sokka’s disgust. “Besides, we all know this place has some true horrors. Ya have cooler duty yet kid?”

Sokka took that as his cue and shook his head. “Not yet. Say, I don’t think I know all that much about these coolers. Could you, ah, well…” he trailed off, unsure how to ask this and not give himself away as not being fire nation. If he really was unlucky, he just did but given the small smile he received maybe he was safe for now.

“Sure kid, it’s a special thing only really used in this prison. Kinda saved it for the worst of the worst ya know? The traitors of our own great nation. They were designed with firebenders in mind.” She glanced to her friend who nodded.

“Firebenders in mind? So they are not used on those who are not one?” Sokka asked, trying to paint a picture of this devise in his mind. He had put a man in it before but did not stick around to see its true purpose. Shit, he had not put ‘a man’ into one he realized. He had put Chit Sang into it. Great, consider that reason enough to help him.

“Mostly, but sometimes those who are not lucky or do something really not good also find their way in there. If those people are lucky, they come out in one piece. It wasn’t made for them, you see.” The man took a drink of his water as if he hadn’t just admitted that the prison actively killed some prisoners.

“Right, yeah, okay… So, what is it exactly? I saw one earlier. Just looked like a tube really. So what makes it so…” Sokka really had to work on getting his words together but the day had just thrown him so badly that even this was almost impossible.

The woman did not seem phased, however, and shuddered “it is what it says it is. The cooler.”

“It is a prison cell made to freeze the fire out of a firebender and render them cold. Cold to a firebender is deadly if left unchecked. Freezing an inner flame is a sure way to kill one slowly if you do it right. The cooler is made to get pretty fuckin’ close to that.” The man stated as if lecturing.

“Put it simply, the longer ya sit, worse ya freeze.” The woman finished and shuddered again as she glanced over at the other guards as they entered and moved around. She was most likely just scanning for her next conversation buddy as Sokka had turned out to be a downer.

Sokka sat with that knowledge and was quick to feel the dread and horror. Something like that was the bare basic form of torture. The cruelest and most brutal way to kill a firebender or to break them down. And it was here in the fire nations prisons. Built by the fire nation. Used against its own people deemed ‘traitors’ for one reason or another. Sokka had locked a man into one. Sokka suddenly knew he would have to do so again if he wanted off this Tui and La forsaken volcano.

## Chapter 3

The lunch space for prisoners was packed as always as Suki was led out of her cell and into the open space. She had always found this routine ridiculous, having to leave the courtyard to go back to the cells for a roll call only to be led back out and into the disorganized space. At least she wasn’t lunch or food duty this month. That was worse. Having to spend days inside in the boiling room cooking spirits knew what to serve to everyone and being the last to eat. You were more likely to be attacked too as you held food to the ration specific guide the guards ordered for the day. Suki knew not a soul was being fed what they should in order to keep up with the work they were doing.

She had also learned a very interesting thing about firebenders while working here. She hated to admit how much she knew about firebenders thanks to this place but one should never overlook a place to learn about their fellow enemies when given a golden opportunity. While Jet was one to hole himself away with any and every non-firebending prisoner in this place, Suki had taken the many months here as a chance to learn. And learn she did.

Her first big takeaway, firebenders need food like fire needs air. Firebending was unlike any other bending. Earthbenders needed earth, no more no less. If an earthbender was tired the bending would weaken, sure, but a hungry earthbender was still deadly for a long while. Same went for waterbending with the added need for water. Suki assumed the same was for air but only Aang would know for sure. Firebenders, however, did not need fire to bend it. They pushed fire out from their own being and willed it into existence. As terrifying as that thought was at first, the more one thinks about it the more exhausting that thought is. If earthbenders could pull earth from their bodies it would be extremely taxing physically. If waterbenders could only use the water in them they would never stop drinking water or risk dehydration. Firebenders, now they were at a huge disadvantage. The more Suki watched, the more she learned. Firebenders were using their own energy as flames and with that energy being burned on two ends they were in constant need of replenishing. In other words a firebender was always needing food or they risked weak flames or worse.

Suki watched as yet another daily argument over rations was started as some of the younger and newer firebenders started demanding more because they had worked hard. She rolled her eyes at them and went to the line to grab her own bowl. Suki knew the outcome of that argument before it started, they would be either dragged away without food and lose the limited meal anyway or end up in the cooler. She didn’t look to see which one it would be this time as she scanned the crowd for her new partner in crime.

He was harder to spot than Suki would have first guessed which was good seeing just who he was. The boy in question was in the shaded corner of the courtyard facing the group of prisoners with a stare that said he was mentally not here in the prison. He was also without a bowl of food and Suki could guess that was an order given down from the warden. Suki had a good few choice moves she was dying to try out on that man if she was ever to be given the chance.

She let her eyes glance over the rest of the prisoners to check for eyes on her and found one pair. A pair of black, calculating eyes. Jet. He was watching her with a stare that seemed to be daring her to choose the firebender over himself and his little group of thugs he had pulled together. Suki knew Jet. He was womanizing, flirts with anyone who could offer him something worth while, and a smart man only blinded by his hate. That blindfold would be his downfall if he wanted to get out of here in one piece. Suki thought she was a smart woman, and she prided herself on being a leader and knowing her people. Sokka was once a part of her people even if for a few days. She knew Sokka, and Sokka needed Zuko. To Suki, that meant Jet would not be touching the boy lest he wanted to lose said finger.

Suki returned the glare and turned her back to Jet to show she was not his toy to play with. She moved towards the figure hunched in the shade and took the time to really look at him. She first took in the prison uniform for a second before her gaze moved to the boy’s face. He was wearing the same thing as her after all so there wasn’t much need to focus on that. The first thing she noticed was his eyes. Piercing fire nation gold. The eyes of so many who have done all they could to hurt millions of people daily. The color of the rich flames that danced over the buildings of her home and had pulled her and her people into this blasted hundred year war after years of piece.

She tore her gaze away from those golden flames and looked then at the scar that marred the teens face in a horrific burn. She had seen burns ever since she left Kyoshi. She had helped many with burns such as his and worse. The fires seemed to take away everything and leave nothing. But, this boy who Sokka was so determined to prove he was on “our side” was a firebender and she knew quite a bit about them and their flames. One fact was true across all firebenders, firebenders did not burn easy. That would be tragic in a way, if they did. She thought about how it would be to wield an element that would hurt you as easily as it did others.

Instead of lingering on those thoughts she let her eyes drift back to his and paused. Where she once saw only the reflection of countless firebenders eyes, now she was met with the eyes of a hurt teen. One who was currently mentally a million miles from this place and unseeing to what was around him. He looked so small hunched there against the wall. His thin frame begged for hundreds of missed meals or from some kind of forced starvation. Firebenders needed a great deal of food. Those eyes the color of mini suns, she noticed when she looked again, glowed differently. One of the mini suns seemed to burn so dull in the shadow. The burn that had left such a horrific scar on the boy must have taken away it’s light and she briefly wondered if he could see from it at all. She was going to test this thought. She needed to know her people after all and this was a big thing to account for.

Suki approached from the teens left as he continued to stare at nothing. She moved in a way that made her foot falls quiet but not silent. The metal floor really made silence very tricky. As she approached the teen she kept her gaze locked him to see just when he registered her approach. Plus side for her is that he picked the shade so her shadow did not give her away and she got near his side without a blink of notice before she tried whispering. “Hey there partner.”

She was halfway down the wall to sit next to him when his whole body jerked as he realized someone was there. He spun his head to look at her and his upper body twisted to assist in a near full turn so that he was facing her. His eyes danced with anticipated action before relaxing ever so slowly as recognition dawned on him. Wherever he was just before, it was not a fun place and he had just been jerked back into another awful reality. Suki scanned his features and the way he had twisted so that he was facing her fully and made three quick assessments.

One: he was most likely partially, if not fully, blind out of that eye or he would have just turned his head slightly.

Two: that ear looked just as bad or worse than his eye now that she was close and he had definitely not heard her coming.

Three: he has been hurt far more times than just that burn.

Suki masked her emotions to these thoughts and gave a small nod before getting up again and just as swiftly as she had sat on his left she switched to his right side. Zuko’s eyes trailed her every movement and he gave a look of confusion as she settled to his right. She flashed him a smile and made a show of leaning back against the wall with a sigh of one who is finally on break after hours of work. This did the trick as the boy relaxed slightly and leaned back against the wall a bit as well. Suki smirked at him and shifted to start shoveling the gray goop into her mouth.

“Why did you forgive me just like that?” his voice broke the silence in a scratchy hush and the question seemed to have been burning his mouth demanding to be let out. He continued to stare ahead at the crowd, not daring to look at her.

Suki sighed and set her bowl down to join him in looking out at the other prisoners without really seeing them.

“Honestly? I didn’t. I just don’t think holding that against you in here is going to be proactive in getting out. Sokka says he needs you and I trust him. So, for now I am willing to trust you too.”

Zuko flinched at her words and he shifted to sit cross legged to let his hands fall into his lap. He twirled his thumbs in a fidgeting manner and Suki waited for him to ask the questions he had.

“I…” the words died in his throat like a snuffed out fire and he sighed. Bowing his head in resignation.

Suki turned to watch him instead of the others. She really looked and had a hard time not seeing a lost, confused, and hurt teen just looking for anyone to show him some kind of kindness. It broke her heart, though she would never admit this. She instead picked up her food bowl to drop it into the hands that were mindlessly fidgeting on nothing causing the boy to jump in surprise. She smirked at him when his wide eyes were turned back to her.

“Listen, Zuko. It is going to take me a bit to trust you. But, do you know of how I came to trust Sokka?” the quick shake of his head told her all she needed to know and she settled back. “Eat, you firebenders seem to never have enough food. I’ll tell you how Sokka royally messed up and how through that we learned together.”

Zuko had hesitated far longer in accepting the food than Suki liked to see, but when he did she began her tale of the people who were dumb enough to tempt the Unagi, of the boy who disregarded everything that was not male in the form of foolish sexism, and of the slow work they had done to break that ridiculous notion down. She was a bit surprised to see the confused looks on the teens face as she mentioned Sokka believing women were not fighters. He had even muttered a small “but, Katara…” but quickly stopped, not wanting to interrupt the story too much. This made Suki laugh and she realized then that Zuko had never once looked at her warriors or other women and seen anything but the fighting figures they were.

“You know how this story ends, I’m afraid. You came in and ruined a lot of good roofs and building fronts. You were actually quite a scary figure for awhile but now that I think about it.” She paused to think over the rebuilding and actions taken that day. “It is kinda funny, where Sokka and the others were quick to dispute the Kyoshi warriors just for being women, you and your people fought us with the attention a warrior demands. You never underestimated us for being women even if you made remarks that spoke otherwise. You still never let your guard down full and only raised fire to flush out the Avatar. Your fires, while they were scary and awful and nightmare fuel, were confined to the main street and your group never entered the buildings or attacked any who did not prove they could and would fight back.

“You pushed forward. You were goal oriented, and you were fierce. You were powerful and dangerous. But, in a really weird way you were also the kindest in an attack that I have ever seen in any Fire Nation raid. You burned our mainstreet, which, shitty. You also retreated when the Avatar left and you ended all attacks without staying behind to finish the fires, kill anyone, or even steal. So, it might take me a bit, but I don’t see it taking long to actually forgive all things considered. If I can forgive Sokka, I could forgive Zuko after I get to know the real you.” She finished with a small smile and noticed how the boy had been staring at her in shocked silence.

“I was blinded by my need for the Avatar. I thought… I… Just wanted to go home. I was wrong, and in being wrong I burned your home and you are now here. Don’t forget. Don’t forgive.” He looked back down at the half eaten bowl of gray something with a heavy sigh.

Suki chuckled and nudged his shoulder with hers to get his attention again. “Funny thing about that, you don’t get a say in anything I think or do, Sunshine.” She winked and gasped as she watched the firebender suddenly flush slightly. He had curled away from her in an attempt to prevent her from seeing, but his pale skin made any color glow.

She lit up and gushed a quiet “Oh spirits! You are so expressive! Anyone ever tell you that you are cute with a little color?” She was enjoying this. She had missed doing this to Sokka and her girls more than she cared to admit and could see now that Zuko was going to be just as easy to work up. Perhaps even easier.

She paused at this line of thought. Suki guessed she really was about to forgive this firebender just like that. She was already lumping him in with Sokka in terms of teasing so that was sign enough as it was. Damn, he was just too easy to push and so honest in everything he did. Hard not to see that trust wall coming down sooner rather than later.

The flush darkened slightly as he looked around, heart rate clearly starting to pick up a bit at her intense attention. He cursed under his breath as he looked back at his half eaten food. It wasn’t his fault that it had been years since he had last heard that little term directed at him. It had brought back memories of his mother and of turtleducks on a warm day which was not what he had needed for this conversation. It had just startled him, that's all.

Zuko growled and twisted to glare up at her “Shut it, no one ever says that-”

“Kyoshi above you can’t lie, either, can you? It’s all in your eyes. Guess that is a good thing to know. Means I can trust what you said before. Look, Sunshine, yes it stuck so deal with it. What happened in the past is just that. Ready to work toward a future?” She stuck out a hand towards him and waited for him to take it. With that shake she was sure, he was trying to be better and she was going to help. He was starting to be a friend, now, after all.

## Chapter 4

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Hello!

This is my first ever fic so any comments, kudos, or overall thoughts are greatly appreciated!

Thank you so much! &#60;3

-Sorry about any changes or mass messages!

Just breaking up large paragraphs to make it look nicer. Nothing new.-

### Text

The food tasted awful. The guards food was at least flavored and palatable, this slime tasted like metal and sat in the gut as such. Zuko groaned as he forced himself to take another bite. Suki had gotten up to go talk with some of the other prisoners to not direct any more attention his way, which he was grateful for. The last thing Zuko needed was attention in a Fire Nation prison. Even if not everyone knew his new look, the scar and name were bound to give him away within moments if those here took the time to pay attention.

Zuko scanned the courtyard once more and stopped when he noticed Jet staring like he was what was wrong with the world. Anger boiled in Zuko and he snarled as he forced himself to look down. Deep breaths. That is what uncle always said, deep breathing when emotions were high. Not that it helped but Zuko always tried anyway. He would always try what uncle suggested unless it was music based.

Zuko took to letting his mind wander instead of thinking about everything. Uncle had said he always acted without thinking. This time he had thought, well, no. Zuko had acted with the thought that Sokka always knew what he was doing. Sokka was a leader, he was the obvious leader to the Avatar’s gang so it was a safe thing to assume that he would have had an idea on what to do, right? He should have realized that was not the case the moment he caught Sokka thinking of taking a bison to a boiling prison. That this was not at all thought through and when Zuko had suggested the war balloon Sokka had not really given it the time needed for one of his usual plans. Sokka hadn’t even been vocal or boasting like he usually was about his own brilliance but Zuko had bundled that all under the category of ‘he doesn’t trust me yet.’

Zuko sighed hard and muttered more to himself “Uncle was right to think me foolish, impulsive, and wrong.” Hearing it hurt more than Zuko had thought and the hole he had felt ever since his uncle had turned a silent back to him felt like ice in his core. He huddled up against the wall and prayed to Agni that Sokka would fix this. Better yet, he may just die here when Jet can’t hold back anymore and finds a way to slit his throat.

The bell chimed for those around to finish the mush they called food and get back to work. Zuko chugged the gray sludge with a shudder. At least the rock in his stomach meant he had something there to burn. He was used to burning his own energy but it was nice not needing to for a bit. He was about to head to the dish return when the bowl was snatched from his hands with such force his fingers stung. Looking up to meet the eyes he had tried so hard to avoid he could only huff and push off the wall to stand.

“Newbies arn’t supposed to eat. Who’d you steal from this time?” the sneer to the words was almost visible.

“No one. Suki left it so-”

“So you are stealing from ‘friends’ now huh? Not surprised. What would Sokka say if he knew you were taking from his girlfriend?” Jet was baiting him, he knew it.

Agni it was so hard not to let him do it. Anger rolled as his inner fire was ready to make quick work of the newly provided fuel. Zuko looked out to the crowd and away from Jet to not retort. Mistake, of course it was. Rule one when dealing with Jet, never turn your back to him.

Zuko had only seconds to realize his mistake as he felt the empty metal bowl strike him on the back of the head hard enough to cause him to stagger. His ears rung as he twisted to hold up an arm in preparation for the next strike that never landed. Instead, Zuko watched in shock as Jet, mid lunge stance, was flipped back and onto his own ass with his own momentum by a figure that had materialized out of nowhere. Said figure was now shifting their stance to cross their arms and glare at the boy on the ground.

Suki, Zuko realized when the ringing had stopped, had just taken on Jet for him and subdued him in seconds. Zuko vowed to never get on her bad side as he rubbed the back of his head and unfolded from his defensively pathetic stance.

Suki had said something to Jet, Zuko realized. Whatever it was had Jet glaring and muttering a few choice words Zuko promptly ignored in favor of prodding a particular soar spot on his head. No bleeding, good, but that was going to smart for awhile. Zuko glanced back to see Jet shuffle off with his group and couldn’t help the relief that flooded when the anger left him.

“Hey, you alright?” Suki’s voice had turned concerned and Zuko took a moment to realize it was directed at himself. He blinked and nodded which for some reason didn’t seem to be the answer she was looking for if her look was anything to go by.

“Let me see” her voice was still so calm and concerned. It made Zuko panic. This was nothing that warranted her attention like this. He turned to face her, keeping the back of his head away from her hand.

“It’s fine, no blood.” That was the main thing right, after all. An injure without blood was not one that demanded attention. Yet, her look didn’t change which made Zuko feel like he was wrong about something he couldn’t quite understand.

“Look, it’s fine. Caught me off guard is all. Uhm, thanks for that. We should look for Sokka. I’m sure he has a plan now.”

Suki gave him one last searching look before agreeing that Sokka needed to at least know what she had stepped in between. Zuko shifted uneasily at the thought. It was his fault after all that he and Jet had such an, uhm, interesting past. If Jet wanted to settle things than so be it. Zuko was more than willing to do so but not at the expense of getting the others out of this place.

The walk together was silent as they weaved through onlookers. Most looked rather put out that their entertainment had been so effectively snuffed out well before it began. They both came to realize looking for one specific guard was a dangerous and tricky thing. On the one hand this was good, meant Sokka was blending in. On the other it made the risk of asking the wrong guard a question very dangerous.

Zuko had been trusting in Suki knowing her boyfriend well enough to pick him out of a crowd. They twisted around a corner and Suki brightened some as she headed towards a smaller figure in the back. Zuko followed but quickly skidded to a stop and grabbed her elbow. While the size was right, Zuko knew just by the figures stance that they were not Sokka. Catching Suki’s confused glance he shook his head to try and convey this realization silently. The last thing they needed was to be vocal about looking for a fake guard. Suki looked back to see the man turn and tensed as she was met with a man very much not Sokka.

Looking back at Zuko she raised an eyebrow, daring him to tell how he knew so soon that the figure was not who they needed. Zuko just shrugged and very much did not flush at the look she was giving him. If he did it was only the damn heat of this place getting to him. So what if he knew Sokka’s relaxed stance habits. It was a good thing to be able to recognize friend from foe in every situation and Zuko was nothing if not hyper vigilant. One did not grow up in the palace with Azula as a sibling and live without learning a few things. Zuko knew Sokka from his stances just like he knew Suki from hers earlier, that’s all. Nothing to dwell on.

They were both shook out of their weird silent conversation by a giant figure approaching them. The massive firebender was heading straight towards them without pausing to make sure no one was watching and Zuko felt the panic in him raise with every step. He looked to Suki who did not seem to share this feeling and tried without any luck to take a ‘calming breath’ as uncle had always suggested. Suki looked at him funny after most likely hearing his rather pathetic attempt at breathing in the face of Chit Sang approaching and swiftly shifted to be between him and the massive man. Most likely she was viewing this as another Jet situation and was ready to step in. Zuko, however, was trying his best not to let the cold vice grip of panic flood him as he scanned around to see if others were paying attention. The sun was high and the area was so hot that most were just focused on finding a cool corner but that didn’t mean they were not being watched. Chit Sang, for his part, simply smiled and pointed to the corner they had been mopping earlier. He then pivoted and walked in that very direction without so much as a scanning glance.

Suki waited till Chit Sang turned around before looking over her shoulder at Zuko.

“Look, I know he is big and most likely not the most subtle but I don’t think Chit Sang is someone to worry about.”

“What? It’s not… I am not worried about Chit Sang being a threat.”

Suki raised an eyebrow and fully turned to face Zuko. Her face was even easy for him to read, which was a rarity really, and it screamed ‘Oh, really?’ which made Zuko sigh heavily.

“I mean, yeah he is big but he has done nothing to give us a reason to think he will tell the warden as long as we work together. It’s just, he isn’t subtle like you said. What if he blows everything by walking up to us like that?”

“Like what, Zuko? Like a prisoner to other prisoners in order to get some help with a chore? Look, I have been here for awhile now and I think he was here before me even. He isn’t dumb, Zuko. He wouldn’t risk his freedom on something so simple. Relax a bit, yeah? We could go sit for a second if you think it would help.” She finished her point by grabbing Zuko by the elbow like he had done with her moments before. Zuko flinched at the touch and pulled away. She did not fight him and was clearly studying his every reaction which just made Zuko feel even more uneasy. He wished she would just stop looking at him already.

“Sokka should be back soon. Let’s just go see what Chit Sang wants and forget about this.” Zuko rolled his shoulder to try and ease some tension. This did nothing and he swiftly ignored Suki as he headed in the direction of the corner. Suki stood for a moment longer before following after him and Zuko did not want to know what she was thinking. He had made enough of a fool of himself to her with Jet, the guard, and now Chit Sang. At this rate he might as well just cut his loss at her wanting much more to do with his level of poor luck and behavior.

&#45;

Sokka, for his part, had been sitting outside the prison grounds cursing and praising his luck at the same time. Just as he was patrolling the outer rim he had noticed that there was a gap where the guard towers could see. This gap, as luck would have it, fell right behind the wall that held all the coolers and would make Sokka’s plan so much easier. He had tested the current with a small paper boat. The boat had been pulled out onto the boiling lake just enough for him to be sure that the current was strong and continued out before it shriveled in the water.

Right, plan cooler boat was a go after all. All Sokka needed was to lock a firebender into a specific cooler and make sure they could do the job of unscrewing everything. That job would not be easy and for once Sokka was grateful to have a massive firebender on their team. Chit Sang had been in a cooler earlier for just enough time as Sokka was estimating they would need. If he could just get him to do it again and use that time to unscrew everything keeping that cooler locked in place then they would be set. Bing, Bang, Boom. Cooler Boat!

Sokka took a last look at the coolers and shuddered. It would have to be Chit Sang. The man was huge and physically should be able to shoulder that cold one more time. Suki and Jet were not firebenders after all and the risk of hypothermia at those temperatures was too great to even begin thinking about. Sokka would do it himself but that fell into the same category as the others. While Sokka had cold in his veins thanks to living in the Southern Water Tribe, that did not mean he could go much longer than others. Just meant he knew what to do to not lose a limb or two when it came to the nasty effects that came with the cold.

Sokka was very much ignoring that they indeed had another firebender in their group. While Zuko would most likely do it just because they asked it of him, the idea of locking Zuko in a cooler did not sit well in Sokka’s stomach. The images of Zuko huddled like Chit Sang had been flashed in Sokka’s mind and Sokka had to shake his head physically to clear it. Nope, not happening. Zuko was too thin to handle it anyway. Compared to Chit Sang, Zuko’s body mass would freeze so much faster. That was reason enough to want to keep a friend out of that horrible place, right?

That thought had caught him off guard and Sokka forced himself to sit with it. Zuko and friend in the same sentence? Zuko, who had chased Aang and everyone relentlessly since Aang first left that iceberg. Zuko, who had burned Suki’s village and been an absolute thorn in their sides all the way to the Northern Water Tribe. How had he even managed to break into the Northern Water Tribe? Sokka was going to drill him about that bit later and the boy would tell him.

Back to listing his shitty qualities. Zuko, who was the reason Aang was shot down by Azula so that he could run home to his little evil family and live a happy evil life. He had hurt Katara with that and Sokka doubted it was ever going to be settled between them. Zuko, who had tracked them down yet again after supposedly running away from said evil perfect life to claim he had always been wrong and that they should take him as Aang’s firebending teacher at the very least. That had been a laugh. Even Aang had hesitated! Toph, however, decided to believe him having not had to deal with his specific brand of shit before. Those two had a weird thing going. Toph was attached to Zuko like a spidermonkey most chances she gets and he lets her. Zuko, the boy who takes every shitty thing said to him with his head bowed as if he has heard it all before and said worse yet to himself. The boy who skips meals because Katara is in too much a mood to look at him. Sokka knows personally that if asked he will say he wasn’t hungry at the time but suspects that is a lie. Ever since Aang started firebending the kid puts away twice his normal amount and that can’t be a coincidence. Zuko, who read Sokka so well that he knew Sokka was going to come to this death hole and did nothing but offer to come along without hesitation. Okay, offer was kind. Zuko had forced himself into Sokka’s admittedly shitty escape plan but still. Zuko was now patching things with Suki and would bend to Sokka’s every need if he asked it of him.

Shit, Sokka was going to be screwed when Katara found out but there was no looking at this in any other way.

Sokka trusts Zuko and sees him as a friend. This was not supposed to be the outcome of this adventure.

## Chapter 5

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

This is my first fic, any and all comments or kudos make my day.

Thank you so much, hope you enjoy some more Jet time. He is on the move now.

### Text

The hall looked clear as Sokka slipped back into the prison. He had spent so much time sitting outside with his thoughts that he was going to be late to their meeting but that would be fine if he could just be sure he had all needed pieces. He had worked out the three key parts. All he needed was a wrench, Chit Sang to just suck it up, and for the others to be ready to move on his word.

Nothing too hard, last time he had the starting of a plan the first part went surprisingly well. Suki and Zuko were at least on working terms and Sokka did not give that a second glance. He knew what Zuko’s shifting look meant and that if Suki told him to do anything he would. Sokka had to just make sure it wasn’t something like ‘jump in the lake’ or some other way to hurt himself. That firebender had less self preservation than Aang when his ‘I’m the Avatar’ bullshit came out in full force.

Glancing around to check if any other guards were around before he opened the first door he found. The inside room was piled high with cleaning supplies that looked mostly unused. Possibly back stock but not anything that would be of assistance now. Sokka had to locate a wrench if any of his plans were to work. This place was built of literal metal, a wrench would be needed for any repairs to that framework. The constant heating of the metal was bound to cause expansion warps and other structural damage pretty regularly. If he could just find the maintenance room and swipe one that matched the parts in the coolers they would be set. Plan Cooler Boat was just that simple, and the simpler the plan the better. The more likely it was to have a few major holes as well but Sokka would not let himself sit on that thought.

Pulling open a few more doors only to be met with nothing useful Sokka groaned as he heard the bell chime for guard rotation. He knew he had only a few more minutes to find this damn thing before either the hall was flooded with people or he was expected in the courtyard to meet the others.

Ducking behind another set of doors as other guards approached Sokka found himself in yet another storage closet. What was with this place and storing everything in disorganized closets that were not even locked? It was a recipe begging for prisoners to break in and use items found to start riots. Almost like that was wanted to some degree.

It was. That was the point of it all. Sokka realized that these closets were not locked because they were a test. They were testing the will of the prisoners and those who revolted using whatever they could find, or tried to hide like Sokka was now, probably bought one way tickets to the cooler. If they were firebenders it was used to tamper their spirits. If they were not, it was a show of ‘look what happened to your friend’ and if that person lived was irrelevant. What this was in the end was a barbaric and cruel way to break people down. To dangle something like safety just out of reach.

Sokka had to take a few deep breaths as he tried hard to not imagine people in the cooler because of him. Suki, they would definitely take pride in freezing her spirit and Sokka had a hard time moving from the image of her so icy cold the shivers did not even shake her form. Sokka knew cold, shivering sucked but wasn’t the worst thing like many thought. It was when the shivers stopped that was the dangerous part. When the inner temperature succumbed to the outer and became too much for the body to regulate.

The next image that flashed was that of a shivering firebender. Shivering was good, so why did this mental image upset Sokka just as much as the one of Suki? The figure in Sokka’s mind shifted and he saw almost as clear as if they were actually there in the cooler. Icy blue lips, pale skin the color of frost, molten gold eyes hazed over, and his large scar pulled tight with cold to the point of splitting in places but too cold to bleed.

‘Cold to a firebender is deadly if left unchecked. Freezing an inner flame is a sure way to kill one slowly if you do it right’ rang throughout Sokka’s thoughts and he had to physically slap himself to wake up from them. Now was not the time. Zuko and Suki were safe-ish for now and neither were in a cooler. They wouldn’t ever be if Sokka had something to say about it unless it was on the way across that boiling lake. He had only to find that damn wrench.

Standing to dust himself off, he hadn’t even been aware that he sat through that little mental spiral. He was going to need a proper fishing vacation after this was all over. Maybe he and Suki could take a private little trip. He was sure she needed one just as much as he did. That was a much better image. Suki in a bathing suit swimming in the lake. He could see her laugh as she waved at him while he was fishing. Maybe he would even catch an elephantkoi! He could see her give a grin that spoke of trouble as she pulled up a bucket full of water and turned toward the shore where a sleeping Zuko lay in the sun like a cat- wait. Hold on. What was Zuko doing in his Suki fantasy? This was a Suki in a bikini with Sokka kind of moment. Yet there he was, snoozing rather rudely on the beach like he damn well fit in this picture. Worse yet, he did. Sokka needed to get some sleep. He was zoning out far too much.

A quick peek out the door told him the hall was once more empty and he slipped out to continue on. This time he would go at this without such a random tactic. He was definitely late, might as well make it count.

He rounded a corner to the maze that was Boiling Rock and had to brace himself as he collided with a figure that had been moving far too fast to be where it was supposed to. Sokka rubbed his chest and groaned as he looked up to see Jet standing in front of him panting slightly.

“Sokka! There you are. We got worried when you didn’t turn up, yeah?” He flashed a large and friendly smile that Sokka did not trust for shit. “What are you doing here? This is really far from the courtyard.”

Sokka shifted to stand up straighter. This was Jet so he knew to tread carefully, but he was also supposedly willing to work with them all to get out. If there was one thing Jet would do, it was anything to save his own skin. That meant for now they had a common goal and Sokka couldn’t let the past blind him.

“I need to find something for my plan to work. Just, got lost in this place. Why are there so many pointless closets anyway?” Sokka knew why, he just wasn’t ready to admit it.

Jet laughed and waved it off “Ashmakers can’t organize anything. This place runs like a machine oiled by mud, Sokka. If you were here longer you’d see what I mean. They have one good scare tactic and it works.”

Jet fell into step beside Sokka as they weaved back towards the courtyard, making sure not to take turns that had people in them. It was clear just by how easily they seemed to avoid groups that Jet, for his word, did know his way around this place. That might be useful.

“Say, you wouldn’t happen to know where the maintenance room is would you?” Sokka peeked behind them just to make sure they were fully alone. Last thing he needed was a Cooler Boat with too many bodies.

“Oh yeah, they got a few of ‘em. What you needing? I might be able to get it pretty easily.” That smile was back and it made Sokka’s insides crawl.

“I need a wrench. Specifically I need one that could be used to unbolt a cooler from the inside.” He was in it now. He just had to hope Jet wouldn’t steal this for himself. Sokka gave that a thought and realized Jet couldn’t steal this plan. For all he wanted out, Jet did not have a single firebender in his little group he had made here most likely. He had a reputation of hating them, why would they help him now? Sokka could relax and let Jet help him. Hopefully.

Jet seemed to think this over and chuckled “man, you really are a genius. Sokka, you are too good for your group you know that? They are lucky to have a guy like you, no really!”

“Look, thanks and all, but right now I just really need to get a wrench and we need to get this in motion. The longer I take the worse it is going to be to get it into action. We don’t have a lot of time as it is.” Zuko’s time specifically was ticking away like a dooms day clock and Sokka could almost hear it.

Jet nodded at this and turned them down a different hall “yeah, yeah. I get it. You need to get out of here and back to the Avatar. Look, if you don’t mind my asking-”

“I probably do, but you are going to ask anyway.”

Jet chuckled and shook his head. “You haven’t changed much.”

“Sorry, just. Last few times we engaged with each other didn’t end with you in the best of lights. I am still waiting to hear your grand tale of survival but will put that off for now. What were you wondering?” Sokka had to try and at least play nice. This man was to be Toph’s punching bag present after all.

Jet gave him a sickeningly sweet smile at this before his face fell to one of pure concern “since when are you working with Lee? I don’t know what he said or did to get your trust, Sokka, but you can’t trust him.”

This gave Sokka pause and he had to physically stop walking to think about that. Did he know someone named ‘Lee’ at all? No, but Jet had used that name once before when emotions were high and had directed it at Zuko.

“Wait, Jet, do you mean Zuko?”

Jet’s face fell into a serious glare and he nodded once “see, that is what I mean! I met him in Ba Sing Se, Sokka. He was living under the name Lee then and was hiding the fact that he was a firebender. He even worked with me for a bit, the lying vipersnake, before we saw him for what he was. Him and his uncle were there, laying in wait to attack the city from the inside during the battle.”

“Jet, I don’t think you have all the facts on this topic” Sokka stated much more confidently than he felt.

It was true that Zuko was in Ba Sing Se. Katara had met him in the prison under the Earth Kings palace. He had to have been there for a reason that was against Azula, right? No, not necessarily. That was also where Zuko had turned on them fully and helped his batshit crazy sister shoot Aang down. He had turned seemingly on a whim but what if that wasn’t entirely true? What if Jet was right and that Zuko had been there the whole time in order to corner them. To help his family win. What if he was here now helping them only to do it again? Was this why he wasn’t in some special holding cell and allowed to mingle with others? They were not actually sending word to his family and he was actually just playing a really messed up long game?

Jet did not seem phased by Sokka telling him off. Instead he opened a door with a quick twist and disappeared inside. Sokka had all of a second to panic before he reappeared and tossed the wrench Sokka had been searching for at him. Jet was lucky Sokka had reflexes or that would have been loud.

“Doesn’t look like you have all the facts on that moment either. Believe me or not, no man who uses two names is trustworthy. Whatever his goal, it is nothing good for you or your gaang and you are better off leaving him here where he belongs.”

“Thanks Jet, I’ll keep that in mind but right now Zuko is not the main concern.”

Sokka pocketed the wrench under his armor and turned on his heel to head towards the courtyard. He hated to admit that Jet’s words were making him think and not in a good way. He had to trust Zuko if this was going to work and the firebender had given Sokka no reason not to since coming back with Aang after their little vacation. He even did that silly dance with Aang every time he was asked even if he grouched his way through. Sokka just needed to see Zuko again. That would make everything clear up. Jet was just being Jet.

Jet, for his part, was silent for all of a minute.

“He is playing Suki now. Did you know that?”

Sokka huffed “oh that I highly doubt, Jet. I don’t know how much you know about Suki but she is not one to be played.”

“Oh but she is. You had said they have history, right? During lunch she went to talk to him even after I offered a space in my group to sit. They spent all of lunch break mostly huddled together and he even took her food. The prison here is pretty shitty when it comes to rations, Sokka. We don’t ever get the food we need to really feel full. She went to talk to him and all he had to do was give his little sob story and turn on those hurt turtleduck eyes. That’s all it takes. He is a master at getting others to do what he wants, be careful.”

Sokka snorted and then let out a bark of laughter he couldn’t hold back. “Wait, wait. Let me get this straight. Y-you think Zuko-Zuko of all people is a master manipulator? Jet, that man can’t introduce himself or keep a level head to save his own skin.” He cleared his throat to lower his voice as the neared the main exit.

“Sokka, I mean it. Whatever he did or said to her has her wrapped around his finger. You’ll see soon enough. I don’t care what you think you know, I don’t trust that ashmaker.”

Sokka stopped and watched Jet lead the way out to the main courtyard. While he had been right that getting part one of his plan had been a weird success, he was not so sure about the rest of the pieces. He was late enough as it was so it was about time to just get this going. Right, part two: put Chit Sang into the cooler and promptly NOT listen to Jet or think about Zuko.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

## Chapter 6

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Small mention of overstimulation and panic in this chapter.

Let me know what you think! &#60;3

### Text

Zuko twisted around to swat away the hand that had reached out towards his head with a glare. Frustration was fighting to burn through some of his extra anxious energy and Suki would just not back off. He let out a small growl as he glared her down which she had the audacity to roll her eyes at.

“You are being ridiculous. Sokka is late and you have been rubbing that same spot for minutes now. Let me look.” Suki moved again, and this time Zuko realized he was in trouble.

This little back and forth had been going on for a few minutes now much to Chit Sang’s amusement. Ever since the guard rotation had sounded and Sokka had not shown.

Zuko couldn’t help but worry and when he did he had a bad habit of putting pressure on things that ached to distract himself. It was a habit built out of years of having aches and pains to constantly adjust to. Years at sea were hard on the body but usually it was his scar that got the brunt of it. Though it no longer felt much of anything, the pressure around it could build to be uncomfortable when tired or stressed.

Jet had just so happened to have given Zuko a new pain to poke at mindlessly. It didn’t even hurt anymore, really, yet he had still brought enough attention to it that he was stuck in a cat and mouse game with Suki. Correction, by her change in stance he was about to lose said game.

Chit Sang barked out a great laugh as he watch the fiery Earth Kingdom girl shift her stance and corner the prince of the Fire Nation with such ease. That action alone would have brought shame to any Fire Nation royalty had they been anywhere but in a prison.

Zuko glared at the beast of a man behind Suki for how loud his laughing was but Suki had been right. No one was paying attention to Chit Sang and his play things. He had been here far too long for others to really care to watch him.

“Back off, I don’t need you-” his words were cut short as he had to drop to the floor and push off the wall behind himself in order to roll under her arm and avoid her grab. Damn, she was fast. He popped up on her right and shifted to try and put distance between them.

“Not bad, Sunshine. You have good footwork for a bender.” Suki sighed out as if he was the one making her job hard.

She had the audacity to simply stretch like she hadn’t just tried to tackle him! Zuko did not relax and only felt a slight raise of panic at her next action. In all honesty, she had only needed to order him to stop or demand he listen to her and Zuko knew he would have. He owed her so much and he was being a jerk by running around. She had every right to attack him but it was not her job to make sure he was alright.

She raised her arms above her head and twisted lightly, flexing her wrists this way and that while tilting her head to the left. Stretching as if she had just finished some kind of work and was ready for a break. Zuko’s eye twitched as he watched her. Not trusting the movements but taking great offense in them.

Zuko huffed and stood up straighter only to be forced to jump back to avoid a grabbing strike. Suki had gone from full open stretch to grabbing at his shoulder in the matter of seconds. If Zuko wasn’t used to fighting hand to hand that would have been all she had needed to snag him. As it was Zuko shuffled to the left and swiftly slammed into what felt like an overly warm pole.

Said pole had just closed around his shoulders with a chuckle and a coo. “Oh, she is good. You didn’t even see her little order did ya? See, she had your full attention.”

Zuko twisted hard and looked up at Chit Sang who had just grabbed hold of him supposedly on Suki’s order. “Let go of me.”

“Oh relax, would you. We are all on the same side and you are being ridiculous.” Suki waved off Zuko’s arguments and slid to be in front of him. “Now, are you going to let me take a look or are you going to continue to be stubborn as a mule-ox?”

Zuko huffed out a thin line of steam Suki quickly waved away. Her hand slapped over his mouth and she looked around. For the first time that day Zuko could see the fear in her eyes as she scanned the area.

Her voice a whisper “keep the fire to zero, Sunshine. They will not think twice at putting you in a cooler just for steam.”

Zuko nodded and willed himself to relax, heart beating rather hard after that little game of chase ending with a jump scare. Chit Sang seemed to know that Zuko was admitting defeat and slowly relaxed his hold around the teens shoulders.

Sinking to sit on his knees between the two of them, Zuko glared at his hands in his lap and bowed his head. He could feel Suki roll her eyes but she sat next to him on his right side anyway.

“Anyone ever tell you you are dramatic?” She was gently carding through his hair and Zuko had to use everything in his power not to pull away.

“Hey, deep breaths, almost done.” Her voice was almost just a whisper in his ear yet it sounded so loud.

Why did everything suddenly sound so loud? His breathing was too loud and starting to go at an odd rhythm. The sun was too bright, the clanging of metal was too loud, the looks- were people even looking?- were too much.

Zuko grit his teeth and closed his eyes tight. Dammit, where was Sokka? Sokka was supposed to be here already. Sokka was supposed to have a plan. Sokka was supposed to… do something! Zuko knew right now that if Sokka would just hurry up and get things back on track then everything would be in motion again, if he would just show up.

He flinched hard as a sharp pain shot from the back of his head. Cracking his right eye open he looked at Suki for her opinion on what she had found.

“Well, it is not nothing but you wont die.” She smiled down at him and winked. “It is going to be a nasty little egg for a few days and probably hurts to knock it on anything, but overall not anything serious.”

“Happy now?” was all Zuko could croak out and even to his own ear it sounded rough. The look he earned from that was the most confusing. She had smiled at him a smile he had only really seen directed at others. Like Katara when she watched Sokka do something stupid but thought no one was watching her.

“Yes, thank you. Are you happy now?”

“I’m never happy.” It was such an automatic response that Zuko didn’t even know he had spoken but Suki was laughing hard and somehow just focusing on her was making things less overwhelming. She felt like a safe person to be near, and that was scary to think.

She might be safe for him here, but the second they leave Sokka will leave Zuko behind in favor of her. He knew it, and had to accept it the moment Sokka had found her here. Zuko was Aang’s firebending teacher, nothing else.

Someone cleared their throat and everyone tensed at the sound. Suki was the first to move as Zuko still had his head bowed to her. He was fully reliant on her reaction and knew it was good the moment she moved towards the sound freely.

Looking up Zuko saw Sokka heading over to them with Jet leaning against the wall where Zuko had been before Suki had chased him.

Sokka had a weird expression on his face and Zuko quickly stood to join Suki in heading towards the two boys. Chit Sang smiled as he too moved to be close enough to listen but not enough to engage more than that.

Jet had said something to Sokka with a smirk that made Zuko’s frustration flare up yet again. He wanted nothing more than to burn that smile from Jet’s smart mouth. Glancing to Sokka, Zuko slowed his steps and came to a stop just behind Suki. Sokka’s face looked pained. Whatever Jet had said had upset him in some way and the simmering anger in Zuko’s blood was ready to boil. If Jet was hurting Sokka, Zuko was ready to risk the cooler.

Whatever the problem, Zuko had a burning suspicion he might be at the root of it all and that made the rock in his stomach shift uneasily. Would they leave him here? It would be their best bet, thinking on it. That look Sokka had given him while he was standing from his spot near Suki spoke of nothing but bad feelings. Was he angry at Zuko already? What had he done this time? Before he could continue thinking Sokka cleared his throat again.

“Right, uhm, sorry I’m late but I think I have a way out of here. So, uh, Cooler Boat.”

Everyone blinked and looked at each other in confusion.

“Sokka, I think we are going to need a bit more information than that.” Suki rolled her eyes as Sokka shifted uneasily.

“Yeah, yeah. Okay. Here it is. I patrolled outside and found a point with no guards and where the towers can’t see. We need to just get one of the coolers down from the main floor to that spot and let the current pull us to the other side. I double checked everything, and I am confident it will work.”

“Wait, how are you going to get said cooler out of the wall?” Suki was staring at Sokka with concern. This was going in a bad direction.

Sokka sighed heavily “see, that is the hard part. Chit Sang, I need you to go into the coo-”

“Not on Agni’s light will I ever willingly go back in there. Get another firebender to do it.”

Sokka growled “look, it would be the last time! I know it is not great and all but-”

“Not great? Kid, you don’t know shit-”

“I grew up in the Southern Water Tribe. I think I know pretty damn well what the temperatures in that thing feel like.”

Zuko looked between the two glaring ice. This was not good and if Sokka was trying for sundown they were losing time quickly. Zuko looked around before catching Suki’s questioning stare. She nodded, seemingly knowing where he was about to go with this.

“I’ll do it.”

Chit Sang sighed in relief at the same moment Sokka turned a furious glare at Zuko.

“No, absolutely not. You are not going into a cooler just because the biggest guy here is scared.”

Zuko’s anger was back. He was not about to let Sokka ruin everything because he had some weird fixation about Chit Sang being the one to do this part. He was also not at all okay with Sokka speaking to him like that when he was trying his damn best at being good.

“Look, Sokka, he doesn’t want to do it. We don’t have time to argue. Give me the damn tools and I will go-”

“No. I said no and I mean it. You are not made to withstand the cooler. You will freeze far more than he will and you have never been in one before. Hard enough regulating in a new environment so -”

“The fuck you mean ‘not made for it' and 'never been in one before’ Sokka!? I literally swam into the Northern Water Tribe by following the turtle seals! I think a cooler, which is not in ice cold water, is very much something I could regulate in!”

This little outburst was met with a duel “you did what!?” from both Suki and Sokka but Zuko ignored it. He could practically taste the fire on his tongue and was livid that Sokka was risking this plan on a foolish idea that Zuko was unable to handle slight cold.

Sokka’s shock bled into his voice as he tried to keep from yelling. It must have been Agni's blessings that they had yet to be caught. “Zuko, you- I can’t- Turtle Seals can hold their breath for 45 minutes! Did you know this?”

Zuko felt his retort die as he took in that bit of information. He had, in fact, only been aware of the fact that they could swim fast and breathed air. The fact of just how long they could hold their breath seemed unimportant at the time. His face must have given everything he had been thinking away as usual because Sokka groaned out in clear frustration.

“That is exactly what I mean! You don’t stop to think about-”

“I know that! Uncle made it very clear I don’t think, thanks for the reminder.” Zuko couldn’t keep the hurt crack out of his voice at the mention of his uncle.

“Zuko, that’s not… I didn’t mean it like that.”

His anger drained as quickly as it had flared and Zuko just felt heavy with everything now. That was a low blow even for Sokka and Zuko could see now that this had nothing to do with the cold.

“You don’t trust me, do you? You don’t think I can or will actually do this. You think I will somehow mess it all up. You are more willing to put your faith in a complete stranger than in me.” Zuko could no longer look at Sokka and closed his eyes to sit with his reality.

Suki was the one to act first and turned a glare onto Sokka that had his insides squirming. She punched his arm like Toph does when wanting attention, which was always just hard enough to hurt.

“Sokka, Zuko said he can do it and we don’t have time for this. You said so yourself that time is running out. Give him the tools and trust him.” She turned her glare onto Zuko who flinched away “and don’t you dare think I am dropping that little ‘swam with the turtle seals’ bit just yet. We will be having a talk later, Sunshine.”

Zuko could only swallow and nod. He was not keen on filling her in on that particular story, or anyone for that matter. Not one of his better anythings if he was to be honest.

Sokka spluttered and almost seemed to pout at the fact that Suki had a nickname for Zuko. Hey, that wasn’t his fault. He had never agreed to it and she was the one using a name that was longer than Zuko’s actual name. He was just used to Toph and her nicknames so he was trying to not let this particular one get to him.

Through all of this one figure had remained silent. Jet was still leaning against the wall watching everything unfold with a grin. He lazily pushed off to slide his way over to Sokka and grab something out his back pocket. Sokka looked back at Jet with a confused stare before seeing the wrench Jet held in his hand. Sokka twisted to fully face Jet and glared hard.

“Jet, what are you doing? Give me the wrench.” He held out his hand expectantly.
