Jet smirked “you need a man in the cooler, right? A firebender, yeah?”

Sokka’s glare hardened as he moved to be in his way more fully. “Jet, whatever you think you are doing to help I need you to stop right now.”

Zuko watched as Jet palmed the wrench and seemingly ignored Sokka completely. Jet didn’t even let Sokka finish whatever he had wanted to say before locking eyes with Zuko who knew instantly where he was going with this. If Zuko was going into the cooler then at least he was going to get to kick Jet’s ass a bit beforehand. That was reason enough to catch the wrench that was just tossed over Sokka.

Zuko tucked it into the under layer of his clothes seconds before Jet was in front of him screaming his loud war cry. If no one was looking before, they sure as Agni were looking now.

Using his forearms to brace against Jet’s full weight body slam, Zuko felt his back slam hard into the metal floor. He was vaguely aware of the fact that someone had just screamed something at Jet but it was clear the teen could care less. He had a golden chance to attack Zuko, and Zuko had the chance to firebend at him. In their books, this was a win-win.

“See, *baby*, what I tell ya? No one trusts the likes of you.” Jet hissed in Zuko’s ear as he had him pinned to the ground.

His words cut deep but now was not the time to think. Sokka’s look of pain and then anger flashed before he could fully stop it, followed by Suki’s look from the morning and one of a warrior in paint surrounded by fire. Zuko let out a growl as he forced himself to focusing on one thing, Jet.

Twisting hard, Zuko used the momentum from his fall to throw Jet off of himself. He quickly pushed up from the ground and spun around to regain his footing before Jet could lunge again. They were a flurry of painful punches, kicks, and raw emotion. Where Jet landed a harsh blow to Zuko’s right shoulder, he was in turn met with an elbow to the face.

The problem was Jet was coming after Zuko too fast for Zuko to actually safely firebend at him. As much as Zuko wanted to burn the shit out of him, he knew the others were watching and the Jet was still oddly part of this team. He had to break away from him just enough to let out a flame if this was going to be worth anything more than pain.

Finding an opening was hard and Zuko was so focused on not burning Jet that he let Jet hook his leg and trip him. Falling hard onto the same shoulder Jet had hit moments before he let out a loud hiss at the sharp pain. He was too slow to twist away from the next punch fully and felt it connect with the left side of his face with a sickening smack as his head slammed back into the metal floor. Spots danced in his vision as pain rang from his entire being.

Zuko ignored his bodies demand to roll away and grabbed the wrist in front of his face that had failed to follow through on the punch. He pulled with all the strength he had in that position, using his whole body to buck Jet off and throw him as hard and far away as he could.

Shuffling up quickly Zuko took this as the chance needed and went through a pathetic display of a bare basic bending form to produce a flame that fizzled out well before reaching Jet who was still in the process of sitting up.

As soon as the fire dissipated Zuko felt a new set of strong hands grab his aching shoulder and force him to kneel.

“No firebending! Into the cooler!” Rang through the gathered audience as Zuko was lifted by his arms.

They were being twisted and held at such odd angles behind his back that they felt like they were breaking. He had to grit his teeth to keep from doing anything more than hissing at the pain this angle caused his throbbing shoulder as he was led away from the main courtyard and towards the hall of coolers.

His first thought was how this had all worked, even if not to what Sokka had wanted. Sokka had come back and it was time that Zuko got to do something to finally prove himself even if the other boy would rather just move forward without him. His second was that he fucking hated Jet and his body was going to hurt after that stunt.

He settled on swallowing hard when the cooler door opened and the blast of cold sent Zuko mentally back to that icy floor in the turtle seal cave where for a few brief moments he had feared freezing to death.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

This is my first time doing any kind of action-y writing so please let me know what you think!

All the love.

-Lynnrose

## Chapter 7

Zuko stumbled forward as his arms were released seconds before he was shoved head first into the tight space. Bracing his face with his forearm, he was able to just prevent yet another collision for the day and spun around to face the door. He glared his best ‘I will kill you’ glare, which he had perfected with his many years of being constantly angry, and listened as the man laughed while dragging the door shut.

“Chill out, kid.” He had the audacity to make light of a torture device made specifically to chill one to death, and this sent a shudder through Zuko’s core that had nothing to do with the cold.

How many had he sealed into this death pit with that very line? How many had that been the last words someone heard said to them? If he was lucky, none. Zuko knew the reality. He was never that lucky.

Glancing around the space he saw layers of thick frost and ice in every direction but no red mixed into it. Either he was actually lucky and no one died here, they went quietly, or they cleaned these things. His money was on that last one.

He dropped into a squat with a shiver and took a moment to just breathe in the cold air. His body had felt it well before he had been shoved forward. They kept these things at dangerously low temperatures and given the ice build up on the tight walls this temperature was a constant and maintained. It was built for a firebender but Zuko had heard whispers that anyone could be detained in them for certain infractions. Escape being the main one.

Taking two deep breaths of cold air, he focused on finding his inner flame which was not as easy as he would have liked given his distracted state, but still so much easier than in the Northern Water Tribe. His head throbbed thanks to Jet’s ‘helping’ and the left side of his face felt far colder than the rest. He had to guess there was a bit of blood, most likely the punch had split some skin on his scar, and it had frozen already.

Coughing once, hard, he cleared his throat of any extra moisture and took one more deep breath. He brought that cold air as deep into his lungs as he could, feeling it trying to freeze him from the inside out, and fed it to his inner flame. Holding it there he slowly let his core warm the air and pushed it back out. The air that left him steamed instantly and he could feel the temperature of his space heating for a second before freezing over again.

Good. He had found his core and it would only take a minute or so to warm his lungs. From there it was just a matter of building energy and spreading it through his limbs. Easiest when huddled so he would have to make slow work of the cooler. That was fine. That was a job. That was a goal to achieve and even if Sokka thought him unable, Zuko never gave up without a fight. He would not lose this one.

Breathing in and out a few more times he decided to start burning some of the energy given to him by Suki. He was going to have to be very careful and calculating on how to use that or they might all be in trouble. When he thought on it, that lunch she had offered as just some kind of peace treaty might just save his firebending.

If he was right, prolonged exposure to this cold would render a firebender ‘frozen’ which was nearly a death sentence on any other occasion. Here it was most likely used to limit or eliminate benders abilities for a given amount of time.

Continuing to breathe, Zuko remembered his first experience with being ‘frozen’ back when his hunt for the Avatar had just begun. He had started training with uncle after months of healing and was furious to learn that his flames were weaker than they had been before. He would never tell anyone that he knew they would be, the sight of even his own fire terrified him then, and no matter how little practice they did he would always dream of flames and pain after.

He had remembered one particularly cold night where he had woken up over heating and screaming. His scream that time was near silent and seemed to not wake anyone so he had taken it as a good sign, but he had also panicked. The fire in the room had been too hot despite the below freezing temperatures outside and he had acted on fear. He had snuffed out the fires and spent the night huddled as far away from any light as he could. That had put a young and pained Zuko under his window throughout a freezing night with howling winds. Come morning, his body was stiff and cold to the core. He had not even realized he was awake really until he felt his uncle pulling him up and into another room much too hot.

Later, when he had tried to firebend he found that his insides were as icy as the outside air and no amount of heat would come to him no matter how hard he tried. He had panicked like only a child could. He had screamed, punched and kicked out in hopes that flames would form, and felt severed from a part of himself like he had never felt before. It was as if a core part of his being had been slowly drained away to nothing without him even realizing. It was terrifying and left the body feeling so weak.

He would never tell a soul, but in that moment he had thought Agni had finally seen how pathetic he was and how unworthy of their blessings he had become. He thought Agni had taken his bending, and he had felt Agni was right to do so. He could still see the faces the crew made towards him during that time. Looks of disgust at the pathetic thing they had been ordered to deal with.

It had taken nearly two weeks for the sun to suddenly feel like it seeped through his very being again and mixed in his core. Two weeks of sitting in that very sun and feeling it only on the skin, as if it had turned away from him. It was horrible and this cooler must get a firebender pretty damn near close to that feeling.

He estimated that, at his current size and strength, a full day with no food or water would be enough to render himself ‘frozen’ for at least a week without his breathing. Uncle had been insistent on that lesson ever since he had frozen himself that night so many years ago. Zuko was beyond thankful for it now.

With one last steam filled exhale he stood up to really look at the structure and get a general game plan. His first thought was to just attack the seal as soon as possible but a voice that sounded oddly like his uncle told him to go slowly. If he went at this with speed he would most definitely be caught by the guard outside and that would prove everyone right. He needed to use what little patience he had if he was going to do this right and Agni above he wanted to do this right.

The structure did not seem to be too difficult to sever from the wall as he had first originally believed. It seemed to him that these things were built somewhere far away from the Boiling Rock and brought in at a later date. They would have made them, tested them most likely in a controlled setting just to see if they could maintain their temperature, and sent them here.

The reason this was a huge jump in luck was because this meant that the cooler was attached to the wall, not built into it. All the weird parts Sokka had talked about, that honestly had made no sense in Zuko’s opinion so he had tuned them out, would come off with the cooler itself. They would not have to pull extra parts of the wall off. If Zuko could get the door loose, they might even be able to leave that behind and not leave a gaping hole.

What Zuko really needed to find was the sensor. These coolers maintained a temperature somehow, so there was bound to be a sensor. If he could sever it now he could see the reactions it caused. At most they would move him to one of the other coolers and just put this one for maintenance. At the least they would leave him in it and shut the sensor off to deal with later. Which would buy them some time rolling this thing away before they realized that it was gone.

To his surprise, he found no sensor. He was unsure if this was true or if he was just not sure what he was actually looking for. If this was made to keep cold like a freezer- that’s it! This wasn’t a normal cell made to be kept perfect this was just a fancy freezer. There was no sensor but there would be a temperature gauge on the outside door. Zuko couldn’t help but smirk to himself. That was a mystery solved and he was damn proud to have been the one to figure it out. See? He could think things through!

Shit, if this thing kept track on temperature and displayed it on the outside then Zuko was going to have to cut back on his breathing of hot air. Last thing he would need was to be moved halfway through his hard work or for them to realize what he was doing.

He had long since realized that, while firebending came from ones core and breathing, most benders could not bring it up through their airways like he could. His uncle was better at it, but he had always been pretty natural at releasing built up fire energy through dramatic breaths or screaming. His fire burned everything in him, it only made sense it followed his own anger up and out.

Now when he let his fire follow those well worn paths though it never seemed to feel like it was singeing anymore. Ever since the dragons if Zuko thought about it. His fire burned brighter but not hotter. It came to him as easy as breathing. It was a comfort now, and no longer hurt his core just by existing. No longer was it fed by pain and anger and burned with such. Now it was his life and burned as a reminder. The breathing was easier than the Northern Water Tribe time because it was life, not him fighting even his own body to cough up those flames.

Zuko caught sight of the guard outside the small window in the door. He was shifting and was most likely going to look in to check on his prisoner so Zuko dropped quickly into a squat. He forced his body to shiver. It was easy with the years of actually shivering while hunting a myth that turned into a child bent on leading you to every cold corner of the world and then some. He was about to stand again when a voice in his head, this time sounding more like Sokka than he cared to think about as it made his anger stir at the thought of him, told him not yet and to keep his head down.

This had turned out to be right as the cooler door was ripped open and Zuko looked up in shock. The warm and cold air mixed instantly, to his relief, as no one would be able to tell that Zuko had not actually begun to freeze yet. Standing in front of Zuko, with the worlds biggest grin of victory, was the warden. Mai’s uncle.

“So, thought you’d have a bit of fun firebending at my prisoners did you? Well, Prince. Your title means shit here in my prison. Here, we freeze those who think they are above the rules.” His grin grew wider as he went through his little speech.

Zuko stayed silent. He just continued to shiver lightly as he glared at the man. He had to bite his own tongue, but he would stay silent. He needed them not to have reason to touch him and feel his warmth or find the wrench still tucked into his clothing.

“Cold, are we? Good. I see it is already doing you some good freezing that tongue of yours. Let’s see just how good we can get that to stick. I think, hmm” he mimed thinking and Zuko couldn’t help the eye twitch. “12 hours in a cooler and no meals for 24 after should do the trick. Have fun, and remember. Here I am the boss, my word is law.”

With that the doors were slammed back shut and Zuko let out the steaming breath he had been holding. Agni, this was going to be a long night but at least he had a job. He had work to do and it would be slow. He could do this. Take the 12 hours and work slowly. He knew the joints to the door and could see the two bolts that he would have to leave till they were ready to leave or risk rolling down and into the lake by himself. He was being smart about this. He was thinking. He was going slow. He would not fail them.

The work was just as slow and cold as he had thought it would be and then some. Once he had started he realized he needed to work on the ones that required him to stand first. He would lose the most heat trying to undo those and should do so when he had his peak amount of energy to burn through. The squats and shifting to warm his limbs before standing again were also going to take some energy and if he really had 12 hours here then he would need to be sure he could move after being freed.

Working on the first two bolts slowly went even better than he thought they would. Once he had used his palm to melt through the ice built over them he then was able to unbolt them fairly easily. He did have to be careful of the loud sounds he made with the unbolting but the guard seemed to just think that was him punching out or moving to keep warm. He did nothing to even peek in to see what Zuko was doing which made him very positive that people fighting to get out was expected.

Zuko was aware of just how taxing this was going to be when, on bolt three, he twisted hard to put his weight into breaking the bolts seal and his shoulder screamed out at the movement. He had dropped to his knees at some point through it and just breathed through his teeth. Fucking Jet. He had known that shoulder had been injured not so long ago on one of his stupid raids that Zuko had so stupidly followed him on like some lost deer puppy. He should know, because he was the one who had helped Zuko set it again afterwards and he had targeted it specifically.

The memory of that moment mixed in his system like a war of anger and hurt. That had been the moment Jet had told him he was needed, and brave, and amazing, and… Jet had stolen his first kiss then and there and Zuko had let him. He had wanted him to do so and it had felt like kissing freedom for the first time. It was wild and free and came from so much hard work. Yet, it had also been such a lie. It had only been given to him when who he really was had been locked away in the box labeled “Lee.” Like a cruel reward for turning his back on Agni and a reminder that “Zuko” would not be worth this. It had felt good, and it had burned him.

Letting out more steam than he probably should have, Zuko stood again and changed the angle he had been working with to pop the bolt. The new angle was not that great but at least the pulsing pain was subsiding. Plus side the freezing temperatures, no ice needed to prevent swelling. With how long he was to be in here he might just be able to avoid the others knowing of his little weaknesses.

Weakness was only an added weight to give leverage to an enemy. One made up for their own pitfalls or fell because of them. He had learned that with his weak bending and Azula’s constant use of him as her personal target. Had learned through his fathers disappointed looks at every failure. Learned it permanently when his father had stolen some of his vision and hearing as a constant reminder. Suffering, he had to admit, was the greatest of teachers. And for once, he had learned.

The work was slow. Agonizingly slow. Four bolts in and his fingers had started to not want to obey his demands. The wrench had turned to icy metal and he was forced to rotate it between hands with each twist and new bolt. Forced to push through his shoulders screaming in protest. He had to be slow. Watch the guard. Drop to a squat if he saw movement. Go. Slow.

Anxiety was not helping and he was burning through too much energy. He had to start counting out his breathing. During one particularly stubborn bolt Zuko had twisted, lost his grip, and listened as the wrench clanged and clattered as it fell. To his horror the noise sounded like a death song. Standing frozen he watched the little window and counted the seconds it would take for the door to open and all to be lost.

1 - breathe

2 - out

3 - in

4 - out, do not panic.

The sound of a door being dragged open rang through the hallway and he shut his eyes tightly. He had done it. With one clumsy action all that work and patience had been ruined.

A scream, was it him? No, his teeth were clenched as tight as his eyes. It was close though, maybe a cell or two down. Begging. The sound of begging and arguing. He couldn’t make out the words but he knew what was happening. He was not caught, by some stroke of luck, because the guards had been forced to deal with putting another into a different cooler.

The voice that sounded so much like Sokka told him to squat and breathe again. Told him to get down and hide all he was doing. That the guards would be doing a round after that and he needed to look cold. Not hard, he was cold. He had been standing too long and it was going to take quite a bit to heat his core up again. Spirits, Suki was going to be his saving light in this and he owed her even more than he already did.

Once again the voice was right and it aggravated him. The guards had done round after round once the other cooler had been sealed. The sounds coming from it were loud. Bangs, screams, clattering of ice shedding from the persons struggling. They were going to burn themselves out within minutes at this rate. Fire was energy. Cold sucked away at that like it was the worlds best treat and it was starving.

Zuko could also understand the persons fear and desire to be free from this prison cell. Losing ones fire was not something he would wish on any firebender, not even Azula. For all she was a twisted and scary thing, she was his little sister and he had to shoulder a great deal of blame for how she had turned out.

It had been his job as a big brother to give her the safe life she had deserved and provide for her. He had thought he was doing well, keeping fathers eyes on himself and away from her. Taking the private lessons he never seemed to learn fast enough so she would be spared and free to be a child. It had changed when she had been blessed by Agni more than anyone Zuko had seen before and it was still not enough for him to wish the gift be taken from her.

He was unsure how long he stayed huddled but it was a good deal of time. There was no light besides the window the guards used to look in so he could not see the suns placement. All firebenders knew where Agni was in the sky at all times. They did not need to see Agni to feel their warmth. They did struggle to maintain this connection when inside for extended periods of time and that was most likely the reason these prisons had so much outside time. It was a byproduct of housing firebenders using firebending guards. To not punish the guards, the prisoners were allowed out for greater times than they probably would be in an Earth Kingdom prison, if they were let out at all.

The cold, however, worked to sever one from Agni’s light and finding it again was harder than even a chilled Zuko wanted to admit. When he did and took the time to calculate the movement it had made through the sky while he was locked in the cooler he was shocked to see that 6 hours had passed. Not good. He was moving too fast, but he was also cold. He had also somehow lost track of at least two hours which meant he had fallen into a meditative state. That was dangerous and he would have to be more cautious. He needed to finish and huddle soon or he was going to have to start burning his own reserve energy.

He set a rhythm. Breathe, warm the hands, melt the ice, warm the hands, pop the bolt. He could do this. He was doing this. Fuck whatever weird shit Sokka had been on, he was going to do this and do it right. No more distractions. Repeat.

Freeing the last bolt had been the hardest as it was on the ground and he couldn’t use his weight to pop it. He was desperate by this point to be done and reckless. He knew he was and yet he still did it. He had melted the ice, set the wrench, and thrown his whole body into a twisting motion at an odd angle wrapped around the bolt. When it popped the wrench went sliding across the bottom of the cooler and Zuko went back first into the door.

He quickly shifted to scoop up all the metal pieces scattered and stuffed them into his shoes as the guard moved to look in. Zuko moved to sit up again and swayed like he had just passed out and was trying to get up. He really did not have to pretend much with all the melted ice he had made freezing into his own personal skating patch. He pushed from the door, actually slipped, and was thrown back against the door like a drunken fool. The guards laughing echoed through the new holes in the seal but he did nothing to open the door.

From the sitting position Zuko heated his fingers as much as possible, they were so red and numb, and with a few hard jerks was able to twist the last bolt off without needing the wrench currently in his shoe and freezing his foot. He felt a wave of relief like he had never felt before as he added that bolt to the others in his shoes.

He had done it. He was soar, cold, and about to run out of the free energy for heat but he had done it. He had thought it through. He had been slow. He had worked hard. He needed a nap.

Huddling back into the center he curled in on himself and allowed himself to just breathe. To heat the space around himself. To just be. He had done his job, now he had to only wait for the others to do what they needed to. He was allowed to be warm and to take a break now, right?

## Chapter 8

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Hello!

Thank you everyone for the amazingly warm and uplifting messages! I read an love all of them so please comment anything if you feel the want to do so! As a first fic goes, this is consuming my every thought.

Thank you again and please enjoy! &#60;3

### Text

Sokka was frozen against the wall. He had been ever since Jet had shoved past him with a scream that spoke of far more than just a chance to fight a firebender. There was so much that Sokka did not understand mixed in that fight and he was struggling to process the last minute or so. Had that really only just been a few minutes ago? He felt like it had taken years and that he had been made of stone through it all.

Everything had gone in slow motion. Sokka had stumbled into the wall and out of Jet’s way when the boy had shoved past him. He had done this in order to get to Zuko.

Zuko, who Sokka had wanted to keep as far away from the coolers as physically possible. Zuko, who was always three steps ahead into a situation that was going to hurt him in some way or another. Sokka had known he would offer but was also sure he would have backed down after listening to reason. He had forgotten a key rule about working with Zuko, ‘reasoning’ with that short fuse firebender was a sure fire way to get opposite results.

Jet had known this would end with Zuko offering. He had been waiting for it and waited till Zuko was emotionally low. When he had offered his ‘help’ it was like Zuko had grabbed onto that chance to prove something with his whole being and had let it happen. Had let Jet slam him into the ground.

Sokka doubts Zuko even knew the sounds that had made but Sokka could still hear them. The thud and clang. The deep growl the firebender had let loose as he through himself into the fight. That fight had not been to get a firebender into the cooler, it had been personal.

The crowd that had gathered around had screamed loudly in excitement while the two had tore at each other like it was life or death. Jet, from where Sokka had watched, looked like that was the end goal. If this was all an act then Jet should win an award. He very much doubted this was an act at all.

He had watched as Jet went hard at Zuko’s side, favoring the right when everything about fighting Zuko screamed to target the left. Was there something Jet knew that Sokka didn’t? He had watched in stunned horror as Jet punched Zuko in the face so hard that the sounds echoed over the crowds cheers. Watched as Zuko’s head had bounced off the metal floor and how it had lulled for a second before Zuko had recovered and thrown Jet.

Zuko had done his job and done it well. He had produced fire at a ‘foe’ and been grabbed by the other guards. Sokka had to will himself to stay and not charge the guards to be the one to take him. He would risk everything by doing so and knew he would never be able to actually close that door.

Zuko could do this. He had to. He was stronger than anyone Sokka knew and he was going to do an amazing job. Sokka believed even as the guards took Zuko out of sight that he would do his best and that was always well above and beyond what others expected he could do. Zuko was nothing if not full of surprises. Sokka trusted Zuko.

Sokka took deep breaths as the crowds started to break up. He needed to calm down. He needed to stop thinking about a shivering Zuko or hearing the sounds of that fight. What he really needed to do now was to stay where he was and run damage control. Jet had just attacked a teammate, and a glance at Suki told Sokka that this was not going to be overlooked by any means.

Correction, Suki was going to be on a one way ticket into her own form of lock down. She looked as though Jet had just admitted to trying to hunt her Unagi. Yes, her Unagi. In Sokka’s humble opinion Suki thought of that thing as warmly as any pet owner. Which means that his earlier statements about how well Suki and Zuko were getting along needed an update.

Knowing Suki, he had figured she was going to treat Zuko as a team member for Sokka’s sake. He had thought that was all this would be and that she would give him the same treatment, to a lesser degree, that Katara did. Clearly he had been very wrong and had miscalculated something. What that something had been he was unsure but he was about to figure it out.

Sokka had spent too long frozen against the wall and thinking. He knew he had but he could not stop seeing the pained look on Zuko’s face as he was dragged away. It was because of this he had missed that Suki was on the move until she was halfway across the space between them and Jet.

If he was going to keep his prison guard look up then he knew she was already out of reach for him to do anything more than watch. If he needed to intervene it would have to be done using Chit Sang and that man looked far too amused by this to end it.

Her eyes were locked on the boy kneeling and rubbing blood away from his mouth. Sokka felt an odd sense of pride bloom at the thought that Jet was bleeding thanks to Zuko. He deserved it after that stunt. He deserved more but Sokka was not in the place to do or say anything on that. For now he would have to let Suki do what she did best and put Jet in his place.

Suki, for her part, had strode up to Jet like a mother polar bear dog after someone had kicked her pup. Her intense eyes were locked on her only target and the tension she was building with every step could only mean one thing. There was about the be a nonbender round two and Sokka had his money on Suki.

Jet had mere seconds to look up and say something too quiet for Sokka to make out. His hand was still on his mouth so reading lips was out. Whatever it was, it had not been the right thing to say. Suki had swiftly grabbed him by the collar of his shirt and punched his face so hard his head had lulled like Zuko’s had for a second.

She had released her grip and Jet crumpled towards the floor only to be met with Suki’s knee to the gut. Suki had used her momentum with that kick to twist and send Jet sliding back towards the main group. Spirits Sokka loved this woman. She was using this moment of attack to kick Jet’s ass back towards himself and Chit Sang even though that was going to cut her hunt time down to nothing.

Jet sputtered and coughed as he shifted onto his knees only to be grabbed by his shirt once more and dragged the rest of the way back. It was only a few feet but it was enough to solidify Suki as the dominant power of what was about to happen. She released Jet once they were in talking range to the group and twisted to glare down at him, arms crossed to hold herself back from further assault.

Sokka had his job cut out for him and he knew it. Damage control was not looking easy and they had only a few hours to figure out part three and four to his four step plan. Jet had just ruined his first version but that would be fine. He could do this. Zuko could do this. Everything would be fine, if he could just prevent Suki from murdering a teammate despite a small voice in his head telling him to join Suki in that line of attack.

“What on Yue’s blessed moon was that bullshit!?” Sokka hissed out. Okay, so maybe he was about to join Suki after all. Damage control would come later. “What part of that was ‘Jet, stop right now’ or are you just deaf to orders that go against your grand need to hate firebenders?”

Jet spit out a large chunk of red that Sokka was going to ignore. He deserved to be hurting and by the looks of some of the fun colors growing on his upper arms and neck Zuko gave just as much as he got. Good.

“What? Got our result in the end. Man in the cooler, that is what we needed and the big guy said no.”

“Yeah, and I stand by that no. Not a chance I can go in there now.”

“See? Why are you so upset? You wanted a firebender in there and were ignoring your team. I listened and got you your inside man. Let’s just figure out your step three already.” Jet stood and dusted himself off. Sokka did not miss how he had ‘staggered’ while standing only to put distance between himself and Suki.

“That was not listening to the team, Jet, you and I both know that. Whatever you and Zuko have going on needs to end now.” Sokka glared at Jet through his little command but what he was really hoping for was some insight into what that ‘whatever’ was.

The look he received made his stomach twist. Jet let out a growl and stepped towards Sokka only to halt instantly seeing Suki match his step with one towards himself.

“I have nothing with ‘Zuko’ so don’t worry your pretty little head. He is all yours if you want that thing.” Jet growled.

Definitely a past there and Sokka was starting to think it was a great deal stickier than just a freedom fighter and firebender. Whatever, he needed to not think about it or the anger that thought brought.

“Thing!? The only ‘thing’ here is you you wheat eating, oral fixation piece of -”

“Suki, please. We don’t have time to deal with all of this now. As much as I hate it, we have to move with the facts.”

“Sokka, his little stunt just cost Zuko a lot more than you think it did. Look, I was okay with Zuko offering because I had thought you would shoot it down and Chit Sang might step in.” She sent a glare Chit Sang’s way and finished with a sharp “that way we could at least have one firebending firebender.”

“Wait, Suki. What do you mean we don’t have any firebending firebenders anymore?”

“She means yer little prince is about to become ‘frozen’ thanks to that rather amusing stunt.” Chit Sang supplied not at all helpfully.

“That is something I’ve learned here, besides other things. The cooler is not just freezing a firebender, but freezing the fire out of them. They call it being ‘frozen.’ Most who come out of there claim they can’t even feel the sun for a few days even on boiling hot days. From what I've noticed the longer they sit, the longer that connection is severed.”

Sokka was once again stone. He must have been. It was the only way to explain how the world had just opened up and swallowed him whole and yet he was still firmly in the same place. His stomach felt violently ill and everything else had just gone numb. His blood ran cold despite the constant heat around him.

Zuko was going to lose his firebending. It didn’t matter that it was only a few days, he was going to lose it because Sokka was unable to stop Jet. It was Sokka’s fault and now that image of a shivering Zuko was back and-

“What does that have to do with step three of getting out of here? I’m sure he can go a few days without the precious warmth of the sun if it means we all leave.”

That did it. Jet, for all he was a wild card, was great at getting Sokka back on track. He would think about what Zuko was going through later. Right now he had a job to do.

“It’s not a question of what feels good, you ass! It is a moral question of severing a bender from their element!” Suki snarled.

“What, like the Fire Nation does to every bender they catch!? Like what they do intentionally and gleefully to anything they can!? There are earthbenders here, in this very prison, from our nation that are away from the earth! Where is this same energy toward them? Is it just because your little pet is going to be a little cold for a bit that you give a -”

Suki lunged and if it wasn’t for Chit Sang finally stepping in Sokka was sure this would have ended in a blood bath. While Chit Sang looked as upset as Sokka felt at Jet’s little statement he was able to grab hold of them both. Suki was out for blood and the heat of the day was not helping anyone as tempers ran higher.

Chit Sang had put himself between the two Earth Kingdom people and had a solid hold on each of them by the shoulders. He had snarled something to Jet that had the boy twisting to break free but had not moved or let either of them go. The look on Suki’s face said that if he let go anytime soon they would be down a team member.

“I do care that my people are away from their elements! I care that my girls are in prison because I trusted the wrong people. That all the benders here, yes all of them, are being forced away from a huge part of themselves.

“Just because one is a firebender does not mean they deserve torture by default. Do I think there are evil ones? Yes! Take the royal family for instance, they could all lose their bending for all I care but that doesn’t mean I have to sit back and laugh at a suffering firebender!”

Sokka flinched hard at the last bit. Suki must not know that ‘prince’ was not a nickname with Zuko yet or she momentarily forgot this fact. He would correct her later, this was not the time for technicalities.

“Ya don’t know what it is like, kid. Ya couldn’t know what it feels like to have a part of yer core self locked behind some ice wall. It hurts, and it ain’t pretty.” Chit Sang cut in.

Sokka had taken those words and let his mind once again show him that image of a blue lipped boy shivering in that torture room. He had so much to apologize for after all this was done and he would need to keep Aang away from Zuko. If he really was to be ‘frozen’ Aang would only upset him by demanding practice that he couldn’t give thanks to Sokka’s poor leadership skills.

“Did you forget that this little firebender of yours was the reason you are here? Did you forget that he willingly attacked and burned your village? Dragged you and your ‘girls’ out into this war? He is the reason so many suffer and he doesn’t even have the decency to realize it! Yes, I think he can freeze if it means we get out.” Jet was getting loud and this was getting out of control.

Suki was about to scream back, Sokka knew she was, but he had caught her eyes and shook his head. Tui and La she was a blessing. She had glared at Sokka for a moment and simply snarled a warning in Jet’s direction giving Sokka the space to talk again.

“We are all on the same team, at least I hope we are.” Sokka turned a questioning look towards Jet who just nodded. “Right, what happened in the past between them is for them to work out and from what I have seen it is going alright?”

Suki nodded and relaxed a great deal. The need for violence in her stance bled away and Sokka nodded back. Damage control might just work.

“Yeah, they are working it out so well that she is falling for all his little sap story tricks and giving into his demands. What, your little ‘Sunshine’ tell you about his big bad family and look at you through that scar to get you to give up your own rations?” Fuck whatever Sokka thought damage control was supposed to be, Jet never fit that picture anyway.

“The fuck that supposed to mean? He didn’t tell me anything! If you must know, I forced him to eat because I knew we needed a functioning firebender. He didn’t even want it! He only accepted it because I ordered him to take it. He probably only takes something out of a need to help others over himself but you wouldn’t know how that looks, would you?”

Spirits everywhere these two were toxic near each other. Sokka had thought Katara and Toph did not mix well yet here he was seeing an even worse combination. He had not believed it possible yet here it was in front of him and he had to somehow get them on working terms. He had thought that would have been impossible with Zuko and Suki seeing the history they had but what did he know.

“Look, I am really glad you helped Zuko out a bit and I know exactly what you mean about having to force help onto him. Not the point of focus now. Now we need to do what we can to make sure his sacrifice is not going to be wasted.” He paused to look at everyone individually, making sure he had all the attention and that the arguments would wait.

“Good. Okay, so part two was changed a bit but that is okay. We can work with this. I need you three to be ready to move on my word.” He looked at them all before turning to face the two men on his group.

“Chit Sang and Jet, you two will need to be the outside men. I will get you outside and behind the cooler first. You will need to be there to support its weight so we don’t make any sounds detaching it. You will also have to help roll it down the hill safely.”

Jet and Chit Sang exchanged a look and nodded. Good, so Jet could work with a firebender if needed. Just not a certain one.

“Suki, you are going to be my plan B on this.”

“Plan B?” She gave a questioning glance around.

“Yeah, don’t worry. Nothing big will change. Just, well, when you are working with Zuko you have to factor for something to not go quite as planned. Case and point, we have the wrong bender in the cooler now.” Sokka sighed.

This had been lesson number two he had learned while working with Zuko and hearing stories of events he had been involved with. Given Jet’s snort, he knew what Sokka was talking about and that made his insides do that weird angry twisting again just thinking about it.

“Okay. Got it. I’ll be backup for that something. So what is this plan B you think might be needed?”

Sokka paused to think. He knew he needed Suki to be his backup for something to happen, but he did not know what that something was. Given that he was working with Zuko it could really be anything from a freak storm to Zuko mouthing off to the warden and getting caught. Zuko was going to be the main part of the change in the plan but he just did not know what that change would be. Best go with all possibilities.

“I need you to do a round after you are freed to check for any stray guards so that we are not caught while we finish separating the coolers from the wall. Zuko will have the hard part covered from the inside but there will be a few bolts that need to come off only when we are ready to go. I trust him in realizing this. I’ll head to the cooler to help with that and have those two ready to catch it as it comes off the wall. Worst case, I’ll need you to help open the door if the cooler shifted at all while he was working and he is now more stuck than we think he is.”

That was it. That could very easily be the situation he was trying to plan for. Something involving the cooler itself. They were just freezers, Sokka had checked that. None of them had any kind of sensor or anything so rolling one away would be fine.

It was the possibility that loosening the structure keeping it to the wall and having a weight inside moving around might cause it to shift. This could possibly trap an already cold Zuko inside for longer than wanted. That sounded awful, so that was bound to be at least a part of it.

Suki nodded in agreement. “So, I am guard watch and inside cooler detachment crew. I can work with that.”

“Good. How long do you think it will take you to do a round of this place?”

“I could show her around. I know all the little twists and turns to getting around and could point out the spots of concern. If she is willing to listen that is.” Jet had looked between Suki and Sokka but it was clear he would need a safety confirmation before he followed through on this.

“Suki, it is up to you but I agree with Jet on this. He knows this place better than most.”

“Because he is never where he should be.” She huffed and crossed her arms. Shifting her weight onto one side to prove some kind of point that Sokka was just not in the mood to figure out.

“And that could be our way of getting Zuko out of the cooler faster than whatever time the warden will deem. Plus it will account for at least part of what could go wrong in plan A.”

Suki sighed heavily and twisted out of Chit Sang’s hold to face Jet. Her stance daring him to argue further.

“If you can keep your mouth shut and stick to facts and the plan, I can agree to following you through this place to time it.”

“I’ve said nothing but facts-” he flinched hard when Suki made a quick shifting motion but her grin told Sokka it was all for show and she had won. “Fine! Fine. Silent tour it is.”

The whole group stood in silence for a moment before Chit Sang patted Jet roughly on the back. He nodded once to Sokka and headed back out into the crowd without a word so time must be running short.

“Great. I trust you two. We can do this and it will work. After dinner you two will take your walk and I am going to secure things from the outside to be sure no one will do night rounds.” They both nodded and with a final glare Jet left to go join whoever would listen to him.

“I don’t trust him, Sokka.”

“No, but right now he is right. I hate to admit it but he did get us an inside man and he is working in his weird way to help.”

“He has done nothing but fight every step of the way.”

Sokka sighed heavily. “Actually, he is the reason we have these steps- don’t look at me like that! I mean it.”

“What do you mean, exactly?” She turned to face him fully, cautious curiosity in her glare.

“I needed a wrench for this to work. Sure, I would have probably found it eventually but not within the time frame need. By the time I would have we would be stuck for maybe a day more.”

“He found the wrench?”

“No, he knew exactly where it was. He is not kidding when he says he knows this place well. He found me after a guard rotation and helped get the needed parts for my plan to work. It is how he knew I had it in my pocket.” Sokka crossed his arm, thinking about that little interaction.

“Sokka, was that before you came here?”

“Yeah, we uhm, made it back in time to ah…” he blushed and looked down. Hard to not think about seeing Zuko and Suki playing this weird game of tag as he exited into the courtyard. The game had ended with Zuko letting Suki touch his hair weirdly enough and they had shared a hushed conversation.

Sokka had felt his face flush then, too, as he had watched them. He had watched Zuko’s body language scream that he wanted nothing more than to flee. He had also seen the second the other boy had relaxed to her touch and had felt a pang of something deep down. A want to see that look more which he quickly filed away in the ‘because I want Suki to do this with me’ box and closed it. Now he was starting to think he would need to relabel the box ‘zuko’ and leave it at that. The name was enough to encompass it all.

“Back in time to what? Sokka, are you okay? You look a bit red, maybe you should head back inside.” She had moved to his right side he noted, and was looking at him with concern.

Sokka waved it off. “No, it’s not the heat, don’t worry. Though this armor is extremely hot. Why do Fire Nation guards have to be so extra.”

That got a chuckle, good. He was doing great at damage control and it only took removing a certain someone from the equation. A lot of someones, actually.

“Suki. About you and Zuko…” He didn’t know how to ask. He knew he wanted to, he needed to know. It was important to know your team. But he was scared to know the answer.

“What about us? Sokka, I don’t understand what you are trying to ask. I’m not a mind reader.”

“Right, I know that. Just, you two do have a lot of history. You seem awfully close really fast-which is great! I am just worried…”

“Do you remember how we met? You were kind of a dick but I forgave you. I’m not over what he did or anything. That is going to take time, definitely.”

“But?”

She chuckled and nudged his shoulder “but I can also see how hard he is trying. It doesn’t take a genius like you to see it. I get it. He is angry, hot headed, short tempered, sensitive, has a kind heart, and is confused and in pain.”

“Yeah… he is all that and the worlds biggest pain in the ass.” Sokka had to admit, that sounded kinda like his list from earlier.

“I could see that. Really, you two have the bigger history so I should be asking you. How are you doing working with him?”

“What? I’m fine working with him now. Sure, it took a lot. Like, a LOT a lot. But I see now that he really is wanting to do the right thing. Without him here I would have been basically turning myself in. He is the whole reason I am here enough to make this plan.”

“Hmm, yeah he is pretty great. Should we be starting a ‘Zuko fan club’ at this point?” She laughed as Sokka stuttered out some mess of a reply. “Kidding, relax. And other than my thinking he is going to be a good friend and needs a bit more hands on friendship there is nothing to worry about.”

“I’m not worried. Why would I be worried.” Sokka slumped against the wall and sighed. He ignored her raised eyebrow look. “Okay, I’m worried but not about that. I keep thinking of him being in that cooler.”

“Yeah, I know what you mean. He is strong and will be fine. I just wish Jet would leave his head alone. Twice in one day is-”

“Twice?”

“He didn’t tell you.” It was a statement, not a question and Sokka felt himself grow cold again. This temperature changing was not good for his health.

Before she could answer further the whistle of guard rotation and prisoner check in rang through the space. She gave Sokka a stern look. “You should go, you missed last rotation. Just. Stay away from the coolers and let him work. He can do this.”

“I know” and it was not a lie. He did know and he trusted Zuko. Now was his time to get everything in motion and not waste it. He wanted to get Zuko out of that thing in under 10 hours time.

## Chapter 9

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Things are still busy so I might be down to one or two chapters a week, but fear not! They will keep coming.

Thank you everyone for the warm welcome into writing fanfiction! I am trying my best to keep these characters true to who they are and dig a bit into a new world.

Any and all comments, kudos, or thoughts are welcome. &#60;3

### Text

The mess hall was an actual mess. The plates were everywhere, food was half eaten and some piles of paper and baked goods were scattered over the floor. The people were just as scattered. Things were being shuffled and tossed around too fast to really understand what was happening. Sokka had to duck as he entered the room as a helmet was thrown at the door with a loud and echoing laugh.

“Newbie! Hey there, just in time.” The man from lunch waved Sokka over to a corner where he was leaning against a table. His smile was friendly and he wiggled a paintbrush in Sokka’s direction. “Well, hurry up! We are already pushing the timing on this ‘cause some people forgot to watch the calendar.”

Sokka shuffled his way to the man and past the utter chaos that was the room. The more he looked around the more it looked like the mad dash to set up some kind of party. There were half hung streamers in Fire Nation colors, people scribbling on a large paper that would most likely be a banner, and the food preparation was going as well as it would if Toph had been assigned cooking duty.

“Uh, right. Yeah. Sorry, but I think I may have missed the calendar as well.” Sokka said as he caught the brush tossed to him. He was still watching as two men tried to count seats and kept counting over each other.

“Don’t feel bad, you got here on the last shift exchange right? Only been a few days. See that” he gestured towards a large poster hanging next to a Fire Nation flag that had scribbles all over it. “That is our shift calendar. Tells us who is getting the next exchange to go home for a bit and who is coming.”

Sokka looked again and swallowed hard. This had never been something he had to think about, but he supposed it made sense. In the Southern Water Tribe they recorded stories through pictures and the words were made years later. He had learned in the Northern Water Tribe that, while it was similar, it was not the same. He had never stopped to think about languages. His sister and himself would sometimes argue in their native tongue, but that had stopped with Aang being around after the boy had tried to join in and sounded older than their Gran-Gran. If the Water Tribes had a language, surely the Fire Nation would have their own as well.

Zuko would have been able to read that, if Sokka had asked. He knew the boy was well educated to a degree just given his status. He was also pretty gifted with language if Sokka gave a moment to think on that. There was a few times that Katara had asked Sokka for something. She had slipped, probably unconsciously, into Water Script and Zuko had started to respond by going towards her requests. Perhaps it had something to do with Zuko liking to read? He would have to ask later.

“I know it is a mess now, lots of rotations. Big thing is that Zhan just got back from his trip away with his grandchildren and is retiring. This was supposed to be his retirement party day but with all the excitement it was forgotten.”

“Makes sense, lots happened in a short amount of time I suppose.” Sokka looked at the paintbrush in his hand and read the inscription of the maker. It was in Earth Script, the base language used by every nation and in schooling. So they used Earth Kingdom tools but on common place things it was in their own language. He swallowed hard again taking the paper handed to him to sign and breathed out a huge sigh of relief. Half was in the twisting lines of Fire Nation Script but the other was in common script so he could follow that lead.

“Ya good kid?” A female voice drifted over to them making Sokka jump.

“What? Oh, yeah, all good. Just, uhm, signing Zhan’s card!” Sokka waved it around slightly to emphasize the point.

The two looked him over. This time they were really looking and Sokka had to duck his head and work on signing the card. What was he going to do? He could not sign his name or the other names he knew because they would see right through every one of them. He needed a common name. One that would be found in the Fire Nation, or at least the colonies.

“Here, signed!” Sokka handed it back and held his breath as they looked over what he had written. He let out a relieved sigh when they both burst out laughing and patted his back.

“Say, not bad-” he looked at the name scribbled under Sokka’s admittedly poor note “Lee. You from the colonies then?”

“Yeah, uhm. The southern ones?” He flushed slightly. He did not know why but he had the strange feeling that Zuko was going to kill him for this.

“Explains the accent and the tan! You got some pretty eyes, too. Bet your girl loves that exotic color.” The woman winked at him as she snatched the card to sign it herself.

“Oh! Uhm, no… No girl, I mean. I mean maybe a girl but-” He bit his tongue and blushed a bit harder. Suki had flashed through his mind but it had felt off somehow. Yue glowing as the moon did not even seem to fit the feeling that had caused the heat currently burning through him. Besides, last thing he needed was to give them a reason to follow him around and teasing someone is a big reason to do just that. Toph had proven that point.

They both laughed again and offered their support. Sokka turned it down as gently as he could. He really needed to start figuring out the cooler rotation chart to take over and free Zuko tonight. That was proving to be hard as the room filled with more people and the only place that had something he could read were the warning and rules posters. It made sense, the place was as Fire Nation as it could get, but it was beyond frustrating.

Before he knew it, he was in the middle of a party surrounded by Fire Nation people. He watched as a few grouped together as clear friends. One pair had slipped out together with grins that spoke of far more than friends which Sokka dutifully ignored. He was on a mission, he could not be distracted even if the food smelled of roasted meats. Whether he liked it or not he was now fully in the party and he might as well eat a bit. Would not help anyone if he was also hungry. He did wrap up a few pieces of jerky and pocket them for a certain firebender later.

Zhan looked every bit a grandfather. His gray hair hung low, his wrinkles spoke of years of work, and his smile was kind. He seemed like a joy to be around but Sokka did not feel the joy in being here at all. Once he was done with his food he was going to leave and-

“Lee!” Damn those two were hard to shake. The man waved as he weaved over towards Sokka who was inching towards the door. “Hey, you look like a spring ready to snap. Want to head to the training pitch for a bit?”

Sokka froze. How big was this place? He nodded, not really seeing what else he could do and maybe he could shake these two wanna be parents there. He followed them to a level under the courtyard which opened to a large square lined with a number of different mats. Some were clearly burned while others had a wall of different weapons next to them. Sokka hated to admit it, but he wanted really bad to try some of those weapons. When would he get a chance like this again to play with Fire Nation stuff for free!? He had a job, he knew he did, but he also had to keep his appearance so what was a few rounds to blow off steam.

“Well, what you think?” The man was looking towards the small group at the far corner Sokka had ignored. Those were clearly the firebending mats and by the heat coming from that direction and flashes of light the people practicing were going hard.

“It’s hot” was the first thing out of Sokka’s mouth. He was just glad it was met with a laugh because even he thought that was a bad one.

“Yeah, you got that right. Care to go a few rounds? I can show you things the academy leaves out.”

Sokka was in trouble and he had no escape. He also needed to figure out who was cooler duty so maybe if he played this right it would be a win-win. He could only hope that was the case. With a nod he headed straight for the long sword on the wall. He had glanced at the duel blade but thought it looked a bit too much and too showy for anyone to wield well. Why would you even need two blades? One was all that was needed for a job well done.

“Oh, like the swords?” The guard Sokka was really starting to think he should find the name of went to the wall after him. “Let’s see. Been awhile but that should be fine.”

“Any chance of calling a no bending rule?” Sokka ventured, hoping it would not be rejected. He really did not need a burn to go back to Katara with after all of this.

The man did not so much as glance at him before answering “sure, you a nonbender? That is the basic rule for training with them, so that is fine by me.”

So firebenders did not force nonbenders to train against fire. That was new, but not something that fit Sokka’s understanding of the Fire Nation. Though, if he was being honest, the more he spoke to the guards here the harder it was to keep that black and white picture. Zuko might be the one to thank for turning it gray in the first place.

They had sparred a few rounds. Going slow. The man had given Sokka the time and space to get a feel for the new blade. He was really missing his sword which was buried on the beach outside. He would never risk it in here, but he might not have been as clumsy to get the first few steps if he had had it.

“Question.” They had gained quite a large audience. Sokka was going to use this to see if he could figure out a few things about the rotation. “I was wondering who had cooler duty tonight? You know, to take people out and put them back.”

“Donno, sorry” The man swung into his step and Sokka blocked the movement easily. This was just running through the basic steps and he had done this many times already. “You lot know who got that golden job?”

The crowd murmured until Zhan stood from the back with a raised hand. “I believe that is me, but I would be willing to give it up. Say, to the winner of a sparring round?”

The crowd went from murmurs to excited chatter in a matter of moments. Sokka knew what he had just done, and he knew what he had to do. He was going to win this sparring match set and get that placement.

This was easier said than done. Sokka, for all his few months of training, was up against some high level fighters of the Fire Nation. They all had years under their belt and were going to be hard to beat. They were also all wanting the title of freeing the prince in the cooler and so no one was backing down.

His first round went pretty well. He had beaten one of the newer guards quickly by disarming them within three basic form steps. He realized this was because he was a firebender and while training with nonbenders they have the no fire rule, this did not mean any bender knew the first thing about a weapon. Maybe this wouldn’t be so hard.

He watched as the next few rounds were firebenders as they worked through a thrown together bracket. Those were terrifying. The fire reminded Sokka of when Zuko had burned everything with fire made out of pure anger. There was no control, and no love loss in these fights. The movements, he noticed, all seemed a lot more harsh. They were aggressive stomps, kicks of fire, punches meant to push balls of fire in a certain direction. They looked like machines. Compared to waterbending which was all fluid movement and earthbending which was scary stomping, this looked like just a fist fight with distance. Zuko never looked this clunky in his fighting. Okay, maybe he had in the beginning, but now even his basic moves looked so fluid. He moved with the grace of a cat and his fire followed.

After a few rounds to determine the firebending finalists it was up to the nonbenders. Sokka swallowed hard, his throat was going to be soar with how much he had been doing that but this was a situation that called for it.

Sokka needs to be the one to let the prince free. He uses this as his driving force through the bottom rounds against those who seem to only be in this for the fun of it. The images of a cold firebender ring through his mind at every clang of his sword against metal. Every spark sending images of a fireless Zuko unable to regulate his own body temperature. Of fire easily burning the frozen skin. Of frostbite the firebender would most likely not know what to do with. These thoughts consumed Sokka, and he used them to drive himself through the ranks of the bracket with determination. If he could free Zuko, then Plan A was as good as gold. If he lost, not even Plan B could save them.

Sokka sat wiping sweat from his neck and face on a rag that had become far to wet for his own liking. This place was hot and only getting hotter as the firebenders continued their weird constant bending practice. Whoever thought to put this under the courtyard and build it out of metal obviously did not take into facts that the boiling lake around this structure was only going to work to trap the heat here. Or maybe Fire Nation just liked to cook its people. He would not put it past them at this point, everything from the uniforms and armor had screamed extra and overheat. It could also be the 15 years or so Sokka had lived in the opposite environment, but he doubted that. He could still become cold after all, so the same must be true with heat and fire.

He watched the next round. It would be the one he would need to focus on the most. Through some stroke of luck, or maybe he really was that good, he had made his way to the final bracket. The winner of this battle would determine his opponent and the final obstacle to securing Plan A. He could not lose this.

He watched as the crowd cheered and realized who were sparring. It was the two he could never seem to shake, going head to head with weapons Sokka had never seen used before. The woman, all the cheering told him that her name was actually Ming, was using the duel blades he had overlooked. Her movements with them were solid and strong, but somehow Sokka doubted that they were what was expected with that weapon. Her strikes almost always came first, as if for fire, and the blades trailed behind. If he had to guess, those were made to work as one with a persons movements and not be a secondary force.

Ruon, the man that kept pulling Sokka into conversations and had apparently passed out his first week here, was using what the people around had called a Jii. It was a long spear like weapon tipped with a blade the size of a short sword. Sokka guessed the short sword was roughly the size of his forearm if he had to give an estimate. The blade itself looked sharp and dangerous, but was far from the worst part of the weapon. Starting about halfway down the blade at the end of the staff were two long crescent moon shaped blades. One on either end of the blades flat sides and making this weapon great for stabbing and slashing in the same action. Ruon was no beginner with it either. He was using the flat base as a push off point to give his strikes even more power and it was clear that the end goal was to disarm Ming.

Sokka had to admit, between the two of them he really wanted Ming to win. While she was using twice the amount of weapon Sokka was used to he could still count a number of openings she left with every motion. He knew when he would have the best chance to disarm and had a feeling she made it this far because of her speed alone. The blades were very clearly not her go to and most likely just used as a fun thing while training with nonbenders.

Wish not granted. Sokka watched as Ruon also started tracking the very openings Sokka had been counting and started going for them. Of course he would, this man was a literal tank. While he was most likely half the build of Chit Sang he was still fast and calculating. If he had not been Fire Nation, Sokka found it easy to think about how he might have looked up to this man. As it was, he was terrified of him. A gasp and clattering sound echoed as Ming’s weapon dropped to the ground after getting caught between the crescent blade and the staff and twisted away from her admittedly poor grasp.

Well shit. Sokka was up against Ruon and that beast of a weapon. Of course he was, why did he think he would be so lucky as to not be. He sighed heavily and grabbed the oddly weighted sword at his side. Even with all the fast pace practice he had with it, he was aching to use his own blade. Better yet, why did they not have a boomerang on the wall of weapons. A quick glance at it again confirmed that, no, they were not sophisticated enough to supply a boomerang in this place.

Sokka moved to stand on the opposite side of Ruon and raised his sword. He shifted his feet into the stance Piandao had drilled into him and took a deep breath. He had to focus. He could not lose here, even if all they saw was some bragging rights this was his ticket to getting everyone off this cursed volcano. He would not lose.

Ruon laughed “relax Lee, I won’t hurt you! Just have a bit of fun with it for once, not everything has to be so dire.” The flashes of red dancing in the distance behind him as the other firebenders practiced did nothing to help his words be anything more than threatening.

“Right, yeah. I know that. Let’s just do this.” His heart was threatening to beat out of his chest. Focus! Forget about the cold, and watch the middle of the staff and not the blades. That would tell him the next angle. Do not let it catch the blade between the moons and staff or it is over.

Sokka twisted his hands around the handle of his sword and felt the blade wobble awkwardly. Did they really not see how poorly some of these weapons were made? No, most likely not. Not many nonbenders here and none of them would dare comment on a Fire Nation weapon as being inferior.

Ruon moved first with a wide and open chopping motion. He lifted the staff high and swung it straight down at Sokka with one of the moons aimed for him. Sokka had to move to block from above with the flat of the blade to keep the weight of it spread out and give himself a more secure hold on keeping that trapping space of the staff away from his blade. The staff was dragged down the blade and to the ground before it was flicked at Sokka’s side with just as much speed. Sokka was on full defense and it was only two strikes in. This was every bit not good.

Another ten clashes in and Sokka was still firmly in defense. He was breathing heavy, his arms ached from the constant heavy impact from the staff at different angles and he was slowing down. He needed to end this but he was being led around the ring like some kind of circus animal. It was clear that without an opening of some kind he was going to lose sooner rather than later. Think, he needed to think. He needed to distract.

“Wh-Why do you want-” a clang and Sokka’s arm throbbed as the vibrations threatened to twist the blade away. He held on “to be the one to do night duty?”

“Don’t really care either way. I love a chance to get a good workout though, and you are pretty good for a newbie.” Ruon grinned as he shifted his stance to swing the staff to the side. Sokka dodged, that swing had not been as focused as the last.

“So you want night duty?” Sokka took another step back, damn the long reach of that staff keeping him away.

“I mean, yeah that bites, but at least this time it comes with bragging rights.” He laughed and twisted the staff in his hands without swinging. Sokka took the chance to take his first attack but was blocked easily. “You fight like this is life or death Lee, what’s in it for you?”

‘It is’ rang through Sokka’s mind before he answered “me? I want payback.”

Ruon froze and gave a puzzled look. His block of Sokka’s next attack was clumsy and the clang echoed. Sokka had felt the staff dip to his strike and knew Ruon’s grip was loosening as he became distracted.

“That bastard pretended to be a guard, see, and he attacked me! I need to be the one to see him ‘frozen’ to really put him in his place.” Sokka felt sick saying it, but the reaction from around him was a mixture of laughs, cheers, and whistles so he knew it was the right thing to say.

Ruon blinked a few times before he burst into laughter. “You! Lee, you were the newbie who froze!? Oh, man, thank you! That is so much better than passing out during roll call.”

A few loud ‘no it’s not’ rang from the crowd but Sokka took the chance he had been given. He thrust his blade into the death zone of the staff between the moon on one side and with a harsh twist he brought it to the ground and away from Ruon. Ruon’s look of surprise was all Sokka needed. He had done it. Might not have been a fully fair win but he had disarmed Ruon which meant that he had won. He was on cooler duty, and Plan A was still a go. Ruon laughed and clapped, not looking a single bit upset at losing his bragging chance.

“Good show Lee, you earned that. You get to put that no good prince in his place. Well done.” His smile was wide and he moved to shake Sokka’s hand and pat his shoulder. He helped Sokka put the weapons away as they began to pack up to leave for rotation. Sokka glanced at the firebenders only to pause.

One of the younger benders, probably early twenties but the distance made that a hard guess, had been punching out fire like he was in a life or death battle. Now he was swaying where he stood and looked oddly pale even from this distance. Sokka watched as the man stood and reset into his stances to continue but the fire looked wrong somehow. Ruon moved to his side and was about to ask when his eyes followed Sokka’s and he cursed.

“Ming! Stop him, he’s going to-”

Whatever it was the Ruon was going to warn about it was too late. Sokka watched as the bender punched out mere sparks and then dropped as if he was a puppet and someone had cut his strings. He had crumpled to the floor lifelessly and everything around stopped. Benders around the area cursed and many rushed to the fallen man.

“What just happened? He was fine a moment ago, wasn’t he?” Sokka looked to Ruon who had a serious stare locked on the body.

“He was. The fool just ‘doused’ himself is all. Should be fine but that shit is scary.” Ruon continued to watch, tension clear in his features.

“Doused? There is no water here unless you count the lake, how did he douse himself?” Sokka felt completely out of his element. This was something he knew nothing about and even Katara had never mentioned being ‘doused’ before.

Ruon chuckled “it’s got nothing to do with water, Lee. ‘Dousing’ to a firebender is when you extinguish your inner flame.”

“That can happen!?” Sokka whipped around to stare at the man on the ground being moved to lay on his back.

“Yeah, not fun. It is no permanent thing so don’t worry. It is when you push your core too thin. Most firebenders can only produce so much fire at a time, it is not infinite like most think it is. It is based on the health of the bender more than anything. If you have a sick firebender, you have a weakened core and in turn less fire.”

That made some kind of weird sense. Sokka was reminded of Zuko when he had first come to them. His fire had been weak and almost nonexistant then. He had felt disconnected with himself and this meant that his core was also affected. Mentally, he was not well and his fire had burned in a way the reflected that. He had also seen that as Zuko had forced more and more fire forward, he had grown more pale and at one point had staggered before he slumped and admitted his fire was gone. Had he almost ‘doused’ himself and stopped just before?

“So, what happens to a ‘doused’ firebender?” Sokka needed to know. If Zuko had gotten that close just because he was mentally disconnected, there was no telling how far he would go after being ‘frozen.’

“Nothing much really. It is important to get their body temperature regulated. That is why the group over there worked to strip the armor off of him like that. After, it is just like treating someone for a fainting spell and making sure they rest. The fire will reignite in them once their body can regulate enough energy to do so.”

“Like, he just passed out from heat and now he needs rest?” Sokka could easily see this, and everything about it, being a future problem.

“Pretty much the basics. Truth be told, I ‘doused’ myself over training the night before roll call and just didn’t feel the full effect until I touched the sun.” Ruon shrugged as if it was a simple statement. It might have been but it had told Sokka a huge fact, ‘dousing’ was not an instant problem. It could come after the fact and not seem to have any connection to the reason for the blackout.

“What should we do?”

“Not much we can do. Come, lets go do patrols and give them space to work. He’ll be fine just gonna be ribbed about this for weeks.” Ruon led Sokka out of the training rooms and into the upper halls. How the halls of a boiling prison could feel so cool he would never understand but they did and he sighed with relief. Ruon chuckled but said nothing as they did a true loop around the floors.

It did not take long for this loop to become eventful. Sokka and Ruon watched as a man twice the size of Sokka was dragged past screaming about rations. He was cursing in ways Sokka had only dreamed about and the path they were following meant one thing, cooler time. The man was pale in complexion, short black hair cut as if in a hurry, and very clearly a firebender by the singes to his red tattered prison shirt. Ruon simply nodded to the guards dragging the bender away and led Sokka back to the mess hall for some time in the cool air there.

Sokka had watched the second firebender be dragged down the hall and towards the coolers about thirty minutes ago. He was sitting sipping water in the mess hall and had to work hard to stay there. He had not worked hard enough as he found himself leaving the room and walking down the hall towards the back. He was not supposed to be around the coolers, he had promised Suki. Zuko needed time to work and he could not risk him failing before he was to open that cooler. That was not going to be for another five hours or so, so he really should not be near the cooler hall. He could not stop himself.

He could not help himself, he had been curious. He had walked towards the hall just to look down it. He was not going to go down it, just look, that wouldn’t be too bad right? The two guards outside the coolers had caught his gaze and waved him over. The action was clearly a common and friendly one, most likely just offering to let him look at the coolers with them. Look at those inside like these were animals in a zoo, and not people.

He had not listened to his brain screaming for him to just wave as if misreading the situation and to continue on. Instead, he walked towards the two who were leaning against a door to one of the end coolers. Not Zuko’s then. He would be towards the middle given the time he went in. Suki was going to kill him.

He nodded to them, recognizing Ming who had lost the last sparring match and was now on cooler duty. The other was a young man was in his thirties or so and Sokka did not recognize him but he had a kind smile for those who were not on the wrong side of these walls. They had him peek into the cooler and he saw the larger man shivering hard and trying not to touch anything around himself. He must have been who was screaming and arguing before, but now he looked so tired. Sokka forced himself in joining in on the laughing with the other guards and turned to leave quickly. He was not fast enough.

He was waved over by Zhan who stood towards the middle of the hall. He had given up his chance to be the one to release the prince but was traded the honor of guarding him throughout the day. Giving a flash of a fake smile Sokka walked towards him slowly.

“H-Hey.”

“Hey, newbie, thanks for your kind words and such an entertaining afternoon. You have to check this out! Sneak peek for later tonight, right? Once in a lifetime see, really, and I should know!” He gestured towards the cooler door excitedly. Sokka’s heart was in his stomach.

“Yeah?” He tried to lean away from the door.

“Come on, take a peek! I wont tell the warden nothing. You are the lucky turtleduck that gets to let him out later, anyway. Congrats on that win by the way, been awhile since Ruon was matched like that. Might as well get a look on how it is going. See what you are up against. I still can’t believe it myself but there he is. The traitor prince, right here in our cooler! They say he was caught breaking into this place, Agni knows why.”
