“N-No kidding. That’s crazy. Who would try something like that?” The fake laugh he gave sounded off even to himself. Zhan did not show that he noticed, most likely passing it off as a newbie being nervous.

“Come on! He can’t bite from here, but I have heard it said that the royal family can breathe fire. Isn’t that insane to think about.”

“Is it?” For all Sokka had known, Zuko was always spitting fire when upset or frustrated. It was his go to way of releasing energy after all and it annoyed Katara to know end. Sokka found it kind of endearing that he felt emotions so harshly they needed a physical outlet. No, that wasn’t right. Zuko was not endearing! Anyway, he also remembered street performers doing what he had thought was a great job at breathing fire. Maybe that is why they were street performers, not many could do it.

This question had also gotten the questioning look Sokka was worried about. “Yeah, wait. You a nonbender? Sorry son, I didn’t mean to assume you a bender. Don’t take it personal. I suppose the nonbender training doesn’t cover the same shit, huh?”

“S-suppose not. So can other firebenders not do that? Breathe fire I mean.”

“Not many. And those who do claim it burns the throat like no other for days. We might be walking campfires but we ain’t totally fire proof. Hold a flame long enough to a firebenders skin and you will get through. Take the prince’s face for instance. One hell of a banishment scar.”

“Banishment scar? I had thought…”

“Training accident? You really must be new or they don’t scare the nonbenders the same in training. Yeah, you run or do something against the great Fire Nation and you have a few options. Death, prison, or banishment.”

“Wait, you get to choose? I mean, isn’t that a little… forgiving to the vile things hurting our great nation!?” Sokka tacked on the last bit, trying his best to curb the questioning stare. It seemed to do the trick well enough because the old man just chuckled.

“Not usually, son. Don’t you worry. Most get death, but death of any royal is rather tragic. The other is prison but that is publicity that most don’t want to have leaked.” Zhan said with a look into the cooler behind himself.

“So the last is banishment… which comes with a…” Sokka was dragging out his words.

He was thinking, and it was not a fun line of thought to be on. He was also thinking he should not be hearing this and that it was personal. Not his place. He could not stop himself from asking about it.

“Some kinda mark to really make you identifiable. Usually a burn and for a bender that’s an ouchie. Takes a lot if they are a firebender. Look at the prince” he motioned to the window in the cooler he was guarding.

“Hard not knowing the prince instantly with that scar he has. Though if you ask me, the placement was just down right cruel. On the face? I had seen early paintings of the royals as they grew up. He had been a cute kid. Looked like his mother and everything. Shame, really, but it did the trick I suppose.” Zhan finished.

The scar was not an accident. Firebenders did not burn easy and that scar was intentional. Someone had done that to him. That thought had stunted his mind. If he was not so stuck on it he would have registered he was moving and stopped. He didn’t. Instead he had followed the guiding hand of the guard and looked inside the cooler.

Sokka had to calm down. If he did not he would find himself ripping open that cooler door. What he was currently looking at was extremely upsetting and his heart was out of his stomach and in his throat. Zuko was huddled tight around himself and shivering a great deal. His skin was pale, a small part of Sokka reminded him that he always was, but his scar had a bit of blue to it from the cold. His face was mostly hidden but from what he could see Zuko was breathing heavily and looked mostly relaxed. This meant he had either fallen into some kind of sleep or he was in trouble. He had been in there for around five hours now, give or take, and that was only half the time Sokka wanted to give him. Not good, but he knew he could do nothing about it right now.

Sokka took a shaky step back and nodded to a confused Zhan.

“You alright there Lee?”

“What-” his voice popped like the teen he was and he cleared his throat. He was pale, he knew it and his legs were shaking “y-yeah! Oh yeah! Just fine…”

“First cooler duty I take it? Don’t worry son, he ain’t dead and he wont die from twelve hours of cold. Not a chance. It takes a good twenty four hours at this temperature to get to that danger zone for a healthy firebender. He most likely fell into a meditation, they do that. It is sometimes called being ‘doused’ around here. ‘s when the core of a firebender is running on reserve energy and trying to reignite. Scary shit, can happen even if you are just running on fumes and pushed your bending too far even without the cold. Some guards even drop from it from time to time. Once that passes and he becomes ‘frozen’ he will start to move around a bit.” He waved it off as if he had been talking about some kind of maintenance and not a person going through a body shut down forced upon them by their own people.

His smile was meant to be reassuring. It was not. Sokka nodded back and just moved down the hall and away as fast as he could. He ignored the dramatic sigh of “newbies” from Zhan and kept moving. He needed to be anywhere but here.

Turning a corner quickly Sokka ducked into one of the many random storage closets and leaned against the wall. He pressed his palms into his eyes until the bright colors hurt. He tried to breathe. His palms felt wet and he ignored it.

Zuko might be a new friend but he was suffering because of Sokka and that was just not something he could handle right now. He needed to think. Get his mind off of it. Stop himself from going back and ruining everything by ripping open that door and pulling the firebender into a tight embrace. To stamp down the need to get the pale figure warmed up quickly. Something on the same line but positive.

The problem was it was cold, right? Cooler equals cold. Frozen. Dousing. Banishment. Zuko was the banished prince but he had had that scar the first time they had met and it had been old- No, think about anything but that. Think happy. Think cold and happy. Not so hard, right?

Sokka thought of the cold he had known all his life. Of fishing with his dad. Of learning to hunt from the men before they had left for the war. Of penguin sledding with Katara before he was to become the man of the village far too young. Of raging blizzards that force them all inside the village hut where the older village members would tell stories of a once great nation. Of polar puppies and begging to keep one. Of a fierce fire in the shape of an angry boy that had chased him away from that cold in search of a new one.

Of Yue. Her white hair glowing in the moonlight. Her smile warming his soul even in the freezing temperatures. Her laugh ringing on the wind like a song. He swore he could still hear it if he tried to listen hard enough on a full moon night.

Of the moon spirit, whom Sokka had tried to refuse was a real thing. Of the moment the sky went red with its blood. Of a brave warrior giving her life to save a fish of all things and how her body had grown cold and heavy in his arms. He was supposed to protect her then, too. Just as he was failing to protect another. Of her cold lips, promising protection and a love that would not be as fast or freeze and hurt him like hers had. He had fallen for the moon, she had whispered that he needed a sun.

He needed warmth, not the cold. Like that found on Kyoshi, the first time he had felt a breeze that did not so much as nip at his nose. Of water that did not try to turn bones to ice and make swimming extremely challenging the longer one tried. The first time he had fought off an attack and admittedly lost within seconds. The first time shame and humiliation had flared hot through him, and had taught him a much needed lesson.

Of Suki, her fiery spirit and warrior nature burned bright. She had given him his first warm smile outside of his family and tribe. His first lesson that had burned and stretched his understanding of the world. Her leadership had inspired him. Her energy had driven him to be better and prove himself. She was the reason he was the fighter he was today. He thought of her lips to his cheek and the first hot flush that had followed that had nothing to do with cold. Of how is heart had tripped over itself and left him feeling drunk on it. Of the fire in the form of a boy once again chasing him away from that warmth. Feeling like he’d never see her again as they flew away with that angry little sun chasing them.

The realization slapped him harder than he would ever admit. Yue had been right. She must have had some weird spirit knowledge though Sokka highly doubted that. He needed a sun, not the moon like his sister.

He had a sun, he was just too blind to see it. He was sure Toph was going to rip him apart when she got her weird feet on this knowledge. His sun had always made his emotions run strong but he was so bent on hating it. Even through the hate, it still caught his attention every time it was near. He had thought it was because, one could guess, the danger that crazy thing brought. But that wasn’t the full story. Wasn’t the reason his heart and stomach were in knots, was it? He had to think, and this time he had to feel.

Suki had been his first ember and if he let her he knew she could be his sun. But, his heart had become focused on another and he had never even felt it happening. His sun was intense, angry, hurting, kind, gentle, warm, and it was freezing in the cooler as he stood. The sun had been chasing him this whole time and burning itself out.

It had been the start of his adventures. It had burned so angry and bright then. It had been the reason he had met Suki, had to leave her, had met so many others. The reason he even had a moon to love at all. Without the sun chasing him north, he would have never met Yue. He would have never learned all he had and become who he was.

Now it was here next to him burning too dull for him to see it, yet there it was. Zuko was there. He had been from the start. He was powerful, strong willed, combative, kind, and always willing to try even if he was bound to burn himself.

Sokka had loved Yue with his love for the water and his people. He would always love her in a way. He loved Suki, he knew he did. Suki loved him too, but besides that excited kiss when they found each other in the prison they had done nothing but speak to each other as leaders. Ever since he had seen her here he had felt that familiar drunk on love feeling, but that was just it. It was drunken and one could never stay drunk forever. She had also not moved towards him beyond that kiss so he was sure she felt it too. They loved each other but they were not in a place to be together. He was sure if he asked her she would agree. She was also safe and she had not really crossed his mind since that kiss hello.

His thoughts had only really revolved around one factor since arriving here, and for awhile but he would not say that. A common factor. His heart skipped a few beats and continued to beat oddly as Sokka followed that thought. A common factor he had been watching so closely he could tell you all the little actions he did when he ate or thought someone was mad at him. Could describe his frustrated habits because he had ‘studied them in case he attacked.’ He had to admit, Zuko firebending he had always found hot. Joke fully intended! Of watching his soft and secret smiles he would send Aang’s way after the kid finally figured something out.

Zuko was a sun burning itself out that he had sent to freeze. He could not help the small smile when he thought about the looks of concentration Zuko made when he was trying hard not to mess something up. The swears and overall tantrum when he did, only to watch him come back and try again so much calmer. He never gave up. The biting of his lower lip when he was concentrating on something. The way he positively lit up when Aang had once mentioned the theater or how he had withdrawn and dulled when everyone questioned him rather aggressively on this.

“No, no, this was stopping right now. Back it up. You love Suki, you said so yourself.” Sokka growled into the empty space before him. “You can’t suddenly catch feelings for another. Whatever this is, it is stress, tired, and guilt over everything. That is it. Where is that box.”

Sokka squeezed his eyes shut and promptly shoved everything he had just been thinking into the newly labeled box of ‘Zuko’ and envisioned it being sealed tight. He had to get back to his station or he would risk the plan. This would all be over soon and he and Suki could talk and actually start their relationship right. She was his real sun. Stress. It was just his guilty stress. He was logical and when he was out of here he would be back to thinking straight. He knew what he had to do, best to focus on that.

## Chapter 10

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

I'm going to be down to one or two chapters a week but more is coming!  
Thank you for following this mess of a story and reading along.

### Text

With the loudest sigh she could muster Suki followed the crowd back into the area being used for the dinner distribution. While it was not the courtyard, this space was one of the widest open spaces on the west wing of the prison. It was often only used when the majority of people were not being allowed out of their cells because of the tighter space and judging from the people spread out that was most likely the case now. Possible that some of those who were in the crowd and cheering earlier got a bit too into the fight and were on a mild lock down for the night. If Suki was being honest, she was surprised that list did not include herself or Jet. A glance to the corner confirmed that the wheat eating nut job was safely tucked into his little group. She rolled her eyes at this and moved forward towards the dinner lineup.

Taking a moment to look over those around she took note of the tired looks throughout the room. Looks like they were in for a hard time of work with half rations after everything. This meant that they were either getting new people and needed those in the prison to reflect the lowest points, or two fire shows in a day had warranted the warden to use the stick up his own ass to crack down on things. Still, she was thankful that ration guarding was not her job this month but maybe if it was she could find a way to stash some for Zuko later.

That thought struck hard and she slowed to a stop. It had been a long time since she was able to work with them but maybe, it was possible they did not all burn. Perhaps that would be more useful than she thought. Sokka had said plan B, but what if plan A needed a bit more help than they realized.

As soon as the crowd dispersed and settled she glanced around to locate the guards, noting that they seemed much more distracted than usual. Taking advantage of this, she quickly dipped to the wall and followed it towards a back corner out of the line of sight. The West Wing had a very tight bend in one of the far corners that angled out roughly. More a tiny triangle than a continued wall as the support beam did not quite meet the seam. This tight triangle rarely gained any attention because it was angled just right to catch the sun from mid day through sunset and did not lose that heat till well after night time lock down.

Walking with confidence was always key when doing this. Suki had learned quickly that the more you looked like you were meant to be somewhere, the less likely you are to be questioned or discovered. It also helped that she was walking into the brightest location on the west side of the prison and not even the firebenders liked staring into the sun like that. Despite being of the sun, they still felt its harsh rays and their eyes still strained if it was too bright. They were human, after all, not some fire beasts like some liked to paint them out to be.

She dragged her fingers over the hot metal with a feather light touch and followed the connection ridge until she found a gap. The buckle in the metal was tucked in the back seam where the points of the wall met the back of the support beam. With a glance around she pushed hard on the buckle and bent the metal inwards just enough to wiggle through the tight opening created. After she dropped onto the other side she quickly stood to use her weight and bend the metal back into place. She held herself against the hot metal in the darkness and waited. The seconds ticked away loudly and she felt as if her heartbeat was echoing just as loud as the metal had in the tight space as she waited for the shouting. None came, she had slipped away unnoticed. Blessed Kyoshi, she was safe for now.

In the darkness she leaned away from the hot metal. Instant relief from the heat washed through her as she sighed and slumped to the ground to sit. This was the hardest part in her opinion. The waiting. She hated being stationary but she could and would sit still for hours if it was needed. Did not mean she had to like it. Fidgeting with her hands, she mimed opening and closing motions as if she had her fan back. She would go through more motions if she could but this space was just not big enough and she could ruin what she came here for. Slowly, probably only minutes but felt like hours, her eyes adjusted to the lack of any light.

She was in a pocket behind a corner support beam of Boiling Rock. Here the structure of the actual prison reaches up to connect with the beam about eight feet above where she currently sat. This created a pocket that angled downwards sharply, but provided just enough space for three people to sit in a tight circle if they needed. Suki had the faintest idea why this pocket was needed but did know that every outer support had one. It was just a matter of if the metal had buckled enough to allow someone to enter the space. If she was really curious she might ask Sokka why such a waste of space was overlooked but she did not really care.

It was also out of the boiling heat so anything that did enter was saved from the harshness of the elements. This made the locations perfect for hiding things. Or for growing things. Suki looked around at the bundles of green surrounding her and could not help the large smile that overtook her. The wave of relief and excitement was almost overwhelming. She slowly reached out and touched one of the plants to feel it. It was healthy, despite everything. It was feeding off the small amount of light let in from the seam, the humidity keeping it moist, and it was thriving. Suki was not one with the earth, even many earthbenders knew little about plants themselves. She knew this plant well and by Kyoshi is was quickly becoming her favorite.

Dumpling weed. It was a hearty little thing that was abundant throughout the Earth Kingdom. Kyoshi island grew a few patches just because they survived the winter well and were a great source of nutrition when in a pinch. These little plants had been her first big reason for becoming one of the inmates on the ‘do not let out of your sight’ list thanks to other inmates helping her grow them. The first main crop had been grown in cracks throughout the prison and the pods had been distributed with meals to increase nutrition. Those who ate the dumpling pods were much less likely to bend to the warden and his ridiculous demands. The firebenders out of the cooler also healed from being ‘frozen’ nearly twice as fast as those who were without the pods.

Suki looked around as her eye sight adjusted more and beamed even wider. Her crop had not only survived the warden and the guards trying to burn them all away after they were discovered, but they had thrived. There was almost no visible flooring and the plants twisted around one another as if to hold strong. She followed the vines of the short plants and found a few pods. Twisting, she picked a pod. It was about the length of her pinkie finger and had three distinctive bumps along the length where the seeds sat protected inside the pod. It felt healthy and stiff. It might not be ripe, but it was edible and would do the job nicely. Hopefully if she could pocket enough of them she could have both firebenders in their group working sooner rather than later.

She worked fast to pick the largest pods she could find, taking care to sift through the plants without crushing any. As she worked she chewed on a few herself and noted the bitter taste as the seeds popped while she bit them. If she had any idea about her group, getting a particular firebender to eat these was going to be a struggle.

Sokka would be vocal about the bad tastes but eat them if she gave him a look. He buckled easy when it came to the looks from women he respected and this thought made a small smile overtake her features. Chit Sang would not make a fuss one way or another. Food was food and he was currently ‘frozen’ so he knew better than to turn anything away. Jet would, she didn’t much care if Jet would anything really. More for them if he was a snob and that was fine by her.

Zuko was her concern. She did not think he would turn his nose up to them or make any comment or fuss vocally. She also did not believe he would actually eat them and not just pocket them for others later if she did not so much as glare him down the whole time. How Sokka had worried about that boy being a threat to anyone but himself while in this kind of situation she had no idea.

Her pockets were full and she had even taken to stuffing her thin prison uniformed under layer that was provided to female inmates. She picked a last few for herself and stuffed them into her shoes. When it came to spare food one did not have the luxury of hiding places after all. If anything, those would be Jet’s if he so much as looked at her boys again.

Sokka had been a part of the Kyoshi team once, he took a high ranking in her books. Zuko was new but Sokka needed him so she had added him to a lower level of her list of people to protect or work with. Was not expecting a great change to this but one apparently could not predict much of anything when dealing with Zuko. One conversation in and she had added him to the list of ‘her people’ with a scary ease. He had been low on that list still, all things considered. Now after everything from his hesitation to accept a simple kindness, that confusion so rooted in his core at a simple caring gesture sent his way, and that desperate need to just be needed or helpful. Well, no one could really blame her if she had moved him up to being near Sokka in her mental list now could they?

That was why she had felt Jet’s attack on Zuko was a personal attack. He had hurt one of her boys and that was not something she overlooked. No one gets to harm her team without repercussions. Jet, for all Sokka wants to help him, was not a needed part of the team in her book. He was therefore, not one of her boys.

Dusting off and popping one last dumpling into her mouth Suki moved to go back to the wall and froze. The light coming in was being distorted, which meant someone was outside and close. Had she been discovered after all? It would not be the worst thing but definitely would hurt Sokka’s plan if she was thrown into lock down after all of this.

A knock to the metal told her that, yes, someone knew she was there. She stood in silence hoping that whoever it was would just move on. Her heart was pounding and she hoped they could not hear it because it sounded awfully loud to her. Her hope was met with a low voice that echoed through the tight space.

“Hey, little lady, got anythin’ good cooked up in yer little corner?” It was a gruff voice. Male, and one she recognized but could not place. She stayed silent.

“Come now, we are partners here. Don’t tell me ya can’t recognize a helping hand.”

She remained frozen.

“Come on. What ya need from me? Oh, I know. Cooler Boats? That good enough?”

Cooler Boats. That was Sokka’s plan, and the name he had thrown together in a way he was probably so deeply proud of but left everyone else groaning. This meant one of three things. One, they were busted. Two, they were adding more people than they could. Or three, this was a member of team Cooler Boat.

“Chit Sang?” Her voice rang loudly in the space even though she had tried to whisper.

“There ya go. Yeah, it’s me. Find anything good?” His chuckle echoed through her space as she breathed a sigh of relief.

“Yeah, actually. I got some weeds that survived.”

“Nice, count me in. If ya got enough, I’d like a pocket full. Gotta feed the group ya know.”

“Yeah, okay, sure. Give me another minute.” She twisted to quickly pick a handful more. She was admittedly in a bigger hurry that she should have been. Her heart was still pounding at the original fear of being discovered. The plants she pulled from cracked and popped as she accidentally ripped a few up. Wincing, she turned toward the crack again and kicked it lightly.

“Got ‘em? Good. Hold on now. The guards are swarming around like mindless scorpion bees. Something has them all stirred up and stupid.” The eye roll was clear in his statement.

“I need to get out of here. Jet and I need to-”

“I know. Jet sent me this way after he watched yer little disappearing act. That kid may be as slippery as a viper snake but he is observant. I can get ya out, for that pocket of weeds. I can even make it worth you and Jet slipping away.” He sounded confident. Suki only made a noise of agreement.

Chit Sang bent the metal back with ease and accepted the bundle of dumplings. He first pocketed them before nodding. “Wait till ya hear that kids voice. He will be over when it is safe for you to leave and slip away. Don’t give me that face, yer the one who agreed to this.”

He walked away with a laugh, leaving Suki to groan and lean against the beam to just listen and wait for whatever he was about to do. Great Kyoshi she hated the waiting game.

Whatever Chit Sang had done, it was very effective. Within moments the quiet of the space echoed loudly with shouts and cheers. She was tempted to leave immediately but forced herself to wait as the cheers became louder. Footsteps echoed as many rushed forward, guards or otherwise, and she realized the Chit Sang had started a fight with someone. That was a good way to get locked down, but also the fastest distraction.

“Hey, ready to get out of here?” Jet’s voice was enough the make her eye twitch as she sighed and pushed the metal to open the space. She would never admit his added help pushing the metal back made this action quicker than it normally was as she slipped through the opening.

She was first met with his cocky smile and noted a lack of wheat in his mouth. The next thing she saw was a large crowd surrounding Chit Sang and another man roughly the same build as him. That man had short hair chopped haphazardly and was currently screaming about rations being owed and that a ‘frozen’ bender was worthless anyway. He was on a fast track to cooler time and this was admittedly the perfect distraction. Catching Chit Sang’s glance Suki watched as he winked towards her and promptly lifted the man charging him only to throw him backwards. Right, ‘frozen’ or not Chit Sang was a beast of a man.

“Come. We have to go now if we don’t wanna risk being seen.” Jet motioned to the door that was currently empty of people. Right, prepping for plan B starts now.

“Remember our agreement. Silent unless needed.” She strode forward and into the open doorway. This was going to be the worlds longest sneak mission and she would rather they finished quickly. She tried not to let the chuckle from him grate on her already thin nerves but it was no use. This man simply breathing was too much for her at the moment and the faster they did this, the better.

Jet was true to his word. He followed silently and only spoke to highlight things such as where different halls led in case of needing a hiding spot. Admittedly, it was Suki who broke their little agreement out of sheer curiosity.

“I thought you always had something in your teeth. What, finally get over that?” She glanced over at him to watch his reaction. He simply raised an eyebrow at her and smirked which made her flush with anger.

“Thought this was a silent tour?” His smirk was even present in his words and she flushed with the effort needed to prevent smacking it off his in her opinion stupid face.

“Yes, well. Forgive me for being curious and being bored.” She huffed.

Jet laughed, “yeah, that is forgivable. Spirits know this place sucks. No, I don’t have the wheat anymore and I was actually hoping to snag another here soon. Your little pet can spit fire, did you know that? Guess the guards missed it too but right after I got him on the ground the first time he spit fire at my wheat and burned it.” He finished with a dramatic eye roll.

Suki laughed, she could not help it. The image of Zuko on his back and taking that moment to burn that damn wheat like so many wanted to do was priceless. Spirits she loved that spunky firebender.

“So you know where to get more? How do you even know something like that?” She glanced around knowing they were being anything but quiet.

“Relax. No one comes down here if they are traveling from the West Wing. I know these things because, as you so helpfully stated, I am never where I should be.” That smirk was back, spirits all around this was testing her will. How could one human be so charismatic and yet so aggravating at the same time?

“Okay, so where do you get more?”

“The kitchens, we are almost there now.”

Suki slowed her pace. “Wait. Are you telling me you actively steal from the guards? How are you not under constant lock down? I’m sure that wheat is a clear sign of just where you were.”

Jet, for his part simply shrugged. “You have no idea just how easy a piece of wheat is to overlook. I will hide it if needed, but usually they are all too focused on the firebenders to give us the time of day.”

“Okay, so why do you need it? Like, what purpose does a stick hold?”

“Comfort, mostly. My family used to farm wheat fields. We had rolling fields as far as I can remember. My father would pick me up high and carry me above those fields on his shoulders. My mother baked the best breads from our wheat. The animals were always happy.” Jet sighed, relaxing into a distant memory as they moved.

“Jet… You don’t have to-”

“Wheat burns fast and hot. Did you know that? I didn’t. I do now. The Fire Nation raided my village when I was only twelve. They burned everything. My families wheat was the first thing to ignite and the last to extinguish. They killed everyone or did worse yet before death. I was twelve and I watched my family burn from the safety of the trees.” He stopped and turned to face her, the mask of emptiness firm over any emotion he may be processing. “I don’t forget, I wont forgive, I will avenge those who burn for this pointless war. So, forgive me if a small piece of wheat feels like home.”

Suki watched as Jet moved forward and dipped into a room with a glance around. Wherever he was going she would give him that space. He had just told her a great deal and opened a pain she could not even begin to understand. His anger towards firebenders made so much more sense now. Not that it did not make sense before, hate towards them was much more common than any other emotions.

She leaned against the wall and sighed, perhaps she should be a bit more willing to hear this guy out. He had to be around her age and she could not imagine how she would have felt or reacted to seeing Kyoshi burn at the age of twelve. Back then she was still training under the old leader of the Kyoshi warriors. What would she have done if she had watched them all burn or worse? She would build on that hate. She would have lashed out either blindly or built on the need to be the biggest problem to the Fire Nation she could be. Jet, for how much she hated to admit, had acted and was acting as she would have most likely done. That was enough a reason to relax her hate on him, but not enough to let him near her boys. He was still very much not a part of her boys.

Jet slipped back out of the room with a piece of wheat in his fingers. Once the door was shut he placed the wheat in his mouth and winked towards her. Suki only rolled her eyes and pushed off the wall to match his lead.

“So, enough sob stories. Tell me how you of all people got pulled into this war. Kyoshi, right? Last I heard that island was untouched thanks to their sea pet.”

Suki rolled her eyes again “Unagi. And yes, we were not in the war. Not at first.”

“What changed?” His voice sounded only curious.

“The Avatar.”

“You too, huh?”

“Yeah, he landed on our island shore. Sokka and Katara, too. They stayed on the island and admittedly brought a lot of life back to it. The people had become so scared of the outside world. Of anything different than Kyoshi. They brought with them the wonder and sense of being more than just an island again. But…” She trailed off, not wanting or knowing how to say the next part.

“They also bring danger everywhere they go. Yeah, I get that. They bring fun and then remind you of the great dangers we are fighting to keep that feeling safe.” He sounded so sure of this fact.

“After they left me and my girls knew we could no longer ignore what was happening. There was no going back to just being a scared island. The Unagi was doing her job in keeping the island safe, and we needed to do more.”

“Makes sense. So all the Kyoshi warriors left?”

“Not everyone. Some of the younger groups stayed to train with the retired warriors. We would never leave Kyoshi island unprotected. The main group under me, we took to the Earth Kingdom and started helping everyone we could. We moved from settlements to colonies to help with burns, rebuilding, and even pushing back against Fire Nation.”

Jet nodded while listening, only making a sound of agreement when needed.

“We were doing great work. It felt amazing to be able to do so much good for so many, but the more we helped the more people needed. We helped a child with a burn and suddenly there were ten more. Twenty. A village burned down. Deaths, some fast others painful. It took a toll on us. Many of my girls were unable to sleep most nights. Others woke sobbing or screaming. It was great to help, but it was also horrifying. War is horrifying.”

“I know what you mean. My Freedom Fighters, they were like your girls. They were those children taken from families either by force or because of death from Fire Nation raids. They fought back and I am so proud of them, still, no matter where they are. They were such brave little warriors who should have never had to have been. They were only kids, after all. They couldn’t sleep, I never blamed them for their screams or crying. No one did.” His voice was almost a whisper, not with the need to be quiet, but with the memories he was trying not to awaken as they spoke.

“Freedom Fighters?”

Jet grinned wide as he peeked down a pathway and motioned for her to follow. “Yeah. My gang of like minded warriors. They were great. Most are just fine, don’t worry. I know where a lot of them are and even if they don’t have it in them to fight anymore I’m still so proud of them. Took a bit to realize that, but I do now and I regret some of my actions.”

“I’m sure they understand” Suki said. She remembered some of her past moments with her girls that left a less than favorable aftertaste. Leaving ones home for the horrors of war did not leave room for many fun moments.

“So, now that we are fully breaking our rule of no talking, tell me about you. You specifically, I mean. Boiling Rock is for the worst of the worst after all and you don’t seem all that scary-” his words cut short as he flinched away from her to avoid the punch she geared up. Suki grinned at his quick reaction and crossed her arms with an eyebrow raised, daring him to retry that statement.

Jet cleared his throat and dusted nothing from the front of his shirt. “Yeah yeah, laugh it up.”

“I will, thanks. I’m here because I was the leader of the Kyoshi warriors. I trained groups and sent them to be the worst pain for the Fire Nation and was on the way to the Earth King himself to help. Unfortunately, we let in people we believed to be just like us. We were teaching them what we could and they betrayed us. They knew I was the leader and connected to the Avatar and his group so I was picked to be sent away. Cut the head off the Unagi, stop the rest of it kind of thing.”

Jet nodded in understanding. He motioned for her to stop as they watched for guards moving around. Once it was clear they turned back.

“That way is the cooler wall. No use trying with your little pet in there. Not a chance they would leave it unguarded. So, you trusted a person who turned out to be Fire Nation, huh?”

Suki sighed heavily “our biggest mistake. My biggest mistake. Azula. She got into my group and took it over from the inside. We taught her group all we could and she burned us down. She is not to be overlooked. She is the worst, absolutely evil.”

Jet grunted in agreement. “That, I can believe.”

The next few minutes were spent in silence, not by choice. Jet showed her spots to hide as they met different guard groups weaving back towards the main area. He was relaxed the entire time and gave no sign of anything being out of the ordinary so Suki trusted his judgment on when to act. After they were starting to do the loop a second time and actually work on timing it Suki’s curiosity got the better of her.

“Sokka said he thought you were dead… were you?” she asked.

“Wouldn’t you like to know?” Jet twisted to wink at her.

Suki shifted and punched the arm closest to her. She was going for a semi friendly gesture but the bruising that had been steadily growing since his little war with Zuko made even that painful. She did not need power to cause pain and thought that he kind of still deserved it.

“Ow- Hey! Alright! Yeah, I was. Bit of a hard thing to talk about, but yeah, I died. Not for long, but long enough.” He leaned away from her and rubbed the spot on his arm with a slight moan. He continued forward without really pausing.

“Sorry, but I have to ask. How are you here then?”

“Simple really. The Dai Lee, they are these security figures in Ba Sing Se-”

“I know the Dai Lee. They are part of the reason why I am here thanks to Azula” Suki cut in with an angry huff. Azula had lied to her and everyone in order to claim the title as a Kyoshi warrior and she had fallen for it. It still hurt to think about. She had learned about the Dai Lee from her as they planned to move to the Earth King together, and thinking about her or those other girls still makes her blood boil.

“Well well. Would you look at that! Another thing we have in common! Our gangs, Fire Nation betrayal, and now a hatred of the Dai Lee. I guess they showed up right as I died and I don’t really know what they did… ‘cause… you know. Dead?”

“Right, so you died and then they showed up to revived you?” She sighed out and rolled her shoulder to release tension. Zuko’s look was right, that really did not do shit to help. “Don’t get me wrong, but they don’t seem the helping type if they are following just who I think they are.” She finished in a growl.

“Your guess is as good as mine, really, but I woke in a prison hospital so that must account for some of it. Once I was healed enough they had me sent here and that’s all I know really.” He gave a casual shrug. As if he was talking about some game and not his own death. Must be a defense mechanism of some kind, Suki thought. A sense of detached reality.

“Wow, that’s a lot to take in.”

“You’re telling me.” Jet said through a quiet but genuine laugh. “Was literally the worst week of my life. Found out my gang was against me, my boyfriend who I had started to think was the sun itself with how intense he could be was actually a liar and the literal worst, got caught by the Dai Lee thanks to him, got brainwashed, and then I tried to help the Avatar only to end up dead. 10 out of 10, would not recommend.” He had counted each point on his hand until he had all five fingers on one hand spread and wiggled them to prove his point.

“Boyfriend? Wait, you’re gay?”

The words had left her before she had time to stop them. She had just been shocked to hear those words come from him of all people. Her voice held nothing but confusion as she tried to riddle something out but was missing some pieces. By the look he gave her he had taken no offense and was giving her the space to solve her riddle. He just smiled warmly and led the way.

Jet had been seen with so many of the women in the prison. That was normal, most here had someone to lean on but the more she thought about it, the more she realized that he had also been around men. Maybe his little gang here was more than just a group of like minded people after all.

Jet’s laughing cut into her little thought spiral. His smile widened as he looked at her again and took in the look on her face. She had to admit, it was a damn nice smile on his features. No wonder people fell for some of the shit that came out of it.

“Wow, just gonna jump on that part, huh? Not the brainwashing or anything? No, I don’t think I’m gay. Don’t like to label it really ‘cause I don’t give a flying hog monkey what someone is. If they are attractive and have a strong personality consider me smitten.” He had the nerve to wink at her again. At this rate she might start thinking he had something in his eye.

Suki twitched towards him as if to punch his arm once more and grinned wide at his automatic flinch away. His face had read of panic for a moment before he took in the fact that she did nothing more than raise her fist quickly in his direction. She never had any actual intent on making contact with that action this time.

Realizing he had been faked out yet again he let out a frustrated huff. Looking at the grin on Suki’s face, however, he couldn’t help but chuckle at his own reaction and they both shared a small laugh at this little game of theirs. He turned again towards a new hall and Suki was reminded of the maze they were currently mapping out.

“Alright, so this boyfriend you had. Winning personality?”

Jet let out a rather amused snort “hardly, he was absolutely hopeless in any interaction. Really, with the anger he had I should’ve known what he would really turn out to be, but damn his willpower was sexy. All you had to do was give him a ‘bet you can’t’ and watch as he indeed could not but would get the results needed anyway. It was like watching a storm. Powerful, unpredictable, and exciting.”

“So they were hot headed, stubborn, quick to anger and would do anything to prove themselves- Wait, you don’t mean to tell me that ‘Lee’ you talk about was this boyfriend, do you?” Suki slowly stopped walking and stared at Jet’s back. He had also stopped just a few feet ahead but was still. This would explain a lot, actually.  
Jet slowly turned to face a short hall with a room at the end. His eyes never left the far door and she could only see half of a sad smile on his face. It looked like wherever he was mentally, it was not happy.

“There, that is going to be your worst spot. The guards like to use this room as a secret ‘meet and greet’ if you know what I mean. They think no one will catch them in this rarely used area so people around here move faster and less predictably.” Jet pointed at the door, and did nothing more.

“Right, got it, this is the danger zone. Jet, did you and Zuko have a thing together? Is that why you two can literally not be in the same space without something going sour?”

“No. I never had anything to do with Zuko.” His expression changed from a dull pain to that of stone cold resolve. “I thought I had everything with Lee.” Turning, he led the way down the hall further.

Suki took a moment to find her legs. She stood trying to process what that look had meant. She had hated Jet for how he had been towards Zuko but this changed some things. She knew from some of her girls past relationships that ugly breaks, no matter who it was with, never broke even. While one was left hurt and angry, the other could be left broken. With the looks those two shared it was hard to see who was feeling what but she knew one thing. This was bad. Really bad.

Jet and Zuko had a past relationship together and in some way they were both hurt by this fact. That much was clear. Jet was strong willed and confident in who he was and what he needed. Zuko was strong in the sense that he never gives up but he was not sure in who he was or why. Jet had been hurt bad because someone he trusted and put his insecurities in to protect them had ‘lied’ to him in the end. She could understand that pain well, Azula had done it to her.

It did not take a genius to know just who Jet was talking about and what lie could cut him so deeply. Lee was never a person. Jet had fallen for a thing that never existed and had actually been Zuko. Zuko, who is a firebender and of the Fire Nation. She had good reason to believe ‘Lee’ was not either of those things. To someone like Jet, that kind of lie would be a foundation crack but she could see him possibly getting over it given the time and with the right reasons for someone needing to recreate themselves. Something more had come from this and it would be dangerous if they decided to settle it anytime soon.

Ten minutes, if she went fast. That was the time she needed to loop the building and check the blind spots. They had practiced a few times before they were expected back in the prisoner line up. Ten minutes for Sokka to make sure nothing was wrong. That was a lot of time for something to go wrong, but if that something was the cooler then it was just enough to prevent any lasting damage. Zuko would be fine for ten minutes and if he wasn’t Suki knew Sokka would get the others from outside to help somehow.

When her cell lock clicked for the night time lock down she wasted no time. She quickly climbed onto the rock they deemed a bed and closed her eyes. If she could steal a few hours of sleep she was going to. They all needed to be on guard and sleep deprivation was going to be affecting at least half the group. Someone needed to keep a level head when it came to this hot mess mix of people.

## Chapter 11

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

I debated breaking this into two or just doing a big chapter. Big chapter won out so enjoy!

I will probably edit it later

Grading summer exams has me busy, I'll try to stick to the one chapter a week &#60;3

### Text

“……ki.” The sound reached her exhausted mind but phased easily into the background. It did not sound angry or threatening so it must have just been the wind.

“……uki?” She groaned and turned away from the noise. It was a lot more persistent than before. Memories of her girls calling after her in different moments throughout their travels flashed through her mind as she tucked deeper into her bed.

“Hey, Suki, wake up already. We gotta go!” The voice hissed, sliding the door open further to slip inside. The clicking of the door closed did the trick and she shot up into a sitting position. The last day caught up to her in an instant and she twisted to look at the guard standing by her door.

“Sokka! Shit, sorry. I was just trying to get some sleep.”

“Hey, no worries. You are not the only one. Jet was an easy wake but Chit Sang took us both pushing to get him up. Should have left him like Jet said.” He rolled his shoulder, no real anger in his words. Suki knew he would not have left Chit Sang even if he was frustrated with the man. His eyes were trained on the window of the door to keep watch.

“So they are in position already?” How long was she asleep for? She was not a firebender so she did not have that weird time telling power that seemed to follow the benders. Really, it was jarring watching as they would all suddenly start moving to different areas as one before the others heard the bell.

“Just about. Jet knows where to go so I left it to him. Oddly enough him and Chit Sang seem to be doing just fine. How about you? How long do you need?” He glanced towards her before twisting to look out the window again.

“Ten minutes, if there is no moments where I have to hide. Fifteen to twenty tops.” She slid off the bed and adjusted her uniform, taking note of the dumpling pods still tucked into places.

“Right, yeah, okay. That should be enough time to handle the rest of the cooler and be ready for a problem. Okay, you do the outer loop and meet at the cooler when you know it is clear.”

“Got it. Sokka…” She watched as the boy did not so much as look at her, eyes trained outside and body twitching in the direction of the coolers. She walked to stand beside the boy fidgeting with his armor. His entire posture rang of anxiety and a need to run. His worry was bleeding out of him and she knew exactly what was the root of it all.

“Yeah? What is it?” He glanced towards her briefly, caught her eyes and turned to face her more.

“This will work. You are good at what you do. Zuko is okay.” She grabbed his shoulder with a reassuring squeeze as he sighed. His whole body slumped against the door and his head dropped to hang low.

“Thanks. I just hope you are right this time.” He scrubbed his face with his palms in an action that screamed of being overwhelmed.

“I am. But Sokka, I’m sorry but there is something you should know, just in case. I learned something about Jet and Zuko while I did the rounds.”

Sokka stiffened and twisted to look back out the window and pulled his shoulder from her grasp. “Suki, we really don’t have time-”

“Sokka, this is important and might help stem any future situations. We are about to put a ‘frozen’ Zuko in front of Jet. We need to be ready.” She knew they did not have time, but they could make a few minutes. Her gut was telling her that this was important and they needed to be on alert.

Sokka sighed deeply and let his forehead rest on the window. “Yeah, okay, what did you learn?”

“Those two have a really complicated history.” Maybe not her best, but this was a hard topic to voice in her opinion.

Sokka snorted a small laugh and turned towards her. Good, he looked less like he was about to fall apart so that was worth something.

“Yeah, kinda figured that one out. Not much we can do other than keep them apart for now.”

“No, Sokka, I mean I think they were involved together romantically. Like, they were dating. Boyfriends from what Jet had said so I think it was pretty serious.”

It was out there now. Sorry Zuko, Suki thought. She vowed to make it up to him later, after all this was his past she was spilling to someone he clearly respected quite highly.

Sokka, she noted, flushed at this bit of information and promptly started to look everywhere but at her.

“Oh?” Was all he could croak out.

“You’re not shocked by this?” She studied the boy in front of her.

No, he was definitely shocked but there was so much more under that shift in posture. She raised an eyebrow, crossed her arms, and cocked her posture to put her weight onto one leg. There was a lot more in this situation than she originally thought.

Sokka watched her change her stance, flushed harder, and stuttered out a “oh no, I am much worse.”

“Uh-huh, how so?” She could and would push him on this.

Everything about him screamed he was embarrassed and potentially jealous. This was not what they needed to mix into the situation but she had always thought Sokka and her were more similar than they realized. This might be proof of that. Suki knew she could fall for a warrior just as easily as a princess and it did not matter who fit what roll. Problem was, which of the two boys in this had caught Sokka’s interest.

“Yeah, I am absolutely stunned to the point that I will freak out later. Right now, we can’t give it the time needed to process.”

Suki was going to figure it out right now. They needed to focus and this could mess everything up if they were not careful.

“Okay, because I was equally shocked at Jet being-”

Sokka waved off her comment before she could continue. He chuckled lightly at his own thoughts before looking towards her again with a slight smirk.

“Oh, that I don’t think is the shocking part. Jet would hook up with a tree if it agreed with his ideas, that I am sure about. It is Zuko that shocks me. He doesn’t let anyone past that wall of hate and he really picked the wrong guy to practice that with.” His face fell into a look of serious and deep thought at the last part and she had her answer.

Everything, literally everything since she had met up with Sokka had revolved around this one point. Internally kicking herself for how long this took to riddle out she realized that Zuko had always had Sokka’s full attention here. To be fair to her skills in reading her boys, it had only been a day since she had first seen Sokka in her cell like he was now. That time had been such a cocktail of emotions that it was overwhelming for both of them. She had kissed him and it had felt right and lacking. He was her knight in shining Fire Nation red for all of ten minutes before that focus was back on the other boy.

Zuko, for his part had put his life into Sokka’s hands like it was as easy as breathing. He trusted Sokka with his entire being and his eyes were always scanning for the other boy when he was not around. He knew Sokka so well he could tell him from another just by how he stood for Kyoshi’s sake! She had thought she knew Sokka, but she knew him as a leader and a warrior.

She did not look to him like Zuko did, as a partner and a protector in one being. Sokka had said Zuko had practiced letting another close, even if the other was a man, and had focused on how he had let the wrong one in. Like he would have been the right one.

She sighed, felt something in her heart break just a little at the hope she had fed with the mix of adrenaline and timing, and smiled lightly. She would mourn what was once hers later, now was not the time. He was never really hers to begin with, and it might sting but she was a warrior and what was a little heartbreak to freedom and watching her boys be happy.

“I guess you are right about that. Alright. I just needed you to know why it is important to keep them apart in case they start something. Give me about 15 minutes to loop the place. For now you should go be Sunshine’s first real knight in shining armor.”

Sokka flushed deep and his head whipped back around to stare at her. His stance rang of confusion and she could not stop the full eye roll as he stuttered out “what?”

Suki chuckled and winked at him, she would hurt about this later. “Go save your man, you idiot. I’ll be there in 15 minutes to help if anything goes wrong.”

She shoved past him and out of the door. Glancing back at him she raised her eyebrow in question, daring him to challenge her on this.

Sokka stammered some nonsense at her about “not having a man” before he promptly hurried down the hall with only one clear goal in mind.

She watched as he tried hard to not stumble with the effort he was using to not sprint the way towards the coolers. Watching him leave hurt worse than seeing him process his feelings in front of her. It was a finality she did not think she would ever really understand. It stung, but in a bitter sweet way as she thought of the boy in the cooler needing so much more than he had ever been given and she thought Sokka just might be able to give that. She huffed and rolled her eyes muttering while ignoring the wet feeling on her face.

“Men, why are they so dense all the time.” With that, she had a job to do and turned to head the opposite way down the hall.

&#45;

Sokka nearly stumbled as he rounded the final corner a bit too quickly. He nodded to the guard there as he walked to the cooler door with as much confidence as he could muster as his heart threatened to beat its way out of his chest. He glanced towards the old man and sighed heavily.

“Zhan, I was told to do this alone to be given the space for whatever I needed.” That was a thing, right? That had to be enough. Yue’s blessed moon let it be enough.

Zhan took a moment and looked a bit upset at potentially missing the show but nodded.

“I understand. Try not to have too much fun here, Lee. The warden still needs him looking nice for later.”

Sokka nodded and watched as Zhan left. He counted a few more seconds and took a steadying breath. Reaching for the door felt like he was lifting weights far above his abilities. His hands shook as he undid the latch and with a final exhale he ripped the door to the side and slid it open.

“I can take you back to your cell now, but only if you’ve learned your lesson” came out as playfully as he could muster while he awaited the answer.

He could not risk Zhan listening in even if it had looked like he had left. He held his breath and took in the huddled figure in front of him who had jerked hard as the door opened. Good, he was alive and that took a large part of the worry out of Sokka’s heart. It beat less heavily in his chest, but not evenly as he waited for the pale and shivering form in front of him to do more than just flinch.

Sokka watched as Zuko, for his part, had taken a moment to hear his words. Zuko shifted to lift his head as if it weighed twice the normal weight and shivered when the cold air brushed his face. Knowing he was without his fire to keep him warm this action sent a pang of regret through Sokka that lasted a whole second at most.

Without much effort, Sokka watched as Zuko took a breath deep into his possibly frozen lungs and released the heat, steam, and sparks to fight off the new cold before opening his molten gold colored eyes. He locked his eyes with Sokka’s and grinned wide, sparks still flickering through his teeth as Sokka’s body grew far too hot and his heart beat much too fast.

“Yes, I have… Completely” was the answer spoken but Sokka was having a hard time hearing it.

Zuko had spit fire, admittedly in the hottest way Sokka had ever seen the jerkbender do something ever in his life. He had fire. He was not frozen. He was alright and he was grinning at Sokka like he had won the worlds best prize. The parts of the cooler settled in his lap for show.

Sokka, for his part, was realizing just how screwed he was and just how much he was going to have to avoid Suki’s knowing looks and Toph’s weird feet. This was not the time or place. This was not the expected result of any of this adventure. In less than 24 hours Zuko had gone from annoying, to friend, to something Sokka was very much going to ignore. He hated to admit that this was a common thing for this kind of feeling for him. Suki had taken a day, Yue was at a glance, he should have seen this coming.

Sokka sighed out in full relief as he watched Zuko stuff the bolts into his pockets and shoes, which he had to admit was a rather smart idea when it came to keeping them from clattering around. Reaching out a hand, Sokka pulled Zuko to his feet and used his other arm to brace the unsteady firebender upright. He absolutely did not take note of the fact that Zuko was almost a head taller than himself. He also did not note the fact the said boy had placed his, admittedly much lighter than he should be, weight into Sokka’s arms with a shy and grateful smile. Zuko almost felt like he fit there. His heart was going to give out from whiplash at this rate.

“Hey, easy there. How are you doing?” Sokka took the moment of stabilizing the other boy to take in the slight tremor in his frame. He was cold, despite the flame show earlier, and was using Sokka as a support more than he probably realized.

“Alright, don’t worry. Was just squatting longer than usual so my legs are a bit tight.” Zuko pulled back but stayed within Sokka’s reach and looked up towards the next task they had. The outer top bolts. Sokka, for his part, had forgotten about those bolts in favor of the fact that he still held the forearms of a trembling Zuko.

His mind was fully on Zuko and his tired look that he did not recognize the voices coming around the corner until Zuko had switched the hold onto his own forearms and pulled Sokka into the arctic air with him. Zuko shoved the door mostly closed while Sokka stood stunned and watched as the tremors in the firebenders body did little to hamper his task. Shaking himself out of whatever world he was just in, Sokka joined in mostly closing the door to listen to the guards pass by.

“Yeah, new arrivals at dawn.” A males voice echoed down the hall towards them. The clear clang of armored shoes on the floor made Sokka momentarily kick himself mentally for missing its slow approach.

“Why is it always dawn with everything? Anybody interesting?” That was Ming’s voice. She must be heading in for the night and met up with the rotation change guard.

“Naw, just the usual. Some high class robbers, couple traitors, some war prisoners. You know, the basics. Though I heard that their might be a pirate!”

“No fooling? That would be fun.” Ming sounded overly excited about this, Sokka could not blame her. He had also been excited about pirates before he had met some.

They stayed frozen to the door for a moment longer, watching to see if any more people would come down. When none did they sighed in relief.

“War prisoner, might be your dad.” Zuko looked towards Sokka.

“Yeah, might be.” Sokka gripped the door handle tighter. If it was his dad, he was about to fail him again without him ever knowing.

“What do you want to do?” Zuko was almost whispering. He sounded far away to Sokka as his mind spiraled.

He had come here for his father. To save his people from the possible pain he had put them through with his failures. To rectify his mistakes. This one action had led him to mistake after mistake that was continually costing other people so much more than himself. Zuko had lost his only means of distance travel and whatever else he had kept in that balloon he had. He hadn’t even been upset much at Sokka shoving his only possession into the boiling lake. He had helped him do it.

Sokka had messed up with Suki, more now than he would like to admit. During that he had gotten Zuko locked into this place and once again failed him. Now he had four people relying on him to get them out and here he was debating risking them all on the chance that his dad might be on the way.

A glance at Zuko was enough to tell Sokka that he would not oppose Sokka’s want to stay. Taking in that it was prince Zuko of the Fire Nation, enemy number two under Aang himself, Sokka needed to get him out of here. That clock was almost out of time if a shipment had anything to do with it.

“Let’s get this thing moving, the others are waiting.”

With that Sokka stood up quickly to rip open the door and promptly lost his footing. With a yelp of surprise his feet slipped on the mini skating rink that was the floor of the cooler and he was slammed back against the back wall.

This rattled the cooler and caused a startled Zuko to also lose his footing. Zuko slipped forward and kept hold of the door as he fell towards the side, pulling the door closed with him. As the door stopped at the end of its path Zuko’s hold was ripped away and he toppled into the side next to Sokka with a deep moan. The cooler shook and groaned with the impact and a loud pop echoed. Sokka watched in horror but Zuko’s eyes spoke of reawakened pain, dazed and very confused.

Sokka looked up at the now closed door and his heart sank. This was not what he had planned for but by the odd angle they now sat he doubted that door was going to be easy to slide open from the inside. Scrambling up, he threw his weight into trying to pull the door back open and was rewarded with only muffled creaks as nothing budged.

Zuko had taken a moment to shake from his daze before he too was up and pulling hard. Sokka growled out and punched the door once before regretting it instantly. The pain that shot through his hand was intense but did little to dampen his frustration. Shaking out his hand he cursed loudly and slumped against the door with a sigh.

“It’s no good, we need something hard to hit it at the right angle or something from the outside to pull it open.” His words were muffled into the door as he was regretting joining in the cooler in the first place. Why was there even this much new ice!?

Zuko looked around like he might find just what was needed in the tiny space before calmly stating “my head already hurts, why not try throwing me into the door and see if that works.”

Sokka slowly turned to face the other with a look he really hoped convey how very much not funny that was.

“Are you joking?”

“Should I be? I’ve been told that I am thick headed and us hitting it just now got it stuck. Maybe repeating it would undo it?” Zuko shrugged.

Sokka groaned out in frustration and let the back of his head fall back against the cooler door with a dull thud. “We are not using you as a battering ram, Zuko, what in the spirits blessed everything is wrong with you?”

“A lot, actually.” Zuko stated as if it was a clear fact that Sokka should know already.

Sokka could not help but snort at this statement before he turned back to the door to try again. He failed, but he was not really expecting much else. Still, feeling Zuko move close to him and try to help made his heart do a funny dance. He sighed and swatted Zuko’s hands away.

“Look, we are stuck, maybe for the next ten minutes. Nothing bad. I have Suki doing a round as a backup plan just in case this kind of thing happened. She will come and open the door soon. She wont fail in this.”

“Unlike me, failing seems to be the only thing I succeed in.” Zuko muttered. Most likely that statement was meant to be kept for only his ears but in the tight space they were currently trapped in it came out crystal clear. Cold air, Sokka had learned, made everything sharp.

“What? Zuko, that is not true.” Sokka turned to face the other boy, back against the door to keep from slipping on the refrozen ice.

Zuko’s face showed just how much he did not believe Sokka. Suki was right, Sokka noted. Zuko was extremely expressive and easy to read if you knew where to look. Right now he was tucking into himself in a way that had nothing to do with the cold and flushing lightly at being heard. How often had he slipped these kinds of comments about himself in and no one had even heard them? With how easily it had come out, Sokka guessed this was well practiced and it made him feel a mixture of anger and sadness he was not used to.

“Tui and La Zuko, you are the farthest thing from a failure. Look at how you started! You were sent on what I can only assume was a fools mission seeing as you were hunting the Avatar before Katara and I even found Aang, and then you were the only one who successfully tracked us through all of the trip from the South Pole to the North!” Sokka watched as Zuko flinched hard. There was a deep pain that had settled in his posture and he noted that the boy had shifted to turn his left side away from him. There was a bigger story there that Sokka would figure out, when the other was ready. Right, time to change tactics.

“Not even Zhao can say that! He only got lucky the few times because other people helped. Even then he was foiled by the Blue Spirit! Man, what I wouldn’t give to meet them. So cool!” This got a deep flush and uncomfortable shift out of the firebender. Sokka had to agree, even with the icy tint of color to his skin that red glow was very endearing and made him look so - no, not the time, not the place. Focus.

“Sure you may have not gotten the Avatar like you originally planned but you pushed us all so hard that I don’t actually think I would be here had it not been for you. Like, we might have never had made it to the Northern Water Tribe without you tailing us and keeping us moving. Look at you right now, too!”

“Is that supposed to be funny, Sokka? Because I don’t think this is your best joke.” Zuko crossed his arms and leaned back against the far wall, about three feet away. Eyes locked onto his shoes with a glare.

“What? Why would I be joking?” Sokka had just been praising him, he thought. Where would he see a joke in that?

“Sokka, I fail at every turn to do the simplest things. I couldn’t even pass as a refugee to just get away from it all. Then I failed so bad I almost killed Aang…” He was getting quiet again.

“That wasn’t you, that was-”

“My sister? Azula? Who helped her? Who was the one to forget the golden rule: Azula always lies.” His eyes had locked with Sokka’s and he could see the hurt and regret burning him from the inside out.

“I did. I believed her when she said I could go home-” his voice gave way to a deep rasp on the last word and he took a shaky breath “that father wanted me home. Th-that he at least felt bad for…”

Zuko’s hand had touched the scar in the briefest of movements as he dropped his head to look away that if Sokka had blinked he would have missed it. He hadn’t blinked, and he didn’t miss it. He did begin to see red and had to force himself to take his own steadying breath. If he was reading this right it was not the time to plot the Fire Lords murder. That was Aang’s job.

Zuko had gotten that scar as a banishment from his home as a child. He was just a kid who had wanted a family. He wanted security back. Wanted what he had known his whole life to be what love was. He was burned by those people and still believed them to be what love meant and that made Sokka’s heart feel hollow and heavy at the same time.

He would think about this later, but he no longer believed he could ever truly be angry at the events at Ba Sing Se. As shitty as they had been, if it was him and he was given a chance to see his family in exchange for turning on those he did not really know… He could see himself doing it with a scary ease.

“Now I am here, and everyone rightfully still hates me. I get it, I hate me too. I’m also in a prison of my people because I was caught breaking into said prison. I made your plans all that much harder just by being here and thinking I could do better. So, not a funny joke.”

“Zuko… we don’t hate you” Sokka shifted to stand straighter. Trying his best not to slip on the ice and catch the other boys attention. Zuko, for his part, stubbornly stared at the floor. Sokka sighed and just spoke louder.

“Zuko, you are right that I don’t understand what you have been through but trust me when I say I want to and will listen to your story when you are ready. As for failing us, I don’t think you are seeing the whole picture here. Without you, I don’t think I would have lasted here in this prison as well as I have been. I am pretty sure I would have been caught the second I flew Appa overhead. You were right, no bison daycare.” He paused and waited for a reaction. Smirking when he watched the other boy shift to just glance in his direction. Good, he was at least listening.

“I wouldn’t have even made it to the shoreline before I’d have been caught and it is only because you sacrificed one of the only things you had physically that I made it at all. Then through a misstep I made, not you, you got caught.”

“It’s fine.” Zuko shrugged. “I stole that balloon anyway. You needed to get here, and you needed to see Suki. I understand.”

“I thought my dad would be here because of my mistakes. Suki was not even on my mind! She is more just a single blessing to come from this spirits forsaken place.”

Zuko snorted lightly. “Prison. Sokka, what were you expecting? Some kind of dance party?”

Sokka chuckled, “would that have been any better?”

This got a slight huff and smirk from the firebender and Sokka counted it as his first major win of the night. Zuko shifted and looked to Sokka, his eyes telling Sokka he was about to be very vulnerable with him and that he needed an answer.

“If you really believe all that… why did you not trust me enough to do this for you?” Zuko unfolded one arm and motioned to the cooler they were currently stuck in. Eyes trailing over the places the bolts used to be.

“Zuko, look. It really has nothing to do with trust” Okay, bad start. Zuko’s questioning look had shifted to disbelief in a matter of seconds.

“I mean it! I did a lot of thinking and it came down to needing a cooler. I wanted Chit Sang to be the one because-” Sokka’s words were getting mixed and he was starting to stutter. A harsh shiver from the cold shook Sokka’s frame and had the firebender shifting to push off the wall and close the few feet of distance between them. Sokka backed up instinctively and kept rambling.

“Chit Sang needed to do this so you-” Zuko had reached out to grab Sokka’s shoulder with an abnormally warm hand. The warmth made Sokka shiver hard as he tried to finish his statement.

“Uhg, why is this hard to say!? Look, Zuko, it was all me and not you. I just really couldn’t get the image of you sh-shivering in a cooler out of my head and it was really upsetting. I just didn’t want it to become reality, yet Jet had to go and act on his own damn ideas. Like he wanted this to happen.”

The last part was muffled into a warm shoulder as Zuko pulled Sokka forward and into a tight hug. He did not feel much warmer than Sokka himself which made Sokka’s brain short out at the action. Sokka was about to ask when he felt Zuko take a breath usually used for diving and released steam and hot air into their huddled space. Zuko’s torso was the first thing to heat up and Sokka burrowed forward realizing just how cold he had gotten leaning against the door.

Zuko shifted them a bit so that Sokka was tightly held to his chest and just breathed. With every breath he became blissfully warmer and Sokka moaned out as the cold left their space. He could feel Zuko’s body heat rise and fall with his breathing, as well as his slightly raised heartbeat which he was just going to mark down as nerves or something. His own heart was doing weird things again and his stomach had joined the party as he very much did not focus on being held tightly by a certain person. It was cold, this was just because of cold.

Zuko broke the silence first. “He did. He meant everything he did. Sokka, I don’t know how much he has told you but Jet… Jet and I have a-a history.”

Sokka couldn’t help but snort, “no shit. A blind man could see that.”

Zuko huffed at this and Sokka could feel the heat change as he most definitely blushed. Sokka shivered at the loss of heat and his legs trembled. The lack of heat going lower was dangerous for his feet.

Zuko pushed Sokka away but before he could protest he was being dragged to sit on the icy floor and turned so his back was against Zuko’s chest. He wasn’t that much bigger than Sokka yet in this moment Sokka could only focus on how his head only came up to Zuko’s shoulder. Zuko wrapped his arms around Sokka’s middle and lined his legs against Sokka’s.

“What are you- Hey, whoa wait, what did you just do!?” Sokka had jerked away as flames danced past his cheek as Zuko breathed out. The warmth had traveled through all parts of the firebender in a blissful heat but the sight of fire so close was not what Sokka was prepared for.

“Relax, I won’t burn you.” The eye roll in that statement was clear and Sokka just huffed in response as another breath of fire sent a new wave of heat against the tundra.

He could feel every breath going into Zuko, feel it heat up against his back before being released. Feel Zuko’s steady heartbeat. The limbs tight against himself. For once, he was thankful for the cold as a distraction but he needed a bit more distraction at this rate. Once the heat was enough to maintain itself for a bit Zuko spoke again, in a hushed tone. His distinctively raspy voice right next to Sokka’s ear sent a wave of shivers through him that had little to do with the cold. Spirits, he was doomed.

“It’s something my uncle taught me, actually. He called it the ‘breath of fire’ or something and said he learned it from studying other types of bending. Said it could be the difference between life and death, I didn’t believe him then. I do now.”

“You know, I think this is the most I’ve heard you talk before- hey!” Sokka flinched away from the hand that had just rudely pinched his side. He settled back into the warmth quickly and chuckled. “Alright, so, you’ve done this ‘breath of fire’ thing before?”

“Well, yeah. It’s called practice-ow!” Zuko hissed as if Sokka’s little awkward punch upwards at his shoulder had been far more painful than it should have been. Right, Jet was an ass and Sokka was going to be looking at that shoulder later. Once they were out of this death pit.

“Alright, Agni. If you must know, I already think it saved my life. Back in the Northern Water Tribe.” Zuko grumbled and rolled his shoulder with a wince. Definitely needed to be looked at, and maybe warranted a trip to Katara once everything was done.

“You mean when you swam with the turtle seals without knowing a single thing about them?” Sokka quipped. He could not help himself. He was both warm and cold at the same time and quickly becoming addicted to hearing the older teen speak. His voice was just so unique.

Zuko huffed a puff of smoke, most likely not intentional if the hand waving it away was anything to go by.

“I know about them! I know they swim, make good oil lamps, and uhm… breathe air!” He had trailed off at that last bit and mutter a quiet “eventually.”

Sokka laughed hard and shivered as he burrowed backwards. That was the Zuko he knew, quick to get defensive or upset but just as honest about things. Really, Sokka had to admit, this boy could not lie and it was damn cute. No, not- Oh forget it. He shivered again and mentally let go of whatever he was trying to shove away. Yeah, Zuko could be cute when he wasn’t being his usual raging ball of fire.

“Say, we have some time. Suki should be here any minute and most likely got caught up in the guard rotation. Tell me about the whole swimming with them while you keep us warm? Why were you in that cave and not, you know, with…” he trailed off and moved to rest his head on Zuko’s shoulder only to feel the boy wince again. Zuko gave him a sheepish grin before shifting so Sokka could rest his head onto his left shoulder instead.

“With Zhao or my uncle? Yeah, long story. And just this once because I am also fucking freezing in here. I am not going to become your personal heater.” He breathed another burst of steam and flames. The fire flitted harmlessly past his lips and licked upwards in ways Sokka was not going to think about. Sokka watched Zuko’s face as he worked through just where to start his story.

“Zhao took all my men from me, after one of the major failures. They had reason to believe me connected to the Avatar escaping him or something. I don’t know, really. I just got so angry I could only hear fire crackling.”

Huh, that was a new one. Sokka knew most would say ‘buzzing’ but maybe that was a firebender specific way of hearing.

“So he stole your men? Even your uncle? Don’t get me wrong, but he doesn’t seem the type to leave you without a really big reason.”

Zuko flinched and sighed out more fire “yeah, I told him to go out into town. That we would figure it out in the morning. That I was just tired and I stayed alone on my ship.”

“Wait, alone?”

“Do you want to hear this or just talk through it?” Zuko huffed. Clearly he was not used to speaking this much and was becoming flustered with the interruptions.

“Right, sorry. I’ll be quiet.”
