“I doubt that.” Zuko swatted away Sokka’s hand that had raised to pinch him and chuckled. “Yeah, I stayed alone but I could not sleep. Couldn’t shake this really bad feeling. Like Agni them self was pulling at my core to be in the light of the moon even though that is not their domain. That feeling saved my life. I was pacing my navigation deck when the pirates bird landed just outside the window. It took me regrettably longer than it should have even if it was dim lighting to recognize that blasted thing, but by the time I did it was too late. My ship exploded and I was thrown from the top and into the icy water of the port.”

“What!? I’m sorry, I know, silent. But Zuko, pirates tried to kill you!? They almost did! Is that why you looked like you lost a fight with a lion seal!?” Sokka had sat up and twisted around to look at Zuko. He could still remember the bruising and deep cuts all over his visible body and could only imagine what the rest had looked like. How was this guy still alive!? Zuko, he deemed, was unkillable.

Zuko flushed and looked away from Sokka’s stare with a huff. He fidgeted a little under the look and muttered “I didn’t look that bad.”

This had Sokka chuckling as he let Zuko nudge him back to sitting on the icy cold floor.

“Okay, pirates tried and failed to kill you. How did that lead to swimming into the Northern Water Tribe?”

Zuko shifted behind Sokka to settle back again.

“I stowed away as a nameless and faceless soldier on Zhao’s ship. Uncle helped keep me hidden and fed. Hush, or I will stop." Zuko had felt Sokka getting ready to interrupt again and shut it down instantly. Sokka chuckled and settled back against him. "Anyway, we got to the boarder and my maps led me to the outer wall. I knew where it was, just not how to get in. I stole a small raft from the ship and rations from my uncle. After that it was a matter of figuring out my way in. I was cold and needed to get in before Zhao did whatever he was thinking of doing and that was when I found the turtle seals. I followed them admittedly longer than I thought they could go and had to melt the ice shelf above myself to get out. There was a moment where I thought it wouldn’t work. Where my vision was going dark and my lungs burned in odd ways. But, I broke free and had to use the breath of fire to get my body temperature under control. Wasn’t easy and there were a few times I thought it wouldn’t work. It did, clearly, cause I am here.”

Sokka sat and listened. He forced himself to not interrupt and just pictured Zuko swimming. Him almost drowning. Him nearly freezing in a turtle seal cave. This boy either had no fear, was extremely stupid, or never gave up. His money was on all three.

“You are here. Spirits, Zuko. That was incredibly risky. I am glad that you didn’t freeze in the end. I need you here.” He sighed heavily.

Whatever comment Zuko was about to make was cut short as a clang and rattle sounded throughout the cooler. The cooler shook a few times before a loud scratching sound cut through the cold as the door was dragged open. Suki was straining a bit with the effort but got it open just enough to have a space big enough to squeeze out. She glanced inside after and broke into the widest of grins.

“Oh, don’t you two look sweet! Snuggled together like two turtledoves. You look so cozy, should I be jealous?” She cooed and looked at Sokka nested in Zuko’s lap. She winked at him before moving forward as if to help him up.

“No, Suki! Stay out there.” Sokka scrambled to get up and out of the forsaken thing.

Last thing he needed was her to slip into it and to start this all over. He quickly exited the cooler and sighed in relief as he was met with the hot air of the prison. His fingers were tingling and he had not realized just how cold his feet had become. If it wasn’t for his body being more accustomed to this kind of thing, he might have been in trouble.

Turning, both Suki and Sokka helped Zuko exit the cooler and turned to undoing the outer latches. Once the Cooler started to peel back they heard Jet and Chit Sang call the all clear. Sokka gave a final shove and watched as the cooler fell free from the wall with a hiss as the air mixed. The plume of steam was enough to make everyone shiver and Jet and Chit Sang caught the contraption and moved it between them to start rolling it down the hill. Sokka closed the door and sighed.

“Right, outside. Hurry.” He said. Suki and Zuko both nodded and followed.

Outside they stuck close to the wall and moved quickly along the side of the prison. Sokka looked back to see Zuko sway hard as he tried to keep his footing on the small ridge of loose gravel they were traveling. Zuko was taking up the rear of the group and Sokka realized just how bad of a choice that had been. Moving to catch Suki’s eye he slowed their descending speed. She gave him a questioning look which was exactly what he needed. Once he was sure he had only her attention he gestured towards Zuko and used a hand motion that he knew Suki would recognize.

One thing Sokka had been proud of was that while in the Earth Kingdom watching Aang and Katara play with magic elemental powers, he had been studying the hand signs made between warriors for silent communication. This particular sign might have taken Suki a total of three seconds to understand but when she did she looked back towards Zuko and nodded at Sokka. She slowed her pace even more to put herself closer to the unsteady firebender without so much as a word and Sokka sighed in relief. He had told her that Zuko was shaky and unsteady and she had listened and understood. Spirits bless that woman.

Sliding down towards the beach felt like a death march and Sokka knew why. He was about to throw everything he had come here for away without a second thought. Okay, maybe with many thoughts but he was not about to say any of them. Seeing Jet and Chit Sang sliding the cooler into place had just made everything so much more real. He was leaving. He was not going to stay and see if his dad was coming. He would get Suki and Zuko to safety and that had to be enough. Tui and La let it be enough.

He ran towards a specific patch of stones and moved them around to reveal his long sword and Water Tribe clothing he had hidden away. Looking back up he froze catching sight of two new people. A shorter woman with long black hair that fell just past her shoulders. She looked to be just a little taller than Suki as she waved to Sokka with an excited grin. Behind her was a man roughly the same size as Zuko but much less lanky. He was thicker, more muscle than anything but still lean build. His brown hair hung in his face as he too waved a bit less enthusiastically.

“See ya finally saw my people. That is my girl and best friend. They are coming with.” Chit Sang grinned as if he knew this was not up for debate. It was not, they did not have the time. Sokka did a quick head count and agreed that adding those two would not capsize the cooler so it should be fine.

“Sure, fine, whatever. Everyone get to the cooler.”

Zuko stepped forward towards Sokka with a concerned look. Sokka tried to ignore that knowing look and stood up, back to Zuko to show he really wasn’t up to debate this.

“Are you sure about this? You were the one who said you wanted to redeem yourself, redeem your honor. Rescuing your dad is your chance.” Zuko’s voice was a hush that Sokka was not expecting. He was being gentle and that put Sokka more on edge than anything as he jerked his sword onto his back.

“Your dad?” Suki had followed Zuko to be closer to Sokka, and copied his way of asking quietly. These two were grating on Sokka’s last nerve and he was just not in the mental space to keep himself together.

“If I had just cut my losses at the invasion then maybe we wouldn’t be in this mess at all. Maybe, it is just better to call it quits before you fail.” He twisted the swords belt to be sure that it was secure before glaring at the ground. The words had burned his throat and stung his eyes.

“No, it’s not.” Zuko’s stern tone was back and this made Sokka flinch. After the conversation in the cooler Sokka should have known using that ‘f’ word would cause Zuko to push back.

“Look Sokka, you’re going to fail a lot before things work out. Trust me.” Zuko was right behind him now, he could hear it.

What he didn’t expect was the almost shy brush of Zuko’s hand on his shoulder. As if he was unsure if his touch was wanted or accepted. Of course to Zuko the only truth in life was that failing was the first and only step towards possibly learning. Sokka had learned just how deeply the bender believed his life to be nothing but failing till something turned out alright. Even still, Sokka was not Zuko.

“That supposed to make me feel better?” Sokka croaked out, pulling his shoulder away from the ghost of a touch.

Zuko’s arm had fallen back to his side but he did not seem to have lost his new need to encourage in his most backwards sense of the world. He followed Sokka towards the cooler and tried to position himself in a way that he would guarantee he had Sokka’s attention. He always had it, even if Sokka was trying not to show this and looked towards Suki who was watching this whole thing like it was being spoken in a different language. Yeah, that was accurate for someones first experience dealing with a Zuko pep talk.

“Even though you’ll probably fail over and over and over -” Great, he was talking faster than his mind works. This was about to get hard to follow if Sokka had any background in it. It also was so not helping. Sokka felt like he was punching fire at him with every ‘over’ and it was just not what he needed.

“-get on with it hot cakes.” Jet’s snark rang clear through the open space as he leaned against the boat watching them. He had rolled his eyes in the most dramatic way possible and Sokka could feel the glare Zuko had sent his way.

“Actually, agreed. Seriously not helping buddy.” Sokka sighed out as he slowed to a stop. He knew that Zuko was not going to stop once he was in this weird mood and it was best to just hear him out.

Sokka had risked a glance over his shoulder and caught the moment Zuko’s glare he had locked on Jet turned towards himself and softened. Zuko looked towards Sokka like he knew exactly what he was thinking, what he was feeling, and that he would follow him no matter his choice. Sokka looked away, his stomach rolling with anxiety and something else. That look had been for him alone and it had been so open. The words that followed that look were so soft and rang with so much raw emotion and experience.

“You have to try every time. You can’t quit…”

Sokka felt that shy touch to his shoulder again once before it was there and solid. Zuko gripped his shoulder and squeezed in a way that was meant to be friendly but to Sokka it burned with his own personal regrets. Since the cooler, Zuko had tried to reach out or touch Sokka three times and Suki at least once when he had slipped and needed a bit of support. Sokka was starting to note that Zuko might be a lot more physical and tactile than they gave him credit. Maybe his weird thing with Toph wasn’t as one sided as Sokka had believed.

“Just because you’re afraid that you might fail. Trust me.” Zuko finished and Sokka sighed heavily.

He felt so heavy, like the weight of everything was pulling him deep into the earth itself. He looked to the moon and saw just how bright it glowed. Had it been that bright a moment ago? No, he did not think it had been but that could have been his focus being elsewhere. Still, a man of science was allowed one belief outside the limitations of possibility, right? Yue, please let this be right.

Sokka sighed again and turned to face the boy behind him. The hand on his shoulder had relaxed its hold but was still firmly in place. Zuko’s expression was so open. His eyes were pleading Sokka to trust in his words and his posture was ready to either pull away or lean closer depending on Sokka’s reaction. That Zuko would even slightly believe Sokka would lash out or try to potentially strike at him made his stomach twist painfully.

“I do.” Was all Sokka could say. It was a whisper even to his own ears.

Jet’s loud groan seemed to shatter any quiet comfort that had formed. Suki, for her part, ducked to the ground and whipped something in Jet’s direction causing the boy to roll out of the way. A dull ‘thwump’ as a rock hit the boiling lake broke the tension.

Chit Sang chuckled at watching Jet glare in Suki’s direction.

“Hey, if you two are done cuddling, can we get a move on? I’d like to be out of here if ya know what I mean.” He called out towards Sokka who felt his face heat up and was suddenly very thankful of the darkness. One more glance to Zuko and Suki told him that they would follow if he said to leave, and that was the final push he needed.

“No, I’m staying.” He turned to look towards the cooler with a final nod. If he left now Zuko and Suki were right. He would never forgive himself and would never be able to rest knowing he was here and possibly left his father behind.

“What!? Sokka, you can’t be serious!” Jet popped up and started heading up the slight slope of the beach towards them.

“I am. Zuko is right. I came for my dad and I’ll never forgive myself if he is brought here tomorrow and I ran away again. I need to know.”

“I get that, but Sokka, think of those out of here who need you.” Jet gestured to the boat as if this was the only solution.

“They can wait a day more. You guys should go though.” Sokka turned his back to Jet and looked to Suki.

He reached up to grab Zuko’s wrist and pulled the hand free from his shoulder. He sighed heavily before he turned to face Zuko who was looking at him with clear confusion. He noted that the look had morphed to add a bit of shock and his arm had flinched but not pulled away from Sokka. He did not realize that he had unconsciously started to rub his thumb slowly over the wrist caught in his grasp. He was far too focused on just how cold it felt. He knew that Zuko was not ‘frozen’ yet he felt as if he had just emerged from the icy waters of the poles again.

“You especially need to leave, Zuko. I can’t have word of you being here getting out.”

“I’m not leaving you Sokka.” Zuko’s voice had come out strained and Sokka swore their was a bit of color coming back to the firebenders face. “Besides, like you said it is just another day. I am sure word doesn’t travel that fast.”

Jet’s loud snort broke whatever had been happening as he closed the distance between them.

“Princess, you know nothing about how fast word can spread.”

Zuko’s loud growl snapped Sokka out of whatever world he had entered and alarm bells went off in his mind. Zuko had ripped his arm away from Sokka incredibly fast but he could still feel the warm tingling in his hand as the wrist he had held had begun to heat up with incredible speed.

“Don’t call me that! I didn’t like it then, I don’t now.” Zuko’s glare was locked onto Jet and his stance had shifted into one of offensive preparation.

Not good. Sokka did a quick mental calculation and realized that Zuko was running on no true sleep for well over 24 hours, minimal food, and had spent a good 10 hours fighting off dangerous temperatures. He was short tempered to begin with but this was an explosive mix Jet was just itching to ignite.

“Oh, that’s right, you purred to-” whatever Jet was about to retort was cut short by a shout from Zuko as he lunged forward, fire trailing his motion. Jet rolled out of the way of the fire and stood stunned.

“Zuko, no!” Sokka jumped into action, the sight of the fire had kick started his body.

He needed to end this now and by the look of every stunned face they had all believed Zuko to be ‘frozen.’ This little fire display might just be what kicks off a nonexistent plan C if not stopped. Sokka slammed into Zuko’s back and wrapped his arms around him to pin Zuko’s arms to his side as tightly as he could. Giving a harsh pull backwards he moved so that his leg was in position to flip Zuko to the ground if needed. He needed to disrupt Zuko’s footing if he had any hope of stopping the enraged bender from attacking Jet.

Zuko snarled a deep and pained sound, fire flickering out between his clenched teeth with more venom than Sokka could remember ever seeing. His eyes were blazing wild with fury and locked on Jet’s every move. Sokka was reminded instantly that this was the prince who had tracked the Avatar from one corner of the world to the other with unexplainable accuracy. That it was this man who haunted some of the Gaangs darkest nightmares and for a good reason. Once set, Zuko was a storm that did not buckle under the world pushing him down.

Sokka grunted when he felt the weight of the enraged bender slump backwards into his hold regardless of his desire to continue his attack. His shaky legs gave out under Sokka’s pull and forced Zuko to shift his stance just to stay upright. This did not stop sparks from crackling around his feet in a warning as he did so.

“Hey, buddy, can you hear me? I need you to put out the light show, yeah?” He twisted hard as he spoke so that Zuko was forced to drop to one knee in order to balance.

The benders eyes were still locked on Jet but the flickering light between his teeth had extinguished. The snarling expression was very much still in place. His fists were clenched at his sides but he made no motion to try and pry Sokka away or burn him.

“I thought he was in the cooler for hours! How does he have any fire left!?” Jet shuffled back as Zuko breathed out more smoke than sparks. So he was trying to control himself, that was a plus.

“That’s it. Relax. Buddy, just breathe with me. You’re doing great. Jet, shut up and get in the cooler boat!” Sokka sent a warning look towards Jet who met it with a glare of his own.

“What, no way! I am staying with you in this.” Jet took a step forward only to shuffle backwards a bit at the sparks and snarl that came from the currently raging firebender at his movement.

“Whoa, wait, what? Jet, I thought you wanted out over everything else in here?” Suki had shaken out of her shock and was now looking just as confused as Sokka felt.

“I did. I do. Sokka will get us out. What’s one more day, like you said. Besides, you needed me for this one. Who’s to say you wont need me for the next plan?” His posture shifted to that of pure arrogance and Sokka momentarily entertained the idea of letting go. He knew he had no time to deal with any of this.

“Fine, your choice.” He huffed out while he twisted his hold again so the taller boy he currently held tightly had to lean back into him for support. Sokka could feel Zuko’s back was rising and falling in temperature with every shaky breath as it was pressed flat against his chest.

“You need to breathe, Zuko. Buddy, I really need you to relax. Fire will get us all caught.”

Zuko took in a shaky breath and let his head lull backwards to be pressed against Sokka’s shoulder. Sokka could feel it in how Zuko’s body shook and how his face was twisting with the effort that he was doing his best to listen but was failing to control the fire burning hot inside of himself. Sokka shifted just enough to rest his forehead on Zuko’s temple which caused the bender to jerk but not twist away from him.

“Hey, that’s it, you’re doing great. Breathe.” He could feel the moment Zuko’s anger broke. The second the fire in him died down. He waited and watched Zuko’s face slowly relax.

“That’s it. Great job. I’m gonna pass you to Suki now, okay? Just work with her to calm down. You’ve had a bad couple hours. You’re overtired, strained, and you must be starving.”

Sokka lifted his head to look towards Suki who was already on her way to close the short distance. She snaked her arms around Zuko just under Sokka’s and gently tugged. Sokka released his hold and watched as Suki was able to whisper to Zuko and led him away to sit against the side of the building. Zuko, for his part, looked thoroughly exhausted as he blindly trusted Suki to lead them far away from the others. Sokka watched them for a moment longer before spinning to glare at the cause of this little situation.

“Jet, I need you to take whatever the fuck game you are playing and throw it in the lake. Zuko is a team member. Play. Nice. You know he is hot headed and whatever you guys had is really destabilizing for him, which means it is for everyone.”

Jet huffed out “not my fault he is sensitive. Can’t even take hearing some little pet names anymore, how was I to know he would react-”

“You knew damn well he would. So either cut the shit, or get in the fucking boat.” Sokka snarled. The idea that Jet was playing with Zuko was enough to make his blood boil just as much as the lake.

Jet raised an eyebrow before he grinned wide as if he had just figured out a great mystery. He raised his hands up to show some kind of defeat and shrugged.

“You’re right. Sorry. I won’t do it anymore. Scouts honor.”

Sokka did not trust this but nodded. He might not like it but he could only go off of Jet’s word. He chanced a look back to Suki and Zuko and was shocked to see that Zuko was much better. He was currently trying his best to avoid having something shoved into his mouth by Suki with the same energy that Katara used with those who refused to eat what was made because it was gross but was also all they had. Sokka could not help the chuckle at watching Zuko lose this and accept whatever it was and pop it into his mouth with a grimace.

Chit Sang cleared his throat and twisted the boat to get their attention.

“Anyone else needin’ a ride?” He had watched and waited for them, Sokka noted. This man was actually a really good guy. He was glad to be of assistance to his freedom.

“No, you go ahead though. Get out of here and don’t make a noise till you reach the other side. It should drift the full way.” Sokka waved towards the far shore.

“Fine with me. Let’s roll, baby.” With that he gave the cooler a shove into the lake and jumped into it with one swift motion. The cooler bobbed with the added weight but started to drift in the very direction Sokka had estimated it would. It would have worked for all of them after all.

“I hope you know what you are doing.” Jet moved to be by Sokka’s side and watched the cooler drift slowly away.

“So do I. I hope we haven’t just made the biggest mistake.” Sokka sighed, no turning back now.

Jet stretched and shrugged. “So what if we have? We can figure it out in time.”

“Not everyone here has that time.” Sokka glanced back at Zuko to see the bender tucked into Suki’s side with his head resting on her shoulder. From this distance he could not tell just how relaxed he actually was.

“Their fault for being here then,” he yawned. “I don’t know about you, but I am going back to my cell to not raise any suspicion.”

“Yeah, that is a good idea. You do that.” Sokka watched as Jet walked off back towards the prison before turning on his heels to head towards the pair resting in the distance against the outer wall. The closer he got the more he saw that Zuko’s breathing had evened out into the tell tale signs of sleep.

“Hey.” He whispered as quietly as possible while he approached.

“Hey, how are you doing?” Suki, in a not so quiet whisper, responded quickly. Her eyes were searching him as he came nearer.

Sokka looked to the sleeping Zuko with a worried glance. Suki chuckled and waved her free hand.

“Don’t worry. Like this his right ear is pretty tucked into my shoulder. He can’t really hear much out of the left so as long as you try to be quiet and I don’t move much we shouldn’t wake him.” She used her free hand to brush some of Zuko’s hair out of his face to prove her point. Zuko did not so much as twitch and continued to breathe in a way that suggested deep sleep.

Sokka nodded and moved to sit on her right, “I hope that was the right choice.”

“It was, even if your dad is not on that transfer. Knowing he could have been would have eaten you so badly.”

“I know. You’re right. Always are.”

“Not always, but thank you.” She chuckled and looked to the sky. It would start changing colors soon.

“What were you feeding him just now? I didn’t see you have any food in your cell.” Sokka glanced at the sleeping figure before turning his questioning stare onto her.

Suki shifted to pull out a dumpling weed pod from her pocket. Taking great care not to shift Zuko too much as she handed it to Sokka.

“This, they are a bit bitter because they are not ripe but are super good sources of nutrition.”

Sokka took the pod and looked it over. If he remembered right these things were great in a stew but tasted like dirt if raw.

“That is actually really good thinking. Thank you. Where did you get these?” A quick nibble told him he was correct, it tasted like dirt and he made a face to show just that. Suki chuckled and slapped his thigh lightly.

“There are these structure pockets behind the corner supports to this place. I grew them in the ones that the metal bends enough to let me into that space.”

“That’s actually super smart. Using the foundation support gaps-”

“Why are they even a thing? Feels like a waste of space.” Suki huffed before Sokka could really get going on praising just how smart he found that idea to be. Sokka chuckled, taking no offense in being cut off.

“They are what keeps this big of a building stable during any kind of quake or rumble. Needed because they built this in a literal volcano like a bunch of masochistic psychos.”

Suki chuckled and rolled her eyes. She glanced at Zuko as he sighed out in sleep but did nothing more.

“I knew you’d know the reason.”

Sokka flashed a smile towards her and they twisted to watch the cooler boat continue to drift. He was unsure how long they had sat but the moon had disappeared and the color of morning light had begun to dust the lower horizon.

“We should head back.” Suki’s voice broke the silence and Sokka jumped just slightly at being pulled back from his zoned out state. He nodded and moved to stretch.

“Sokka, watch it.” Her voice was a quiet warning that made Sokka freeze and look to her. What could he possibly need to watch for more than he already was? Suki met his gaze and tilted her head slightly in the direction of the sleeping bender still tucked tight to her left side.

“Zuko feels really cold. He was fine before he fell asleep but he has been trembling since.” Sokka looked closer and saw that she was right. It was hard to see, but there was a very present tremor to the sleeping figure that rippled through him in waves.

Sokka nodded in understanding. Cold to a firebender was not good. He needed to watch Zuko and they had to keep the fire shows to a minimum.

“Yeah, we should keep him in the sun as much as we can. They said a healthy firebender would be fine in that thing for a day but I don’t trust Zuko to know what ‘fine’ is supposed to look like after the battles hes had. He can still bend too and that had to have come with a price.”

Suki nodded before she moved to brush the hair out of Zuko’s face with a bit more contact than before. She whispered in his direction as she twisted from under him.

“Hey there Sunshine, wake up. That’s it.”

Zuko groaned before shifting to sit up and blinked a bit dazed. Looking around he flushed slightly and muttered out “sorry, didn’t mean to fall asleep like that.”

“Hey no worries, buddy. You needed it. Come, we should start heading in. They said at dawn.”

Zuko twisted to look at the sky confused and stared in shock at the colors dancing along the horizon. Sokka had known he was tired when he had not so much as twitched with the first light but did not think it warranted such shock. So he had slept in a bit, why did that make Zuko look like he was in trouble and Suki watch him as if she agreed. A bit of sleep was good, wasn’t it? Sokka was clearly missing something here but would have to ask later. With that he stood up to stretch and reached back to help Suki up. She flashed a grateful smile before she turned to help Sokka pull Zuko to his feet and help stabilize him. A glance at Suki told Sokka that his sun thought might be more correct than he wanted it to be.

“Let’s go see if your dad is here.” Zuko stated, mission clear in his voice.

Sokka had to shake his head lightly. This bender was so far into the mission he was ignoring the way he had swayed with just standing but they were on a time crunch. Sokka was going to make sure he found time to let Zuko rest even if the bender did not think he needed it.

With a final glance to the speck on the water that was the cooler Sokka turned to lead his team back inside. Time to see if it had all been worth it or if he had just doomed the people he cared for in worse ways than he could imagine.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

Thank you for reading! I hope you enjoyed this chunk as much as I did writing it! We finally have our boys talking to each other!

Any comments about this chapter are welcome! I really struggled getting it all in order. Let me know what you think &#60;3

## Chapter 12

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

It's so hot, I am melting.

This chapter fought me a lot on how I wanted characters to engage and I may just come back and change it if I ever redo this. As for now, as a first fic goes it is what it is and I want to get to a specific moment so bad.

That being said, enjoy! Comments are always welcome and make my day &#60;3

### Text

The walk back up the side of the prison was slow. The sun had begun to crest over the rocks of the edge of the volcano and reached out towards the metal building. As the sun crested the rocky ledge, the three moving along the wall felt the metal quickly begin to rise in temperature from cool to near body temperature. It would be painful to touch the walls in a few hours at this rate. The path along the wall that snaked its way up the building became easier to see, but the people on the outer wall also became more visible as the moments ticked by.

Sokka glanced over his shoulder to take stock in how they were doing. Suki, while she looked tired, was otherwise fine. Her steps and shuffle along the wall were solid and she met his look with a questioning one of her own. He nodded to her and turned back to keep moving.

They needed to reach the third floor if they were going to peek at the gondola arriving without being seen and this outer walking path was the only one that would get them there in time. This was a maintenance path for outer wall panels most likely only ever used in emergencies so the likelihood of being caught here was low, but not impossible. With this mix of people, Sokka new every bad thing was always possible.

Suki had pointed out that they did not have time to fake being in their cells so they had decided to risk the chance that the guards would not take count of the prisoners this morning with the distraction of new arrivals. The likelihood of this they all knew was low, but not impossible. If they needed to they could just use Jet’s little group as an excuse because they were never where one expected them to be. Sokka slowed their pace when he heard voices above them on the second floor. They only had one more outer ridge to travel and they would be behind the look out tower. He took the pause to look back at his group, this time he focused on Zuko.

Zuko, for his part, looked awful. He was extremely pale, had dark rings around his eyes so deep that the scar even looked off colored, and was breathing harder than this kind of climb would usually warrant from him. When he noticed they had stopped for the time being he leaned back against the hot metal and twisted so that as much of his skin was being kissed by the sun as he could manage. Sokka did not think he even realized he was doing that little movement but it was clear that his body was demanding connection with his element in the most basic of ways. Zuko closed his eyes to most likely listen to the voices but the action let him tilt his head back to rest it against the metal and face the sun. He looked like he was glowing. That the sun was physically recharging him as Sokka watched transfixed.

Suki waved a hand in Sokka’s face with an eye roll. She smirked as he jumped at the sudden appearance of her hand. She raised an eyebrow at him and Sokka flushed, huffed, and turned to keep moving forward.

“We are almost there, just a bit-”

His words were cut short by a sharp sound that echoed from the basin as alarm bells rung from every corner of the building. A quick look out to scan for the cooler boat was all he needed. He easily spotted it where he knew it would be, but the direction of the guards running was enough to tell him that he was not the only one to find it. The plan had failed. Chit Sang and his friends were as good as caught and there was nothing they could do about it. A wave of guilt for his failed idea and relief at seeing Zuko and Suki safely behind him made his already tight stomach feel nauseated.

“What’s happening?” Suki twisted to look out, she had to squint against the sunlight reflecting off of the boiling lake.

“Plan Cooler Boat just failed. Come on, we have to get to the third floor before they do a sweep of people!” Sokka turned away from whatever the fates of those in his boat was to become and shuffled even faster up the path. He motioned for the others to do the same. This would be the only time they got to shuffle up noisily and risk little, he was not about to waste it.

Hopping the railing on the third floor, Sokka quickly helped pull Suki and Zuko up and onto the platform. With Suki he had squeezed her arm just to remind himself that she was real and here. With Zuko he wanted to say he played it just as cool. That he was not giving away his newly discovered problems. That he was keeping himself to the boundary of a good friend. That is to say, he wrapped his arm around the firebender’s back to avoid hurting his already touchy shoulder and helped pull him over the ledge. Only when Zuko was able to show he could stand on his own, and not lean his weight into Sokka with his hand gripping the other boys shoulder for support, did Sokka entertain the idea of letting go. He did not, in fact, let go.

Zuko twisted in the side hug to give Sokka a confused look and was clearly about to say something when his words cut short. Sokka could not say why, but he needed this. He needed to hold Zuko. He needed to hold the one thing he had hurt and almost lost so many times on this foolish mission. The person who had given everything from his own possessions to his own health just on a slim chance that one of Sokka’s ideas would work out.

Sokka twisted his side hold on Zuko and pulled the taller man into a tight hug. Being that he was about a head shorter, his face was buried into Zuko’s shoulder and he felt the second that Zuko’s heartbeat picked up. He doubted the bender was used to this kind of touch but he would have to get used to it. Sokka hugged his people, and especially those he felt like he had wronged. He was probably going to have to hug Katara and Aang after all of this, but maybe not Toph for self preservation reasons.

He took a deep breath, meant to calm himself down at feeling the slightly warmer than himself body in his arms and caught the dull sent of frost having been on clothing for too long before melting. Under that was a smell of musty old cloth, basalt, and a hint of some spice he could not place. His mind all but stopped when he felt tentative arms wrap around his own back. The motion was almost nothing, but Sokka could feel the warmth and remembered when those arms were around him just a few hours ago in a need to keep warm. He knew how warm they could be and determined that Zuko would probably give amazingly comforting hugs if he practiced.

“This is sweet and all boys, but I see the gondola coming and the guards will likely come this way. Besides, Sokka, Sunshine looks like he is either going to have a panic attack, cry, or pass out so you might want to let him go.” The smirk in her voice was crystal clear.

Sokka pulled away from Zuko, heat that had nothing to do with the day cooking his face as he glared at her. He was right, she had the biggest grin and gave him a look with a knowing glint in her eyes that he very much ignored. He cleared his throat and did not look back at a most definitely confused Zuko. Sokka tucked back to the wall and led them to a corner behind one of the observation towers that was perfect for watching the gondola without being seen. All they would have to do was to be quiet, they could do that, right?

The gondola pulled into the port on the edge of the platform just as the guards finished lining up. The warden was in the center and was giving orders but they were too far to make out anything he had said. The guards did some complicated line shuffling and position moving on the platform that Sokka was unable to follow or understand. Why did they need to change how they were standing in a line before the gondola opened anyway?

“Bender rotation. Those in the center and behind the warden are going to be strong offensively but slow. Those on the end are most likely fire whip specialized and need the extra space in case someone runs.” Zuko whispered to the group. Sokka wondered if he was answering his unspoken question or just providing information he thought they may be lacking. It was interesting, but Sokka could not bring himself to care.

The three hidden in the shadow of the watch tower all held their breath as the doors to the gondola slid open and the first massive figure stepped out. It was a man in the prisoner uniform that towered over all the guards, tattoos crisscrossed up his arms and piercings littered every possible part of his face. Sokka sighed, having hoped the first one would have been his dad and forced himself to not feel despair just yet.

“Is that him?”

The question was whispered but so genuine that it had caught Sokka off guard. He twisted to give Zuko a look that he hoped clearly showed how not amused he was.

“My dad doesn’t have a nose ring.”

“How was I to know that? You sighed, I had thought-”

“Boys, shush. There are more people. Let’s just keep watching.” Suki nudged Sokka to turn around to watch as a woman followed the beast of a man.

Sokka watched as prisoner after prisoner walked off the gondola. There were Earth Kingdom and Fire Nation prisoners in an even mix but not a single Water Tribe person had stepped off of the cursed thing. With every new person, Sokka felt his heart soar with hope and plummet further and further into the boiling lake. It stayed there as the line up came to a stop and their was no sign of his father.

“They have stopped, that can’t be everyone.” He needed it not to be everyone.

“I’m sorry, Sokka.” Suki’s hand followed her words to rest on his shoulder. He twisted away from the touch. It stung for reasons he could not understand.

That was it. It was done. He had brought Zuko to where the Fire Nation would likely take him back to his family. Handed him back on a golden platter with a note that said “from the Avatar” in his opinion. He had gone on a fools mission. The only good thing to come from said mission was Suki and she was just as doomed to rot here as the rest of them. He was almost more cruel to her than any others. He had given her hope, and he had failed.

It was his mission that had gotten his tribesmen caught in this war after so long, and he had just gone and done a 2.0 of that very failure with his own friends. Katara would never know where he went, Aang was out a firebending teacher with the comet getting closer every day, and Sokka was just riding a failure streak that only resulted in others taking his fall.

That was the main problem, after all. To Sokka, it was not that he was failing. In the Southern Water Tribe he had failed many plans. He had gone on planned hunts, set traps, and even planned buildings for heat retention. Many of those had failed. The difference was that those failings hurt no one but himself.

A failed hunt may mean less food for people but he was the one who lost out on that day’s energy and could just try again. Setting traps was a solo act and if one went off in the process then the only one injured was himself. If a snow house fell on him while he tried a new way to build, well he could just go to another house for awhile and warm up. No one suffered, no one hurt, no real stakes unless supplies ran low.

Even in the early days of adventuring he had learned that some things just failed. That was fine, one needed only to just move to another thing and push onward. Suki had taught him to take a moment to learn from defeat and that was a gift. Using that Sokka had learned to fail when stakes were low and often times they were. When they were not, usually his quick thinking was enough to get the group where they needed to be. That was the reason he was around, after all, wasn’t it?

He had no magic. No funky powers. No connection to things outside the realm of what should be possible. He was just a man, in a world of those who were so much more. His own little sister was one of the most gifted waterbenders in all the poles and he was just Sokka. Sokka, the boomerang man. Sokka, the newly made sword guy. Sokka, the plan man. But he really wasn’t anything.

Sokka’s plans when stakes were low went well enough because they could be adapted in real time. Ever since that blasted library, every plan Sokka had voiced had ended up with someone else being burned. It was like a curse, Appa had been taken for months because he stayed and angered the blasted talking bird and Aang had felt that pain so deeply. Toph had felt it too, had had to make a choice to save them over Appa. Ever since then it had felt like every time he spoke others hurt.

His father was a deep wound to his heart. He had made that plan. Had wanted with his entire being to see it work. Had ignored the very obvious answers that showed it would not. Firebenders collectively lose their bending on those types of days being that they are connected to the sun, so why did he even believe the Fire Lord of all people would not have a multitude of escapes or fail safes? It was because he had blinded himself that his father and others were taken to spirits know where, and he had come here with the hope that he could fix his wrongdoing.

Instead, he had made it so much worse. He had dragged Zuko into a prison that he had known to be number one for the Fire Nation. Had impulsively met with Suki at the price of Zuko being caught. Since then Zuko has been in a world of hurt while Sokka flits around the prison mostly free. Sokka has been getting at least semi decent food, learning information that has not helped much, and if he wanted he would most likely have a nice place to sleep.

Suki was here on her own actions so her situation he felt less guilty for. Zuko, however, has been here a full day now and spent half of that in a forced tundra. He has been a punching bag for Jet, Sokka was going to get his revenge for that one when all this was finished. Sokka feared one big thing. Zuko had not said it to be true, but with how easily Sokka could slip into his cell on the grounds of wanting to put him in his place. It was hard to think others here would not have that very idea and have the authority to act on it more.

His spiral of negative thoughts was cut short as the voice of a guard echoed clearly through the quiet of the morning. “Hey, you! Get off the gondola!”

Sokka whipped around to see that one person had yet to disembark. One person was hiding in the gondola. He had one sliver of a chance left and he had to fight to keep his hopes from soaring. Sokka’s entire world exploded into color too bright for him to see anything but the figure that had walked off the gondola. The man was darker skinned than those around him, held his head up high, and glared at the guards with eyes as blue was the frigid tundra waters of their homeland. The two blue beads braided into the familiar wolf cut hung by his face and glinted in the morning sun. There was no mistaking this person.

“Dad.” Sokka wheezed out as he kept his eyes locked on the man and blinked rapidly. His father was here. He was not hurt and he was here. Sokka was right, and he had stayed for the right reason. He could do this, maybe.

Suki beamed at seeing the man and grabbed Sokka’s elbow. Sokka twisted to smile back towards her, feeling as light as Appa must when he flies. They shared a silent conversation of pure joy and relief before Sokka caught Zuko’s eye.

Zuko was not looking at Sokka. He was watching the line of prisoners and his stare was locked on Sokka’s father with such intensity that he thought he was trying to set fire to him from a distance. His jaw was set tight and his pale face looked to be even more colorless than before. Before Sokka could make any comments the commotion from the platform stole his attention back.

His father had lined up, but he was refusing to follow some kind of order from the warden. He was looking anywhere but the man and Sokka felt like he deserved it. He too would have refused to listen to him. Sokka gasped and felt two hands of different sizes grab his arms as they watched the warden kick out to force his father to the ground. He saw red. He wanted to pull out his sword and charge the warden. How dare he put his father on his knees!? The hands tightened their hold and pulled him back into the shade. He had not even realized he had taken a step toward the stairs leading to the platform.

The warden twisted to leave and Sokka recognized what was about to happen only seconds before it did. It was an action Sokka himself had fallen victim to with Bato a few times when he was younger. They watched as his father twisted the cuffs to catch the wardens foot and send the man flailing to the ground. The guards were in a confused hurry to help if needed and the moment ended with the prisoners being led away earlier than they most likely were planned to move.

Sokka chuckled and had to work hard not to full out laugh. Suki was gripping his arm and giggling along at the display.

“Man, Sokka, your dad is great.”

“Yeah, he is.” Sokka sighed out and grinned back at them. He froze and took in Zuko’s posture. He had not changed his stern look locked on the line of prisoners.

“Zuko, what’s wrong?” Sokka asked.

Zuko jumped slightly and looked towards them. “What? Nothing is wrong.”

“That’s… wow you really are shit at lying.” Sokka rolled his eyes and set a stern look on his friend. Something was bothering him and it had something to do with his dad.

“Wha- Sokka, I’m not lying! Nothing is wrong.”

“Then why do you look like you just saw a spirit and it cussed you out?”

“That doesn’t make any sense.” Zuko gave Sokka a puzzled look.

“I’m just saying. Ever since my dad got here, which was the whole reason we came in the first place if you remember, you have been glaring a hole through the metal. So what is it?”

“Nothing! Why does it have to be something? It’s great that your dad is here. Well, not great to be here but…” Zuko trailed off and began looking anywhere that had no person in the space. He shifted to look at the sky, squinting at the brightness which Sokka could not understand why he had held that look for so long if it was obviously uncomfortable. He settled for glaring at the wall. “Maybe my face is just that way-”

“Sunshine, you do have a bit of a resting scowl but not like that. Did you know the warden would do that?” Suki asked in an admittedly more gentle of a tone than Sokka was using. Zuko relaxed slightly and turned to give her his attention and not Sokka.

“No, but I had a hunch. One never disrespects those above them. Trust me, the consequence of such thing could have been far worse.” He stated this in the same way one commented on the weather. That way of waving off a comment reminded Sokka of Jet which only made him feel more irritated at the conversation.

“So it wasn’t the warden. Then what was it?” Sokka cut in. He had just gone through an emotional ride equal to a first flight on Appa. He was going to get to the bottom of this before he mixed his now even bigger group. A good leader needed to be prepared for any problems, after all.

“Can’t it just be nothing!?” Zuko fumed. Whatever patience he had gained from his little nap was quickly evaporating.

So much for figuring things out, Sokka was about to match his energy and he knew it. He could feel the fog blocking his more rational thoughts. He was relieved at seeing his dad and knowing his choices were right, upset that the cooler boat plan failed at the price of others, and agitated by everything under the whatever fucking spirit ruled the sun. Sokka knew the signs of when one should drop a topic or back off when it came to Zuko’s temper and decided to ignore it all. Tui and La he would regret this but this was supposed to be a happy moment and Zuko’s usual randomly pissy self was ruining it.

“What do you have against my dad then? Do… do you know my dad?” He locked a glare on Zuko even if the others look would not meet his eyes.

“What, no. Why would I?” He looked confused and caught off guard by the question. It might have felt like a sudden jump in conclusions but Sokka could not think of any other reason for that stern glare and attitude of his to have come out so quickly.

“I don’t know… you have been on edge ever since you saw him. How would I know if you two had met on the seas before-”

“Just because I was sailing for four years doesn’t mean I know every other person who was sailing. Besides, I was on an old Fire Nation ship. I’m sure if we crossed paths he would have attacked us with good reasons.” The tone was sharp and cutting. Something in the last few minutes had upset him and Sokka was scrambling mentally to piece it together.

“What do you mean four years? We have only known Aang to be around for the last year at most.” Suki asked. Zuko shifted uneasily at his potential slip being questioned. He crossed his arms in front of his chest tightly as if the guard himself and turned his fiery glare back to the wall.

“Forget about it. Point is I don’t know him.”

“I’m not going to ‘forget about’ anything, Sunshine. Why were you sailing for four years?”

“Because my banishment started four years ago, happy!?” He was struggling to keep quiet, Sokka could see the fight in him building. He was building his defenses, yet he was still answering her instead of shutting her down and storming off. That was new.

“Banishment? Zuko, I’m sorry. I don’t think I’m following. I thought you were recently banished.” Her voice was quiet, she was trying to be as little a threat as possible but Zuko was exhausted and that did not help his already short temper.

Zuko slumped back against the tower and sighed deeply. “I was. That was my second time. My first was four years ago with the amendment that if I brought the Avatar to the Fire Lord then I would be welcomed back with all titles returned. I sailed for years hunting a myth with all my hope sunk into it. I met many ships, most of which turned hostile if they were not Fire Nation. Some were Fire Nation and still turned hostile, but those were easier to avoid. So, if your father knows me or if we had met, it was not on good terms.”

“My dad wouldn’t attack you!” Sokka had jumped to this conclusion but it was the only thing keeping him from lingering on the idea of four years of fear of attack from everything around you.

“He would have if we’d met at sea, and with good reasons. What is to say he wont now? He might still just on the grounds of my past against you guys and I would not blame him.”

“He wont if I tell him not to.” Sokka said. He knew his father. If he said Zuko was a friend then his father would believe him. He might not fully trust it but he would never attack Sokka’s friends.

“Well isn’t that nice, what it must be like to be so trusting-” Zuko’s muttering was interrupted and he glared at the floor.

“Boys please. We don’t have time. Sokka, go talk to your dad and figure things out. Zuko, you need to relax. I get that you are running on low everything and we just pushed on a touchy subject but please just hold it together a bit longer. You and I will join the other prisoners. We can solve whatever this is after we actually talk to the man.”

Zuko huffed but nodded. He pushed off of the guard tower and turned to leave towards the hall for the prisoner cells, glare still locked on the floor. Sokka felt a flare of panic at seeing him disappear around the corner but it did not last long. Suki rolled her eyes and nudged Sokka in the ribs with her elbow.

“Really, Sokka. You and I both know that he is the last person to trust new people easily. If you say to do something he will, but you can’t make him love your dad just because you do. He will relax in time.”

“I know. I do! Just, I thought he was here to help get my dad out and now that he is here and our first goal is back on track he is being weird.”

“No, he was never here for your dad Sokka. He was here for you. He trusts you, let that be enough for now. You can introduce them later. I, for one, would like to be out of here by sundown if possible.” Suki moved to stand in front of him to make sure he understood.

Sokka wanted to push back against the idea that Zuko would do anything for him but came up short when he thought about everything that had happened. Zuko would probably jump if Sokka said to. That did not mean he had to be afraid of Sokka’s dad just because he was actually here now. That did not make sense to him.

“Right, I’ll go see if my dad has any ideas. To be completely honest, I am all out of them.” Sokka sighed. It felt good to be honest.

Suki gave his shoulder a squeeze before she turned to follow Zuko down the hall to the prisoners cells. An idea hit him and he quickly jogged to catch her.

“Suki wait!” She turned and gave him a questioning look.

“Do you have any more of those gross pods? I need to check on Zuko and he should probably eat something.”

Her shoulders relaxed and she flashed him a wonderfully warm smile. She moved around a bit and pulled a handful of pods out of places he was unsure were physically possible but chose not to question it.

“Thanks Suki, you are a life saver.”

“I know, now play nice and figure out a way out of here please.”

“You got it!” He pocketed the pods and pivoted to head to the basement where Zuko’s cell was. If he did this right he could get everyone back on the right track and get out of here in one piece.

He ignored some guards calling towards him in a way he hoped showed he was busy. He felt semi responsible for the emotional spike the bender had displayed and had a feeling food might help him regulate a bit without direct contact with sun. He knew others did not so much as look towards Zuko but he had studied the firebender for what he had believed to be safety reasons. Now he was unsure how true that was, but it did nothing to change the fact that Sokka knew Zuko was on edge and he had just attempted to shove him off.

Stopping just outside Zuko’s cell he gave a quick look around and knocked. He stood on his toes to peek into the window slit of the room but it was too dark to make out anything. His heart skipped a few beats and he almost let himself panic at the thought that Zuko had not made it to the cell after all when he caught movement within the shadows.

“Zuko, hey buddy. It’s me.” He kept his voice low to not draw attention.

A moment went by before gold eyes became visible in the window slit. They looked dead tired without the full image of the man to distract. “What is it?”

“Nothing, I just wanted… look, I’m sorry for going after you after seeing my dad. It wasn’t right. You didn’t deserve that-”

“Sokka, I get it. Don’t worry about it. Not important-”

“It is important to me. You are important to me and I have been treating you like a tool and not listening or choosing to ignore what is happening because it is easier than taking blame.” Sokka sighed, this was not how this was supposed to go.

“Sokka, I’m not… I get that this place sucks and I need to do these things, you need me to. You shouldn’t be here.”

“What? What do you mean I shouldn’t be here? I know if I listened to you before and not have come-”

“I meant, you should be with your dad. He is why you are here. Why are you talking to me when he was the reason we are here in the first place?”

“Because, I wanted to apologize you self sacrificing ass.” Sokka chuckled and relaxed hearing an answering huff. So he had not ruined their weird whatever they were at least.

“Look, you need to eat something. Suki gave me more of the gross dirt pods and I need you to finish them.” Sokka laughed hearing the half hearted groan and worked on shoving the handful into the peep window at the bender. Said bender stammered out and had to work fast to catch most of the pods while the others hit the ground.

“What was that for?” He huffed and a light glowed from his room as he most likely made a small fire to see where the others fell.

“You have a job, actually.”

“Yeah?” The light extinguished and the gold eyes were back. They were locked on Sokka with a need to be of use and Sokka felt a bit bad for what he was about to do, but not that bad.

“It is really important.”

“What?”

“I actually don’t know if I can trust you to do it now that I think on it.” Sokka mimed thinking hard and the slight hurt that flashed through the gold eyes stung his heart. He felt bad but he needed to get Zuko to agree with it fully.

“What do you mean- I-I have done all you asked. Of course I will help with whatever I can!” He was worked up, now or never.

“I need you to promise me you will do this to the best of your abilities.” Sokka locked his serious glare with those mini suns trapped in a dark room. They burned with determination.

“Of course you can.”

Sokka nodded, “alright. I need you to eat all the pods and then sleep until I come back. I will bang on your door like I did now. I need you to rest. Actually try and sleep a little. Can you do that?”

“… You are a jerk.”

Sokka laughed, “yeah, but can you?”

“Yes, and I said I will so I will try.”

“I know you will. Thank you, Zuko. I’ll come back with a plan, promise.”

“You better.”

Sokka smiled at the door before he turned and headed down towards the new prisoner wing. That had gone different than he had thought it would but the end result was much better. Zuko was going to try and rest and that would help cut back on some of Sokka’s worry list. He had said he would find time to force the bender to rest, and he did. His plans were starting to work. Hopefully this was a curse broken kind of sign!

He had to work hard not to run up the stairs towards the new prisoners cells. His heart was beating so hard that he could barely hear anything as he fixed his helmet back into place. He needed to be sure that attention would not fall on himself as he slipped into the hallway. Not only was he not where the guards were expected, Chit Sang and that group was bound to be back in the prison somewhere and he could not risk seeing them.

Checking the hall, he was met with the sounds of the new prisoners screaming and worse. Loud bangs and echoing cries of distress filled his ears as he made his way past. He hated to admit it, but those made his hunt for his father much easier. The people screaming and fighting their fate were clear markers that he had yet to reach the right cell. Standing on his toes to peek into the few silent cells he located his father in the middle of the group. He admittedly did not think his entrance through.

Popping the door open with a loud creak, he slipped inside and quickly clicked the door closed behind himself as quietly as he could. He stood on his toes again to glance out and check before twisting to see his father watching his every move with eyes that spoke of icy warning. He took no warning and grinned wide through the helmet.

“Thank goodness you are okay!” Sokka took a step towards the man still sitting only to jump back confused as his father jumped up and into a fighting stance. His fists raised in a way Sokka had forgotten about after all his time training and fighting in the Earth Kingdom. Was he really so disconnected with his Water Tribe stances? Did he ever truly learn them?

“Come any closer ashmaker, and you will find out just how ‘okay’ I am.” The tone was harsh and threatening. Sokka took a step towards the door stunned. His father sounded like Jet. He had never known him to use that term for firebenders before. Why would he use it at his own son- the uniform, spirits Sokka cussed himself out for being so stupid twice.

“Dad! It’s me!” He ripped off the helmet and held his hands up to show he was not a threat. His eyes were stinging slightly at seeing his own father and his gut had twisted at the reaction he had caused.

Instantly the situation changed. Hakoda dropped his fighting stance to stand and stare at the figure in front of himself as if he were already seeing things in isolation. His blue eyes softened and tears fell freely.

“Sokka! My son.” He reached out a strong hand and pulled Sokka into a tight hug that felt like home. Tears fell from Sokka just as freely. He had his dad in his arms and that made the pain of the last few hours worth it. “You should be more careful, my son. I about took you out in that little outfit of yours.”

Sokka couldn’t help the snort and chuckle that escaped him. He had a flash to when Suki had knocked him onto his ass in seconds for believing him to be a guard. To be fair, that one ended in a kiss so he was not too upset about it. This had ended in a manly, tearful hug so it seemed to follow the pattern nicely. He vaguely wondered if he surprised Zuko if he would also get something like a- Nope. Stopping that. Dad. Sokka reminded himself that he was currently crying in his fathers arms and not in a space to entertain those thoughts.

“Yeah, funnily enough I get that a lot actually.” Sokka said through his chuckles. He slowly let his father go just to look up at the man and take in him being here. He looked healthy and without any lasting marks or injuries. This was a relief in and of itself.

“So, where are the others? Bato and everyone who joined in the invasion?”

“They are being held at a prison near the Fire Nation Palace, unfortunately. They singled me out as their leader and sent me here.” His father sighed and nudged Sokka to give him space. Before Sokka could pout too much about having to let him go he moved to sit on the floor and patted the space next to himself. Sokka slowly moved to join him and leaned into his fathers side. A strong arm wrapped around his shoulders to pull him into his fathers side more and he could not help but feel so young and small. Like he had just failed a hunt and needed to cry to his dad for support.

“But before I left them all in that other prison I met a group of people who said they knew you.” Hakoda glanced at his son tucked into his side and smiled. “They were Earth Kingdom. The… Oshinama Fighters?”

“Kyoshi Warriors?” Sokka had scrunched his nose at the horrible pronunciation and hoped his dad would never say that near Suki.

“That’s right, that was the name!” Hakoda chuckled.

“Their leader Suki is here, actually. She is a good friend of mine and she is going to escape with us. I think you will like her, dad. She is really something.” Sokka grinned thinking about Suki and his dad getting along. “We also have another Earth Kingdom man in our group named Jet. You will have to meet him on the way but trust me when I say take his words with a grain of salt.” The grin fell from his face and settled into a serious gaze into the empty room.

“Sounds like you have quite the mix of people.” Hakoda remarked with a nod.

“Yeah, we really do. Hey, dad. Do you know Prince Zuko?” Sokka asked. He was unable to stop himself for questioning this point. He needed to know.

“The son of the Fire Lord? I don’t know him personally but I know of him. He is the one that was banished for speaking out against something and sent from the Fire Nation? Wasn’t he also the one you all were being chased by if the rumors we heard were true? Why do you ask?”

That was a lot of information to work through and Sokka was going to have to do it one piece at a time. His father seemed to know a great deal more about Zuko than he did and he would have to be ready if it came out in some way around the bender.

“Dad, what do you know of his banishment?”

“Which one?” Hakoda gave him a puzzled look but Sokka had set his gaze onto the far wall to work through his storm of thoughts.

“The first one.”

“Well, let’s see. Sailing has a surprising perk of hearing a lot of rumors. Every port has different rumors or the same in different ways you see. I think, between all the different versions of the story, we put together that the prince had indeed been banished. Beyond that was not really needed information so not much stuck.”

“Please, I need to know what you do.”

“Alright, it’s not pretty. From what we heard the prince of the Fire Nation had joined in some high ranked war meeting. Why they let a kid in there I can’t even guess but they did and he did something or said something or was just a kid in a place that they do not belong in and it angered really powerful people.”

Sokka swallowed hard and nodded. He would apologize to Zuko later, but this was already filling in a lot of holes about things that had been said or remarked on.

“Sokka, the rest is really more than I’d like to say. The end result was a very recognizable mark and a banishment. I guess he had some deal that returning with the Avatar would grant him his claim to the throne again and that is part of why he is such a thorn in your side.”

Sokka sighed heavily and nodded. He would hear Zuko’s side whenever the bender was ready but for now that was enough. It explained why Zuko was so invested in the war and operations of those who held more power than himself. Why he would have to avoid his own people. Why he was always on edge even with Fire Nation, and sometimes more so with Fire Nation than any other.

“Why did you ask, Sokka?” Hakoda tightened his arm around Sokka to try and draw his sons attention away from the wall.

“Just, trying to understand him a bit better is all. Don’t worry about it. Dad, if I asked you to not attack someone even if they were a firebender would you listen?” Sokka turned his questioning gaze onto his father. He needed to know. Needed to believe him.

“Of course. Just because one is a bender does not make them evil. If you know a firebender then I will treat them with the respect they treat me with.”

Sokka sighed in relief and sagged into his fathers side. “Thank you.”

“Of course.”

“It’s Zuko.” Sokka blurted.

“Who’s Zuko? The prince?”

“Yeah, he’s here too.”

Hakoda tensed and his arm gripped more protectively over Sokka’s shoulder. Sokka moved to sit up and pulled out of the hold to turn and face his father. His face was neutral but he knew he was about to argue with his father on the behalf of Zuko. He would do so until his father understood, even if it was to the same extend he did before this whole adventure had started. He knew in his gut that he could not in good conscious mix his father and Zuko if he had any feeling that his father would treat him as Katara does. He could not say why, but he knew it would be devastating to something.

“Sounds like a major problem for us then, Sokka.” Hakoda crossed his arms and nodded to himself in thought. Whatever that thought was, Sokka needed to end it now. He would protect Zuko.

“What? No, not a problem at all! Actually he’s on our side now.” He raised his voice a little just to make sure he had his fathers attention.

Hakoda sent a very puzzled look Sokka’s way and was still working out something that made Sokka’s insides twist with nerves. He needed to make his father understand. He needed his fathers help to leave but he would not be willing to move if it meant hurting Zuko anymore. He was done hurting others for his plans.

“I know, I know.” Sokka threw his hands up and closed his eyes to show he did understand. “I had the same reaction. After all the shit he had done, it was really hard to trust him. But trust me when I say this dad, he’s really proven himself! I never would have found you without his help and many sacrifices.”

He hoped his father would forgive the slip of language as he locked eyes with him. He almost felt like begging him to understand but knew that would get nowhere. The knot in his nervous system was tightening with every second as they stared at each other.

Hakoda broke first with a sigh and slight nod. “Alright, I trust you Sokka. So… Do you have a plan?”

Sokka sighed out and slouched as the knots throughout his body release simultaneously and redoubled with the fact that he did not actually have any ideas.

“We had one, but some of the other prisoners got involved and ruined it. I Don’t know if there is another way off this island.” Sokka slid back to the wall and settled once again in his fathers comforting hold.

“Sokka, there isn’t an island in the world that can hold two water tribe geniuses!” Hakoda squeezed his arm and smiled wide.

“Then we better hurry up and find two.” Sokka mumbled. This joking comment had helped undo some of the worry but still left him feeling so weighted down with the fact that everyone was looking to him for this. “I just wish I would stop failing everyone.”

“You know, Sokka. Most of my plans actually failed well before I had any real successes.” Hakoda chuckled when he caught the disbelieving look sent his way.

“It is true! Why, Bato had to bail me out of some bad or humiliating fails or rescue me on the really bad ones. One of which involved a turtle seal den and a smashed boat.”

“What is with people following turtle seals?” Sokka huffed out.

“Hmm?”

“Nothing, go on. Failed plans and why I need not be upset at literally locking my friend in prison and sitting as a fake guard while they all suffer…” Sokka hugged his knees.

“What I am saying is that just because a plan as a whole failed, it does not mean that parts of it did not go as planned. Take that and build on it. Did you get something out of the last one?”

Sokka flushed and hid his face into his knees like he had seen Zuko do when Toph would not leave him alone. He thought about what he had lost: freedom, weight due to constant sweating, Suki and that one still hurt. Had he gained anything? He knew he had. He had gained Zuko in ways he never thought possible.

He knew Zuko well, and he knew that he considered the bender a dear friend. That did not feel right though, not anymore. The feeling of the cold bender holding him tight to protect him from the dangers of the freezing temperatures of the cooler flickered to the front of his mind. How it had felt to be protected and yet also protecting at the same time was exhilarating. How a simple hug had lit a spark in his body that demanded attention. Demanded to be close to the bender. Demanded Sokka listen for once and hold Zuko close to protect him and stop letting him run off on half baked impulses that got him into trouble. Sokka was so screwed. He knew he fell hard and fast. He had not expected his plan for escape would end with him falling for the moody Prince of the Fire Nation of all things.

Sokka realized too late that he had spent too long thinking about Zuko in some weird, face flushed, kind of way when his father chuckled softly. “You really are my son. What, she knock you down and now you are head over heals?”

The light heat on his face burned from his cheeks and down his neck. He was busted and did not know how or if he should set his dad straight on just who was actually holding his heart now. It had been Suki before, maybe it would be again if he let this little crush die as they all settled. Some said adrenaline made you fall in love fast but once it was over you realized it was not actually true. That could be what was happening so best to wait it out.

“Something like that…” Sokka muttered and chuckled along.

The good mood building died instantly as a long bang rang through the room. Someone was knocking on the door to his fathers cell. Sokka had once again been in a prisoners cell too long and was about to lose everything. With a worried glance to his father he looked around desperately. He felt something nudge into his side and looked to see his father handing him the helmet he had dropped. Sokka gave a puzzled look at his father who whispered in a gruff tone he had not heard for years. It had been a long time since Sokka had heard another male use Water Script but his words were clear and commanding.

Sokka nodded and slipped the helmet on. He would follow his fathers order and take up his position as the fake guard again. If he was questioned for being in here he would claim personal reasons to hate Water Tribe and hope that was enough. He stood and with his resolve in place marched towards the door to peek out.

## Chapter 13

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

So, turns out I am bad at cliff hangers because I get just as excited for the next part. Sports day is happening so I will be busy for the next week or so. Thought I'd just give an update now.

Posting this without really going through it. Turns out I can't write a short chapter... thought I would try for a quick and small update and it just suddenly got super long.

### Text

Sokka took a deep breath and stood on his toes to peek out into the hall. Curse his shorter stature, he was almost 16 and he had yet to really grow. He blamed that on the fluctuating meals and constant stress of fighting a war before he was even able to go true ice dodging. He scanned where he could see and came up with nothing. No one was standing in the doorway space. No guard, no anything. Just an empty hall. That was almost worse and Sokka’s heart sped up in anticipation.

A hand came into focus for a brief moment before the loud bang on the door sounded again. Whoever was knocking was standing just off to the side and away from the direct line of sight. That was odd. No one did that unless they were sneaking around. Sokka had done that to Zuko’s door just a few minutes ago. Whoever this was, they were either mocking Sokka or they were not where they should be- shit. Sokka knew just who was on the other side of the door and with a grunt of frustration he ripped it open.

Jet. He was leaning on the wall with one arm crossed over his chest and the other still half raised in the knocking position. He had a new piece of wheat in his teeth and smirked when the door opened. His eyes looked Sokka over and then glanced to the other figure in the room.

“I see we didn’t make a mistake after all. Good, I’m really happy this worked out for you both.” Jet said and smiled at them as he pushed off the wall and moved to enter the room. Sokka did not move from the doorway.

“Jet, how did you know we were here?”

“New prisoner wing. I’ve been here for a bit. I like to sweep through the new people if I can and try and see if anyone who comes through are people I know.” He shrugged with a single shoulder.

“Right. Okay, uhm, change of plans. Dad, this is Jet. Jet, this is my father.” Sokka twisted to give Jet room to see his father but not enough to enter the room. He did not know why he was being so protective of this space but he just did not like the idea of this guy around his family any more than he had to be.

“Nice to meet you, sir. I’ve heard a lot about you and the Southern Water Tribe warriors actually. Huge fan.” He flashed a charming smile towards Hakoda before turning a questioning look onto Sokka. “Uhh, Sokka. Mind moving to let me in? Kinda not a good look having the door wide open on a new prisoner.”

He pushed lightly to get past Sokka who slowly relented and let him in. Sokka sighed and slid the door closed behind Jet and inwardly cursed. He had warned his father to take caution when dealing with Jet but would that be enough? He knew Jet, Jet was a smooth talker who could butter his way in or out of most situations that did not have anything to do with Fire Nation. He would have to trust his father to have better judgment than himself or Katara.

“Nice to meet you, Jet. I am Hakoda, Chieftain of the Southern Water Tribe. You know my children I hear?” Hakoda smiled back at Jet but it was guarded.

Sokka felt a swell of pride in his father taking his warnings seriously and moved to sit near them. Jet followed Sokka’s lead and sat so that they were in a triangle facing each other.

“I do, actually! Katara is one wicked waterbender and Sokka has to be the smartest guy in this place. They are really something, you should be proud.” Jet beamed. Sokka glared. He had sounded so genuine but Sokka just could not let what had happened these last few hours not fog his judgment.

Hakoda chuckled and nodded. “I am. Every day. So, as much as I love to gush about my children I fear we do not have that time yet.”

“Dad’s right. We need a plan.”

“I heard the alarms. Cooler Boat failed then? Bet it was those in the boat’s fault, too. They were not patience, no firebenders that I have seen are.”

Sokka huffed. “You haven’t seen Zuko when he is in a certain determined mood then-”

“Oh I have. Trust me. He can be very determined and obedient, but patience will run thin quickly when pushed.” Jet winked and Sokka’s insides rolled with a hot, sticky, and angry feeling that spread throughout his body. He grit his teeth to prevent himself from speaking and just sat to identify the feeling. He knew what it was. It was the same feeling he got when Hahn would say gross things about Yue. He was jealous and disgusted and had a need to defend.

Hakoda cleared his throat and rubbed the back of his neck. “Boys, if you please. I think we have more to deal with than firebenders and their short patience.”

Sokka tore his glare from Jet and nodded. “Yeah, like how we are going to get out of here in the first place.”

“I always say, if the problem is too big-” Hakoda started and Sokka joined. Both echoing, “break it down.”

“I see the resemblance now. Okay, that is great and all, but how?” Jet looked between them.

“Sokka, what is the first thing you absolutely need?”

“Easy, everyone together. No matter how much we think through, if everyone is scattered it is pointless and a waste of energy.” Sokka rubbed his chin in thought. “Right now we are all scattered and under a kind of lock down. Dad, you are here. Jet can clearly be anywhere so that is not a problem. I am sure I could get Suki out of her cell as long as guard rotation does not change. The problem…”

“I see, so the problem is how to move your prince to where he needs to be?”

“Wow Sokka, you move fast.” Jet snickered.

“It is not ‘prince’ in the way you use it. It is Prince Zuko of the Fire Nation.” Sokka rolled his eyes and ignored his fathers raised eyebrow. Jet, for his part, seemed to power down for a moment as he worked through that added information.

“No, you have to be pulling my leg. Lee is not prince of the Fire Nation. Not a chance that clumsy mess of a being could ever be a royal! Let alone of the evilest family- well, actually that last bit I could see.”

“Yes, well, he is and he is not Lee. He is Zuko and you need to process this on your own time. For now, how do we get everyone together?”

“Well, I think you already answered that.” Hakoda smirked at the confused looks he got. “Think about it. Jet is obviously able to flit around as he pleases. He could potentially let the prince-”
