“No. I’m sorry dad. That may seem the obvious answer but mixing those two is like mixing oil and water. Add a spark and everything goes up in flames.”

“Hey, we are not that bad.”

“Name one fucking time I have seen you two in the same space and you haven’t raised a hand against Zuko or provoked-” Sokka was getting angry and his tone showed it.

“Sokka. Language.” Hakoda barked out over Sokka’s accusations to cut him off. His face showed that he was not amused at this display but understood there was more happening than he knew. “Alright. I amend my suggestion with a simple change. Jet gets Suki out and you find Zuko.”

Sokka shifted to look at the floor to visualize this and not meet his fathers disapproving eyes. He knew better than to fight with a teammate but he was just so tired. His defense was at a low and Jet grated his every nerve. He really did remind Sokka of Hahn.

“Yeah, that could work. Okay. Say we get everyone out. We make a meeting point. Then what? We sit and have a picnic? I’m sorry dad, I just can’t think of anything past the Coolers but we failed with those.”

“I don’t know how you got here, son. But I do know of one way on and off of this island.”

“No. Can’t be done.” Jet stated.

“Wait, you’re not talking about the gondola, are you?”

Hakoda grinned and nodded. “I got here on it so it does work.”

“Hate to agree with Jet on this, no way.”

“It is true that there are too many guards. That is the next problem to work through.” Sokka sighed and nodded. He would entertain his fathers idea on the grounds of it being the only one.

“You two can’t be serious.” Jet looked between them in disbelief. “The guards would fry us long before we got CLOSE to the thing!”

“Then we will need a distraction, now wont we.” Hakoda smirked.

“Nothing short of a full riot would distract from a gondola pulling away from the… that is it, isn’t it? We need a full riot. That solves three problems. How to get everyone out, in one place, and distract. Dad, that just might work!” Sokka looked up to meet his fathers eyes.

“Sorry to be the wet blanket but no dice. That would just get all the guards onto the deck. They would surely notice a gondola leaving even in that mess. What do you think the warden would do?” Jet leaned back and crossed his arms, frowning at the floor trying to follow the logic before him.

“He is right. The warden takes far too much pride in his title and record to let anyone go. He would rather cut the line than lose it.” Sokka sighed. Why was this so much harder than the cooler boat had been?

Hakoda flashed a wicked smirk and added “not if we have a hostage.”

“A hostage? Who would we take with?” Sokka asked.

“Well, would your prince work?”

Jet snorted and rolled his eyes. “I highly doubt that, sir. He is on a dead or alive listing. They would be in a hurry to cut the line with him on board.”

“Alright, if not the prince than another guard? I’m sure you know of a few you could get to help or maybe take down, son?”

“Nice to see you have faith in these people, sir. But none of that would work. The warden is far too prideful to not sacrifice anyone on board to keep his record over lives. A hostage of any kind wont mean a thing to him.” Jet sighed and rubbed his face.

An idea struck Sokka and he beamed. Jet was right. They would cut the line no matter how many they stuffed into that gondola. That record was worth everything, but who was keeping it? Maybe this would work after all.

“Jet is right. The warden takes far too much pride in keeping HIS record clean.” Sokka’s tone got Jet to look up and recognition dawned on him. He paused for a moment before he too grinned and snickered.

“You’re a genius.”

“Boys, mind cluing an old man in?”

“He is thinking of taking the warden as the hostage.” Jet stated while keeping eye contact. Sokka grinned back and nodded. For once it was nice to have Jet on the same page.

Hakoda nodded along and crossed his arms. “I’m impressed, that might just work. Grab their leader, break their foundation.”

“Okay, we have the frame. Release the prisoners, get to the main floor, start a riot, grab the warden, gondola away.” Sokka listed out. His heart sank, this was complicated and left room for so much to go wrong. He would have to be ready for everything.

“Hmm, that’s an awfully long list.” Hakoda nodded along. “Best work out the main parts to be clear.”

“I think I can help that first and second point. See, I know this place well. I think I can get into the master control room and switch the master release. I’ve seen it done before, I know how. Just need a time frame.”

“My, you really do know this place well! Good group you got here, son.” Hakoda grinned and winked at Sokka who just gave him a puzzled look.

“Jet. You think or you can? I need to know for sure.”

“I can. You can count on me.” Jet locked eyes with Sokka to make his point clear. Sokka did not like it, but he was going to have to trust this to him.

“Right. Jet, you are in charge of releasing prisoners. I’ll do a loop and inform everyone to meet by the corner where we first met. Dad, just follow the stairs and it’s around there. Jet or Suki will grab you from there.”

“And prince Zuko?”

“Leave him to me. Trust me, dad. Something is going to hold him up and I have a feeling it is guards being cruel.” Sokka sighed. He would be ready to run in and get Zuko himself if he missed the deadline.

“Alright. When should I flip the switch?”

They all spent the next few minutes going over and fine tuning the new plan. They decided to use the suns path and shadows to be sure Jet would be able to know when was good to open the cells. After that Sokka had them work through a few problematic situations such as Jet getting caught, the warden stays inside, or spirits forbid Sokka is detained. They spent near thirty minutes on the finer details and when they all had nothing left it add Sokka sighed and hung his head. This was it. It this failed they would all be trapped here or worse. He was gambling their lives but there was nothing for it.

Hakoda patted his son’s back and smiled. “Sokka, you have a great plan. Even if this doesn’t work, I’m proud of the work you have put into everything. I don’t think it won’t work, don’t get me wrong, I believe Tui and La may just have blessed us by bringing us together.”

“Thanks dad, but I don’t think spirits have anything to do with this.” Sokka sighed and rolled his shoulder.

“Perhaps not, but I choose to believe in them and see their gifts for what they are.”

“Yeah, alright. Better pray to that thing in the sun then too. He’s the one we have to-”

“They.” Jet said without clear thought.

“What?” Both water tribe men parroted

“You said ‘he.’ The sun spirit isn’t gendered. I made that mistake, too. The moon is a ‘she’ after all, only made sense. I learned from Lee-… Zuko… that they view ‘Agni’ as genderless and all powerful.” Jet shrugged.

“Right… and that is important because?” Sokka questioned a bit more sarcastically than he had meant to.

Jet huffed and winked at him regardless. “Because, if you are to woo a certain firefucker you may want to respect his spirit.”

Hakoda groaned and rubbed his temple. “Is it even worth saying ‘language’ at this point? Jet, could you head out and scout? I need a word with my son.”

Jet nodded and moved to stand. With a dramatic bow he swiftly exited the room and whistled as he went down the hall. He was being cocky and Sokka did not have time to deal with it. If he couldn’t release everyone, the whole plan fell apart and that was terrifying.

Sokka glanced from the door to his father and froze. His father had moved to face Sokka and was waiting patiently He was sitting on his knees in a way that screamed this was going to be an uncomfortable conversation. He slowly turned to face his father, heart starting to pound in panic.

“Dad?”

“Sokka, you have grown so much in so little time. I really missed out on some fundamental moments and I could never begin to repay you or your sister.”

“It’s fine, really. I mean, no, it’s not. It sucked and it hurt and we might be more upset at being left behind than even we will admit… but we understand. War rips everything apart eventually.”

Hakoda gave him a sad smile. “You are such a strong and kind young man. When did you become such a great leader?”

“When I was the only one left.” He hadn’t meant to sound so brutally honest, but it was true. The day he watched the boats of his tribesman sail was the day he felt his childhood end.

“With becoming a man comes with many new and exciting things.” Hakoda pressed on. Sokka froze. He had a feeling where this was going now.

“Dad? I… don’t think this is the time…”

“No, perhaps not, but I just need you to know you can talk to me about anything. And that I will accept anyone you feel is worth your affection.” Hakoda finished.

Sokka sat frozen. He was not sure what he had been expecting, but his father claiming that he was accepting of anyone Sokka chose to have feelings for was not it. His father had just given him his blessing. Open and honest. To love whoever he wanted and something deep inside himself snapped. He had not noticed he had worried about others reaction to his sudden new feelings. Suki was clearly fine with it and Jet was just Jet. A part of him knew he was hoping this new feeling would die because he was scared of Katara’s reaction, mostly. He had internalized something along the way and pushed away anything that suggested a male was attractive.

He knew Zuko was hot, and that he had in some way found Jet to be attractive that first meeting. He knew he had moments of liking men, just like he had with women. That he fell fast and hard. He had just never let himself do so with a man. There was never time or chance. Even if there was, he would have ignored it and moved past.

His face felt wet and he was suddenly enveloped in his father’s arms tightly. Sokka let himself break. Let the pressure of everything since the failed mission during the eclipse to the last two days to finally fall off of his shoulders and onto his fathers. He let himself feel the emotions that had been building under the surface. He felt the joy and the heartbreak at the idea of Suki, the tornado of negative and positive emotions that circled around the last few days, and the deep regrets that had been building since he left the North Pole.

He cried for his sister being forced into the role of a warrior no matter what she might have wanted at such a young age. That he was no longer strong enough to protect her. He cried for Suki being ripped from her home because of actions he did that brought Aang to that island. He cried for Yue, for the loss of the moon and how it took her from the world. For the life he could have had. For the life he was having now. And, he cried for the pain in his heart at the thought of everything Zuko has been through that he had chosen to ignore.

His father held him through it all and hummed an old song his mother used to sing when Sokka or Katara was upset. The song helped, in a weird way, to ground Sokka back to reality and out of his head. He slowly took a moment to gather himself before he pulled out of his fathers hold. Rubbing his face Sokka refused to meet his eyes, he had just cried so hard like a child.

“You are an amazing young man, my son. Do not fear your emotions. We all need to let them out and I find a good cry often helps clear the mind.” Hakoda’s words were soft.

Sokka wiped his face and huffed. “Like you cry.”

“All the time! Ask Bato next time we are together, he had to deal with it usually.” Hakoda chuckled. “Crying does not make one any less than who they are. Remember. You are Sokka of the Southern Water Tribe. I am so proud of you. Now, lets get this plan of yours into motion and save your growing family.”

Sokka nodded and flashed a smile towards his father. He was right, of course. They had a lot to do and only two hours now to do it. Emotions and talking could come later.

He had chosen to head to Suki first for a good reason. Zuko needed rest and he had not wanted to interrupt that any time soon. The problem was the number of guards on the upper levels. They were all heading to somewhere and looked a mixture of worried, confused, and excited. It was not a mixture he wanted any part of. He had missed too many fake rotations as it was and was pushing just how long his little act was going to hold up. Pivoting, he headed towards Zuko instead and vowed that he would make the bender rest the second they were out of danger.

The hall in the lower level felt deserted with the guards all heading towards the upper levels. It was almost too easy for Sokka to slip up towards the cell door of Zuko’s and knock hard a few times. He was met with silence. Knocking again, he stood on his toes to peek inside and was met with silence and darkness. Worry spiked within him and he looked around haphazardly before he manually undid the outside latch and slid the door open.

Light flooded the space and he was met with the sight of Zuko laying on the floor mat. He took a moment to assess for any problems in this image. Zuko looked exhausted, even as he snored lightly in deep sleep. He was pulled into himself loosely as if he had started in a tight ball and sleep had moved his position outwards as he relaxed. His pale skin looked near snow white in the lighting and made Sokka shiver at just how cold he looked despite being in a literal volcano.

Sliding the door shut, Sokka flipped the switch to ignite the candle in the glass of the cell. He did have to admit that little invention was ingenious and if he had the time he would have loved to get his hands on it. Shuffling forward, he slipped to the floor to sit just in front of Zuko’s sleeping form. The scar side was buried into the mat but it was clear that the bender was so deeply exhausted that he was virtually dead to the world around him.

Taking the moment for what it was, Sokka pulled off his helmet and reached out toward Zuko slowly. His hand was visibly shaking and his heart had joined in the non existing party. He was just going to shake the bender awake, why did he suddenly feel so nervous about making any kind of contact? He knew why. He knew what he wanted to do and what he was trying to do were two different things.

Zuko would kill him if he did what he wanted- no wait, maybe he wouldn’t. Sokka sat with his hand mere inches from the sleeping figure and thought about it. Zuko, in some way, had at least a chance at not killing Sokka. He had dated Jet, after all. The way Jet spoke made it sound like it was at least also based on attraction of some kind. Ignoring every thought that brought with a hard swallow it let Sokka conclude at least one thing. Zuko, at least in some way, liked men. Sokka was a man. He also in some way liked men. He liked women too but that was just complicating the matter. Just because Sokka was a man did not mean Zuko liked him the same way he was accepting that he felt for the bender. It was at least helpful to know, and gave his fluttering insides hope.

He settled on mixing the want to do and the should do action. Gently, he closed the distance and brushed the damp from sweat hair out Zuko’s face. He took care in making sure the touch was just enough to let him know that he was being touched, but gentle enough to hopefully be taken as caring. Sokka’s heart skipped a beat as Zuko’s eyelids flickered slightly.

“Hey buddy. I’m sorry about this but I need you to wake up.” Sokka whispered. His hand moved to cup Zuko’s face and let his thumb rub at the bags under his right eye as if he could gently smudge them away. His breath hitched when Zuko’s face shifted to be more securely in his hold as the golden eyes fluttered open.

“Sssokka?” Zuko slurred, shifting slightly to be able to look towards the voice confused.

“Yeah, hey. It’s me. How are you feeling?” He smiled and held his hand steady for a moment longer before letting it slip slightly to rest on the back of Zuko’s neck.

“‘m fine. Sokka, what’s wrong?” Zuko looked to him confused. Eyes now much more awake and flicking to look between Sokka’s left arm and Sokka himself. He was clearly confused by the touch and Sokka really should stop but he just could not pull his hand away. The bender felt just as warm as he was, which was cold for a firebender and his worry had redoubled. Turn’s out Zuko could lie if he himself believed it.

“Nothing is wrong.” Sokka spoke in a hushed tone as he leaned forward and pulled lightly on the back of Zuko’s neck. If he did not want to follow the ask he merely had to push back. He had to only say so. Had only to twist away and Sokka would let him.

Zuko, for his part stared at him still very much confused but his eyes had widened quite a bit. He had a dusting of color on his face that was deepening to a beautiful shade of red and Sokka could feel that his heartbeat had sped up. Slowly, he let the hand guide him up slightly.

If Sokka was being honest it was his fault for what happened next. He had bent forward, rested his forehead against the benders and closed his eyes. He really did not have a plan for this. He was acting on pure impulse which was a new territory for his overactive mind. He just knew he wanted to be close. This close he could smell the prison on him, but also that strange spice again which he wondered if that was just a firebender thing. He sighed heavily.

“I’m sorry, for everything. Zuko, if I could take back how we treated you when you first came-”

“I would have believed you all mad if you had just welcomed me with open arms. I hold no negative feelings towards you guys, Sokka, I get it. What’s wrong? Why bring that up all of a sudden?” Zuko cut him off and took the moment to twist so he was propped up onto his left elbow but did not pull away from the awkward hold. If anything, he had moved himself further and more securely into Sokka’s hold and was clearly watching what he could see of his face with the continued contact. That was a good sign at least, right?

Sokka smirked. “Yeah, I guess I would have too after everything. Still, Zuko. I just… the last few hours have been rough. Forgive me, but I just really need you to understand how much you mean to me.” He felt as Zuko’s breath caught and his body twitched as if shocked that someone could admit they care for him.

“Thank you for coming here. I really cannot tell you how much I needed you here. I am sorry for having you take the pain of my failures in this.” He finished and the smile fell as he sighed.

Zuko shifted up more to force Sokka’s head to move and get him to open his eyes. The action had their noses bump awkwardly and Sokka pulled back just enough to fully look at Zuko’s face. His ocean blue eyes met with glowing suns, mini and intense and completely focused on him. His breath hitched when those molten eyes had flicked to his lips in an almost shy way. He knew he was doomed to fall harder into the pool of whatever was happening the second Zuko settled back to look at him with a clean question. Sokka let his hand slip from Zuko’s neck slowly and move to his shoulder to grip lightly for support and everything changed.

He watched in panic as his light touch was met with nothing but clear pain. Zuko’s whole body had jerked and he slumped back down and away from him. He hissed out a few words Sokka did not understand as his face scrunched up in the attempt to regulate himself. Sokka pulled his hand away quickly and moved back to give Zuko space.

“Shit, sorry! I-I forgot. Your shoulder, is it that bad? Let me see. It didn’t feel swollen but that doesn’t always mean it is not in need of some help.” Sokka twisted to sit up on his knees and moved over the bender huddled in a clear wave of pain.

Zuko put out his left arm to prevent Sokka from reaching out again which only worked to aggravate him. Zuko was in pain, Sokka had caused it. Why would he not drop whatever weird guard he had and let him help for spirits sake!? He tried to swat the arm out of the way and was met with surprising resistance. Zuko usually relented when others pushed him but this time he refused to let him close. He kept his arm up and in the way and eventually settled his hand on Sokka’s chest with surprising strength to hold him back.

“Sokka, forget it. It's fine and not anything to worry about. Sit the fuck down, Agni.” He huffed out and shoved a final time to make his point clear. Moving to sit up, he rolled his shoulders without so much as a wince to prove some point and glared at him. “You said you would not be back without a plan, well?”

Right, mood officially ruined. Good going Sokka, you had a chance and burned it. Spirits know when or if a moment like that will happen again.

“Yeah, alright. But I will be checking it once we are free unless you want a straight trip to Katara.” Sokka glared back. Zuko huffed, rolled his eyes, and relented with a nod.

“Good. Right, we do actually have a plan. I probably don’t have much time seeing as I have yet to inform Suki but we are getting out of here tonight.”

“Tonight? Alright. What do you need from me?” Zuko moved to sit and fully face him.

“I need you to be in the main courtyard in two hours. Jet is going to free all the prisoners and you need to be sure to make your way to the location where we first met Suki. Where you were mopping. Can you do that?”

Zuko nodded.

“If you get held up just know-”

“You will leave, I get it.” Zuko nodded in agreement.

“What, spirits no. Zuko, I will be staying here with you.”

“What!? No, you can’t! Sokka, you need to leave-”

“Not without you I don’t. We started this together and we finish it together. End of story. Sorry buddy, you don’t get a say in what I do.” Sokka stated and crossed his arms as if that was the end of it all.

Whatever Zuko was going to most likely loudly retort back was cut short as scraping from the outside told Sokka he had seconds before the cell was pulled open. Scrambling, Sokka shoved his helmet back on and grabbed Zuko by the shirt collar. He ripped the startled bender to his feet and slammed him against the wall just as the door to the cell opened. He instantly regretted how much force he had used as he watch Zuko’s face twist in pain yet again and his body shuddered in the aftershock of everything. Zuko’s hands came up automatically to grip at Sokka’s but they did not feel the slightest bit warmer. Zuko, even if he had been suddenly attacked, had not even instinctually reached for his fire to free himself from Sokka.

The door slid open with a loud bang and two clear surprised gasps followed by a deep chuckle came from the doorway.

“Lee, there you are! No wonder you missed the fun, you were taking the moment to play with your prize.” The man laughed. Sokka knew that gruff voice. That was Ruon. He could only guess the other with him was Ming. They were a package deal it seemed.

“Alright, newbie. Time to drop yer little toy. We have orders to take him.” Ming said through a clear smile.

Sokka felt a strong grasp to his shoulder pulling him back easily but with the care of one not wanting to hurt anyone. He let his grip on Zuko’s collar fall and followed the pull of Ruon’s grasp to step away.

Zuko rubbed his neck and glared at them. “What do you want from me? I haven’t done anything.” He rasped out.

“That is not for us to decide, traitor.” Ming snipped out and moved to roughly twist Zuko’s left arm behind his back. Sokka had to watch as Zuko grunted but let her lead him away.

“Where are you taking him?” He asked, hoping to know just where he would need to be to find him later.

“Ming is right, Lee. It really is not our place to ask questions. We have orders from the warden to move him to an upper questioning cell. That is all we are to know.” Ruon glanced towards Sokka with a questioning look.

“Lee, you really did miss out on one heck of an all guards line-up.”

“Oh? I’m sorry, I must have missed the memo.” Sokka looked to the door, not wanting to risk making eye contact with Ruon in case he was searching for something.

“Yeah, turns out we had a traitor in our mix all along. Did you know that?” He was fishing for a reaction and had kept his grip on Sokka’s shoulder to prevent escape. He knew something and this made Sokka want to panic. He could not. He had to play it cool.

“Really? I had no idea. I thought the prince was the only one foolish enough for that.” Sokka kept his tone neutral. He needed to not show his nerves.

Ruon chuckled and shook his shoulder lightly. “Relax Lee, Agni you are tense! They caught him, don’t you worry. Turns out he had been the one to help Chit Sang with the whole Cooler thing. Did you even know that had happened? You seem to miss a lot of the meetings.”

“Oh! N-No I had… I had no idea! Sorry, I am just really uhm…” Sokka was shocked and confused. They had caught a guard but it was not himself. He was still Lee and right now Lee had been a really bad new guard.

“Can’t read Fire Script, can you?” Ruon said in a tone that was almost like pity. “I get it. You said you were from the newer Southern Colonies right? Hard to think you would know everything. That area is really lacking with their education of the youth from my understanding. The Earth Kingdom makes travel there challenging and I am sorry we don’t use more Common Script. I have said that the use of only Fire would eventually do this to our younger and newer population. I’ll try to find you and get you a schedule you can read soon, okay?”

Sokka hated this. Hated that Ruon was treating him with such a focused kindness. That he did like this man in some small way and that he felt bad for all the lies. This man was kind, thoughtful, and had been nothing but helpful. He was also a Fire Nation guard at Boiling Rock Prison and that was reason enough to shove those feelings deep into the lake.

“I… I had thought I could get around it without others knowing. Didn’t want to look like I wasn’t Fire Nation just because I came from lands away from the mainland, you know?” Sokka lied. He locked his eyes onto the floor.

“Oh, Lee. You are every bit Fire Nation. Look, just because you come from a colony does not change the fact that you are a citizen of ours. It is our failings to the colonies in not trying more to give you all the education you have a right to for joining the great nation. If you want, we could study together. I always did enjoy teaching. If it wasn’t for the whole war and needing benders thing I might have just signed up to go teach in the colonies myself.” Ruon squeezed his shoulder once and let go. “Think about it, yeah? I’ll get you things you can read in the meantime. No wonder you are always tense.”

Sokka watched him leave and felt the mixture of guilt and sadness. He had to remind himself that, just because they were at war did not mean he was expected to hate everyone. There were innocent people fighting, and good people doing awful things. This was just an example of it. With that, Sokka pushed on. He had little time left to get the rest of this moving and get to where he needed to be.

Knocking on the cell door for Suki, Sokka slid the door open just enough to slip in. He quickly took off his helmet and fixed his wolf tail. He was sweating heavily with anxiety and heat and that helmet was just ruining his hair. Once he had it fixed he sent a shy smile to Suki who was leaning against the far wall and watching him. She raised her eyebrow in question but made no other move.

“Hey, so. Big things.”

Suki pushed off the wall and moved close so they could be quiet. “Yeah? Everything alright?”

“I think so. Everyone is in motion at least.”

“Motion? How so?”

“Well, they just took Zuko to a questioning cell for one.”

“Questioning? Sokka, that is-”

“Not good yeah, I know. Not much we can do about it right now. besides, I think we have a Plan C after all and we are running low on time. If I am going to locate Zuko, we’ve got to be quick.”

“Okay, what is it?”

“While it does mean I’m keeping my word of getting out before sundown, I have a feeling you won’t like it.” Sokka rubbed his neck nervously. Like it or not it was in motion and all they had.

“Sokka, what did you do?” Suki asked in a worried tone mixed with a hint of exasperation.

“Nothing yet! That’s Jet’s job. We are getting out of here in the gondola.”

“The wha- no. Sokka. That’s suicide!” Suki gasped.

“No, no! We worked it out, see! It can work!” Sokka waved his hands to try and keep her from doing something, he just wasn’t sure what that something was.

“Sokka, that’s crazy! There are far too many guards for a start.”

“See, that is what I said. But dad said they wouldn’t if we take a hostage with us.” Sokka was desperate to get her on board. If he could not convince Suki he had no hope of getting everyone onto the death trap.

“Okay” Suki huffed and waved a hand in a tight circle to think. “Okay, say we grab a hostage. That doesn’t solve the number of guards problem. If anything it makes it harder.”

“Dad thought of that too. Said we will need a distraction. That is why we are going to start a full prison riot!” Sokka exclaimed a bit too excited about this part of the idea. He had to admit, he was looking forward to seeing what an actual prison riot looked like.

Suki crossed her arms tight over her chest, pivoted to fully face Sokka, and cocked an eyebrow. She was not amused by any of this. If anything, Sokka could feel the doubt rolling off of her in waves.

“Okay, let’s say by some miracle, this all works and we make it onto the gondola. The warden will still just cut the lines, even if we have a captive.”

“That’s what I said, too. But Jet pointed out they wouldn’t if the warden IS the captive.” Sokka smirked and watched as Suki worked through that part. He really needed her for that one, she was their fastest member with Zuko as unsteady as he was. She would be needed to at least corner the warden if this was to work at all.

Suki raised both eyebrows in surprise and nodded. She slowly uncrossed her arms and sighed. “Alright, yeah. That just might work.”

“See? It’s crazy, but just crazy enough to tip in our favor.” Sokka grinned and moved to hug Suki tight.

“Sokka. I really don’t like this. It’s a huge gamble.” She muttered into his shoulder and returned the hug.

“I know, but it’s the only one we’ve got. We either all win or lose. No going back now, either. Jet is already on the move. Once your cell opens as the master switch is pulled I need you to meet Jet and help my dad get to our little corner where the first meeting was held. Can you do that?”

“Yes, of course. And Sokka…” She trailed off as she pulled back from his hold.

“Yeah?”

“Make sure Sunshine makes it. I noticed he wasn’t on your list. I’m not leaving without him.”

Sokka chuckled. “I’ll get him myself if need be. Don’t worry, I’d stay behind before I’d ever think about leaving him.”

Suki nodded and smiled wide. She gave him one more quick hug before pushing him out the door on the grounds of keeping things moving. He had to agree with her, based on the shadows. He had an hour to locate Zuko and be ready to-

“Lee! Over here!”

Sokka cursed internally. He really needed to learn how to shake these people. Slowly, he turned to glance back at Ruon who slapped his back with a friendly gesture.

“Glad I caught you! I was looking around just now and found some things I think you might like!” He grinned and nudge Sokka forward. He had no choice.

Following Ruon felt like doom weights were being strapped to his ankles. With every step he could hear the tick of time as Jet most likely made his way to the master controls. Ruon, for his part look proud of something as he led the way to the mess hall and over to a table with scrolls and papers.

“Here, I was able to dig up some old scrolls left behind and found one on Fire Script! It is a bit dated, so know that if you only learn through this you are going to sound ancient, but it does cover basics.” Ruon slapped the stack of papers and scrolls so old that a small cloud of dust rose with the action.

“You… you didn’t have to go through all this for me.”

“Nonsense. We have some time before roll call with the visitors so why don’t we get a head start?” Ruon moved to sit. That question was not an actual question and Sokka knew it.

Slowly he pulled out the chair and settled for what was to be his first official lesson on Fire Script. It would probably be the only one, but he might as well learn from it. He had only to hope letting out prisoners would cause a big enough commotion that he could slip out.

Ruon, Sokka had to admit, would have made a wonderful teacher. He was gentle with his questions and never became frustrated when Sokka could not follow the writing. He was in every sense of the word patience despite Sokka knowing him to be a firebender. He explained the differences between characters and why. Even laughed or agreed when Sokka called one character stupid for looking just like a different one. By the end of it all Sokka was able to write and read specific times, days of the week, and was starting to understand location words specific to the Boiling Rock.

He hated to admit it, but learning this had been fun. He had enjoyed learning and challenging his brain on something where failing had only been met with a gentle correction and not pain for others. Had enjoyed Ruon teaching him the basic of basics before it all went off track.

A loud bell rang from the halls and echoed loudly throughout the room. Blaring in warning of something happening and by the way Ruon tightened it was not good. He glanced to the man and was about to ask when Ruon stood.

“Lee, stay here. I’m going to check it out.”

“What is that alarm?”

“Master release. All prisoners are being let out but that can’t be right. Not with who we have visiting.” Ruon grabbed his helmet and moved to leave.

“Wait, who’s visiting?” Sokka asked.

“It’s a surprise, trust me. It’s worth the wait.” He smirked and left the room to run down the hall.

Sokka for his part, counted to twenty before he two ran out and towards the courtyard. It was now or never. Part one of Plan C had started.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

Thank you so much for following along with this madness! First soft moment I have ever written and I am a bit nervous. What did you all think? Let me know! &#60;3

Plan C is in motion!!

## Chapter 14

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Have fun with the mini chapter!

Warning: A lot of swearing in this chapter

I am going on a vacation so the next bit might be later than a week, sorry!

### Text

The halls were empty as guards continued to shuffle in an unorganized mess. They were like a bunch of rat coyotes with how they moved around as if scuttling through some sewage system. Where they belong in his humble opinion, but the world was not so fair as to grant wishes.

A quick look around told him that he was definitely alone, which meant he was free to act as he wanted. This was how things usually were, but it was exhilarating today with the added bonus of getting out of this pit and fucking over those who rightfully deserved nothing more than their own free fall into the boiling lake. With a smirk and a little flick of the tongue to shift the long since flavorless wheat stem into a more secure hold, he let out a little whistle and ducked towards the outer wall paths. Those were never used and never checked. They were his favorite little paths and never failed.

Jet quickly turned the lock pad on the outer door into the code that had been written on the memo pad in the mess hall. Knowing where to find things was easy if one knew where to look. These people left their shit laying out in the open as if they were all powerful and impenetrable. It never stopped being easier than stealing a piece of wheat out of a wheat field to get what he wanted. He knew everything about this place and the warden and guards were blind to it all.

These people never even took notice when he had one of his replacement goons fill in for him in his cell during his block checks. Absolutely pathetic. His Freedom Fighters would put these morons to shame. They would have made this place into their own in a matter of weeks, but as it was he was alone in here and had made a drastic error.

He knew everyone here, and therefore he had known Suki was from Kyoshi and was here in more isolation lock down than anything. She had apparently caused a few stirs while he was in the hospital before his transfer, yet he had considered those a one off thing. She had, from every point of angle he had seen, given up and was a broken warrior like the rest of the prisoners. He had neglected the idea of a fighter waiting for her opportunity and instead chose to wave her off and focus on the newer people and ones who were more vocal. He had written her off as defeated and now realized just how wrong he had been. If he had only paid more attention to this idea and spent the time needed to work his way into her good books, then he would not be on the receiving end of that obviously overprotective drive she had going with a certain worthless bundle of mistakes.

The lock popped as it accepted the code and the door slid open just as obediently as ever. A quick look around to be sure and a shuffle sideways and he was out in the blistering heat of the day. As nice as these outer paths were they were also extremely open to the elements and the time of day played a big factor. One could quite literally cook themselves with the outer reflection on a clear day if they were not careful. Today, it seemed as if Agni was granting some kindness to Sokka’s plans if one chose to believe in spirit mush. The clouds rolling through were thick and helped to cut down on the reflections to give safe access to the outer ridge paths.

That brought back a nasty little reminder as he started to follow the path towards the control rooms on the upper levels. Something he had been trying to just file away in the ‘to be forgotten’ section and never thought about again. The thought of Agni itself seemed to slap it right back to the front and demand his attention.

Jet huffed out a frustrated groan and let it. Lee, whom he had believed to once be a refugee like himself and had thought shared his passions, was Zuko. He still refused to really call him anything but Lee if he could help it. This was not because he could not accept Lee as Zuko, but because it always got that frustrated eye twitch from the man that Jet had always loved. He was still so easy to rile up no matter what name was tacked onto that man. No, what had struck so hard to his core was the idea that Lee, who was actually Zuko, was actually Prince Zuko of the Fire Nation.

That was not lining up. Nothing about Zuko screamed royal anything but a pain in the ass. The most he could lead was an army of turtleducks into a tea shop once by accident. The memory of that moment caused Jet to snort a laugh and shake his head.

Those little ducks had followed Lee around like he was their mother for hours. Nothing he did shook them or scared them away. He had yelled, thrown towels down well outside of any range to hit them, and stomped his foot. He had even tried hiding on the counter but those five little ducklings had found him in seconds. His uncle had found the whole thing far too funny for someone running a restaurant where wild animals should be frowned upon.

In the end Lee had let them follow him around the shop as he tried to serve tea. He still yelled at them when they were in the way but never raised anything but a gentle hand to them. Scooping them up only to find himself with ducklings using his shoulders, head, and apron pocket as perches. Jet had nearly burst a lung watching the distressed Lee try so hard to be not liked by those relentless things and fail. He did get a bit jealous at the coos from some of the women in the shop but had felt pride that his wink and words were the cause of Lee’s blushes. That memory was one of the best from Ba Sing Se, and it was all ruined.

Now if he thought about it, those things probably could care less who it was they chased. What they had wanted was a warm body and they knew a firebender when they saw one. They were lucky said bender did not decide he was done with them and roast them alive. The thought burned and was replaced with an image of Lee surrounded in flames. It fit, and it burned.

Zuko was always surrounded in flames. He breathed fire like a dragon and burned everything he touched. This did not have to be with true fire. He had burned Jet’s heart with his smoke and lies. He burned Suki’s village physically, Jet still could not understand how she was willing to just let that slide and baby someone who was only good for pain. Zuko had burned Sokka and everyone else so many times. Mentally and physically. Why did they all choose to side with that wildfire!?

Jet slammed a careless fist into the hot metal and cursed. Shaking it out he sighed deeply. This was getting him nowhere. It did not matter what kind of smoke Zuko was using this time to hide behind. He never kept control over the fire causing the smokes he used and they would either see through it or be burned sooner or later. Zuko was a liar. He was not worth anything. He was not even worth his own peoples love if the rumors had any truth. There was a reason for that. There had to be. Sooner or later everyone was going to see that Zuko was not worth this time. That any firebender was not worth the time.

Making his way along the ridge he shook his head to focus. He had wasted time shuffling down memories that only caused anger to spike and would do them all no good. If he was honest, he hoped the guards kept the so called prince under a tight leash and that he missed the launch. He belonged here more than any of them, after all. It was a prison of his people and for his people. The most fitting place for a prince of the cursed nation.

Once he reached the door to the upper level he shoved as lightly as he could. Feeling the lock hold strong, he felt along the wall for the hidden lock pad. Finding it, he entered the code once more and felt the latch give. He counted to 60 slowly, like always. These people had no patience. If they were to try and catch him out on these ledges or investigate they always moved around the 30 second marker or sooner. No one had lasted a full 60 waiting for the door to open.

Feeling confident, he slid the door open and slipped into the dark hall. This was another down side to the ridge path. One needed time to adjust to the sudden change in lighting. It also brought up a fun little thing he thought many may not know. Firebenders were blind as shit in the dark.

When he remembered raids in dark rooms with Lee he had always thought Lee was clumsy and loud. He tended to move much slower the darker the room and even held onto others if there was little to no light at all. While Jet and his Earth Kingdom people could adjust to low light settings fairly well, Lee had never seemed to be able to see unless there was some light. Jet had written it off as something to do with the scar, now he thought it might be more universal than that. They were things of light after all. Children of the sun or some shit.

Blinking a few more times Jet was able to recognize where he had entered. He was fairly close to his needed location and with plenty of time to spare.

Shifting the wheat once more he started down the hall of the upper level and towards the wardens office. The door had looked shut from the distance but as Jet got closer he realized that it was cracked open just enough for the natural light of the windows to glow through the gap. Slowing, he judged that the gap was not big enough for anyone to make out who passed by, but it was enough to hear into the room.

Shuffling silently to the wall, Jet tilted his head to listen. If the warden was in his room then he would have to figure out how to get that man to move around soon.

“I don’t know… That guy had a really icky aura. All dark and gray. You sure he wasn’t the one to do whatever the warden said he did?” A female voice asked. She sounded young and… upside down? No, that could not be right. She might just be sitting or short. Whoever that was, she sounded clueless.

“Oh please.” A harsher toned female responded. She was definitely sitting at the wardens desk without a care in the world. The rasp in her undertone was oddly familiar, too. “That guard was nothing more than a revenge target. Clearly.”

“Clearly? But he looked so angry…” the sound of little scuffing told Jet that she had indeed been upside down and was now facing the right way and thinking harder than she probably should be.

“Of course he was angry. His own coworkers had taken the word of a prisoner over him and locked him away on the grounds of aiding their attempted escape. Do you know the punishment for that? If lucky, he becomes a prisoner. If found guilty, execution. Besides, one look at that man was all I needed to know it wasn’t him. Far too stupid and high on his position here to gamble it.” Something had flicked into the wall with a dull thump.

“I don’t think Mai will like it if you keep doing that to her knives, Azula. She gets upset when they are all dull…” The voice was getting quiet. She was timid. She was scared to voice something against whoever this ‘Azula’ was.

Wait… Azula. Jet knew that name. Suki had said she was the reason her girls were caught. That she was the one who had lied and gotten them locked up. That Azula was… oh fucking spirits this was bad. Princess Azula.

Maybe this idea of Zuko being a royal family member held some ground after all. She sounded as though she were a female version of him by her distinctive rasp and tone if one removed the sight of her. Must be a family thing to sound so unique and recognizable.

She was much bigger a threat than her brother though. If Jet had heard right, she was the ruthless and methodical conqueror of Ba Sing Se, and she was here and just sitting in the wardens room. This was every bit an unplanned for problem. Even Sokka hadn’t thought of how to work around this kind of situation and there was no way to tell him now.

“Mai is just lucky I am as nice as I am.” Another dull thud as presumably a knife was thrown hard enough to cut into the wall.

“She is, you are the nicest friend!” It sounded like she really believed that, the fool. Seems the royal siblings could use the same smoke of lies to gain others trust.

“I know I am.”

“It was really kind of you to let her see Zuko-”

“It was not kindness. It was needed. Might well and be her last chance to settle things with dear Zuzu, after all.” A dramatic sigh followed her words but Jet felt the truth to them. She knew something.

“What do you mean? We will see him later after we work out the release-”

“There is no release. Dear Zuzu is not leaving here Ty Lee. Not alive, anyway.”

“W-What? But… but we are to bring him home. That is what you said-”

“It is. We are. My brother is to come home alright. But not alive.”

A loud gasp, most likely the girl called Ty Lee.

“But… But… He will be told he is being transferred to the royal prisons. The one with your uncle. They - they would lie to him? Why?” Tears. He could hear her tears and he felt something roll in his stomach. He did not care for Zuko, but the thought of them discussing his death and his own sibling sounding like she was doing nothing more than reading a scroll made his insides boil.

“Ty Lee, there is no reason to cry over this. Zuzu knew what would happen if father ever got his hands on him again. First time he had done enough to warrant that kind of punishment and father had been kind. He had spared his life. He had given him that reminder so gently and then told him his way home so clearly. It was Zuzu who had failed then.”

Sniffling. The girl called Ty Lee was close to sobbing and Jet felt a strange draw to go console her. He did not move.

“What did Zuzu do when we finally were able to help bring him home? What was he like when we finally gave him all that he wanted.”

“H-He… he… he was…”

“Angry. Uncaring. He was cruel to us and he broke Mai’s heart.”

Sobbing. Ty Lee was crying too hard to answer with more than a hic and a sound of agreement.

“Oh, Ty Lee, it is okay. Mai has her time now after all. We are making sure of that. We are here for her in this.” She sounded so caring all of a sudden that a chill ripped through Jet as he listened. She, he decided, was far better than Zuko at controlling her smoke.

“W-Why can’t… why can’t we just… just bring Zuzu home?”

“You know why. He shot lightning at father. At the Fire Lord. He attacked him during the eclipse. There is nothing we can do… but… if it will stop your tears I will think hard and see what I can do to maybe get that transfer.” She sounded so kind.

Jet knew, if he could see her she would have looked every bit the kind and caring friend. That she really was going to try. And he knew she already knew how to do what she was saying. She was using this to further cement this Ty Lee to her side. That she would suddenly come up with an answer to all this and Ty Lee and Mai would believe she was working with them. That, or she would let them execute Zuko here and claim she tried her best to stop it. She would wail and sob with them at her brothers death with a grin through it all. She, was dangerous.

He had listened for a few minutes and noted that they were most likely going to wait in this room for this Mai girl. The one Zuko had apparently broken the heart of which was an odd thought. Jet tried to think of Zuko with a woman and it was like trying to fit two pieces of a different puzzle together. It could work but it would never be the right image. Shaking his head he had tried to listen more and found what was being discussed to make little sense. Things about girl stuff, Fire Nation things, some hobbies as they waited for their friend.

Jet knew then that he had one option. He had to master seal this door shut. These two were dangerous. Sure, it sounded like there was a third somewhere but that was not something he could deal with right now. He would have to just hope that the warden would not return to his rooms before he could unlock all the prisoners.

Reaching into his pocket, Jet pulled out the master key he had swiped ages ago. He always kept it with himself and it was how he was able to break out of his own cell so often. Usually he used it to unlock his gang members, but today it would serve a greater function. He twisted and located the lock on the outer door. He would have to be quick.

Quietly and as slowly as he could he slipped the key into the lock and twisted it slightly just the check the responding time. The key worked in the lock like it had on so many doors, which meant it would lock the room from the outside. He had seconds to do this. If Azula caught on it was over. He would be the first in her body count today.

With a silent sigh to steel himself he looked to the door handle and aligned his hand so that he only had to reach out and slide it shut. He counted to three, feeling his heart beat counting much more than that with how hard it was pounding, and reached out. His hand squeezed the cool metal and he pulled as hard as he could to slide it closed. He heard a whistle and felt a breeze but focused on the task at hand. The door scraped loudly and in a second he had it closed and the key twisted with an echoing clack.

Not thinking too hard on it, Jet pulled out the wrench that Sokka had failed to retrieve from the cooler earlier and jammed it as hard as he could into the latch gap. Even if they had not heard the door shut, they would know now but that would have to be fine. He could not risk them having an easy time getting out. Twisting to make sure it was wedged in as tightly as possible he backed up slightly and looked into the window of the door to see eyes on him.

They were not what he was expecting. The face of a young woman was staring at him but it held no anger. She looked confused, curious even. She glanced from the door handle which did not so much as jiggle when she tugged and then back to him. Her gray eyes, rimmed red from recent tears, clearly asking the questions of ‘why’ and he glared back at her. Her hair was pulled back into the fire nation top pony and flowed down in the longest braid down her back that he had ever seen. He could not see where it ended, but he knew it was long. Ty Lee, he realized, had seen him but could do no more than look at him confused.

Jet quickly ran down the hall and away from the door. He had to get into position. He had a job. It did not matter that she had seen him, once he mixed with the others he would be just as hard to spot as any other prisoner. He only needed to make sure that he did not do anything else that could fuck up the plan. Getting to the control room he was shocked to find it empty. They must still be doing that weird performance or interrogating people thing. Whatever, fine by him. These fools could do anything if it meant an easier time for him to get off the cursed place.

Jet took stock of all the keys and buttons. Making sure he knew exactly which ones to work with and which to avoid. It was easy to determine now that he was here alone. He would have to be quick. Once the lever was pulled he needed to be out of here and ready to leave. If this failed, he was as good as dead… again.

Looking to the shadows and the counters to tell time he noted that he was right on schedule. He had only minutes to wait in the shadows of the room. They felt like hours. Each pop of the rooms counter candle seemed to drill a new hole into his psyche. He had known his mind had not been the same after that whole lake whatever.

He refused to even think the name. If he was in a really rough time he found his sense of self slipping. Would find that time just blinked by. On the really scary moments he would wake in areas he had no memory of going to. That was why he kept his gang here close. Some of them knew about the Dai Li and they were a great support for those bad times. It felt like someone else would take over and Jet himself was locked in the darkness until whoever had his body let go. He found that chewing wheat helped keep himself grounded, but only for so long.

The loud pop of the candle marking the start of an hour dragged Jet out of his thoughts and he shot out of the darkness. It was now or never. Part one was finally a go and he had mere moments to make sure parts two and three started. With a grunt of exertion he pulled the two heavy levers down and locked them into place. The release bell chimed loudly through the prison and Jet bolted from the room before anyone could round the corner and stop him. Jumping over a ledge, he slipped into the forming crowd and followed the movement towards the main courtyard.

With a final glance up to the control room he noted that there were now two guards in the room. Both were looking very confused and yelling, but neither could stop what was happening. Jet let out a laugh as he ran the rest of the way out towards the light of the courtyard and felt the hot air greet him. He let himself smile towards the sky and felt the joy of a job well started. This was his element, after all, fucking over Fire Nation.

Jet spun on his heel to scan the growing crowd for the Water Tribe Chieftain and saw Suki as she slipped towards the meeting area. Good, that was one. Jet made two, so he had only to locate a total of three more people. The Chieftain was easy to spot in a sea of Fire Nation. He really did stand out with that hair style of his. Jet watched as Suki darted forward and pulled the man to the area. That was three.

Jet jogged over to join them knowing that the other two in the group should know how to find them. They needed to start the next part pretty soon or the people around may just listen and go back to their cells on the grounds of a technical error or something.

“Jet, good work.” Hakoda said as he gripped Jet’s shoulder in greeting. Jet nodded to the man and grinned.

“Thanks, no problems here-”

“You’re bleeding!” Suki cut in. She was looking at his arm in concern.

Jet followed her look and found that he was, indeed, bleeding. He had a thin, but fairly deep, line cut across the top of his right forearm and blood was trickling down the arm. Drips were falling from his fingers and he had just assumed it to have been sweat. He had not even remembered being cut or running into anything. When… The door. The wind that he had felt must have actually been a blade. Azula had caught him but could do no more that cut him once.

“I guess I am. Might have been for a few minutes now.” Working fast he ripped a sleeve off of his shirt and tied it around the cut. It would have to be looked at later but he needed to end the blood trail now. He might have already left a good enough trail to lead a smart enough person right to them. “We need to move, now.”

“Why?” Sokka had slipped into their group as Jet worked on tying the cut on his forearm.

“I may have made a small trail right to us. Better start your riot.” Jet grunted as he tugged on the wrap to secure it tightly.

“Leave that to me!” Hakoda grinned. He turned, scanned the spread of people around them and decidedly walked right towards the biggest one he could see. He shoved the man hard and crossed his arms in a show of power.

The man, Jet realized, was one of the benders he had seen taking the ‘calming courses’ offered by some weird guards as a time management thing. He was not shocked at the fact that the man turned out to be the most sensitive and useless being on the planet for starting a riot and quickly scanned the area for a better hot head. Too bad they were out their resident hot head. This was the only use Jet could see him having and he had the nerve to be late.

“I knew ya lot would have a new idea. Count me in! What ya needin’?” Chit Sang snickered as he slipped surprisingly quietly into the mix of people. Not the resident hot head Jet was thinking about, but he would work just fine.

Sokka seemed to agree. He had spun around, grinned at the man like he was a friend, and pointed to the crowd. “We need a riot. You got any ideas how to get the biggest prison riot started?”

“Ya need a riot? That’s easy. Watch this.” And with that Chit Sang pushed past the group.

He walked into a mix of people and snagged one of the smallest people he could. Lifting the guy high above his head he started to toss the man slightly into the air and chant ‘Riot’ until the crowd around followed his chanting. There was a quiet second after and then madness as a full riot broke out. Benders were bending anything they could and nonbenders were giving their all into fist fights. Chit Sang, for his part, casually tossed the man into a group of people fighting and walked back to them dusting his hands with a grin.

“Well, that is one way to do it.” Hakoda remarked with a grin. He patted Chit Sang on the back and turned towards his son.

“Alright, riot started. Head count? Cool, we gotta go!” Jet motioned towards the gondola now open.

“We need the warden!” Sokka remarked, Jet inwardly cursed. He had forgotten that bit in this and looked around.

“We haven’t found Zuko yet, either.” Suki was looking around as well but it was clear her eyes were scanning for her little pet.

“He knew the time. We can’t wait.” Jet huffed.

“We can wait a moment more! The riot is not in full swing yet, anyway.” Sokka glared.

Jet rolled his eyes and looked again on the upper levels. “There! The warden is on the third floor lookout!”

“Shit, how will we get him from there…” Sokka whipped around to look and saw that Jet was right. He was on the same floor they had used to watch the unloading earlier that day.

“Right, you guys get this in motion and get the warden down. I will go find Zuko. If we are not back by the time you are ready to go, don’t wait. Just go.” Sokka glared at them and before any could retort he spun on his heel and ran towards the fighting crowd.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

How did we like our first time with Jet POV??  
Do we like? Not like? Want more? Never again?
