## Chapter 15

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

&#42;Tigger Warning: Mild mentions of past suicidal thoughts&#42;

### Text

Zuko’s shoulder was screaming as his arms were once again twisted behind him. The guard shoved him from the room and down the hall. He hissed out as she used the position to guide him towards the stairs. Dark spots were dancing at the edge of his vision as his body screamed out. He must have said or done something because the grip behind him loosened just enough for the tearing feeling in his shoulder and chest on the right side to lessen.

The guard shoved again and they continued through the hall to wherever she had been told to bring him. Zuko tried, he really did. He tried to memorize the way. To remember where they turned and twisted. To learn where they were going. He tried, but the spots that were dancing before must have done more than just dance. There was a few different times where he was suddenly in a new hall or the guard was near dragging him around a corner. He had fainted at least twice from pain alone and that did not make the situation any less terrifying.

The hall they entered looked deserted, but the guard took the moment of confusion to shove hard once more against Zuko’s back and slam him into the wall next to a door. He grunted but kept quiet. Last thing he needed was to spit fire and end up in some cooler. The door was ripped open and the guard shifted the force to shove Zuko into the room. He was unsteady and the sudden action mixed with the shooting pain rushing through his body made his knees buckle. He slammed into the chair in the middle of the room and almost thanked it for being the reason he did not fully fall to the ground.

“What is this about? I didn’t do anything wrong!” Zuko grunted out before turning to glare at the guard. She huffed and closed the door as if that was an answer and Zuko’s insides burned with frustration.

“Come on Zuko, we all know that’s a lie.”

The burning feeling was snuffed out instantly as Zuko froze to process the voice in the room with him. Slowly, he stood and used the chair to help balance as he looked toward the wall behind himself. He saw a person leaning against the wall. They were dressed in Fire Nation red, shorter than himself, arms crossed, and anger rolling off of them in waves. He could not, however, look the person in the face. Instead his eyes locked onto her shoes after falling before they ever reached her face.

“Mai. How did you know I was here?” Zuko almost whispered but in the small room he might as well as shouted with how his words echoed painfully in his own ears.

“Because I know you so well.” Her voice dragged out the ‘so’ and he flinched. She did not move.

“But how?” Zuko twitched, his eyes flicked up toward her face but he still could not look above her crossed arms. He watched as her fingers grip tightened and he let his gaze fall once more.

“The wardens my uncle, you idiot.” She snapped. It was quiet, but it still stabbed into him as if she had screamed into his ear. He could not admit that he had indeed forgotten this fact.

She sighed heavily when he made no move to comment. “Of course my uncle told me when he got you. He boasted to my family the moment you were unmasked. I had to message right that second to have this moment. Even if he is lame, he listened when I asked him to hold off on informing the Fire Lord about your capture to give me this time.”

“What do you mean?” Zuko finally looked at her face. He was right, she was beyond mad. Her look was that of steel and that meant that all other feelings she was having were being held at knife point deep within herself. Even if one thought Mai always looked like this, there were tells. For one, her jaw was set.

What had really caught Zuko’s attention was the idea that his father did not know he was here yet. Mai had said her uncle was giving them this time, which meant he had withheld information from the Fire Lord and that was serious. It also meant that there was a chance that he could get away before anything really got reported and that would be great.

“He is giving me this moment, and then requesting actions for your handling. You really are stupid, aren’t you?” Mai scoffed. She was pissed and her words were cutting.

“But… why?”

“He is family. That is kind of what family does-” she stopped short and shifted to lean back more firmly into the wall “well… what family should do.”

Zuko gave her a puzzled look but did not push. That made sense in a way. Not much, but some. If he thought about it then his uncle came to mind but that burned his heart so he snuffed out the thought and looked elsewhere. Katara had put aside things she had wanted just for the sake of her brother or Aang. Toph had fought with the whole group on Zuko’s behalf, Agni knows why. Even now, Sokka had gone on this doomed from the start scavenger hunt in the name of family. He supposed some people are just like that even if he did not understand it.

“You know he was banking on benefiting from our arrangement, just as much as the rest of my family. You leaving as you did didn’t just make me look suspicious. It hurt their position as well. He is hoping to use this as a chance to gain back his advantages.”

Zuko groaned out and let go of the chair. He let his head fall into his hands and sighed deeply. He had known that a royal arrangement meant a great deal to all members of the outer family, but he had not given a second thought as to the reaction they would have to his leaving. “The truth is, I really thought I knew you.” Her volume dropped and if it was not for the tight space he would have missed the rest. “I did know you once, but I guess I don’t know this you at all. All I get is a letter? Really?”

When Zuko did not so much as drop his hands Mai raised her voice to demand his attention. “You could’ve at least looked me in the eye when you ripped out my heart.”

“Mai, I didn’t mean any harm-” Zuko mumbled as he slumped into the seat. He let his hands fall and hung his head low to stare at his own feet. He had ruined her life by choosing to listen to Azula’s lies. He had hurt her. Had given her hope and ripped it away. Why does everything he did burn others?

“You didn’t mean to hurt me? Too bad.” He heard her move from the wall to instead slowly pace the room as the sound of paper being moved echoed and scraped. She cleared her throat and poorly mimicked his raspy voice while reading “Dear Mai, I’m sorry that you have to find out this way but I’m leaving.”

“Stop, this isn’t about you! This is about the Fire Nation.” Zuko snapped. He had twisted in the chair to look at her only to be met with the letter she had been reading to the face.

“Thanks, that makes me feel all better.” She bit out.

“Mai, I never wanted to hurt you. But I have to do this to save my country.” Zuko retorted as he threw the letter to the side to be forgotten.

“Save it? You’re betraying your country! You shot lightning at the Fire Lord, Zuko! We saw the lightning residue, don’t lie. I thought you were dead. I thought you had died trying to kill your father.”

“I’m alive?” He stated without thinking. He flinched as memories of Jet doing the same to Sokka flashed and he cursed Agni for how twisted he felt about being similar to that man. “Look, I’m sorry, I couldn’t risk telling you I was okay. They would have hurt you. Thought you were acting with me.”

“And just what are you doing? You are betraying the Fire Nation for what!? Nothing.” Her voice was raising. She was beyond upset and losing her control over the trapped emotions deep within.

“That’s not how I see it.” Zuko stated and tried to keep calm.

“Then how exactly do you see it, Zuko? Because from where I stand, I see a man who threw everything away for no reason at all and wouldn’t even try to work things out when everyone was there for you!” She was shaking lightly and it broke something deep within him. He had done this. He was still doing this.

“Mai, there was no fixing this. No fixing me. I thought what I had wanted was to go home, but home had never wanted me.”

“That doesn’t make sense! You know it doesn’t! We wanted you home, why could that not be enough?” She was shouting. He was struggling to keep calm and instead matched her energy.

“It has nothing to do with you! I thought I wanted to go back! To have what I had before I ever foolishly opened my mouth in that meeting! But I was wrong, Mai! I was wrong. I wanted to be accepted and loved for who I am but I didn’t know who that was! I know now, and it is not a person welcomed in that place. If I am to go home, I need to fix it first.”

“Fix what!? You are stuck here! We could have figured things out together if you would have just talked to me! Instead you just got angry, and then quiet, and whenever we were in the palace you felt like stone! Then you went and shot lightning-”

“I shot nothing at anyone.” He cut into her rant. It hurt too much to let her continue.

“Don’t. Don’t you learn how to lie to me now! Dammit Zuko we saw the marks and the Fire Lord said-”

“I can’t shoot lightning! I am not the perfect bender that Azula is! He shot lightning at me, I redirected it.” He matched her tearful glare with a burning one of his own. He needed her to understand.

Mai blinked in shock. She stood up straight and looked to the side and crossed her arms as if to hug herself loosely. “You… redirected lightning? You… can do that?”

“You thought I shot it, why is redirection so hard to believe?” He asked and move to sit up straighter.

“Zuko, shooting it is terrifying and powerful. It is like a finality. Nothing stands up to lightning. Nothing but you apparently. You… are a lucky moron.”

“I’ve never been lucky, you know this. Uncle taught me how after Azula shot him-”

Mai gasped and spun to look at him. “She shot General Iroh? But… why?”

“I don’t know. Maybe because we had her cornered and he was her biggest threat.”

“I stand corrected. You are all morons. You and that little group of toddlers. Zuko, you are telling me you retaliated against the Fire Lord on the grounds of self defense? This could be what saves your life-”

“Sort of… I did go to him during the eclipse with my swords to force him to listen for once.”

Mai groaned. “As much as I like swords, you have a death wish. Zuko, they are going to execute you for this. You do understand that right? Nothing I can do will help. Once my uncle sends the message, your fate is sealed. Bad enough you left and ruined everything with a note, then you had to go and get yourself caught…”

“Don’t worry. I wont be here long.” He wanted to comfort her. To do something right for her but everything he had done before was what had brought them to this point. He did not know what he could do to correct it or make it easier.

“No, you won’t.” She sent a sharp glare his way. The kind that always made him defensive. The one that reminded him that she was Azula’s friend before she knew him. “You will come home in a body bag because you could not just sit and be-”

“And be what, Mai!?” He jumped up and spun to fully face her. “Be my sisters play thing for life? Be this perfect little figure for the Fire Nation as I sat and burned away bits of me slowly? Be your little toy? Sit by and watch as the world is burned away by my people because of my family? I can’t do that anymore!” He squeezed his eyes shut as flashes of memories from his short time home sprang forward to fuel his fire. Moments of fear for speaking, of dread for what he was hearing, of false praise, and false warmth. He raised his arms over his face and forced them down quickly to release the energy rapidly building. He felt the flash of heat and knew in some way that fire had followed his movement weakly.

Mai twisted from her spot to block the flash of heat and glared at him. “Sit down, you idiot! You are a spark away from a meltdown and we both know it.”

Her voice was commanding and Zuko found himself slumping back into the chair again with a groan.

“Zuko. I knew I could never be enough. Could never be who you truly needed, but we were somewhat happy. We could have at least made the most out of the situation. You could have at least talked to me.”

“And what? Keep lying to ourselves? To each other?” He looked up at her and felt miserable for what he was about to say. He just could not stop himself once he started. It was like a floodgate had opened. “I could have done that before. When we were first matched and the arrangement papers were signed. When we were put together for everything from royal outings to private dinner parties just to make sure we looked the part. I could have burned away the thing I felt deep inside. I did for so many years. If nothing had changed I could have hid what makes me wrong and different in the eyes of the Fire Nation to make everyone happy. To make us work. I can’t anymore, I’m sorry.”

Swallowing hard, he let his look slide down to his hands in his lap. With a mournful sigh he let his face fall to meet them. He felt awful. He was cold, his inner flame was a mere flicker, his emotions were rung tight and his body hurt. He had said the thing he had meant to keep inside. He had finally admitted, even to himself, that he did not fit into his own nation and never had. That he had felt this divide far longer than just the Agni Kai. That he was different and wrong in the minds of his own people for more reasons than they even realized.

Slowly, he let his hands move. One settled into the hand print the forever marked him as unwanted by his people. The other hand moved to rub his neck where Sokka had held him so gently just an hour or so before. It was a conflicting feeling. One of pain and fluttering hope. The look in those blue eyes that had been both hesitant and full of want for something he was not saying. Zuko wondered just what he had wanted in that moment before his damn body had given out. Fucking Jet.

“I would never have asked you to cut that part of you out!” Mai cut into his minds wandering. “I can’t cut it out of me, either. You knew that! It is a part of who we are, but dammit Zuko, we could have worked it out! We could have talked about it! But no, you had to go and act without thinking and burn everything we worked so hard to build!”

“Mai, I’m sorry-”

“Are you? Are you really, Zuko. Look at me and tell me this was worth it. That being here right now on the wrong side was worth burning our future.”

“It was, because I’m going to fix our future. Mai, if I do this… if I fix everything that the Fire Nation has done then maybe… maybe we won’t have to feel wrong anymore.”

“Zuko, there is no fixing this. You are in prison. Worse yet, you are going to be on the list to be executed.”

Zuko’s head snapped up to look at her. “What? They wouldn’t-…”

“I’ve said it three times now, thanks for listening.” Mai raised an eyebrow at him and spoke with heavy sarcasm.

Zuko groaned and slumped back. So that was it. Either he escaped with Sokka today, or he dies within the week. Fitting really. When was anything he ever did not a life or death gamble. Funny thing, death was not the part that scared him the most. He has had thoughts of it before. Hard not to when you are burned so badly by your own father that you lose half your vision and hearing. Did you know that your ear affects balance? Zuko had not known, and had learned instantly just how bad his life was about to become. His balance was shot, his depth was shot, and he had feared his own element. As a young teenager with no healthy or known outputs for such traumas it was a wonder he was still here. He had been sent to rot on the seas on a fools mission and some parts of his did just that. Other parts of him, those that were loud at night or on really bad days, thought the ocean could look so welcoming at times. It had been a long time since those thoughts had drifted through him.

No, he did not fear death, he feared for those he would drag with him. For Suki and Sokka. If he let himself feel, he also feared for Jet’s safety though he would never admit this to anyone. He could hate people with his very being, he could not wish execution by fire on anyone.

Mai sighed heavily. “Zuko, when you came home with us I thought that was going to be us rebuilding what we could. Going back to before it all changed. But you came back to us different.”

“Years at sea, it does that.” Zuko muttered automatically without moving.

“No, it doesn’t do this.” Mai countered with a snort. “You came home to me, but you were no longer able to be mine as I was to be yours. To play that part we practiced so much before. To love one another. I love you, Zuko. I know you love me just like I love you. That used to be enough to use to play into our perfect roles even if it was not the same type of love needed for that.”

Zuko shifted to look towards her. She had lost the tension and now just looked tired and sad. She still had the harsh glare, but her posture was more relaxed.

“I came home on a lie.”

“No, you came home with a hole in your chest.”

Zuko flinched and looked to the wall again. He had known his hurt from Jet attacking him and hurting him both physically and emotionally had bled into his performance. That he was not able to play the same role as before but had hoped she had not noticed.

“News flash, you are an open book. I did notice. I did not ask, even though I watched you bleeding out of that hole in your chest when you thought you were safe to do so. I did not want to push you to tell me who did it. Who hurt you like that. You were home and adjusting, after all. Will you finally tell me who it was? I think I deserve to know.”

Zuko shifted uncomfortably. He wanted nothing more than for the earth to swallow him whole. She had seen that he had been hurt. Jet had hurt him bad enough that he had brought that pain home. He hated that man. Why would Jet not stop hurting him every chance he got and leave him alone already?

“Who was he?”

Zuko flinched hard and snarled. The image of Jet in any way was enough to spike his anger and he glanced towards the door as if he could send his glare straight to him. With his luck, Jet was on the other side laughing at this whole thing anyway. “Not worth it. Forget about it all, will you?”

“No. I don’t think I will. Who was he? He hurt you.”

“I hurt you, too! Focus on that.”

“I can focus on more than one thing at a time.”

“Fine, then hate me, dammit! Hate me enough to drop it and like that I was hurting.” He had not moved from sending his glare out the door.

Mai moved to speak and froze. Instead of yelling or saying whatever it was she had been about to she followed his glare towards the door. She stood confused before realization clicked.

“He’s here.” She stated as she used her peripheral to watch Zuko.

Zuko, for his part, gave it all away as his eyes widened and he looked once more to the door before glaring at the floor. Cursing under his breath he huffed out steam.

“Mai, leave it alone.”

Mai stood still and seemed to be thinking on what to do with the new information. For a moment Zuko let his insides relax as he thought she might just listen. He was wrong and knew it when and hand grabbed his shoulder and forced him to sit up straight so she could slam her fists on either end of the chair. Most likely she had meant to make him sit up and listen, but all he had done is cry out with the sudden pain and sat twitching as it subsided. If she had said anything he had missed it and her eyes were boring into him with pure concern.

“Zuko?”

“Forget it, nothing you did.” He hissed out and twisted in the chair.

“Who then?”

“No one-”

“Was it the same guy?” She cut in. Eyes hardening to steel with a glare that could cut someone with just a look.

“Mai, it’s nothing-"

“Name.”

“Mai, don’t… Please.” Zuko let his gaze drop. He felt so powerless. His right side was still pulsing with a dull but mind numbing pain and his fire was so weak. She had him trapped and she knew it.

“Zuko, give me his fucking name already. I waited patiently while you were home for you to tell me who cut your heart out and ruined what could have been our safe life. You will tell me now that you have cut out mine.”

“It’s not-”

“I finally get to see you after I had no idea if you were alive or not and I learn that same person physically hurt you. Did he always hurt you? Answer me.” Her words were sharp. Zuko opened his mouth to speak but found he had no answers for her. He looked towards the door instead and wished someone would come and end this.

“Either the next words you say are a name, or I pull out a list of all the prisoners who are in this place and read it until your look tells me everything I need to know. I know he is here, and you can’t hide shit.”

Zuko glared at her and tried to match her intensity. It was a battle not unlike a sword fight, only they were using sheer will. He was so tired. With a deep growl he spoke while flame flicked around his teeth. “Jet.”

Mai stared searching for a moment before she nodded and let the sides of the chair go. She leaned back and crossed her arms with a smirk. “Was that so hard?”

“Why do you even care?” He bit out.

“If you can’t figure out that bit at least, then I can’t be the one to tell you.”

“That’s not fair!” Zuko retorted much more like a child being told they would learn something when they are older. “Mai, you of all people know that I’m shit at understanding social things! I get it, I’m just- ahg!” He slumped back and scrubbed a hand through his hair, pulled slightly, and breathed out smoke. He had hardly realized his other hand had moved to dig at his scar until Mai’s hand ripped it away.

“Hey. Stop that. What are you doing? Do you want to make that worse?” She looked him over as he glanced everywhere but her and sighed out dramatically.

“Fine. You are right, I’m not being fair. Zuko. I’m mad at you, but that’s because I care for you and you hurt me. I get to be mad but that doesn’t mean I don’t get to still love you and I can’t cut a bitch who hurt you.”

“I don’t… I’m not… I was awful to you-” He was tired and he was struggling to find the words needed for this situation. They were off track and he had lost track of the conversation awhile ago.

“Yeah, you were. You were also hurting and changing and I chose to ignore that in favor of forcing things back to what they were. We both fucked up, but that doesn’t mean I can’t be pissed at your dumb ass for being dumb.”

Zuko huffed and tugged lightly to try and take his hand back. Mai glared at him in warning before releasing it. He could only guess that warning was against touching the scar again. Nothing else seemed to fit unless he had missed more than he knew.

“Zuko, you look like death.” Mai finally stated after a long and painful silence.

“Thought you liked that look?” Zuko tried to joke. Taking in her not amused eye roll he deemed it was a 50/50 on if it hit or not. He went back to picking at his thumb nail and wondering how much time he had lost in this room. Where were the others? Did the plan start? Would he miss it?

“When have you eaten last?” She asked quietly.

“I-” He stopped. When did he last eat? A few hours ago he had those gross dumpling pods. Some of them had tasted almost like a shoe and he really did not want to think about that. Realizing those were not what she had meant he chose to stay quiet. Whenever he had answers that would upset, it was always safest not to, after all.

“Slept?”

“I was napping before the guard took me here.” So much for avoiding bad answers.

“Zuko, you know what I meant.”

He flinched and picked at a piece of dry skin a bit too hard, causing it to bleed. Wiping it on his pants he sighed out “I don’t know.”

“Agni, Zuko. No wonder you are overstimulated and walking on coals. You moron.”

“I know what I’m doing-” He tried to counter weakly.

“Do you? I’d like to believe that even you are not this stupid. You wouldn’t be here without a reason. My uncle said you broke in here. That is a death wish and you know it.”

“I needed something and failed, okay?”

“No. You need nothing from this place. Don’t even try to say it that this ‘Jet’ was what you needed because I will cut off very important parts of his if I ever get the chance.”

“Why-”

She raised and eyebrow and reached out to grab at his shoulder. Zuko pulled away to guard himself and realized she had never actually intended to touch him. He cursed as he had just proven her point for her.

“So, who has your full trust, Zuko? Who are you willing to follow so blindly that you are self destructing for them? You know how this ends. Whoever they are, they are going to use you and leave you behind.”

“Sokka’s different! He wouldn’t-” Zuko snapped his mouth shut as he realized how much he had just given away. By the fiery glare Mai’s eyes took on, it was all she needed. “Shit, Mai, forget-”

“That water tribe boy? That means he is also here. Funny, didn’t hear that bit from uncle.” She turned to glare back out the door. “I’ve met him before. He would.”

“No. He won’t.” Zuko stated, he sounded much more sure of this than he felt.

“Zuko, he would use you to get what he needed and toss you out for being exactly what you are. Just like that Jet of yours. You can’t change who or what you are. You are a firebender and that is grounds enough for him to hate you. The fact that you are the Prince of the Fire Nation is just fuel to the fire. Add in your past and it is grounds for only you getting hurt in that blaze when he finally ignites it. You really know how to pick ‘em, don’t you.”

Zuko hopped up. He was ready to defend Sokka but her look was so confusing. She looked hurt, clearly, everything about this conversation had been so painful. It was a finality. They both knew they would not be anything more than… something. They just did not even know what that something was and Mai believed Zuko to be on death row. Add in Sokka and there was a new look mixed in that he just could not identify. It was like she hated Sokka without actually needing more reason than that Zuko was about to defend him.

They both jumped well loud alarms ripped through the tiny room and red lights began to flash in warning. Something was happening and Zuko had a pretty good guess as to what that was. Jet must have released the prisoners, which meant that plan C was a go after all. He had to get away, but how?

The door to the room was ripped open as a guard had rushed forward. It was the same female guard from before and Zuko moved to be more center in the room and away for Mai.

“My Lady! There has been a malfunction of equipment.”

“I can hear that” Mai rolled her eyes.

“I am here to protect you.”

“I don’t need protecting.” Mai bit out, clearly upset at the very idea that she was not strong enough to handle something like this.

“Trust me, she really doesn’t” Zuko couldn’t help but add with a slight smirk. Even if she was mad at him she had been right. He loved her like something close that he really could not put a name to. Maybe like how a sister should feel? Family? Whatever it was, it meant that he found this interaction very amusing and her annoyed glare only made him chuckle.

“Be that as it may, I have orders to protect and guard you.” The guard declared as she stepped a few paces into the room.

Seeing his opening before Mai did, Zuko struck. He rocketed forward and pulled the guard in and against the wall. Before she could counter the hold he backed up and shot fire that he knew would be either blocked or do no more than singe clothing. The guard reacted just as he has hoped for and pushed Mai into the corner with her body and became a bigger obstacle than she knew. With the guard so close and in the way, Mai had no chance to grab or throw her knives effectively.

Zuko quickly spun and left the room. He stopped just as he exited to turn and pull it closed. With a final twist he pulled the outer wall latch down and securely locked it. Only once it was locked tight did he look up to catch Mai’s hurt glare through the window. He knew she could not hear him, but he felt he had to say something.

“I’m sorry, Mai. Stay safe. I love you.” With that he spun on his heels and bolted down the hall as fast as he could.

The alarms blaring through the halls seemed to only make finding his way out harder. The walls echoed with the rings and screams from all angles. His head was pounding and the whole building felt like it could crumple inwards. He knew it would not, but the sounds, dim lighting, and overall pain and panic coursing through him made the walls feel like they were reaching out to slow his escape.

Crashes and the sizzles of fire mixed in suddenly as he rounded a corner towards the main courtyard and made knowing if anything was closing in on him impossible. He forced his eyes to stay open through the onslaught of it all and continued. Mai had been right, everything was far too much and he just needed to hold it together a little longer. Even his inner flame had threatened to douse itself with everything but he just needed to keep fighting. He would not give up.

Without slowing down, he pushed forward. Towards Sokka. That was the thought that had helped him keep it together the few times he had been so close to a meltdown here in the prison. Sokka had kept his word. Had trusted him. Had actually listened to him. Had tried to kiss him? No. That cannot be right. That was not what had happened.

Sokka had been worried and overworked. He was far more physical a person than Zuko and liked to be close to people. Zuko could hope that earlier just meant that maybe he finally had something like a friend. Sokka hugged his friends, hung off of peoples shoulders, and he kissed Suki.

Zuko had seen that through the door when they first got to her cell. He had seen the way they clung to each other and felt the way a rock had formed in his gut. A protection and a finality. He had not even realized it at the time, but he knew it now. Best to not let this go any further. He had told Mai that Sokka was different and he believed that. But, he was also aware that Sokka liked Suki. Even if that did nothing to stop Zuko’s stupid heart from falling hard for anyone with dark hair, a strong build, and a sword. He really needed to extinguish this or Mai would be right, he was only doomed to hurt himself.

Sokka had also said he would stay behind if he had to wait for him. Agni, Zuko hoped he was not dumb enough to follow through on that. With his luck Zuko was bound to miss the meeting time and by the sounds he already had. If anything, let the others get away. No one else should have to die here with him.

He burst through the final doorway and skidded to a stop. Firebenders were not great with light changes at the best of times and he was exhausted, running on fumes, and working with limited vision to begin with. Squinting in the sudden change of light from the bleak halls of the prison to the bright and hazy air around the courtyard he tried to make sense of things. There seemed to be a massive smoke cloud that had settled on the courtyard and was obscuring everything. The smoke stung his eyes and Zuko blinked rapidly before looking over the cloud towards the sky for relief from the burn.

His eyes were watering and the haze muffled the light from the clouded sky just enough that he almost thought he saw a figure floating just above them. Blinking hard to clear the vision he noted that he could not make the figure look anything more than a humanoid shape of a pure golden shimmering mass hovering just above the prison yard. If he focused hard on the image his eyes were rewarded with the familiar burn of one staring into the sun, which forced his right eye closed and through his scarred vision it looked like whatever that was had moved to be reaching towards himself. Zuko blinked again only to see he was indeed staring into the sun as it had broken through the clouds and a single strand was casting straight at the spot he stood rooted.

His skin, which he had not paid attention to the feel of, suddenly felt as if he had been plunged back into the poles and was just being pulled out to greet the arctic air despite being in a volcano. He shivered hard and watched as the strand of sunlight cut through the haze to send heat and energy that easily broke through the cold barrier of his frozen outer layers and left him feeling both weak and warm. It was as if the light had melted some unknown block around him and was reaching into his very core and cradling the tiny ember clinging to life within him. Like a hand was pulling it up more and a warm essence was feeding it. It was not enough to ignite his fire into the usual steady blaze, but something that would last through whatever they needed before burning out. It was unlike any other time he had presented himself to the sun and it made his knees shake with the effort of standing.

Squinting against the light and through the haze above the courtyard he tried hard to see the figure he thought he had seen more clearly. The harder he tried, the less he saw and the more it just felt like he was staring into the sun like a fool. He was a fool, he had believed a figure of pure light had guided the light of the sun to himself like someone worthy. It was something Uncle would have believed, which meant it made little sense. No way Agni, if they were anything more than a story, would extend that kind of energy for someone like him. Someone lucky to just have been born and whom Agni had ignored for so long, anyway. It was just his scarred eye playing tricks of vision again, unable to see more than distinct shapes and filling in the blanks incorrectly. He was overtired and fishing for anything to believe.

A quick blink and whatever had been above the courtyard was fully gone. The sun had once again been swallowed by the dense clouds that spoke of rain. Instead, what he now saw was the courtyard which was pure insanity. He remembered that a part of the plan had been a riot, but he had had no real understanding as to what that might look like. He had squashed a few scuffles on his ship over the years and had thought that maybe this would be similar. That could not be further than the truth. People were, in lack of better terms, trying to kill one another for nothing more than the freedom to do so.

Fire and physical fighting styles ranging from disciplined forms, to freestyle, to things that really could not be classified as actual fighting were mixing everywhere. The noise of it all was the next thing to break through and he felt frozen where he stood. It was horrifying. The screams of anger, pain, and everything under the sun were slamming into him like waves. The sight of so much fire brought back flashes of a figure towering over himself. Of words he could no longer hear over the cheers. Of a hand, so warm and gentle, of pain-

“Sunshine!!” A voice that was very much not his fathers came out of the figure.

No, that wasn’t right. His father never called him that. He was also not on his knees, those were shaking but very much holding him upright. His father was not here. He blinked rapidly and shook his head to clear away the vision and ignored the burning sensation it always brought as he moved to scan for the sound. He did not have to look long.

“Su-oof!” Was all he managed as said warrior barreled into his chest at full speed. Her arms latched around his middle and he was very sure that his legs had finally given out at the impact despite not falling. She was the thing holding them up even if she looked to be leaning onto him.

“Zuko, buddy! Thank everything you made it! I was about to charge in and find you myself.” A hand gripped his left shoulder and squeezed.

Zuko looked over to see Sokka staring at him with intense worry. He swallowed down whatever he was going to say and nodded. Zuko knew he always looked a mess after a vision like he had just had and nothing he said now was going to help them. They needed to move or they would all never make it.

“Great, good, wonderful! We have everyone. Now lets move!” Jet barked out as he swaggered forward towards the huddle. Suki slowly let go, giving Zuko the time to make sure he could stand without her, and slipped out of the middle to give the others room. “Right. Okay, now all we have to do is grabbed the warden and get to the gondola.” Sokka looked up the wall towards something that Zuko could only guess was the warden. He still had a tight hold on Zuko’s shoulder and that was making everything a lot more manageable at the moment.

“How are we going to do that?” Zuko asked. He looked to Sokka expectantly and was startled at the hesitant look he got back.

“Yeah, that’s… I’m actually not sure.” Sokka released his hold on Zuko to rub the back of his own neck in a guilty manner.

Zuko blinked a few times to settle the angry crackles and pops that had sprung up in his mind. He let out a frustrated growl and threw his hands up and twisted to look towards the sun. His right hand dragged through his hair and pulled as the left rubbed at his scarred eye hard enough to see colors. He ignored the pain in his shoulder at the movement and took a deep breath, letting out steam.

He is the one who is supposed to not think. Sokka always thinks! Where Zuko’s brain is crackling with feelings over actual thoughts that are too muffled for him to understand, Sokka’s is a constant storm of thoughts and ideas.

“I thought you thought this through!” Zuko finally let out, still pushing on his scar to ground his anger.

“Hey, you are the one who said I don’t always have to think everything through!” Sokka retorted as he gripped Zuko’s wrist to pull the hand down and away from his eye.

“Maybe not everything, but I don’t know if you know, this is kind of important!” He let Sokka guide his hand away only to fix him with a glare. Twisting, he pulled his wrist free and gripped his right shoulder just enough to feel the sting.

Sokka left his hand hovering just a foot away from him as his face twisted from worry to anxiety. Zuko watched that anxiety quickly engulf Sokka as he pulled his hand away and fidgeted with his sword strap. His eyes had started to dart around, searching desperately for anything that could be used as a fix to the situation all because Zuko had to go and snap at him for something even the adults did not have an answer for. Guilt settled heavy in his stomach as Sokka’s shoulders began to droop with the weight of the situation resting solely on him.

That was not the intention. As much as Zuko wanted and needed Sokka to have answers, it was unfair of him to push all that expectation onto a single person. He was the Fire Nation here. He was the one who knew Fire Nation protacall for things and had a good feeling that the warden was on the upper levels. If anyone should know what to do to get him down, it should be himself. He did not, and that was causing Sokka to bend under the pressure of it all. He wanted nothing more than to take that worry and pain and to put it onto his own back. Sokka did not deserve this. He had never done anything to deserve it, unlike Zuko. Reaching out slowly, Zuko tried to get Sokka’s attention again if only to apologize. His hand never made contact as a sudden sting causes him to pull back and away from Sokka.

“Well, you don’t have a plan either!” Jet bit out, using the hand that had just slapped Zuko’s away to point an accusatory finger at him.

Zuko snarled. “No, why would I? I just spent the last hour or so getting interrogated by my ex-girlfriend about leaving the Fire Nation!”

“Ex? Wait, Mai is here!?” Sokka snapped out of his anxiety spiral and looked over Zuko in shock. He shuffled to stand closer to him and nudged his shoulder into Zuko’s side as he looked into the doorway behind him. Most likely, he was expecting to see Mai charging down the hall, knives out and ready to pin them all to where they stood.

Zuko leaned against Sokka’s shoulder just enough to get his attention and nodded. His voice was a softer tone than he was expecting as he stated “yeah, but don’t worry. I locked her in the cell.”

“Seems like we had the same idea.” Jet huffed but was unheard over the growing screams as the fights around them grew.

“I’m not sure that will stop her for long, but I am also not totally worried about your knife woman. It’s more the company she usually keeps.” His tone match Zuko’s as he looked from his dad to down the empty hall again. Panic shot through Zuko’s entire nervous system at the thought of his sister being here but he shook it off. If she was here she would have made herself known. She loved a show, and Mai cussing him out would have been one she would have loved to have seen. His pain was her pleasure. This meant that either she was busy elsewhere, unlikely, or she was not here…yet. His money was on the second.

“Won’t matter if we don’t figure out a way to get that dickhead down here.” Jet huffed louder and looked out towards the gondola.

Zuko twisted to Sokka and nodded. “Hey, if… I have an idea but you won’t like it.”

“No.” Sokka turned to look towards the crowd, effectively turning his back on Zuko and raising the burning anger once again.

“Why not!?” He snarled out.

“Because, you said I won’t like it so I won’t hear it. There is another way. There has to be.” Sokka shot a glare over his shoulder before turning back to look over the crowd.

“Son, it is best to hear all ideas. Even if they are unfavorable.” Hakoda moved to be near Sokka and Zuko stiffened reflexively. He did not trust this man, but he could not say why.

“No, dad. You don’t know him like I do. I won’t hear it.” Sokka set his glare onto his father and Zuko’s blood pressure soared. What was he doing!? If the guards did not kill them all, then Hakoda would.

“No, I would actually love to hear the fire fuckers idea.” Jet snipped in. “Not like you got any.”

“Jet. Drop it.” Sokka growled out.

“Look. You all need to get off this place. I could… I can bend. I could be your blockade as the gondola leaves and you all-” Zuko rambled, he needed them to get away. Mai was right, if they stayed they were all going to die and it would be his fault.

“Zuko, stop.” Sokka cut in. He had turned to face him and his look showed he would hear no more. “I said we would not leave you. I meant it. If you stay, then so do I.”

“But-”

“Zuko, please.” Sokka’s voice fell into a plea and he moved to be at his side again. “Please. For once. Think about yourself and your safety?”

“When does he ever not?” Jet snorted with a dramatic eye roll.

“Hate to cut into… no actually I don’t. Ya lot have a lot to work through, yeah?” Chit Sang cut in. He looked fairly amused even if he sounded annoyed. “Better than training camp drama with ye, honestly. But I think your girlfriend’s taking care of things for us, no thanks to ye loud lot.” He motioned out above the crowd to where Suki had bolted away without anyone noticing.

She was currently charging through the crowd at a high speed and used the fighting masses as a stepping stool. Once above the crowd she began to hop through the fighting groups, using heads as if they were stepping stones to cross a river with ease. She made crossing the chaotic courtyard look as simple as jogging through a calm forest. Once she made it to the ledge she kicked off the tallest mans head and vaulted herself up to the second floor.

With the ease of a warrior she slipped onto the deck and kicked, flipped, blocked, and tossed all three guards who had charged her right over the ledge. They fell into the brawl below and were easily swallowed by the chaos that was the riot. Not even glancing at those below Suki had already spun on her heel and was using the rivets and dents in the sun warped metal like a tree. With scary speed, she scurried up the wall and was making her way easily to the third floor before any on the deck knew what was happening.

A whistle brought Zuko and the others out of their shocked thoughts. Jet, who had been the culprit of the noise let out an amused “that’s a heck of a woman.”

Sokka huffed. “Don’t even think about it, Jet. Let’s go. We should give her back-up.”

With that the group all turned to follow her lead. Instead of taking the spider monkey style, they opted for the stairs and ran to meet her on the third floor. They were met with the sight of Suki, glowing despite the low light as rain began to fall steadily, and the warden hogtied on the floor under her foot.

“So, what’s after part four?” She laughed.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

## Chapter 16

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

PLEASE mind the tags!

Massive cliff hanger on this one. If you are not okay with that I HIGHLY suggest waiting for the next chapter before reading.

### Text

Sokka stood stunned for a moment to just take in the sight in front of him. Suki was grinning as if she had just won the best prize, and he kind of had to agree that she had. Under her foot with a rope tying his hands together and something stuffed into his mouth was a very angry warden. The rain did little to dampen the giddy feeling this brought. Plan C was actually going well. That was, until the warden moved.

The problem with firebenders over other types of bending, unless you included air but that was limited to a single bender, was that they did not need fire present to shoot it. In the height of all the excitement it would seem they had forgotten this small detail. At least, Sokka had forgotten. That was why he was shocked at being ripped backwards as he went to move to help Suki pull the warden up.

Fire flashed in front of him close enough to feel the intense and sudden heat of it but no sting of contact. Instead, he felt a familiar form wrap around him in time to, from what Sokka’s mind could understand, take the hit for him just before he was shoved back and into his fathers familiar and strong hold. His arms had come up automatically at the flash of orange to shield his face so he missed the scuffle that followed.

Forcing his arms away from his own face Sokka looked again in panic, hoping everyone was fine. He took in Suki who had backed away from the man on the ground and was looking back at him with worry. She looked alright, startled but not burned.

Sokka turned his attention to the cause of the fire and saw that the warden was fully pinned to the ground. Chit Sang had his foot on the mans back to hold him in place and Zuko was on his legs and binding them in a way that looked particularly uncomfortable. He was twisting the mans feet towards each other and using the rope provided as a belt to bind them as best he could. Once he had finished he sat back on the mans legs and hissed out something Sokka could not understand. Whether that was because of the panic still ringing in his ears or because of a language thing he was not sure. Either way when he caught Zuko’s attention the firebender simply nodded like nothing had happened.

He needed to make sure. Zuko had put himself between Sokka and fire and he needed to see that Zuko had not been burned. He would toss the man into the lake himself if that was the case. Breaking free from his, most likely confused, fathers hold he jumped forward and fell to his knees in front of Zuko. Zuko looked a mixture of startled, worried, and confused. That was fine. Let him be all that and more as long as he was not burned because Sokka forgot a basic rule about firebenders.

“Sokka, are you alright?” Zuko asked.

“I’m fine. Are you?” Sokka was looking him over but could see no signs of anything even being singed.

“What? Fine. Why would I not be?”

“Sunshine, you just charged into direct fire. You shoved us both away.” Suki had moved to his other side with what Sokka could only imagine was a mirrored look of concern.

Zuko looked between them, clearly even more confused than before as he shifted uncomfortably with the attention and coughed out “Firebender. We don’t burn easy and he couldn’t put his true strength into that kick. That was nothing hard to defuse.”

Suki nodded and helped him stand as Sokka sat. Right, Zuko was a bender. He was a firebender. If anyone could charge fire and be fine it was him. If his heart would catch up with his logical brain then they would be good.

“Great, bender secured and dumb ass checked up on. Can we PLEASE go now!?” Jet was shifting his weight between his legs and looked close to bolting.

“Kids got a point. Time to catch a ride. Sorry, not sorry, warden. But yer our ticket outta here. Meet ya down there. Don’t be late.” Chit Sang remarked as he dragged the man up off the floor and threw him over his shoulder. With that, he jumped over the ledge and onto the second floor platform with the idea to continue his path all the way down and most likely charge the gondola.

“Right, we gotta move. Everyone, follow him!” Sokka motioned out towards the ledge and watched as Jet wasted zero time vaulting over and dropping out of sight. The splash of his feet hitting the second floor as the rain picked up reminded Sokka that this might get slick as he moved to also jump. His father went to the ledge and was about to follow when something caught his attention and he motioned for Sokka to look.

Suki had Zuko pinned to the wall and they seemed to be holding the worlds quietest argument. That, in itself, was very odd. Everything about them had screamed a strong bond had been forming. Even when she had been iffy about him she had never attacked and Suki was not one to jump on an ally for no reason.

Shit, what did he do!? They had been just fine literally a second ago! Sokka slipped off the rail and rushed towards the pair to hopefully defuse whatever situation had sprung up so close to them leaving.

“Hey, hey, whoa! Suki, what’s wrong?” Sokka rushed to her side and looked between them. Zuko was pale, a bit shaky, and pissed so that checked out to be no real change since the cooler. Suki, however, was matching his anger and that was the concern.

“You are NOT jumping over that railing!” She snarled. “Quit being absolutely ridiculous and get on my back! I’ll get us down.”

“No, I’m too big for that. I’ll-”

“What? Squish me? I’m not a bug, Sunshine. Now stuff your pride and let me get you down from here. We don’t have time!”

Sokka looked between them again and realized the problem. Chit Sang had led by example and taken the warden over the ledge, dropping his way down. They were following and Zuko would follow that lead. They had not even paused to consider that one of their members was seemingly always swaying or shaking lightly with the effort to stand even if he remained silent. Suki had, and she had stopped said person from potentially hurting himself further. Said person was also a stubborn mule ox and was not accepting the help. Easy fix.

Sokka waved for his father to come over. “Hey, guys. We really don’t have time but I agree with Suki on this. Zuko, if it wasn’t raining I’d agree you can do this. Add the rain and your fatigue and I really think you should let us get you down quick.”

“Shut up, we are wasting time as it is! Just, let me down! We could have all been down by now!” He snarled back.

“Son, he is right that this is taking time.” Hakoda stated as he hurried over. “What is the hold up?”

“Sunshine here thinks his wobbly kitten legs can take the impacts of jumping right now. I disagree. While I know he is strong normally, he won’t accept that the last 48 hours has been filled with constant torture and won’t let me carry him down.” Suki huffed, keeping her glare of will power locked with Zuko’s.

“Ah, well. Easy solution. I shall carry the prince down and we can continue.” Hakoda stated while reaching towards the teen pinned to the wall.

Sokka watched as his father quickly removed his hand as the boy went from glaring to sheer terror. Zuko had looked towards the hand and paled to a deathly white. His body had sagged away as much as possible from where he was pinned and he quickly bowed his head in some kind of defeat.

What had just happened? He had been so defiant a moment ago, most likely out of some kind of pride or embarrassment, and the moment his father looked at him he crumpled. Something was still very much going on there and he would find out what after they got off this island.

“I’m sorry-” Zuko choked out.

“Shush, just. Grab on.” Suki cut in and sent her glare towards Hakoda before releasing Zuko and turning her back to him. Shockingly, Zuko did just that. He grabbed around her neck gently, gripping her shoulder for support as his right arm tucked between them. He let his face hide into her shoulder as he slumped forward.

“You need to trust me and give me your weight. Trust me, Sunshine, you are not as heavy as a fully dolled up Kyoshi warrior.”

Sokka stood and watched as she shifted and stood to fully pull Zuko up and onto her back, hands under his thighs for support as she guided him to wrap them around her middle. With the ease of someone lifting their normally a bit heavy camping pack she moved to grip the ledge and whispered something Sokka could not hear over the admittedly concerning storm that was growing. Within a moment she was up and over the ledge and they were both gone and out of sight leaving Sokka and his father to stare at the spot they had been.

“Got to agree with Jet, that is one heck of a woman.” Hakoda chuckled as he too followed the admittedly more dangerous and stupid way down.

Sokka agreed and followed up and over the ledge. When his feet hit the second floor he slipped enough to have to use the ledge to prevent himself from falling. If they had taken the stairs, they ran the risk of guards. Even with that little time delay of pride versus needed support they were still making it down far faster than the stairs. Still, this did not make rainy free falls any less dumb.

Suki had done this with Zuko on her back and Sokka felt like a fish out of water. It was weird, he was used to landing on slick ice that was either hidden or partially in the water. Wet metal was a whole different kind of slippery and he stumbled to his knees on the courtyard after the final drop.

A warm hand gripped his shoulder and helped pull him to his feet. Sokka followed the pull and let Zuko drag him forward. Suki was waiting a few feet ahead with his father and they wasted no time running through the crowd of screaming people. What had felt like hours had most likely been a total of five minutes, ten at the most. They would have another ten if they were lucky before the riot was snuffed out and they had to move.

Dodging the fire and people fighting anything that moved at this point, they broke through the main riot and bolted towards the gondola and a group of stunned guards. Sokka felt the hand on his shoulder tighten and pull to slow him down and he listened. These were Fire Nation soldiers and most likely firebenders. If anyone knew how to navigate charging them it would be Zuko and Chit Sang.

Chit Sang and Jet were already well on there way to the gondola. Jet was locked in combat but the guards around the entrance to their escape way seemed very hesitant and confused. They did not know what to do when faced with the warden being a potential target which was exactly what they needed. That hesitation was their ticket out.

Sokka was forced to redirect his attention back to the path at hand when a familiar sizzle and flash caught his eye. The guards around them had little hesitations about attacking. Zuko also had zero hesitation and he pulled Sokka back behind himself to stick his hands into the fire and with a quick shift in stance he ripped it into two harmless wisps. His eyes burned with focus and the gold in them seemed to be dancing along with the fire.

Lightning flashed above them and Sokka felt the moment the rain changed to being an angry storm. This was bad, the storm would help the guards stop the riot and delay their escape. They did not have time to allow the lightning to pass over and were going to have to risk being a giant metal target over a lake that had a higher heat base. Who’s bright idea was this place, anyway!?

Zuko moved through basic steps that Sokka had begun to recognize through the princes practice with Aang. The fire around them died on contact whenever it touched the fire prince and his own fire seemed to burn a brighter gold than the angry red flames he cut down. It was incredible. The guards shouted, grunted, kicked, and punched to send fire towards them. Zuko simply pushed, twisted, and danced silently with such focus as he cut a path forward through it all.

Sokka and Suki stuck close to his back as Zuko worked to move them forward. He was primarily focusing his energy on breaking through fire thrown at him and kept his own fire to a minimum. Whenever they saw an opening Sokka and Suki quickly joined in the battle to try and take out one of the guards. Together, they seamlessly took down at least four guards and joined with the others against the final group blocking the gondola.

“Zuko, how you holding up?” Sokka moved to be behind said bender as he ripped a fireball in half.

“What do you need?” Zuko grunted out. Eyes locked on the two guards blocking their path to the doors to the gondola.

“Suki, get with the others and get everyone onto the gondola. Zuko, I need you to cover me so I can start it.” With a nod Suki broke off and joined Jet to help clear the path. Sokka needed to get to the lever and activate the machine. If he could not get it moving then they were just sitting turtleducks.

Zuko glanced towards the lever in question and turned to raise his arms above his head in a way that reminded Sokka of when Katara pulled water into a wave. Instead of the water around them moving, fire flickered to life from his fingers and as he pulled up a unique wave of glowing warm and near white fire that sizzled in the pouring rain formed. Zuko stepped forwards and pushed in the same waterbending way Katara used as the wave of fire followed and rapidly rushed out and expanded. It pushed all in the way of the lever to the sides as they were unable to block or cut such odd fire and were instead forced to avoid it and opened the path completely.

Sokka wasted no time running around Zuko to dash towards the lever. He slammed his entire body into it and gripped hard. Trying to use his momentum, he twisted to hang off of it but the water and rain made the grip slippery. He tried a new angle, bearing his weight down on it and growled as he tried to make the lever budge. To his horror, it would not. Trying and vocally begging it to move Sokka felt more than saw the waves of heat as Zuko continued to block him.

Suddenly, a weight crashed into him and he gasped out. Hands were over his, adding pressure to the lever and pushing down on both Sokka and it.

“Come on! We can do this!” Jet grunted out and tried to put his weight into moving.

Sokka twisted and slipped on the wet metal. The momentum of him falling while Jet pushed into the lever broke whatever had kept it locked into place and it screamed as it dragged down. Jet moved quickly as soon as the lever clicked into the active position and shoved a staff he most likely stole from a guard deep into the mechanism. After sparks flew he grabbed Sokka’s sword strap to drag him up and towards the gondola. He did not slow even as Sokka stumbled and slipped.

“W-Wait! Jet-” Sokka twisted to look back and scream, “Zuko, come on!”

Zuko glanced towards them and pivoted to run towards the gondola. This was working. They were doing it. They were going to make it, even if the only reason Sokka was making it was because Jet was dragging him. Jet shoved Sokka up past the small gap that had begun to form between the gondola and the platform before hopping up himself.

Sokka twisted to watch Zuko being forced to stop the run towards them as more guards joined the fight. His heart dropped and he screamed out for him to keep running while he watched. Zuko instead turned towards the guards and mimicked Aang by pulling in as much air as he could as he squared his stance.

“Sunshine! What are you doing!? Run- Whoa” Suki yelled before being stunned silent. Sokka could not blame her.

“Spirits, he is a dragon.” Chit Sang muttered and Hakoda hummed in agreement.

Zuko, for his part, had just turned towards the guards and breathed out an insane amount of fire. It looked like a dragon had just roared and reminded Sokka just how powerful the prince really was. How that energy was only his core will power at this point after everything. He was running on fumes, but he still just roared out a river of fire that had colors he had never seen mixed in.

As soon as the fire stopped pouring from the prince he once again pivoted and charged towards the now moving gondola. Sokka scrambled to kneel and reached as far out as he could. He called for Zuko to hurry. He watched as he ran through the rain towards them. As lightning flashed in the background. As he felt Zuko’s hand grace his own and felt the swell of relief for a moment just to have it burn away in a flash.

Deep red fire sliced where their hands had met and force them to pull back before they could get a hold on each other. Zuko skidded to a stop at the base of the platform and turned to meet the guard who had prevented him from joining the gondola. Sokka felt his stomach drop as he realized just who was standing behind the prince. Ruon.

“Zuko!!” Sokka screamed as Jet dragged him away from the open door to the gondola, preventing him from launching back towards the courtyard like he had tried to do. Sokka twisted and pushed at the thin arms that felt like hot metal digging into him. “Jet, let me go!”

“No! Forget it! You won’t make that jump, Sokka!” Jet bit out as he motioned for someone to close the door.

Zuko was still on the platform. He was still in the prison. All Sokka could see once the door was shut was the flashes of color from the fire fight below. All he could hear was the pounding rain. All he could feel was dread.

Suki suddenly gasped and rushed to the side window. “No! Sunshine don’t!”

Sokka stomped down hard on Jet’s foot to force him to let go. Once free he rush to the window with Suki and saw what made his heart rate skyrocket. Zuko was charging the edge. He was going to jump. He was insane but spirits Sokka wanted nothing more for him to be in the gondola with him.

It happened so fast. Zuko had jumped. Sokka leaned as far out of the window as he could and reached. He stretched till it hurt and watched as Zuko closed the distance rapidly. He was going to make it. Sokka would not let him fall. He would not fail his heart again.

A thin strip of warmth snaked across his chest but he paid no attention to the feeling. He had locked eyes with Zuko and they knew they had each other. That was, until Zuko’s eyes widened just as a harsh pull ripped Sokka back into the gondola and away from the window. The warmth was suddenly a mass and Sokka realized someone in the gondola had pulled him back in and away. Someone had taken away Zuko’s only way to survive.

Sokka saw it in Zuko’s eyes. Saw the hurt and recognition. Saw as the image of Yue flashed and matched the look Zuko wore as he disappeared from his sight.

He had failed. He had come here and made yet another pay the price of his actions. His heart shattered as he heard Suki scream and reach out in his place in desperation. Sokka felt his earth lose color, his mind went numb, and he screamed.

## Chapter 17

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

For those who went ahead and read the last chapter, sorry not sorry but I loved all the comments to the moon and back! It makes me so giddy to see people following this.

To those who waited, I hope you like them both!

### Text

“Zuko, how you holding up?” Sokka moved up to stand back to back with him as he yelled over the sounds that surrounded them.

Zuko took a moment to catch the words and knew that this was not actually a question of if he was alright. He was, in fact, far from alright. The fire was a mild irritation but his overall feeling of energy was rapidly depleting. His inner fire was flickering and threatening to not reignite with every forced usage. His shoulder was aching with every movement, forcing him to rely heavily on his blind side to manage incoming attacks. No, he was not holding up well, but that was not the real question being ask was it?

“What do you need?” Zuko grunted as he plunged his left fist deep into incoming fire and threw it up and over them. He glance back to see Sokka busy barking orders to others but all he could hear was the sizzle of fire falling apart and evaporating in the heavy rain.

“I need you to cover me-” Sokka was talking to him now but the fire that Zuko had pushed up was hissing too loud for him to catch just what was needed. He looked back again and caught that Sokka had locked a calculating look elsewhere. Following the look he realized that it was on the gondola’s lever and what was really needed clicked. Zuko nodded while he counted the three guards that were positioned just spread out enough to make that a tricky path to take. Of course this would not be that easy.

They needed to move and move fast. Suki was helping the others get into the gondola but if they did not get it moving then they were sitting kindle. Chit Sang could help break and block fire but not produce it. That little benefit only worked for so long. Firebenders don’t burn easy, but they will burn given enough power or time.

Shit, he needed to get Sokka safely to that lever, but how? The guards were spread too far to hit quickly and his own fire was dancing on low. He was burning through the energy gifted by the sun far faster than he had thought and needed to be more calculating. He needed to think. Be anyone but ‘Zuko’ right now. What did Katara do when she pulled water into that one wave?

Zuko exhaled deeply before he slipped his stance into what he vaguely remembered Katara used. Knees bent slightly, right foot slid forward, and hands open and low. On his inhale he felt the air for any lingering heat and inwardly pulled while he let his own fire travel through his fingers to help ignite it. Sliding his right foot back as he pulled up through the inhale he was shocked and almost faltered the next step.

Not only did the static in the air from the storm happily ignite with his guidance, but he actually seemed to pull an admittedly tiny wave by Katara’s standards. The fire was hot, white, and crackled with excited energy almost too much to control. Zuko pulled hard on the fire and threw his weight forward to push it out and away before he lost his hold and blinked in surprise. The white, static fueled fire exploded outward and ignited the lingering static and embers still floating in the air from past attacks. The small wave that he had pushed rapidly grew and rolled into a powerful one. The wave of searing hot fire hissed and roared as it forced all in its way to the side.

Before Zuko could process what had just seemingly come from himself, Sokka had bolted around him and charged the lever. Right, job to do.

Following just close enough to guard Sokka’s back, Zuko pivoted and punched out a warning shot that was deflected easily. He felt his inner fire dim slightly like a candle under a breeze and knew he had to be sparing with his own attacks. That stunt had taken more than he had originally hoped. Thankfully, the guards were staying a safe distance while sending fire forward after the wave. He had to admit, he would be freaking out about that one later as well. If someone had done that to him, he too would keep a good distance.

The fire around him sizzled as the rain started to whip around with the wind. A large blast connected with his outstretched hand and was almost lukewarm as his fingers dug into the center to pull it apart. The angry red color flashed around him and smelt of scorched metal. He kept sending blast after blast into the ground as he waited for Sokka. It was taking far too long to start the gondola but if Zuko moved they would both be cooked.

Glancing back he saw just how much Sokka was struggling to move the lever and his panic spiked. Not good. They were sitting turtleducks like this and Zuko could not move to help. Desperate, he looked towards the gondola and saw that there was only one yet to board.

Jet. He was currently pushing against one of the guards that was trying to rip them off the platform. As he watched, Jet twisted in the hold and flipped the guard into the ground while the staff that had been against Jet’s neck clattered away. He hated this. Everything about it. The simple idea made his stomach boil, but they needed help.

“Jet! Sokka-” Zuko yelled out and was cut off as a fire whip cracked against his forearms, sending him sliding back and onto his side. He kicked out a small amount of flames in retaliation as he quickly scrambled to his feet. He caught sight of a figure darting past him and followed it for a moment. Jet had apparently heard him and was rushing towards the lever. At least he was helping.

The fire whip bender pulled back and struck again, this time going for Jet. Zuko dove into the strike to buy them time and cut the whip into two. Within seconds it was coming back around, this time going for Zuko’s feet. Zuko shuffled back and out of range while trying to slice the whip whenever he was unable to avoid it.

A fire whip specialist was deemed weak on fighting formats and often were talked poorly about, but one thing was true. They were fucking annoying and tricky to handle. Even the strongest of firebenders struggled when fighting them and the whips could bring most opponents down given the usual distance and endurance of the benders. Now, he needed to take out this bender over all others or they were done for.

Zuko realized quickly that he could not let the whip push him to the side or he risked opening the path to Sokka. Instead, he tried to stomp it out on the next strike. He missed, horribly, and the whip connected with his ankle and pulled his foot out from under him. Landing hard on his back on the wet metal was a shock and he had to force his body to roll to the side to avoid the whip as it cracked straight down on the wet surface of where he had been. It hissed angrily. Pushing himself up, Zuko punched out with a small amount of fire and glanced back again only to see no one at the lever.

“Zuko, come on!” Sokka yelled out. His voice gave Zuko a direction to run and without a look back he pivoted towards the sound. He ran as fast as he could and watched as Jet helped push Sokka up and onto the gondola. Fire cracked in front of Zuko as he ran, forcing him to skid to a stop. The whip wielder pulled back, ready to swing out again.

Zuko spun around to face off against the whip and realized that he had been fighting as a firebender. Right, improvise time. It worked once, why not try it again. Zuko did not think fire being pushed through his limbs would work as well as last time given the state of his inner flame. Besides, they were wise to that move as it was. Time to borrow from Aang and feed his core.

This time he widened his stance and squatted lower. He breathed out any extra air and on the inhale he moved his arms out wide and towards his face. He once again tugged on the storms energy in the air and sent it deep into his lungs. The fire in his core that had been flickering like a candle instantly roared to life. What had formed rapidly became too much to keep down. It flowed through his system and poured from the only exit he provided. He breathed out and forced the fire to flow through his lungs and out with as much control as he could muster, but he did not think that was much at all. Zuko had no way of knowing what the fire had looked like or if it was even slightly controlled to where he needed it. His eyes stung with the heat that pour from around him and the sheer light and colors had swallowed his limited vision.

The sounds of the fire crackled, sizzled and roared as he forced as much of the burning inner flame out as he could. As soon as it burnt out he slumped forward dizzy with the sudden flare of pure energy and the expulsion of it all in the matter of seconds. A shout from behind him had him blindly twisting and charging back towards the gondola which had pulled away from the platform by a few feet.

He dug deep and pushed his shaky legs to move as fast as he could. He locked his hazy sight onto Sokka’s outstretched hand and reached out with his left. A pull like this would destroy his other shoulder, that at least he was functional enough to realize. Relief washed through his system as their fingers made contact.

Zuko remembered one thing very quickly. He was never this lucky and things were never this easy. He had thought this was not easy and hoped that it was over but of course not. Agni forbid he actually got out of here in one piece. The fire whip cracked against the back of his hand and stung as he quickly slid to a stop.

Without thinking Zuko spun around and punched out a large flash of fire with a roar of frustration. He had put a bit too much into the blast and pushed the fire at the guard as fast as he could. The ball made direct contact with the guard who looked to be shocked at seeing Sokka hanging out of the open door of the gondola. That worked, Zuko was not about to question why Sokka had been the factor that had stopped the pain in the ass whip wielder.

The guard took the blast to the chest with no time to defuse it and was forced back as he fell with a wet thud. Two others rushed forward without hesitation and Zuko dropped to the ground. He forced his body to spin as he tried to ignite the air once more into a mini vortex. The fire was weak but did the job at stopping those rushing forward.

With a glance over his shoulder he noted that the gondola was just far enough that reaching would no longer work, but maybe… It was now or never. No time to think. It was time to do what he did best and he was already pushing it on just how far the gondola had moved. It now hung fully over the lake and every second made his next action less likely to succeed. Zuko shoved himself back to his feet and ran into the courtyard a few feet before he pivoted and charged the edge of the platform. He had heard Suki’s scream, he just ignored it and every other part of his system dully saying not to. His ‘not to’ was never really that loud, anyway.

He was not smart. He knew this. He did not think, blindly jumped into things and was often reckless. Maybe ‘jump’ was a bad thought right now as he charged forward. This, he would admit, was probably close to the number one instance in his ever growing book of “dumb shit Zuko has done.” He was sure he could make it, that the gondola was not that far. As soon as he had pushed off the last possible solid step on the ledge with all his might he realized that he was with half average depth. That ever since the Agni Kai he had always struggled with just how far something was from him and this might, maybe, not work. Too late now. Agni, please, just this once give them all a little luck.

Zuko felt the rain pelting him as he flew through the air towards the gondola and saw his answer. Sokka, who looked beyond panicked and desperate, had just leaned his whole body out the side window and was reaching towards him. He was trying to close the distance and give Zuko a place to grab while he realized he would not make the window ledge like he had hoped. He reached out and felt like this was it. That this was the answer. They had made it. Sokka would catch him. He had almost smiled, almost.

His eyes widened as he saw an arm snake its way over Sokka’s chest far too fast to call out. His heart plummeted as he locked eyes with Jet. Jet had looked to Zuko while he moved forward, face set in his tight and emotionless mask. He had a look of stone. His eyes showed no joy or grief in what he was doing. Zuko knew that look. Jet had worn the same one when he had seen ‘Lee’ use fire. Had hid behind it when he turned on ‘Lee’ and attacked for the first time before the anger had broken through.

Jet was the one to break the connection with Zuko first. He had closed his eyes tight as if burned by the very sight of Zuko and twisted backwards, ripping Sokka back with him. Zuko watched in horror as Sokka, equally horrified, quickly disappeared from the window and back into the gondola.

In a flash of lightning Zuko felt acceptance settle deep in his bones. It made sense. He was never really meant to leave this place. Everything had been stacked against him from the start. The moment Jet had walked back into his life he had known nothing would go right. From the cooler, to Mai, and now this. It was fine-

Duel screams ripped out of the gondola louder than the thunder that rumbled overhead. Zuko was snapped out of his acceptance. When had he ever accepted shit, anyway? Never, and he wasn’t about to learn how to now.

Suki, who was one of the mixing voices screaming out, was trying to reach out and replace Sokka. Problem was that she could not reach the same distance that Sokka had before. She would also not be able to hold him with how desperately and quickly she had moved to replace his safety net.

He desperately searched for his next answer, the side seams. In a flash he fed as much energy as he could to the flicker of fire he was trying to keep burning. It flooded his system with a dull pulse and he tried to push all the fire he had out of his feet while he kicked out in the hopes of gaining any extra height. Whether it worked or not, he could not say, but two things did happen in quick succession.

Zuko missed Suki by a good few inches despite her trying to dip lower to catch him by any means she could. Instead, he connected with the gondola and slid along the side far faster than he was prepared for. He desperately scraped and scrambled against the side to slow his fall and hopefully find a grip. The problem was any groove or grip he scraped over was far too slippery to gain any purchase and he was quickly slipping downward.

That was, until his right arm screamed out as it was caught by something sturdy. The dead stop to his descent and harsh pull back towards the side of the gondola sent a sudden shock wave of pain ripping through him. His shoulder cracked loudly, things deep in the joint felt like they ripped, and the following pop was felt throughout his entire body. White hot pain bleed through him as he choked on his scream and the continuous rain pelting him. He swung back into the side of the gondola with a harsh thud. Immediately, he tried to scramble up to relieve the lava of pain flowing from the joint. His eyes were squeezed shut tight and he barely felt the sets of hands grabbing at his sides to assist in pulling him in.

As soon as his feet hit the floor of the gondola and his arm was released he collapsed into the figure that had pulled him aboard. Pain rippled through him as he pulled his right arm against his chest. He hissed and groaned with the effort of moving it. His shoulder cracked audibly and he thought he felt the figure holding him flinch at the sound. He ignored the tension in his right side and tried to take calming breaths to stop his body from shaking as violently as it was. He was sure his vision and consciousness had blinked out at least once while he leaned against the figure, but seemingly not long enough for anyone to take notice. A quick attempt to move his arm was rewarded with another mind numbing wave of pain. It was dislocated, at the very least, but he was alive and that was worth it.

“Ye alright there, little dragon? Easy does it.” His mind finally let him process the words being whispered. He was sure this was not the first thing that had been said but it did not matter.
