The man he was leaning against continued to speak in a hushed voice and blocked the others from approaching.

Zuko laid in their arms violently shaking and trying to breathe normally for what felt like hours but was most likely seconds. Once he could feel his legs again he leaned back and away from the figure just enough to see who it was. Chit Sang. Chit Sang had been the one to catch him. To save the prince of his nation who by his knowledge should not be worth anything. He had risked himself by doing so and Zuko had no words to express how many feelings he had at the moment. The man was looking at him with clear worry and sighed deeply in some kind of relief. His mouth had moved in a question but Zuko could not bring himself to listen or answer.

“Th-thank-”

“Naw, save yer thanks for someone who deserves it. You woulda done it for me.” Chit Sang said, stopping Zuko from trying to form words. Zuko could only nod, it was true that he would have done the same without thinking about it.

Zuko took a second to look around and realized he really had made it. He was in the gondola. His arm was basically useless until Katara could maybe look at it but he had made it. He had jumped. He had done the admittedly dumbest thing in his life by far, rivaling that of the turtle seals, and survived. An arm be damned, he was alive.

Nodding, Zuko shifted back and away from Chit Sang just enough to notice Hakoda hovering. He looked like a man made from worry and it was confusing. Why did he look like he had just seen Sokka make the jump like a fool? Why was that look locked on Zuko and not looking towards his son? He must have helped drag Zuko into the gondola and that brought up mixed feelings. Before Zuko could voice any form of thanks to the confusing man a mass slammed into his front with a shout.

It happened just as fast as hitting the gondola had and his brain was struggling to keep up. Sokka, it was clearly him given the build and wolf tail, had enveloped Zuko in the tightest hug of his life. Sokka had even jumped slightly to wrap his arms around Zuko’s shoulders, one hand grasping his left tightly while the other buried itself into the hair at the base of his neck. Pain exploded from his right arm and chest but his brain paid no mind to it. Instead, it focused on one singular point of contact.

Lips, somehow both wet and chapped at the same time, had smashed into his own partially open mouth with a desperate force. It was more a clash of teeth and pushing of two faces together than anything but the contact was solid, if a bit painful. Zuko felt his lower lip sting, most likely split from the impact, and he groaned at the multitude of sharp pains coursing throughout his body. His knees were shaking hard and buckled under the sudden weight. He did not have far to fall as another form collided with his back in an equally tight embrace. He was not sure if they had meant it as a hug or to keep them upright.

Just as fast as the lips had smashed into his, they pulled away as Sokka lost the height his little jump had given him. It had been far too fast. Far too rough. But Zuko’s inner fire burned brighter and his lips tingled at the loss of contact. Even if he had wanted to reciprocate, it had happened too fast and he had not been ready. Sokka had pulled away and buried his face into Zuko’s chest while muttering. His hold on him had not changed, however, and the hand in his hair was numbing everything else. Zuko could not tell between the two of them who was shaking more, but it did not matter.

“Oh spirits, Sunshine. What were you thinking!?” Suki wheezed out as she shifted her hold on them. She was the thing keeping the two of them upright Zuko realized and felt it was fitting. She always seemed to be the one supporting them.

“Uh…” Good start, Zuko, great job. He realized just how that must have sounded. He had just nearly dropped to his death in front of them by his own actions and he could still not form a single word.

Sokka huffed and shook his head. “You didn’t, did you?”

“I…um… You’re the plan guy-” Zuko croaked out and was startled when the two crushing him began to laugh almost hysterically.

“Yeah, alright, plan guy. Tui and La, Zuko, I thought-” Sokka’s voice faltered as he spoke and fully gave out before he could finish his thought. Zuko simply nodded. He knew what that must have reminded him of.

“I’m sorry.”

“Don’t you ever do that again! I thought I had lost another person due to my own failure.”

“Sokka, that- That is not-” Zuko did not know how to voice his thoughts. He admittedly did not have any. His brain was really lagging after the jump. The pain that was still rippling through him was no help. He was also trying to understand if Sokka had actually meant to kiss him just now. No, that was easy. He liked Suki. He had jumped wrong. That was not, maybe? Then again, his hand was still rubbing the back of Zuko’s neck and that made him shiver and wonder if just maybe…

“Sunshine, we mean it. Don’t you EVER do something like that again. Do you understand?” Suki demanded. The sudden change in her voice startled Zuko out of his momentary spiral and he swallowed hard.

“He can’t promise something like that and you know it. Your little sun-whatever doesn’t ever think before he jumps.” Jet snarked, the eye roll clear in his voice.

Zuko felt more than saw as the two holding onto him tighter than anything has ever held him before twisted to glare in Jet’s direction. He had to admit his own anger was catching up now that he was on solid ground. Jet had pulled Sokka away. Had personally made sure that Zuko’s line to safety had been removed. Jet had nearly gotten him killed.

“Jet. Shut your fucking mouth before I push you off this gondola personally!” Sokka growled.

Jet, for his part, looked greatly offended. “Sokka, what the fuck! Is that the thanks I get for saving your life?”

“Saving- how was that saving anyone!? You pulled me away from catching Zuko-”

“No, don’t you go and twist this around just cause you like him! I did no such thing. I pulled you IN.”

“What fucking difference does that make!? It was away and it meant certain death to one of our teammates!”

“It was that, or certain death of two!” Jet roared back.

“Kids, what was done was done. Now is not the time for this.” Hakoda cut in before the argument could spiral.

It was hard to admit it, but Zuko hated that he might just understand what Jet had meant. It made sense in some back part of his jumbled brain. If the choice had been between himself or Sokka being hurt in any way he would have picked himself. Had been picking himself since they got to the prison. Jet had taken his lifeline, but potentially saved Sokka somehow by doing so. Maybe, if his word was to be believed. Zuko hated how twisted he felt inside about this. Jet was just that good at making things he did look good and one could never fully trust him.

“Dad, he almost killed-”

“But he didn’t. Best not dwell on the past when it is shrouded in “if” and move towards the next problem at hand. If we must for the sake of team strength we can revisit this when we are safe.” Hakoda gave a look that demanded that be the end of the conversation.

“Did… Did you just tell me to put a pin in this?” Sokka stared at his father in shock.

Hakoda chuckled. “If you want to use Bato’s words, yes. I believe it is best we look at what we have now and move forward. The prince is here and we are moving away. Focus on that-”

“Oh, shit.” Jet groaned out. Catching the attention of everyone. He was looking towards the platform they had just left. “Guess I didn’t do as good a job as I thought I had.”

“Who’s that?” Hakoda asked.

“Oh shit. That, is the definition of a problem.” Sokka groaned.

“What is?” Chit Sang asked, leaning around them all to see. “A girl?”

Zuko twisted out of the hug he had been trapped in and moved to see just what had gained the attention of the others. His entire body froze. There on the platform stood the very definition of his luck.

“Not just any girl. My sister, Azula.”

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

It has happened! Contact was made!

Thoughts?? &#60;3

## Chapter 18

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Azula makes her grand attack! Will they make it off the island??

### Text

Agni forbid this day could get any worse. Azula was here, he had bet horribly wrong on the fact that Mai had come alone, and she was currently taking in the gondola like a tiger wolf.

“At least we are here and she is there. It does not seem like they can stop this thing or they would have, yeah?” Chit Sang was looking back at the platform and clearly very confused about the rising panic.

“That won’t stop her. Not for long, anyway.” Sokka stated. He was already looking around the cramped space as if he could find the answer to how to prevent what was potentially coming.

“Not like she can fly. Last I checked firebenders were ground bound.” Jet glanced towards the platform.

“You have yet to deal with that particular problem. Trust me, she will come for us.”

“Bring it, Lala. I’m ready for you this time.” Zuko huffed, more to himself. He knew she would use her little name for him, she always did and it never failed to get under his skin. He was hoping to avoid that little power move of hers by getting his brain to focus on ‘Lala.’

He had to steal his every screaming nerve and set up his walls to trap the pain in his body into a corner of his mind for later. It was not as hard as it should have been, years of practice had made this a useful skill for survival. Locking down the pain of his body to a dull ache in the back of his mind he steeled his nerves and watched.

The gondola erupted into a buzz of panic behind him but he gave it no attention. His eyes were locked with his sisters as she grabbed something off of a nearby guard. He watched her jump up to the line, much like he had just done. Instead of jumping straight out, however, she jumped up and clasped whatever she had taken from the man on the ground around the main cord of the gondola. Using a thin line of perfectly controlled fire from her fist and heels she rocketed towards them.

Zuko had only a mere moment to feel stupid about his death jump after seeing just how well that worked before he was scrambling up and onto the window ledge. No way on Agni’s damned ass was he about to let his little sister anywhere near them. Not if he could do something about it. Twisting, he reached for the seam between the door and the roof when a large hand grabbed his thigh and made his grip slip off the rain soaked ledge. He looked down to glare at the person who was stopping him only to freeze at the sight of Hakoda.

“What do you think you are doing!? Get back in here, right now!” He demanded and ice flowed through Zuko. He had a moment of fear and gripped the ledge of the window as tightly as he could in hopes that when Hakoda decided to react he would at least have a chance to catch himself from falling.

“Dad! What are you doing?” Sokka demanded as he moved to try and wedge himself between them.

“What am I doing? What is he doing! We just got him in here and he is climbing around like a spider monkey! For what?” Hakoda huffed far more annoyed than Zuko thought he had a right to be.

“That is my sister. We have unfinished business. You have your happy family reunion, I am about to have mine.” He stated and the second the hand holding his leg in the gondola loosened he hauled himself up and out.

The wind whipped around in a way that actually made staying on the gondola easier with how slick it had become as it pushed him back against the side. Ignoring the shock waves of pain that leaked out of the corner of his mind, Zuko fully dragged himself to the roof and turned just in time to see Suki pop up on the other side. She nodded and looked down to signal that she was not alone and Zuko pivoted to allow Sokka to join on the roof as well. It was odd just how much comfort having them close was. Even if they were all being extremely ridiculous and this was way more dangerous than smart.

“Kids! What in Tui’s sky kingdom are you doing!? Get back in here at once!” Hakoda roared out as he hung half out the window.

He was too big to fully climb up and that in itself was a relief. Let him punish them later, or at most punish Zuko for this seeing as he had started it all.

“La, give me strength!” He dipped back into the gondola and Zuko spared him no more of his attention as his sister got within a few feet.

Ty Lee had joined her on the line and was running at them like it was no different than running on solid ground. She never failed to terrify Zuko with just how much the laws of anything natural like gravity did not seem to apply to her. Zuko moved to be in front of Sokka and kicked out a weak flame to try and force Azula to stay off the roof of the gondola with no such luck. Her boots hit the wet roof like they were magnets and she waved the flames away with an annoyed sigh.

“Oh, Zuzu. You really are that weak.” Azula drawled as she waved away the lingering wisps of his admittedly pathetic attack. His eye twitched, resolve to not let her get him worked up quickly dying. “Look, this is pointless and you know it so if you just sit like a good boy and listen for once I’ll finish this. Maybe we can get you a cushy cell back home. Your choice.”

Azula had spoken in her annoyingly slow way when she wanted to get his blood boiling. It always worked, and this time was no different. The only real difference that it had was the added benefit of buying Ty Lee the time she needed to swing up an onto the other side of the gondola. They were trapped, on top of a slippery roof hanging hundreds of feet above a boiling lake. Really, he should have seen this coming.

“Look what they have done to you. You are the prince of the Fire Nation!” Azula stated loudly, as if trying to make sure all could hear her. She dropped to the roof and spun out a wave of blue flames just as fast and pushed it towards them.

Zuko moved on instinct, jumping into the flow and slicing down with his left arm to split it. He had to keep his right arm tucked tight to his side or middle if he was to avoid any waves of pain that might cause too much of a distraction. He already was thankful that it had been far too long to have anything in his stomach or the pain earlier would have made him lose it all.

Zuko could not attack like this, but he would not let her burn Sokka or Suki. Sokka seemed to read that thought and side stepped around Zuko as he swung out with his sword in an attempt to force Azula back and away so that he could continue to have the space to break any fire.

“Oh? Seems you have spent some time with this pathetic lot. Zuzu, are you really that confused? Is your self worth so low that all they have to do is dangle the idea of ‘friendship’ and you are willing to be their bending slave? Shame, I had thought that maybe your brain was salvageable. Tell me, how is it being the Avatar’s scapegoat and punching bag?” She dusted soot from her shoulder as if the threat of a sword was nothing. It probably was nothing but Zuko felt something deep inside heat up. He took a deep breath to refocus and glared in warning.

“I am not some tool-”

“Really? Then riddle me this. Why is it that every little nonbender here looks well rested and in good health yet you, the firebender, look like a bad step away from a dousing? I knew the nonbenders were ruthless but surely you are not just sitting back and letting them beat you down. Do they really have you on that tight of a leash-”

Zuko growled out and in a quick twist forced as much fire as he could out from his left in an arching motion. Sokka grabbed his elbow to prevent him from charging forward to follow through. He had said something but the crackling of anger and sound of the rain was too loud for Zuko to really focus on what it had been. Azula had easily side stepped the fire but was giving one of her calculating looks that made Zuko’s insides squirm. What was she reading?

“Right!” Azula barked out much to Zuko’s confusion.

Sokka used the moment to once again move around Zuko and strike out. This time he followed through and pushed her to the edge. His footwork was solid and Zuko was impressed, but he was slow. He did not actually wish to hurt her and that would mean he was giving far too many openings. They were lucky as Azula seemed to also not be fully into the fight. She had the upper hand on every page and clearly knew it.

Zuko moved out to break the fire she had kicked out before it could connect with Sokka at a close range and felt the odd breeze of someone striking out from behind. He pivoted just in time to lean back enough to avoid a chi blocking jab. Ty Lee had broken away from Suki just long enough to try and target his right shoulder.

Everything clicked. Azula had noticed that his fire and movements were lagging and tried to get him to strike out in any way she could just to pinpoint the exact location to his weakness. This was why he never liked others knowing just what was wrong, they always used it against him. She had told that to Ty Lee and now he was cornered protecting Sokka and avoiding her. If it was not for Suki getting between them and forcing the physics defying terror back they would have been finished minutes ago.

Sokka noticed the problem and quickly switched places with Zuko. He moved to be with Suki and assisted in forcing Ty Lee back and off the edge. Zuko had only a moment to panic at the thought of her falling before he heard the telling sound of someone colliding with her inside the gondola. Azula took that pause and struck out again but was forced back when Zuko sent the fire back towards her.

“Hey, let me go!” Ty Lee yelled as the scuffling sound continued. Zuko could only guess Jet had gotten in her way, which was good.

Between the pitching forward to cut fire and the need to give Sokka space, it was a wonder they could hear anything at all. The storm was raging now and the longer they were on the roof, the harder it was to keep it that way. The puddles were making every step tricky as they kept up this cat and mouse fight. Suki had helped for a moment until Ty Lee swung back up to join, clearly having broken away from Jet.

It was with some kind of luck that they all kept their footing mostly as the gondola came to a sudden and screeching stop. It pitched backwards harshly and sent Sokka toppling to his side and sliding down as it swung forwards. Zuko dove and grabbed his outstretched arm to slow the descent as he looked to Suki for help. She rushed to his side and helped pull Sokka back onto the roof as Ty Lee jumped to see just what had caused the sudden stop.

“They’re about to cut the line!” Ty Lee screamed in horror.

Panic flooded Zuko. He looked to the platform and found the rain obscured his vision far too heavily to make sense of the dots. He would have to take her word and that was far scarier than anything.

Azula whipped around to glare towards the platform as she too saw the danger in the situation. All her upper hand had just been suddenly removed and the threat of her own drop was enough to force her to look around. She quickly locked eyes on the incoming gondola from the other direction and the coy smile grew again.

“I would love to stay and bleed your fire dry, dear brother, but it is time for us to leave. Goodbye, Zuko.” She winked and twisted to once more release an insanely controlled flame to rocket herself into the air. Zuko’s heart dropped for a moment as he thought she would drop over the lake, but he was reminded that she had the luck in the family. With more grace than she had a right to have she landed on the roof of the passing gondola and waved to Ty Lee. Ty Lee for her part looked over those on the roof of the doomed gondola with worry before she too flipped towards Azula and onto the safe one.

Sokka twisted and ran to the side of the gondola to look into the window, most likely informing the others as to what was about to come. Zuko felt his own heart rate skyrocket as he thought about the fall and how he had doomed all those with himself even after everything. It was only by some miracle that he heard anything at all.

“Incoming!” Jet screamed, it sounded as if he was facing the platform and Zuko spun to see just what had warranted that extra panic.

“Everyone down!” Whoever that had been did not matter. Zuko had seen what was causing the hysterics and his body was already in motion.

Azula was still on the roof of the other gondola but she had widened her stance. With large rotating motions the energy of the storm was eagerly sparking to life and feeding into the crackling of her own fire. That fire, was nothing but lightning and she was gearing up to cook them all well before the line even gave. In some ways, Zuko almost thought this was her way of being kind. To end them quickly before they felt the horror of a fall to their death.

Just before she even aimed her lightning Zuko had slid to the corner of the gondola. He reached out towards the strike and ignored the screams from behind himself. The lightning released with a crack and ripped through the sky towards them in a matter of seconds. He had those mere seconds to think this might not be a great idea after all when the energy connected with his fingers and he tugged it into himself as much as he could. He pulled it deep into his core and noted two things.

One, that Azula’s lightning felt much different than fathers. Where his was searing heat and power bent on cooking those it hit, hers was far more energy and almost fed off his own fire the moment it came into contact with him. Hers was both invigorating and dangerous.

The second was that his shattered shoulder was without a doubt destroyed. While the lightning past through it he felt the energy crackle and spark through the unnatural structures and his walls keeping all the pain sealed away fell within an instant. It took all of his will power to pull the lightning beyond that point and through the rest of this system. He knew he was screaming just as loud as the lightning was crackling.

Finally, in a last moment of conscious thought he pointed towards the platform and let go. He watched as the blinding light rushed from himself and rocketed towards the island. It struck true, knocking all those who were cutting the line away from the motor. The pole that had been used to stop the gondola fell to the ground. The water from the rain made approaching the motor again impossible for the time being and the gondola jerked back into motion.

Zuko watched as those on the platform scattered. As the strike hit. He felt the gondola move once more moments before as arms wrapped around his middle and the world faded to white.

&#45;--

Voices. That was the first thing to cut through the heavy fog that had settled over him. They were chatting excitedly and seemed to be moving around too much to pinpoint. The next thing that became clear was that he was moving. He was laying, no, that was not right. He was not laying, but he was not upright. Something was holding him and they were moving.

“Take it easy, little dragon. Ya keep throwin’ yerself into shit like it’s yer job. If you are not ready to wake up, I suggest ya don’t.” Chit Sang chuckled.

Zuko felt his words vibrate around him and took a bit longer than he would have liked to admit to realize he was once again in the mans arms. They were moving around but the sounds of the rain was quieter. How long had he been out for? With a groan he forced his eyes open and immediately regretted it. The dim light of a storm receding still shot right through his throbbing head and forced his eyes back closed.

“Everyone off! We don’t have much time-” A deep voice boomed and caused Zuko’s throbbing head to pound once more.

“Hey, water tribe man, might wanna keep that voice of yours lower. We got a delicate flower who doesn’t know how to stop. Oh, and they will find us faster if ya keep belting our location.”

“Sunshine?” Suki slowly inched forwards towards the figure laying in Chit Sangs arms. Her concerned face slowly came into focus as he forced his eyes back open and took a moment to try and make it so she had only one head. Was not easy, but at least he did not have to guess which Suki was talking, he hoped.

“Hey-” His throat was extremely dry and painful. His voice scratched deep into his chest and felt beyond raw. Blinking rapidly he cleared his vision just enough to take in the side of the volcano they had exited on. They had made it and were alive, despite everything. Sokka was shuffling around and he realized they did not have a plan for getting this far. They needed off this island, but how?

Zuko ignored everything in him screaming to stay quiet and croaked out louder, hoping others would hear him. “-my sister.”

“Zuko! Buddy you’re awake! Spirits, you scared us.” Sokka rushed over from the edge of the gondola he had been looking around. Zuko grunted and knew they were not yet done, and that laying around was helping no one so he pushed against Chit Sang to signal the need to be put down.

“Whoa, buddy, take it easy! You took a massive hit and are lucky that roof was wood for some reason.” Sokka reached out to catch his side to help him stand.

It was clear in how he pushed himself into being Zuko’s main support that he did not believe Zuko well enough to do things at the moment. It was enough to ignite the flickering candle within him once more in agitation. He was not weak, and that was not the first time he’d taken a hit like that from family.

“My sister. She is on the island.” He croaked out. He needed to get his swimming thoughts in order and make Sokka understand.

“We know, buddy. Kinda why we gotta keep moving right now.” Sokka moved to hold him tighter and Zuko grunted in frustration. He pushed surprisingly hard against the hold and twisted out of it only to stumble into Suki who quickly replaced Sokka.

“No, I mean she came here.” He ignored the protests from others as he pried himself from Suki and stumbled towards a ledge. He did note that Jet merely side stepped and watched him curiously, not wanting to stop what was happening. He reached the ledge and pulled himself up to look towards the hidden docks and saw just what he expected to see. A massive airship just waiting for people. “There. That’s our way out of here.”

Suki hurried up the ledge to his side and looked out with a gasp. “Oh! Sunshine, that’s brilliant!”

“Alright, we gotta hurry!” Sokka rushed to join and looked towards the steep path they would have to take down if they wanted to keep their head start. “Uh, Zuko, you okay to climb down the hillside?”

Before he could even try to say anything in response, Chit Sang appeared from behind far faster than Zuko was prepared for and he felt himself leave the ground. He will not admit to the squeak that left him, but Jet’s laugh told him it was not as unnoticed as he had hoped. Within a second he found himself cradled, once again, in the mans arms and looking towards the sky in shock.

“Naw, he gets the royal treatment after all that.” With that, Chit Sang slid down the hillside with the ease of a man carrying nothing more than a bag of feathers. Zuko, for his part, tucked into the hold and clung onto Chit Sang for the feeling of support as they rapidly descended. He was not sure how long it took and just how they got into the airship, for it would seem he had once again slipped into an unconscious state when he had closed his eyes tight against the falling feeling. This time, however, it was more likely some kind of sleep because waking from it did not feel like dragging his soul back into his body. The fog was not as heavy in his mind, either.

“I didn’t see anything like that on my check, sorry.” Suki had stated, worry clear in her voice. Why was she worried? They were clearly inside the airship, they were fine.

“Great. Just how are we supposed to get this thing moving!?” Jet bellowed.

Zuko realized the problem instantly and pushed to be put down once more. He hated just how many times in the last hour he had found himself being carried and was ready to not be for a long time.

“Fire.” Was all he could say and he kept his footing wide to prevent swaying. He was feeling a great deal better and with a shake of his head the foggy feeling dissipated. “We need fire to get it going, like any other balloon.”

“Of course!” Jet growled. “You Fire Nation all have an unhealthy obsession with having everything circle around fire, you know that?”

“Yeah, I do.” Zuko looked towards the hallway and back to the group. “Look, I can get this thing moving.”

“You sure about that?” Sokka asked, he had moved forward towards him and was clearly not happy about the idea.

“Does it matter? You need fire. Just, let me do this.” Zuko glared and waited for Sokka to give him some kind of sign. Sokka took a moment before looking out at the volcano and sighed heavily. Without looking back at him he nodded, which was enough for Zuko who instantly spun on his heel to hurry down the hall.

He easily found the boiler room and hurried to push out as much fire as he could into the burner. It took a few moves and a full kata to get the fire to form, which should have been enough of a warning. When had Zuko been good at reading the signs or warnings? He really knew he was walking a thin line but he had a job to do.

He had no way to know how long he had been at the burner, feeding it as much of his fire as he could. He did know that it had worked. They were in the air, and they were on the way away from the prison. He had a job. He was going to pour his all into that job. He had to keep them in the air and moving. His vision might be tunneling but that was because he was focused. His fire was coming out in smaller flickers but that just meant he needed to push harder.

He twisted into a punch and noted the his body felt like he was swimming rather than moving. His limbs felt heavy, and his head was floating. He followed through his punch and realized far too late that no fire formed. In one more shove for flames he watched as yet again nothing formed. His vision narrowed to that of only the burner blazing in front of him. He knew in that moment that his luck had run out, along with his own fire. With that realization, the room around him pitched forward and he fell into nothingness.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

Blastoff! We are in the air and off the island! But for how long??

Thank you all for reading along with this chaos! Let me know what you thought of this little number &#60;3

I have had another story eating away at my mind within the ATLA universe. It is not as formed and only just started but if you would like to check it out and maybe give it some love I would love to hear thoughts!

It's "Sundrop" and will be a Zuko/Sokka eventrually, after the spirits backstory ends.

## Chapter 19

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

Zuko is not the only one going down.

#### Chapter Notes

WARNINGS: Medical inaccuracies! Seriously, these fools are not doctors and neither am I. Do NOT take medical advice from them.

Warning 2: Panic Attack featuring Sokka. If you are not up for this skip from: Hakoda gripped his son’s shoulder and squeezed.  
to  
"How are you feeling?"

### Text

Sokka stood frozen as he looked down the hall where Zuko had disappeared before anyone could stop him. Zuko had been right, they needed to get this massive balloon moving and only fire could do that. This did not mean he had to like it. He actually hated this fact with his very being and was ready to follow down the hall to figure this out some other way. Before he could take that first step, however, the balloon shuddered and creaked as the air rapidly inflated it causing an uncontrolled lift.

Hakoda was the first to move as he rushed to the controls. He was trying to make sure the balloon could take to the sky but had no idea just how to do that. Others had swarmed different parts of the navigation room and busied themselves. The balloon gave another loud pop and groan as it began to lift at an uneven tilt. Sokka found his feet in that moment and rushed to the panel. He elbowed his father out of the way to take over and scanned the switches and levers. He had to find the hatch to release some of the extra steam Zuko was creating at a far too fast rate or they would roll.

The symbols made zero sense but they mattered little. Sokka had helped design the first balloon all those months ago and the pull hatch lever, while very fancy, was still easy enough to locate. With a great tug he heard the following pop as it gave and the balloon whistled like a tea pot. Fiddling with just how much air was being allowed free the balloon slowly rose and pitched forward. He was not in a mental space to manage navigation so he hoped someone who was would step up to that task.

Sokka just could not stop his mind from wandering back to the prison as he manned the air valve. It was not a hard job as it mostly settled once they were high enough to maintain forward movement with the released steam. The balloon was cruising far faster than expected and that could only mean Zuko was putting his whole self into feeding the burner. This also meant that Sokka had admittedly the easiest job of the navigation crew room and gave his thoughts time.

Those thoughts often drifted to what he had just endured. He had gone to that prison because of a mistake he had thought to be his greatest got others into trouble. He had believed all things Fire Nation easy to deal with and neglected every fact that he was a child, nonbender, finding things easy only because he often had the ability to just flaunt or hide behind the Avatar. The Avatar, who was also a child. They were all kids playing war and he had learned this reality by having others pay for it.

It should have been him who was captured, who was tortured, who was anything. Instead, he stood just fine and others were in pain or working far too hard just because he was some nonbender who had delusions of being better than that. No, that was not… Bending had nothing to do with this, and he knew it.

That was an old jealousy thing he found that always crept up when he was overtired or just worn so thin from problems others would never understand. They were lucky just because their birth came with a visible and useful skill. He had just survived a number of moments that should have killed them all and his body felt heavy.

The clouds around them rumbled with dying ripples of the storm that had fought them and a flash in the distance caught Sokka’s attention. With it brought thoughts of lightning, blue and chaotic. He had heard Jet scream and saw the sparks.

It had taken him far too long to register just what was about to happen and how, but it had not mattered. Once he had figured out that Azula intended to kill them all in a single strike Suki was already screaming, but it was not about the incoming threat. Instead, she had screamed out for Zuko and Sokka had felt his heart join his stomach as they both plummeted to the lake bellow.

He had watched as Zuko charged to the edge yet again only to reach out towards his sister. It was as if he could stop her somehow. Like holding up a stopping hand towards his own family would be enough. Maybe if that was Sokka’s sister that would have worked, but Sokka did not think Azula was one to stop just because someone asked. Sokka realized his mistake in judging the situation when he saw the hands that had reached out towards family willingly connect with lightning that had ripped through the space between with a deafening crack.

Zuko had looked so serious. So confident, so in control for once. He had looked more than ready to face lightning and the realization that this was not the first time he had taken this kind of hit was just as shocking. That move, the motions, the stance, it had looked so practiced. That was until Zuko had pulled the strike into himself and screamed with the power of the storm.

Sokka sighed heavily as he adjusted his hold on the handle to allow more steam to escape the balloon. He would have to find a way to tell Zuko to cool it with the fire soon or it would just be wasted energy. His fire was not going to last like this. This was also a great way to pull himself out of his spiraling thoughts.

That scream still rang in Sokka’s ears every chance he gave it space. It haunted him as he worked hard to hear and think about anything other than lightning. The flashes outside the windows helped absolutely zero percent in keeping the spiraling thoughts at bay. That clear, tortured, world shattering sound of pain while Zuko pulled crackling lightning into himself had exploded out of him. He had screamed so hard that his voice had blown out within the few seconds that attack had lasted and something in Sokka had snapped.

He was still unsure what that was, but it was part of the reason he needed to busy himself or he risked charging to the engine room and pulling said firebender away from the burner. He would work hard to bundle the probably pissy mess of a jerk into a bed. If needed, he would sit on him until he promised to stay. Sokka might just join in the bed for a quick nap. Right, the faster they settle things in this room the faster he can stop fighting this desire to actually hold the one person consuming his every thought.

He had let Zuko fall once, never again. He had already put that resolve to the test as he had watched the firebender begin to dip as soon as he released his sisters lightning. Sokka had charged forward on that slippery roof without thought and pulled him into the tightest hold as soon as the flash had dimmed. Zuko had crumpled like a doll and the sudden weight change had almost pitched them both off the side. That could have very well have happened if it were not for Suki who had helped drag the body back onto the roof.

Together they had lowered him into the gondola and from there Sokka had spent the rest of that ride in sheer panic that he would not wake. Zuko had gone from pale to sheet white and would shake or tense randomly. Even when he had woken, his color had not returned and even now Sokka thought Zuko had looked beyond cold. He had looked worse than he had ever seen him and Azula’s words were eating at his core.

Two specific things were circling like a vulture hawk as he tried to let working the valve distract him. ‘How is it being the Avatar’s scapegoat and punching bag’ always surfaced when he thought about all the little moments where others lashed out towards Zuko, mainly Katara.

Sokka had not been any better, he had also lashed out and done awful things before their time at the Boiling Rock prison. That did not take back or make better all of the things before. That would be changing from now on. Zuko was a part of this team now, no matter what others thought.

‘Why is it that every little nonbender here looks in good health yet you look like a bad step away from a dousing?’ This one chilled Sokka and he shivered. It was true, and it hurt.

He had tried to make sure that Zuko slept but that had not been much of anything. He had not worked hard enough to make sure he had food. Had ignored clear signs of problems. In his defense, he had just learned a lot about firebenders in a very short amount of time. That was no excuse for his treatment of the only one on the team, even if said firebender never voiced any problems with it or actively fought to do things that would hurt.

He hated just how much Azula got under his skin, but she was right. That every person in their group was fine and overall alright, and Zuko had taken physical attacks and torture sessions just on the idea that it was something he could give freely to be helpful. He had been in such a deep need to prove himself that Sokka had ignored the pain Zuko had been in.

The feeling of falling for a split second snapped him from his thoughts as he adjusted the valve of the balloon for the sudden dip in heat. Zuko must be taking a break, good. Nothing they could not cover for with the air already trapped. He had been so gone that he had missed the fact that they were already far further than he had expected.

A look towards the sun placement told him that no, they were on time, he had just lost a few hours to his thoughts. Sokka felt for his feet, it took a bit more effort than he was ready for, and slowly moved away from the valve controls to see just where they were going.

“Oh, hey, welcome back.” Jet flashed a smirk as he looked up from the maps spread on the captains table.

“How are you feeling?” Suki asked at the same time she punched Jet’s arm hard enough to make him recoil. Sokka felt he deserved more but said nothing.

“Sorry, just needed a moment I guess. I’m fine. Where are we?” He moved to take in the map and tried to find the marker for the balloon. He hated the second he realized it was all written in fire script and groaned out, “you have to be kidding me.”

“We were trying to read it too.” Suki sighed. She rubbed her face in equal frustration and looked towards the window as if it would teach her the words needed.

“So, no one can read this shit?” Jet huffed. “Great, we have already been flying for hours. How do we know where to go?”

“I know where to go. I just need to know where we are now and I can get us in the right direction.” Sokka had bent over the table to get a closer look, hoping the topography alone would hold answers, when a hand nudged his shoulder to sit up.

He slowly moved back and watched as it then moved to tap a spot just under where he had been covering with his chest a moment ago. He glanced up confused, only to realize the man who was tapping the map was Chit Sang. The Fire Nation man they had taken. The one who had proven more times than Sokka could count just how important to their mission he had become.

“What is it?” Suki asked.

“That, is the prison. Says so right there.” Chit Sang had followed that statement with a word that sounded far too pretty to be a prison but Sokka was not about to question it. He was looking around the area Chit Sang had pointed out only to prove that it was indeed the volcano.

“That’s, wow. Thank you! He’s right. Look at the shape and the distance from other land masses. What side has the docks?”

“Wait, you can read this?” Jet asked as if ‘this’ was just scribbles on a page.

“Wait, you didn’t think to ask the one Fire Nation person in the room to look at it?” Sokka asked and was met with two guilty expressions. If he was being honest, he had also not thought of it but he had just come to the realization that it was fire script. They had had a few hours.

Sokka sent the others away from the map in a huff and spent the next few minutes questioning Chit Sang. He needed to get a general idea on where they were heading now versus where the air temple was. They were not too far off course, at least. Suki had known they wanted to move west and that had meant they only needed a slight correction if Sokka was following the correct line.

He quickly marked the path they were to take with pins left by the previous captain and took his first actual look around. The room was far bigger than he had thought. It may have been the panic, but he remembered them not having much room to move around. That was not true at all. They had room to spare and this was only the navigation area.

Jet had taken a chair in a corner as far from the hallway door as possible and was busy digging through any drawer that was not locked. Not a shock, but Sokka did not care to stop him or even watch. Moving to the other side he saw that his father had moved to cover the vacant job Sokka had left at the front. He was manning the switches a bit clumsier than Sokka had but it was working to keep the balloon steady. Hakoda was a man of the sea, not sky. Even with those two a few feet apart of each other, there was still plenty of room to add two or more people comfortably into the front of the navigation room.

This balloon had carried far more than just the princess and her friends. ‘You look like a bad step away from a dousing’ echoed through his mind and spurred him into action. Sokka began to tear apart the navigation room and pulled all useless items that felt mildly burnable into a crate. He did not care that he was making a mess or how loud he had suddenly become as he worked as fast as he could.

He was on a mission and the only thing he could think of was keeping the fire burning. They did not have a way to feed it otherwise without using Zuko. If he was lucky, he would find a way to fan the burner or pump in air at a faster rate and that would mean only one person would be needed to keep it roaring. If they could do that then Sokka was actually going to force a certain bender into one of the bedrooms this massive thing was bound to have.

Another idea hit him just as fast and he was turning towards the hallway with far too much speed to control and promptly slammed into a solid mass. They both landed with a painful thud onto the metal flooring and Sokka’s hard work scattered.

“Sokka, the fucks gotten into you?” Jet groaned out as he twisted to untangle them.

“Not my fault you got in the way. There is plenty of room, why were you behind me?” He did not have time for this.

“Why were you ripping apart everything you touched?”

“Wait, were you trying to dig through the crap I had in the crate? Dude, this stuff is worthless to us! Why are you so into getting your hands on it?” Sokka glanced towards the scattered items and noted that Jet had a few papers. He felt his anger rise despite it being pointless. Maybe these papers meant something to him but they meant far more to the one person keeping them in the air, and Jet loved to make sure that person suffered.

“I’m trying to see if there is anything worth looking over. This is a war balloon from the royal family! It could hold attack plans or other things.” Jet waved the papers in the air as if to prove some point.

“Sorry, but I have to disagree. This is a balloon of the royal family, yes. I doubt it would have anything like that on it though. They were on a single mission, collect the imprisoned prince and go back.” Sokka snatched the papers roughly out of Jet’s hand and stuffed them back into the crate. This was pointless. He felt the balloon dip slightly until his father was able to adjust and knew they were losing altitude. A year on Appa made one real good at feeling the slight changes in hight. Zuko was taking a longer break than usual.

“How can you be so sure that was all they were going to do?” Jet huffed.

“Because, they would not have left this thing unmanned for a second if they had sensitive infromation on it. Jet, I am not in the mood to deal with you-”

“What is that supposed to mean?”

“It means I am still very much upset at past events that almost killed someone! I would really like to leave it at that for now until we are at the air temple! I’ll hold this conversation for when Toph is here. Otherwise I may regret my actions.” Sokka turned to kneel while he worked to shove the scattered items back into the crate.

As much as he wanted to just scream at Jet or pitch him off the side, he had meant what he said. Toph would tell him if Jet was lying and that was worth waiting for. Sokka reached for one of the old maps he was sure was out of date only to have it be snatched away. He let out a frustrated growl and ripped open a nearby drawer instead, dumping all burnables into the crate.

“Kids, what is going on? Why are you two arguing and what is with this mess?” Hakoda huffed as he looked back over his shoulder.

Sokka felt his anger spike just as irrationally as it had with Jet at his dad chiming in like he had any authority. He instantly felt guilty for it. That was his father and chief. If anyone did have authority over him it was that man, even if it had been many years since he had someone hold that kind of standing.

“Hey, not me. Sokka is going insane.” Jet stated as he pulled yet another pointless thing out of the box of burnables. Sokka snatched it back and dragged the box away from Jet’s reach with a growl of frustration. What was his deal?!

“I am trying to work so that someone who has been saving our ass constantly can take a fucking break! Just because the two of you can’t be in the same space-”

“Sokka!” Hakoda boomed as he fully turned to face the boys.

The balloon groaned and they dipped lower yet. Odd, they should have leveled off.

Hakoda waited until he knew he had Sokka’s attention before chiding. “Language.”

Sokka saw red. He had been working so hard and that was what his father had gotten stuck on? Some weird obsession with the idea that kids could not swear? He had to admit it was weird saying things like that in front of adults but he had been doing it for months now. Spirits, sometimes it got his point across faster than anything! He had not been some child to be chided in years and his father was acting like he could just slip right back into the perfect dad role. That, along with the fact that Jet had yet again moved to take his box was enough of a strain for Sokka to lose all sense of self restraint.

“Fuck off, dad!” Oh, he was in trouble now but they were just wasting time at this point. Zuko had been keeping them moving for a few hours by now and if the balloons descent was anything to go on he was in a serious need of some help. Sokka was rung dry. His emotions were beyond frayed and he was at the tipping point internally. If he was this bad, there was no telling how bad the others were.

“Sokka!” Hakoda glared his ‘I am your chief’ look that usually worked, but right now Sokka could not stop.

He wanted nothing more to go and pull a self loathing firebender into a pile of blankets and sleep for days. With that, his filter shattered and he stood up to face his own father with an equal glare. This man was his father. Had once been. Had not been for years.

“What, dad? What do you care that our language has become a bit more loaded? Since when have you cared?”

“What are you saying? I have cared, never stopped caring.”

“Yeah, I know you cared. I bet you kept caring, but that did not mean you get any say in it now. You can care about it all you want but you can’t take back the years we had to grow wondering if you were even alive! The years we lost as children! I get it, dad. I do. You felt moms death harder than anyone at the time. Felt the pull to war on that day. But then you left and left me behind. Left Katara behind. And what did you tell me? You told me I was the man of the village now, and from that moment I became a man. I was no longer a child, and haven’t been for years. Neither has Katara, who you left to grieve mom alone all those years ago. You took away our father after we lost our mother. So yes, dad, I may be your son but I am not a child so you will not treat me as one.” With that, Sokka spun on his heel to stomp his way towards the hall. He was going to find the rooms and pull apart as many beds as possible to add to the crate of burnables.

Suki was in the opening to the hallway and looked to have been stunned. Sokka supposed that was warneted after such a blow up on his part but he had little care for it at the moment. He would apologize later- or not. Not like he had said anything that was not true, he had just thought he would never say it at all if he was to be honest with himself. He loved his father, had risked his life for the man, but that did not mean he did not harbor any hurt or anger towards him.

“Sokka…” Suki had whispered as he pushed past her and he reluctantly slowed to a stop. With a glare he had not meant for her in any way, he glanced back. She stiffened but showed no other sign of being upset by this. “How can I help?”

Spirits, she was amazing. She had taken one look at the situation and known that now was not the time to push. That what was needed was a helping hand, not judgment, and that the others had done nothing but push.

“We are losing altitude. Steadily have been for a bit now. I think Zuko is tired and taking a break but without his fire we can’t stay steady. I need a way to feed the fire.”

“That’s why you have that box? Alright. I looked around the back rooms already so I can go grad the unneeded things to burn.” She was already turning to lead down the hall when a harsh shuddering rippled through the aircraft.

They had only moments to look around just as confused as the others before they were all pitched backwards as the balloon creaked and dipped violently. It had deflated rapidly and was clearly not re-inflating which meant there was no fire. Without fire there was no lift. They were going down and that only meant one thing: Sokka had forgotten a key rule with Zuko. He never took breaks. They were too late.

In a matter of seconds he had pulled himself off of the floor and used the crate to help Suki stand. Gripping the wall he steadied himself as his father worked hard to seal the valve and trap as much hot air as he could to slow their descent. It worked as they began to level off just enough for Sokka to drag Suki and the crate down the hall towards the engine room. He steeled himself for what he would find. The top priority was the balloon, everything else must come second no matter how much his heart screamed against that.

Together they forced the door open and rushed into the hot engine room. The burner was in the center and had the smallest fire flickering at the base. It took tunneled focus, which was helped by the balloon dipping drastically once more, to ignore all other things in the room. The hardest was the fact that they had to step over a figure laying motionless before the burner.

Sokka ripped open the door to the burner, noting the burns to his hand and adding that to his ‘ignore for now’ list as they moved to shovel papers into the dying flames. The fire flickered as if it was being smothered and Sokka shoved his already burned hand into it to move the papers around more evenly. The fire hungrily lept up to greet him and with a loud pop it grew quickly. Suki fed larger and larger items to it as the balloon creaked happily. It was working, they were leveling off once more.

They just needed a way to feed the fire. He continued to shovel things into the burner and felt Suki drop the other side of the crate to rush off into the background. He did not have the time to look and see what she was doing or where she was going. He had to trust her as he heard loud crashes and clatters.

A triumphant exclamation and few moments later Suki reappeared holding a large triangular contraption. He had no time to ask what it was as she shoved the metal tip into the newly burning papers and squeezed. Air rushed out of the tip and into the fire with such force that it burst into a roaring blaze once more. The balloon groaned an echoing sound and he felt them once again gain in altitude.

Sokka quickly realized that this thing was going to take a lot of energy to pump air and pushed Suki off of the air hose. He was going to take that role because it was the only thing in this room keeping him from losing his mind at what was at their feet. He wanted nothing more to check on Zuko but he knew better. Of all the things he was good at, healing had never been on that list.

Sokka twisted to watch as Suki dropped to the floor murmuring to the snow white body. He noted that his father had entered the room at some point, followed by Chit Sang, but paid them no mind. As he pushed the sides of the odd triangle together again the sparks flooded the space and forced him to lose his hold to avoid being burned more.

Chit Sang hurried forward and scooped up the device. He hip checked Sokka out of the way as he stood fanning his hands to cool them. Sokka stumbled, but before he could comment he watched the beast of a man continue to pump air into the flames without a care as the sparks died on his skin harmlessly. Right, firebender.

“Sokka.” Suki’s voice cut through his thoughts and drew his attention back to the form limp on the floor. She had her hand on his neck and his panic from the listing fall they had just been in changed and doubled. Was she not finding a pulse? No, that, no. That couldn’t be it.

“He’s so cold…” She looked up towards them in pure panic. “Like ice even in this boiling hot room.”

“Not a shock,” Chit Sang huffed as he squeezed air into the burner. “He doused himself, surprised it took this long actually.”

“Doused? There is no water in this room. How could the kid douse himself?” Hakoda asked as he took hesitant steps into the room. His eyes shifted between the kids, lingering on Sokka, and settled onto Chit Sang.

“Naw, that is a water way of thinking. Ya gotta think about that word from a fire perspective. What happens when ya ‘douse’ a fire?”

“It goes out?” Hakoda asked, far more confused about what was happening than he cared to admit.

“There ya go. The kid killed his fire.”

“Wait, no. A firebender can’t lose their bending. That can’t be right-”

“Just cause it is not common, doesn’t mean it can’t happen. I am doused for the time being thanks to that damn prison. The prince just did it to himself. Not a permanent thing, kinda just the bodies way to force itself into healing after traumatic or extreme times.” Chit Sang grunted the last words as he continued to work the fire. He had found a rhythm and the balloon had felt like it had leveled off at a stable altitude.

“So, he needs rest?”

“That, is the basics. For the fire to return rest is best. The other things that kid put himself through may demand far more than rest if ya ask me. Might wanna check him over while ya got the chance. Don’t see him stayin’ down or still like that for long even if it is the best thing for ‘em.” He chuckled and shook his head as if he found this funny.

Suki took those words to heart and quickly moved to roll Zuko off of his left side only to gasp loudly. Before anyone had a chance to ask or stop her, she started to rip his shirt from the collar down. Once it gave at the bottom she moved to do the same with the sleeves and opened the shirt without lifting or moving his body.

“Suki, what are you- oh.” Sokka had started to question before his words died in his throat. Zuko’s entire right side, starting at the shoulder and spreading through the chest and most likely back, was a mar of angry colors. Purple, blue, and black were twisting throughout the muscle groups with the deepest coloring being his shoulder joint and down the forearm. The shirt had worked to hide the injury, and Sokka had to wonder just how long it had been this bad.

Would Zuko have every even asked for help if they had not seen it? What was wrong with his arm that was causing all of that? It looked unbearably painful and Sokka realized that scream that haunted him had not come from the lightning itself, but the power of that mixing in something so broken.

He was stunned, again. He felt like stone. His eyes were locked on the snow white form on the floor the did not so much as twitch when the others touched or moved him. The sweat on Zuko’s bare chest had begun to bead freely and Sokka could not tell if it was from the heat or shock. Suki was yelling out to the others and things were being moved around but Sokka heard none of it.

Instead, he felt that feeling of letting the world swallow him once more as the others rushed to do things. He had known Zuko was injured, had even reminded himself of that fact when he had maybe tried to kiss him that first time. He had let Zuko brush it off as nothing. Obviously, Zuko’s definition of nothing needed reevaluation. The closer he looked, the more the colored ends of the massive bruise looked like they were a spiderweb, most likely spread lower than originally via lightning.

Chit Sang continued to work the fire, that was clear as the room felt the random bursts of heat. His father had joined Suki on the floor on Zuko’s right and was working with her to poke and prod the body. It did not seem to matter what they did, as Zuko lay lifeless. That was until his father had gripped the blackened shoulder.

Zuko’s whole body had responded. His eyes had shot open yet unseeing, his body tensed, and choked out a silent scream before he settled once more into a lifeless form. Sokka shook his head clear, he needed to hear what they were saying. Needed to know what was wrong. Needed to help if he could.

“What do you think?” Suki had whispered. She was running her fingers through Zuko’s hair while holding his left side firmly to prevent him from sitting up or twisting away. Zuko was now panting as his body trembled slightly when touched and Sokka wondered just how much pain he had to be in to have it seep through his unconscious form.

“Might need a bit more than rest.” Chit Sang piped in as he continued to work the flames.

Hakoda hummed in agreement. “Dislocated for sure. Must have happened when he was caught after that ridiculous stunt of his.” He huffed as if he was holding back from scolding the lifeless firebender and moved to poke at the muscles around the joint.

Zuko, for his part, shuddered and let out a pained groan that sounded more like a plea to be left alone than anything. Sokka’s body moved on its own, taking him a few shaky steps forward and closer.

“Looks like he really ripped it up bad, too. I’m not a medic, Bato was the better one of the two of us, but if I had to guess I’d say he has possibly severed quite a few major muscle groups. Potentially even the joint connectors themselves if that is even a thing.”

“Wh-what do we do?” Sokka’s voice was hoarse as the words felt far more forced than they should have been.

Hakoda glanced back to him and gave a small, reassuring smile. “It’s alright, son. We will get him as stable as we can before we make sure he rests.”

“We need to stabilize the joint. We can’t move him and risk any more problems, but he can’t stay here or we will never get his temperature to regulate.” Suki was looking at the arm with deep concern. Most of her words were going over Sokka’s ability to comprehend but they sounded bad. “We should set it at the very least. It is going to become harder to do the longer it is out of place.”

“Agreed. We should set the joint now and compression wrap it to prevent the swelling from getting out of control.” Hakoda had turn back to his task and winced at taking in the sight of it again. The joint was indeed swelling rapidly and the longer they waited the worse it was going to be. Hakoda placed his palm firmly to the joint and pushed slightly to test just how it moved.

Sokka felt all the blood drain out of his body as he watched Zuko’s body shudder and gasp. His eyes had fluttered in a way that spoke of only agony and his back had arched off the floor in an attempt to pull away from the pressure. The sound that had been forced out was a mix between a gasp and yell but had been cut short. His breathing was irregular and overly loud to Sokka’s ears as he felt his body freeze over.

“Hush, Sunshine. Easy, breathe, that’s it. We are here. It will be alright.” Suki was back to combing through his hair but had moved to be almost laying on his left side. She was using her weight to pin him to the floor and Sokka realized just what they were about to do. He wanted to be anywhere but here, but he could not find his feet to move. He also did not want to leave Zuko when he was like this even if he did not know what he could do to help.

“Sokka?” A voice broke through the ice in his veins and Sokka turned to look at his father.

“Son, we need a few things. I need you to go find something we can use to wrap his arm with. Can you do that?” He waited and Sokka nodded slowly. “Good, if you can find a med kit and ice that would be best. Can you do that?”

Sokka swallowed hard and nodded. He realized what his father was doing, giving him an out so that he did not have to stand and watch. He gave a final glance to Suki as she moved to sit firmly on Zuko’s waist before laying against his left side told him that he was very much out of his element. He nodded once more and forced his heavy feet to take him as quickly out of the room as possible.

He had not meant to slam the door to the engine room like he did, but the moment he was out of the room he slumped against the door and scrubbed at his face roughly with his palms. He leaned there shaking and trying to catch his breath before he would try walking and realized just how bad of an idea that had been.

Within the next minute or so the hall filled with a piercing scream that had matched that of the lightning strike. Sokka felt his body move before he registered. He was about to be sick, that was, he would have been if he had had anything in his system to bring up. Instead, he found himself heaving and leaning against the wall for support until he could get his system back under control.

Never before had another persons screams of agony cut him so deeply. Never had he felt violently ill with the very sound of them. He shuddered and gasped, desperate to get his heart and body under some kind of control. He had to find that med kit and ice. He had a job. Focus on that. Zuko will be fine, that was the last of it. From now on he will no longer be put through that kind of pain. He needed the med kit. He needed ice.

The sounds from the room had cut short and Sokka could only assume Zuko had fully blacked out. He had to hope he would not remember the last few hours. Had to get his damn body to move and find the med kit and ice!

The next few minutes and rooms were a blur as Sokka forced himself to dig through everything he got his hands on. He had not even remembered his path as he ripped open the next door out of the uncountable number of doors before. He was startled by what he found.

The Fire Nation symbols hung from massive banners embedded into the walls, a large four poster bed sat against the far corner with the nicest covers of fire red he had ever seen. The wood of the frame had gold trim and a desk sat on the opposite wall. Dresses and armor were hung in the open closet with such care that even the rough ride had not disturbed them. It was without a doubt a room made for a royal. Azula’s room.

For some reason that idea felt weird and Sokka quickly shut the door to move on. He had found a few medical kits ranging in size but had come up short for ice. He was hoping one of these rooms would have been a kitchen but he could not figure this thing out. Nothing made sense to him as he opened doors and expected one thing but found something completely different. The only plus to all of this was that Jet had taken the chance to hide in the navigation room and follow Sokka’s instructions written on the map.

Sokka gave up on the ice, he had spent too much time on that factor as it was and hurried back to the engine room as fast as he could. He took a few deep breaths and steeled himself for what he was potentially about to walk into. He knew there was no blood or external things other than that nasty coloring but it did little to settle his rolling system. With a final huff he slid the door open and stepped inside.

Zuko was still on the floor but his right arm, which was still colored in dark and angry marks, was tucked into his side and his hand was resting across his middle. His head was being propped up by a bundle that looked like it could have been a bag of some sort. Suki was busy wiping any sweat from his face and whispering.

“Find anything?”

Sokka jumped and spun to see his father pumping the fire with air. He must have switched with Chit Sang for the time being. The fire looked far more stable than it had previously which meant that a constant stream of air was not needed. Sokka felt silly as he reached towards his father, arms full of the random packs and thin blankets he had brought. Hakoda moved to set the air contraption aside and gently took the bundle from Sokka.

“I-I have… I couldn’t…. I’m sorry…” Sokka’s voice was shaking harder than he had realized. His whole body was, if he let himself feel.

“For what?” Hakoda asked softly as he handed the bundle to Suki. Suki, for her part, got to work instantly and began compression wraps.

“I couldn’t… no ice.”

“Oh, son. That is alright. This will do until we can make it to Katara. Come here.” Hakoda gripped his son’s shoulder and squeezed.

He tugged gently and Sokka felt his body crumple into his father. The last few days caught up to him as he watched Suki and he felt his legs give out.

The room was suddenly far too hot and far too small. His chest felt far too tight to breathe and his gasping breaths were too loud. Somewhere deep in the back of his mind he knew he was having a panic attack, he had seen Aang have one a few times. On nights that he thought no one woke, he had heard Zuko go through them but had tried not to let him know. They were not friends then, they were now. The thoughts, the almost losing him twice in a matter of moments, this whole situation. He couldn’t breathe, and he just wanted Zuko to wake up.

A hand was on his back and scooping him to sit firmly into someones lap. Sokka found himself cradled tightly against his fathers chest. Felt the rumbles as he talked to someone. Felt him take deep and exaggerated breaths and realized that he was meant to copy the breathing. It was silly just how hard breathing had become. His face was wet, too. Was he crying? That was just great. Suki would think him weak after all of this. Couldn’t help Zuko, couldn’t find ice, and now he was close to passing out because he could not even breathe right.

Sokka pressed his ear against his fathers chest and focused on the heart beat, strong and steady. His chest relaxed as things slowly became more manageable. Once he was sure moving would not result in him blacking out he pushed away from the hold.

“How are you feeling?” Hakoda questioned. His concern was palpable.

“Fine, sorry. I… don’t know what came over me.” Sokka groaned. His body felt like it had been sat on by Appa again as he stretched.

“Nothing to be sorry about. You have been through a lot in such a small amount of time. Gets to the best of us at some point.”

Sokka huffed. “Yeah, like you or the other tribesmen do this at seeing someone they lo-… seeing a teammate hurt.”

Hakoda chuckled and rubbed Sokka’s back in a circular motion like he had done after any time Sokka had come home crying. “Of course they have. I have as well. It is not something one can predict, but it affects all who have reason to know true fear. Even those who do not have true reason can suffer the cruelty of a panic attack and they are never weak for it.”

“I was so… I’m sorry for what I said earlier.” Sokka leaned heavily into his fathers hug once more. The day was just so much and he was regretting so many things at once that everything felt like it had happened months ago instead of hours to minutes.

“It is alright. You were not wrong in what you said but that is a conversation between adults for a later date. Right now I think it best that we move you kids- sorry. I think it is best to move you all to beds for some much deserved rest.” Hakoda squeezed Sokka tight before he pushed for them to break apart enough to allow him to look over his son. He smiled and gave his shoulder a final squeeze before moving to Suki’s side. “How is our prince?”

“Better, actually.” She flashed a small smile towards Sokka as well in a way to be reassuring and leaned back. “The compression wrap is just what we needed. Now with the arm pretty much tied down I don’t think he will be moving it and hurting himself anytime soon.”

“That’s very good to here. Let’s get him out of this insanely hot room, yeah?”

Sokka had a moment of panic as he spun to look at the burner. He was met with a wink from Chit Sang who was monitoring the flames and had caught Sokka’s momentary second of forgetting about just what was keeping them in the air and moving. Nodding, he slowly turned his attention back to the floor only to watch his father scoop Zuko into his arms as he stood slowly.

Zuko was still far too pale to be healthy, shining with sweat and the bruising that peeked out under the large amount of bandages stood out in stark contrast. His right arm was wrapped tight from his shoulder to his hand. The shoulder was wrapped across his chest leaving most of his upper body to be hidden behind bandages and his right arm was tied across his chest to prevent any form of movement. It looked over the top for something that was not external but Sokka could not help but feel it was actually lacking. He had failed to find the ice, after all.

Zuko had made the smallest moan as Hakoda shifted him into a more secure hold against his chest. It sounded so tired and pitiful. Sokka could not help but feel relief at hearing it. That little groan had not sound pained for the first time in almost two days. He realized just how right Suki and his father were about the care they just did even if it had sounded heartbreaking.

Speaking of heart, he was going to really have to process his own situation here soon. He followed his father to one of the rooms with a bed and watched as Suki piled blankets and pillows into a makeshift nest before helping to settled Zuko into the center of it. He watched, and he let himself feel openly. It was quick, and it was strong, and he was so unbelievably screwed.

Sokka had let himself fall for the sun itself. He had also almost lost them within hours of this and the hurt of that was going to be long lasting. Something in him had hoped this feeling was brought on by adrenaline. That being forced together was going to be the root of it. It was very possible that it had been the start of whatever this was, but it was not going to just end with safety. If anything it was growing and he wanted nothing more than to join in that bed and hold the jerk of a prince.

Hakoda had made sure the prince was settled comfortably in the pile before he made to leave. He paused at the door and nudged Sokka’s shoulder to get his attention. Once he had it, he motioned to the outer hall and fully left. Sokka shared a confused look with Suki before he slowly followed after his father. Leaving the room hurt far worse than his hand was starting to but he had to face his actions at some point.

“Dad I-” Sokka rushed to speak but Hakoda merely shook his head and held up a hand for him to wait.

“Son, this is not about earlier. I said we would talk about that later and I will hold firm to that. That conversation is between men, this is between a father and his son.” Hakoda looked over Sokka before he glanced towards the door to the room they had left.

Sokka felt his insides twist uncomfortably. He held his breath and waited.

“That is one amazing woman. She is strong and brave. Reminds me a great deal of Kya if I think about it.”

“Wha- Suki? Suki is like mom?” Okay, not what Sokka had been ready for. How was Suki even remotely like mom had been? It was true he had lost his mother at a young age, but still. Suki was combative and set in her way. Sokka had never seen his mother remotely raise her voice.

Hakoda chuckled, “That she is. I can see how she won your attention so quickly.”

Sokka began to panic. “What? Dad, Suki and I are not-”

“I can also see you two are a great team. That is important for any relationship.” Hakoda continued, seemingly unaware of Sokka trying to speak.

“No, dad. Listen, it’s really not like that anymore-”

“She would have made a great addition to any team and I can see she is-”

“I don’t love Suki like that!” Sokka blurted. He felt bad, Suki was just a doorway away and there was no way she did not hear him. She was most likely listening very closely now to them and that made the nerves spark in a completely different way.

“Sokka?”

“Dad, if you would just… Listen. Yes, Suki and I were like that at one point. Actually, only recently and for a very brief amount of time. No, we are not that now. At least, I don’t think we are. I’m not, anyway.” He sighed heavily. This hurt his tired soul just trying to form words about it. He had yet to have this conversation with Suki and now he was in it with his father. Spirits, he was tired.

“Forgive me if I read things wrong. You had seemed like such a great team. Speaking through only looks and all. I had hoped that-”

“What? Hoped? Dad, I thought you said you did not care who I gave my attention to? Was that- Is that not true? Or is it just because of who I have chosen to do so with.” Sokka was glaring. That anger from before was building once more but he was so tired.

Hakoda waved a hand to try and stop whatever angry thought had crawled into Sokka’s mind. “Forgive my foolish assumption, son. It was true. It still is. I had only read the situation from my point of view and she reminds me so much of Kya. I should have asked.”

Sokka sighed deeply and moved to lean back against the door. The day had just been too much. “Look, I’m really tired-”

“Tell me one thing.”

“Dad, please.”

“Sokka, please. Tell me who, if anyone, and give your old man the time to process. I want to support how I can but I can’t if I don’t know.” Hakoda gave a pleading look and Sokka caved.

“I don’t know how yet, so don’t ask. All I do know is that I will do anything for him. That this whole situation was shitty and all I want to do is go lay with him even if he would hate it. Dad, I think I-” He bit his lip. It was so hard to say. It was terrifying, and he could not find the words despite knowing them by heart.

“So, it is the prince then. I had my suspicions before Jet even made mention of it after how quick you were to defend him, but had thought Suki proved it otherwise. I am sorry I put my past as a blanket over this and I am so proud of you, son. He is a powerful person, in more ways than one. He is also going to never give you a moment of peace, I hope you realize this.”

Sokka could not help the smile as he thought about just how true those words were. He nodded in agreement and breathed out a quiet “yeah. I know.”

“Good. I’ll… leave you to rest.” Hakoda said. It had sounded like he wanted to say more but he held back for reasons Sokka could not figure out. He was just so done with the day and all it had brought them. “I’ll come get you when we reach the mountain range.”

With a nod Sokka watched his father head back up the hall and towards the engine room. He was most likely going to rotate air pumping duties with Chit Sang but right now Sokka’s entire being was screaming for sleep. Without much thought he turned and opened the door only to watch as Suki shuffled away with a sheepish smile.

“Hey, sorry. I heard my name and wanted to make sure everything was alright.” She quickly added.

“Yeah, no biggie. I had a feeling you would.”

“You look awful.” Suki added as if an afterthought as she looked him over.

“Gee, thanks. And here I thought I was at the peek of my beauty.” He rolled his eyes only to watch her snicker.

“You better hope not or else Sunshine may think twice about this.”

“Okay, ow. Wound me why don’t you?” Sokka mocked hurt before he sighed heavily and turned to face her. “Suki, I think we should talk-"

“Really, Sokka. That can come later, trust me I think we may be on a similar page at the very least so don’t worry so much. Just go get in bed and get some sleep. Oh! And don’t you dare move Zuko around or I will kick you out.” Her tone was sharp but Sokka could only grin as he hurried to remove his armor.

She had given him the okay to do the one thing he had been thinking about on and off since they reached the balloon and he was not going to wait for her to take it back.
