Once the last bit of the heavy and hot metal hit the floor with a admittedly too loud bang he spun and crawled up onto the side of the bed. They had made things easy for handling Zuko’s care by laying him so that his right side was on the edge, but that put Sokka between Zuko’s left and the wall. It made crawling up to join him a bit tricky and Suki had clucked her tongue in annoyance when the movements made Zuko moan lightly.

Once he was up far enough on the bed Sokka flopped down between the bender and the wall. He had every intention of not touching Zuko and just being happy to join in the bed, but the makeshift nest was making that hard. The layout of the pillows and blankets was like a slide. No matter how much he scooted back or away he found himself moving down into the cold figure next to him.

“Would you quit moving around? Keep it up and I will drag you out.” Suki huffed.

“Look, I am trying! These things are slippery.” He huffed and scooted back yet again.

“Then quit fighting to put a weird distance and just let Zuko syphen your body heat. Besides, that helps them regain energy.” She rolled her eyes and pulled a few blankets back to allow Sokka to settle next to the chilled firebender.

Sokka felt his face heat up as he stopped fighting the pull of the blankets. He let them pull him flush against the left side of the cold body. Felt Zuko shudder and sigh as Sokka settled into the nest of blankets Suki threw over the two of them. He thought he would never actually get any sleep like this. That was, until he had relaxed into the nest and let the sound of steady breathing lull his world into nothingness.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

This chapter fought hard and I am not quite sure how I like it, but instead of leaving it at no update for a week have a beast of a chapter.

Let me know your thoughts on this!

## Chapter 20

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Pair this chapter with a nice cup of something warm, we are going into some much needed conversations.

### Text

Warm. That was the first thought that came through. It was warm and cozy. The last few hours tried to come forward but the warmth kept the memories in a tight hold as he chose between sleep and waking. It was just so warm and his pillow was moving in such a calming way.

His pillow was moving. That felt off somehow. He had not fallen asleep on Appa that he could remember, so how was it his pillow was moving? Sokka cracked his eye open to take in the room and the last few hours came rushing forward.

The fight with Azula, the lightning, pale skin, the air balloon, Zuko on the ground. Zuko, who was currently his pillow as he continued to sleep. Sokka shifted enough to prop himself up onto his elbow to really look at the firebender. His hair was soaked with sweat. He was still extremely pale, even for him, and the dark rings around his eyes stood out just as badly as the scar.

Sokka let his focus travel down towards the bandages that had taken over the majority of Zuko’s chest. The color of the bruising was still peeking out of the edges to remind one of just what was being concealed.

“He is doing better, you know.” Suki whispered. She had come back into the room carrying a small bundle of towels in her hand. As she moved into the room he saw that she also had a small bag which she set on the nightstand with care before she dipped a towel into a bucket of water.

“I thought you said he is better.” Sokka stated as he watched her move the damp towel to lay it over the sleeping benders forehead. That was what Katara did when someone had a fever, and those were not good.

Suki only chuckled and grabbed the small bag which crunched in her grasp. “He is, his body has been through a lot though and sometimes a fever is just a response to it all. Once his system relaxes it should break.”

He watched as she brought the crunchy bag over to the tightly wrapped shoulder and gently placed it. Zuko instantly reacted with a pained grunt and shudder but could do nothing more. Suki ran a hand through his hair and whispered calming words as Sokka reached to touch the bag. Cold. It was ice cold, which meant she had found the ice that he could not.

“Right, how long was I asleep for?” Sokka pulled his hand away from the bag and moved to push off the bed to sit up. He was given the sharpest of looks from Suki when the attempt was met with a whine and shudder from the firebender.

“Not long, and don’t you dare move him around. He takes forever to get to sleep, you know that?” Suki huffed.

“Takes forever? Wait, has he woken up!?” His voice was admittedly a bit louder than he had intended and the glare he got for it had him shrinking back down into the bed. He curled back up against the firebender and gave a guilty grin.

“You are ridiculous.” She rolled her eyes but the look in them was soft. “Yeah, he has woken up twice now. Once every hour, though I wouldn’t really call them waking up. More just trying to move and delirious. Last time I think he was with us for a moment when he saw you. It was cute, really, how red he went until he fainted.”

Sokka felt his face heat up at the thought of being seen by Zuko and turned to hide in the blankets. If his plan worked, he would never see the light of day again. Of course he never got his way as Suki started to laugh way harder than she had any right to.

“You really like him, don’t you?” She chuckled, trying to control her laugh as best she could as she watched him slowly peek out from the covers.

“I was hoping it was just a matter of adrenaline, you know?” Sokka grumbled.

“Oh? And you have reason to think it is not?”

“Suki, I…” He sighed. He had known they needed to talk but he was just at a loss for the words.

“Look, Sokka. If it helps I think we are on a similar page. I also heard you and your dad talking yesterday. I am not dumb, I know just who caught your eye. He is pretty eye catching after all.” She winked and moved to pull a now overly warm towel off of said persons forehead.

“I had hoped that once we left that place things would settle and go back to some kine of normal.” Sokka groaned and moved to lay his cheek on top of Zuko’s left shoulder. He curled close and moved so that his hand was resting on the hand strapped down.

“Uh-huh, and how is that going?” She asked while wetting the next towel as she glanced towards the two boys with a knowing smirk.

“I don’t think I understand what normal is.” He mumbled.

Suki laughed hard, caught off guard. “Yeah, me either.”

“Suki?”

“Hmm?”

“Is… is it okay?” Sokka asked in an uncharacteristically quiet voice. His words burned and the fear of saying something wrong was rising.

“Is what okay?” Suki asked as she placed the damp towel and sat back to look towards them. “I don’t think I am following where your mind is going and it sounds important that I do.”

“Is… is it okay… if I like him?” Sokka’s face had once more begun to burn up as he focused his attention onto the hand he was currently holding. He was trying hard to avoid looking towards Suki, fearing what he may see if he did.

“Oh Sokka, of course it is.” Her hand settled onto his and he flinched. She did not remove the hand, only gripped his tighter. “Nothing is wrong with this. With any of this. Sokka, look at me. Please.”

It took more than he could ever begin to put into words to drag his look away from the hands all laced together. Slowly, he allowed his sight to shift until he was looking towards her. She looked like she was about to cry and Sokka could not see how that was any better. This was his doing. He had made her upset, just as he had let his love for her go and be replaced.

“I am not upset, if that is what you are worried about. Sokka, I love you, but I also understand. Besides, he is a really great guy and is trying so hard to right his wrongs. If anyone has any problem with this then you send them to me. I’ll set them right, you hear me.”

Sokka could not keep the eye contact. It hurt and he closed his eyes when the sting of something more became too much. Suki, for her part only held his hand as he let himself fully accept that this was real. That he was in love once more, and Suki was supportive of this despite it not being her. Maybe there was hope?

“Thanks, Suki. Have I told you how amazing you are?” Sokka sniffled as he shifted to rub his damp face with the end of a blanket.

“Many times, but it is always great to hear. So, you really like him.” That was not a question. He knew it wasn’t.

“Yeah, I think I really do.” He sighed and moved just enough to sit up slightly and look towards Zuko’s sleeping face.

Without a thought he leaned forward slowly and placed a ghost of a kiss to the hot skin of his left cheek. The scar edge was rough, but the skin just under it was soft even with the feather light touch.

“Careful lover boy, I think you two should talk about this before you get all touchy.” She chided with an eye roll for extra measure.

“Yeah, yeah. You are right. I haven’t been thinking things through lately, sorry.” He flushed.

“Just, be gentle and give him time to think. I don’t think he has a good background for this kind of thing and heavy affection might just scare him more than anything.” Suki sighed heavily and directed a sad glance to the sleeping teen. She reached out a hand and gently fixed some hair out of his face before looking to Sokka.

“He really doesn’t have that great of role models for this stuff, huh? I mean. I can’t imagine his family was a real great start. Don’t think they know what a hug is if one isn’t holding a knife.” He snorted and smirked at hearing Suki match his laugh.

“Sokka, I am serious. Be open and honest, but let him come to you.”

“Yeah, yeah. But, do you… you think he will come to me?” Sokka glanced towards her, hope clear in his words even if he was trying hard to hide it. He had thought this would be something he would just hide away once they got back. Maybe put some distance between himself and the jerkbender for equal measures. Maybe, just maybe, he wont have to do that.

“I don’t know. It is his choice, really. If you are asking if I think he likes you though I think you both are dense. Sokka, he followed you around like a deer pup. He looks to you with such deep trust and the last few times we have all been together he looked to no one but you.” She leaned back in her chair and crossed her arms, mocking deep thought.

“I mean, that could be because I am the plan guy.”

“Could be. There is always that chance, but the odds of it being the start of something more are pretty good. Besides, he can’t lie for shit so we can always just ask.” She flashed a wicked smile that made Sokka wonder if introducing her to Toph was a good idea after all. No telling what those two together would create.

“I suppose I have more of a chance than I originally thought at least.”

“How so?”

“I mean, I don’t really understand it much but he and Jet clearly had a relationship. You said so yourself that you thought it went far deeper than just physical.”

“I did. I believe it, too. How does this mean you have a chance?” Suki asked, clearly not drawing any connections.

“Well, for one it means he likes guys to some extent.” Sokka sighed and laid back into the bed. Suki just stared at him with a blank expression before she rolled her eyes and chuckled.

“Yeah, alright. I’ll give you that one. Yes, him having a past with Jet means that at the very least guys are not out of the question.”

“Right! See, now you get it. Still can’t see it really. Jet and Zuko, it is just so… impossible, you know?”

“No, I don’t know. You really can’t see it?” She sounded serious and it made Sokka shift back up to look towards her again. She looked equally serious and this made him think over all the interactions between Jet that he had seen. No, none of them spoke of anything but hatred.

“Are you saying you can see it? From where I sit, all I see is two people so opposite each other that they would burn the world down before they got anywhere close to a relationship.”

“I’m not saying it was a healthy one, but I can definitely see it. Easily, actually. Those two would have been a force to be seen. They would have been a true power couple.”

Sokka snorted. “You are really stretching things now, Suk. No way they would be anything but a powerful enemy.”

“Oh really? Think about it. Jet would have been the idea behind the action. He would have been the brains and the talker. Zuko would have been his storm. He would have given the trust and energy he does to you to Jet and his every idea. Would have pushed him harder than anyone. They would have been intense in everything they did. They probably were. That kind of excitement was bound to burn bright on every level and can be intoxicating. It also risks fizzling out just as fast.”

“Wow, Suki. That…” Sokka was at a loss for words. He was too busy thinking of Jet. Of him being a smooth talker and Zuko falling for it just as all the others had before. Of Zuko’s desperate need to prove himself putting him in the position to fall ever deeper into some kind of trap that is Jet. Of Jet watching the teen with such passion and finding himself equally captivated. The two of them would still clash, but all of Jet’s little jabs suddenly made so much more sense.

He had not just been poking fun. He had seen that Sokka was growing to like Zuko and taken the chance to rub it in his face that he was to be second to himself. As if that mattered. It did not matter that Jet had some weird past with Zuko, or that that past sounded like it went far beyond anything Sokka had ever even thought about.

What did matter was that it had hurt Zuko. It had cut him deeply and if that pain affected his self worth or reactions to relationships then Sokka would fly Jet right back to that prison himself. Maybe he would just drop him into the boiling lake depending on just what he had done in the past. Zuko had little understanding for what was toxic and unhealthy when it came to people interacting with him and if Jet used that to his advantage then he was not going to hold back.

“Sokka.” Suki’s voice was quiet as it cut through his thoughts. He sat up slowly, careful this time not to jostle Zuko. “About us.”

“Oh, yeah. I suppose that is a topic we have kinda danced around, isn’t it.”

“A bit, yeah.”

“Look, Suki. I am so sorry. I never meant for this to happen-”  
“Sokka, please. It’s okay, really. I get it. Besides, I had a feeling this might happen.”

“Wait. You had a feeling I would fall for Zuko?” Sokka looked to her very much confused. How was that possible?

“No, not that. I will admit that bit was a twist. I meant that we were not going to fit like we might have done months ago. That we were not gonna make a good relationship at the moment.”

“Oh, what gave that away?”

“Honestly? The kiss.” Suki gave a shy smile.

“Wha- H-Hey now! Are you saying I’m a bad kisser!?”

“No!” Suki laughed. “No, not at all! Just, that it felt lacking. It was good, just, no spark if you get what I mean.”

“Yeah, I do. I felt that too. Like kissing Katara.” He scrunched his nose at the very thought. He hadn’t kissed his sister in years, and he was not about to start now.

“Like kissing one of my Kyoshi warriors. I agree. So no, while I may be a bit heartbroken, I am not upset at any of this.” They shared a smile and sat in silence for a few minutes.

“So.” Sokka pushed.

“Hmm?”

“I’m not a bad kisser?” He asked just to poke fun.

“Yes, you are.” Zuko’s usually scratchy voice sounded awful as he croaked out those words. Sokka felt his face explode into heat and he looked down only to be met with one sliver of gold looking back at him.

“Zuko! Hey buddy, how are you feeling?” Sokka dropped back to his side and propped himself up just enough to look over the benders face. He was scanning for any signs of pain or discomfort and easily found it.

“Feels like I got hit by a war balloon. ‘m cold.” Zuko shuddered as if to sell his last point and Sokka moved to tuck the blankets closer.

“Hey Sunshine. Can you tell me your name?” Suki asked as if that bizarre question was normal.

“Hm?” Zuko scrunched his eyes shut tight again in thought “Zuko. And before you ask 17, burner room, everything hurts but nothing major.”

“Okay, help a guy out. What is happening?” Sokka looked between them. What was with this weird conversation?

“I am testing to see if he is with us this time or if he is still in the fever. I’ve asked him these every time he wakes so at some point he remembered them. I start with the persons name, age, last memory in his case, and check in how they are feeling.”

“Oh, and how did he do?” Sokka looked back down to see that Zuko was awake but had yet to pull his eye back open. He reached out and brushed the back of his fingers to the warm right cheek and was rewarded with the eye snapping open wide to stare at him.

“Sokka, hands off. I mean it. Talk before touch or I will hang you to dry.” Suki slapped the hand away from Zuko’s face as she moved to check his pupil. “He did fine. He is with us, aren’t you?”

Zuko nodded, eyes ignoring Suki’s instructions only to be fixed onto Sokka in question. Suki snapped to try and get the attention she wanted to finish her checks. When it was met with little to no focus from the bender she huffed and smacked Sokka’s shoulder as he sat up.

“You went and did it now! What did I just say? Now, you two talk or you are kicked out of this bed until you do.” She threw her hands up and turned to check the melted bag of ice. Grabbing the bag, she stormed out to provide some time for just what she had told him to do.

“Suki, wait!” Sokka flinched as the door slammed. Great, he really did need to talk to Zuko but he felt a bit guilty for distracting him during her check.

“I’m sorry…”

Well that was one way to snap out of a guilty feeling. “Not your doing. You did nothing to be sorry for. If anything, I should apologize to you. I’m sorry I am in your space and that I touched you without asking.”

“s’okay.” Zuko was mumbling. He did this when he was unsure of how to answer. Sokka sighed and sat up fully. Where was he even supposed to begin?

“Are you and Suki…” Zuko asked, though he did not finish his question. Sokka did not need him to.

“No, we are not together. We came to that understanding just now, actually.”

Zuko flinched and mumbled, “oh, sorry.”

“What for? You have nothing to be sorry about. It was a mutual choice, so no worries.” Sokka flashed a quick smile down towards Zuko still laying back. He pulled his knees up to his chest and leaned back against the wall, feet touching the fever hot benders side.

“Hey, Zuko.”

“Hm?”

“What do you mean I am a bad kisser?” He supposed that was the best starting point, given he had no other ideas.

Zuko, for his part, turned bright red and looked anywhere but Sokka. He stumbled through a few different starts to a thought only to grumble and glare at the far wall like he could melt it. Sokka grinned wide. That was a promising reaction.

“I couldn’t hear you, buddy. How do you know I am bad?” He teased. He was testing his luck but maybe it would be worth it.

“Cause.” Zuko grumbled as he ran his tongue over his lower lip, pausing to touch a cut that split it.

“That is not really an answer, dear Zuko.” Sokka let himself slide along the wall to once again be laying next to the bender. The blankets did their job and pulled him up against his left side. Zuko looked back towards him startled and the dusting of pink across his cheeks grew to a deeper shade as it spread downwards.

“B-Because it was all teeth, the first time.” He swallowed hard.

Sokka watched as Zuko’s line of sight had traveled to his own lips and something deep inside sang in giddy joy. At least on a physical level, Sokka had an answer. Wait, split his lip? That first time?

Oh, right. The gondola, when he had thought he had lost the sun. He had been so relieved that he had smashed into Zuko full force. He had also stolen a kiss without thinking and had even forgotten about doing so. It had been so unplanned and things had happened after it that Sokka had forgotten it completely. Clearly, it was not the same for Zuko.

“Oh, Zuko. I- I am really sorry about that. I should never have-” Sokka watched the second Zuko’s masked clicked into place. The very moment his words had been heard, memorized, and misinterpreted. He was not about to let this happen again. Not after all they just went through.

“Zuko, wait. I- Look, I need you to listen to me. Really listen. No thoughts allowed because you always think the worst and that is just not true.” He waited until the look of cautious curiosity settled onto Zuko’s features before continuing.

“It is true I never should have kissed you, but not for why you may think. I should have not done it as an ambush. It should have been something we talked about. Something agreed upon. Something both of us were comfortable with and not something stolen.”

Zuko’s eyes had blown wide again. Sokka could practically hear the gears working in his mind as he thought through the words. He waited, giving Zuko the time he needed to think. He also noted that Suki was gone for a long time on a simple ice run.

“…Okay.” Zuko finally broke the silence.

“Okay.” Sokka repeated. He took a deep breath and felt his face burn as he looked back toward the golden suns.

“Okay, so. Here goes everything. Zuko, you are the biggest pain in my life in both the worst and best way possible. Ever since you joined this mission you have literally been my every thought and I would very much like to try an actual kiss.” He rushed through the last bit, hoping he would not have to say it again.

They sat in stunned silence for a good deal longer than Sokka thought should ever be legal. He was ready to take the rejection, felt it in his very being. He had even begun to slide back to get out of the bed when he heard the quietest ‘okay.’

He froze, it was possible that he had misheard. It had been so quiet. Slowly, he looked back towards Zuko who was once again looking at the far wall.

“What?”

“I said okay.” Zuko huffed and turned to glare in the most guarded way he could. Sokka only met the glare with the largest smile and moved to be propped up by his head once more.

“You sure?”

“Yeah. Go ahead.”

“No, hold on. Don’t do this because I want it. If you don’t then that is it, done. I won’t bring it up and we can forget-” Sokka was rambling but he was concerned. He had not wanted to pressure Zuko into anything and knows he is one to give if he thinks it will help others.

“Sokka, shut up. I never said I did not want it. Hurry up or I will change my mind.” He challenged, the glare clearly in place to hide something more.

Sokka swallowed hard, his heart beat was out of control as he looked towards the door once. Slowly he closed the distance, Suki’s words still making him hover just before any real contact. He had made the move, but it was up to Zuko to complete it.

Zuko let out a frustrated noise and with a twist Sokka felt the soft, warm lips he had somehow forgotten connect with his own. He pushed closer, his mind singing at the contact as he melded his lips to move with Zuko’s. They were warm and so soft.

Sokka’s free hand snaked its way to cup the back of Zuko’s neck and tilt his head in just the right way to send a spark through the kiss. Twisting his fingers into the hair at the base of Zuko's neck, Sokka pulled slightly and shivered at the small gasp that escaped the bender. The lips he was ever focused on parted and he had had every intention on taking advantage of that fact when the door clicked.

Sokka pulled back fast, jostling the bed hard. He instantly regretted it at the pain groan that escaped Zuko. Zuko, for his part was working hard to regulate his breathing as fast as he could before the door opened to allow Suki entrance. No matter what they had done, her sharp eyes picked them apart and she grinned.

“Good talk, I take it?” She shut the door and winked as both boys flushed. Sokka groaned.

“Suki, must you?”

“I must. Sunshine, did he ask?” Suki set the new towels and ice bag on the nightstand and busied herself with only a quick glance to Zuko when he did not answer.

“What?” He croaked out in confusion. Sokka did not blame him. He was missing quite a bit of context.

“Did he ask before touching you?” She sent a glare towards Sokka.

“Oh, uh. Y-Yeah. An annoying amount, actually.”

“Good, and you answered clearly?”

Zuko only nodded in return.

“And you are okay with this?”

“Not sure what ‘this’ is.” Zuko huffed. Suki glared at Sokka.

“Hey, buddy. I think she means us.” Sokka nudged Zuko’s side only to be met with a confused look.

“What about us?”

Sokka groaned. Right, he had not been as clear as he had thought. “About this. Kissing and stuff.”

“What the idiot is trying to say, is that he would like to maybe try a relationship, if you are wanting. Give it time, think it over. You do not have to answer right away.” Suki gave a gentle smile towards the bender as she placed the new ice pack to his shoulder.

“Yeah, she is right. No rush or anything! Just, if you wanted to try. We could… go on a date or something! Maybe actually try fishing? Or training! I could even help you make that gross leaf juice!” Sokka was trying desperately to think of ideas. Just what did people actually do for ‘dates’ when they were on the run?

Zuko stared between the two of them as if they had suddenly grown more heads. He let his head fall back with a pained sigh and shifted like he was going to sit up. Suki had a hand on his chest within a second and was actively preventing his movement.

“Absolutely not. Zuko, you are burning up and need rest.”

Zuko only groaned in response as he slumped back into the bed with a shudder. He blinked a few times and a hazy sheen seemed to dull the sharp gold of his eyes.

“Hey, what’s wrong? What just happened?” Sokka leaned forward again to turn Zuko’s face towards him. He noted that this gained little reaction, only lazy blinks of confusion. It was as if Zuko was sleeping with his eyes open suddenly.

“He’s delirious. Sorry, Sokka. There is a pretty good chance he won’t recall much of what just happened without a reminder. Even then he may think it all a dream.” Suki sighed and began running her fingers through his hair.

“Is there… can I do anything?” Sokka wanted desperately to be able to do something. To help in some way even if just a little.

“Actually, we are pretty close to the western mountains. Maybe you should go to the navigation room and help them. I will stay with him and get him to sleep, don’t worry.” She flashed a smile in his direction before turning to return to the task of preparing a cooling rag.

“Right. Yeah, okay. I’ll go do that.” As carefully as he could he shimmied off the bed, trying hard not to disturb the muttering bender.

If he tried he could make out words but they made little sense. Most likely, Zuko was speaking in the Fire Nation script but one name cut through. Iroh.

He was asking for his uncle even now and Sokka felt his chest squeeze tight. Wherever that man was, he would be sure to tell him just how hard Zuko had worked to better himself. That he was loved, and that the number of those who supported him was growing every day. Sokka had a hard time believing that jolly old man would ever really turn his nephew away. If he would, then he no longer deserved him in Sokka’s opinion.

With a final glance back towards the two huddled together, Suki using her arm to keep the clearly confused teen down, Sokka left the room. It hurt to do so but he was needed elsewhere.

The tingling in his lips had yet to settle as he brought his hand up to his mouth to hide the grin. Zuko had started that kiss. He had kissed back, and he had said he had wanted to. Sokka did not care that the fever may have stolen that memory away for the time being, it was his and he would just work harder to remind Zuko of his affection later. Before that, he needed Katara to look over that shoulder so he could have a proper cuddle time.

He rushed through the hall and towards the navigation room. His heart felt far lighter than it had in a long time and he knew that he had the biggest grin but he could not help this reaction. It might take a bit of work, but he was pretty sure he was about to come away from a prison break with a boyfriend and that was worth letting the world know he was happy for the first time in awhile.

Okay, he was going to have to hold back a bit. Zuko had to give him an answer before he got too far ahead or Suki would skin him where he stood. Since when had she become so protective?

He pushed the door open to the navigation room and quickly looked around to see just who was there. Jet was manning the valve for air control and keeping the balloon on the correct path. He was focusing hard on his task, which meant he had yet to notice Sokka had returned. The way his brow scrunched as he looked from the pins to the mountains ahead spoke of deep thought and Sokka had half a mind to sneak up and scare him. He would wait, while they were almost out of the danger from the last few hours they still had some time before they reached the cliff edge.

A loud bang from the back of the room drew Sokka’s attention and he watched as his father kicked something on the wall once more. He hurried over to see just what had gained the need for such actions.

“Dad, whoa, what’s with the kicking. We worked hard for this beauty, remember?” Sokka flailed a bit. He would very much like to keep this balloon if he could. It was a nice piece of tech, and he was dying to dissect it later.

“Sokka, how are you feeling?” His father smiled towards him and pulled one of the bolted down drawers back as if trying to break it.

“Yeah, great. I’m fine. All better and thinking clear again. Just one question, what are you doing!?” Sokka tried to push back against the drawer to keep it down. There was no telling what this kind of damage would do while they were literally hundreds of feet in the air. Not to mention a certain firebender currently so out of it that a fall was bound to do him far more damage than the others.

“I heard a crack form I believe but I need to see for myself. It has been whistling as well. We think a bird hit it and Jet says the air pressure is demanding Chit Sang pump the burner more than before.” He finished the statement with another rough tug and the metal groaned.

“Alright, so maybe we took a hit. That’s fine. We are okay, dad, really!” Sokka pushed back once more. “I can go out on the walk line and see. Just, stop tearing apart the inside before we land please!”

Hakoda let go and backed away from the wall. “You sure? I don’t think I like the idea of you going out there on the walk line. Besides, the sun just set.”

“Wait, the sun set?” Sokka looked around once more and realized that it had indeed dipped low. There was still a good dusting of color so he had not noticed but it made sense. “That explains it.”

“Explains what?” Hakoda asked as he bent to look at the damage to the wall he had caused. Sokka rolled his eyes, sea folk were just not meant for the air.

“It explains Zuko.”

A snort from the front told him that this was not a private conversation.

“Nothing explains that mess.” Jet chuckled and glanced over his shoulder towards the two. He froze, looked them over, and grinned wide. “But maybe you know that already.”

“Jet, not now.”

“No, I think now is good, actually.” He turned to check the air gauge once more before glancing back. “How was he?”

“He is fine. He will be better when he gets to Katara.” Sokka ignored the eye roll that answer received and turned towards his father.

“That is good to hear. I was hoping he would wake a bit but last time I checked in he had yet to do so.” Hakoda pulled Sokka into a side hug and held tight. Years away and Sokka had forgotten just how physical his family used to be. No wonder he could not stop thinking about being close to people.

“He woke up for a bit while I was there, actually. I think he was with us until the sun dipped low. Then his fever kinda overtook him and Suki is working to get him to sleep.” He leaned into his father, finding it comforting despite not being a child anymore.

“That is wonderful! He gave us all a good scare.” Hakoda chuckled.

“That’s about all he is good for.” A loud whistle followed the statement and Jet worked quick to shut the valve to the balloon.

“Jet, leave him alone would you? He is not even here-”

“No, but you wish he was.”

“What- What is that even supposed to mean? Of course I do! If he was here that would mean he would not be bedridden because of me!”

“Son, this was not your doing-”

“Dad, I respectfully disagree.” Sokka twisted out of the hold that had gone from comforting to far too constricting. He sent a glare towards Jet. Just what was he trying to get out of him, what game was he playing?

“Come off it, Sokka. I’m only joking around. That man will always end up in a bed at the end of an adventure, one way or another.” The look, the wink, the knowing eyes. Sokka was gonna commit a crime and he would not feel bad for it.

“Whatever you are trying to say, I’d stop while you can. Remember, we are on our way to my camp. To my friends. To my sister.” He emphasized every point.

“Actually, that is something I wanted to ask about.” Jet simply hummed in thought. This man was infuriating! How could he just switch from talking about Zuko like that to a completely different topic as if it was nothing more than talking about the weather!?

“Before we do, I would like to know just what you meant by the prince ending up injured every time. That seems to be something important to be aware of.” Hakoda pushed. His concerned fatherly voice was seeping through and Sokka could not help but smirk at thinking about Zuko being babied by him. The bender might just implode at having a loving father look towards him.

“Ah, I wouldn’t worry about it too much just yet. He is accident prone at the worst of times, but he is active at the best. Though that might be more of something to talk to your son about. From that grin he had when he got here I’d say he is already figuring out just how willing and receptive he can be.” Jet flashed a large grin in Sokka’s direction and immediately ducked as the nearest tool to Sokka’s hand found its way into the air. It was thanks to years of using a boomerang that even with the duck Jet let out a pained grunt upon contact.

“Ah, I see. So… You two talked?” Hakoda looked between his son gearing up for another throw, this time with a compass, and Jet glaring in challenge.

“Maybe!” Sokka said far louder than he had meant as he lobbed the gadget at Jet with less accuracy than before.

“Sokka, maybe that is not the best way to handle this.” Hakoda chuckled. “While I agree that words like those against our loved ones often invoke strong reactions, might I suggest waiting till we land?”

Sokka groaned and dropped the map weight he had grabbed. His father was right, this could wait. He would just add it to his ‘reasons to commit murder’ right along with the ‘gondola conversation.’

“Dad, since when did you like putting a pin into things to look at later?” Sokka sighed while Hakoda barked out a laugh.

“Since I started spending every waking moment with Bato, I suppose. He is a good influence.” Hakoda smiled warmly as he glanced out the window. Sokka did not miss that look and filed it away to talk with Katara. He was not going to jump to conclusions, probably.

“Yes, great, all good. I’m happy for you, Sokka, really. I didn’t mean anything bad about it. Was just joking around now that we have a thing in common.” Jet huffed and rubbed his shoulder.

“And just what would that be?” Sokka retorted and regretted it instantly seeing the smirk it earned.

“We’ve both had our tongues down the same persons throat- OW, Fuck! Why would you through a weight!?”

“Sokka, please, he is by the windows so stick to lighter things.” Hakoda sighed.

“Fine.” With that he swiped up the map and marched forward to hip check Jet out of the navigation port. “And we have not.”

“Oh? Not yet, then. Don’t worry, he gives as good as he takes.”

“Do you ever know when to stop?”

“Nope, but I am getting this all out now so I won’t feel the need to do it around all the others. You’re welcome.”

“Gee, thanks.” Sokka rolled his eyes and looked over the mountains around them. Instantly he recognized some of the edges and quickly took over turning the ship to dip further into the ridge line.

“We close? I couldn’t tell, sorry. Got a bit harder to read your markings the further over the land we got.” Jet leaned his hip against the control panel to watch as Sokka made quick work of all the adjustments.

“Yeah, actually. We are not too far now. Probably make it there right after full darkness.” He nudged Jet to shift further back to give him room to release the air and start a more controlled descent.

“Cool cool. So, they know we are coming back.”

“I mean, yeah. I left a note saying we would be back in a few days. They should be still here.” Sokka had no doubt that they would be.

“That’s good.”

“Jet, what are you trying to ask?”

“Just, do they know that we are coming back in a massive Fire Nation war balloon?” Jet asked as he looked out over the trees coming up as they slowly sunk down towards the cliff side.

“I-… shit.” Sokka groaned. That, could be a problem.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

This is my first time writing anything like this so please, let me know your thoughts, feelings, reactions.

Lots of really needed conversations and a bit of fun! I think we all deserved this.

## Chapter 21

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Might edit later, but this week is going to be extremely busy so have an early chapter!

### Text

The tree line came up to just under the balloon as the sky fully faded to dark. While that was great for getting to the hideout without leading others right to them, it also made their direct course much more suspicious. In the large room of the navigation deck it was clear that no equipment for sending a message was going to work while they were in a free fall. They would have to be trusting in the fact that those in the camp would not risk being exposed while the balloon looked to land.

Sokka was trying to find a flat space to bring the ship down just above the air temple and far enough away from the camp. If he could just put the ship down and get out he could run blocker on any ambush. He could also get to Katara before she went absolutely insane on them. He doubted Aang was going to be a problem, Toph would know it was him if he could get his feet onto the ground, but Katara would strike with good reason and he could not let her hurt them and regret it.

“Jet, might I have a word?” Hakoda was leaning in an equal posture the Jet had taken near Sokka, a deep frown set on his features as he was watching the two boys.

“Uh, yeah sure. ‘Sup?” Jet pushed off the ledge and slowly followed as Hakoda motioned to leave Sokka to the navigation around the rock faces.

“Just a few things I’ve been noticing that I’d like to settle. Nothing a good talk should not help with.” He opened the door and watched Jet leave before looking back towards Sokka.

Sokka had watched them both with a very confused frown. Just what had brought this change to his fathers mood? Sure, Jet was annoying and had moments of obnoxious comments but that was just him. He had been a leader of a bunch of orphans, no telling with what he was used to being okay or not. His father just smiled towards him and nodded.

“If you need us we are in the next room over. We should not be long, do not worry.” With that, the door shut and Sokka was left alone.

He shrugged, they were free to do whatever. He had a task to focus on and it would potentially be the difference between a safe landing and going down hard. He watched as the funny looking gauges and dials continued to move and flicker, none of which really made any sense. The balloon he had made had been far simpler than this beast and landing was going to be tricky.

He remembered very little of the Fire Script that he had been taught thanks to Ruan, but he could make out enough to locate ‘exit’ and directional keys. One was marked ‘down’ and he thought that it may be especially helpful but he was not quite ready to test his theory.

The balloon took a bit longer than he had thought to glide around the mountain range. He was getting far too close to the side with the temple and just had to hope they would slip by at least somewhat unnoticed. If they were noticed, he had a good reason to suspect that those in the camp may just hunker down instead of attack. At least that was Aang’s way and if they were lucky Aang would be the voice of reason.

He began to scan the rock ledges once more as he watched the air temple slowly come into view. As he was getting ready to start testing buttons the door opened once more and Jet returned. He walked right up to the navigation table and slapped Sokka’s hand away from the ‘down’ button.

“Nope, not that. That detaches the balloon from the aircraft according to Chit Sang. Why that button is a thing I have not the faintest idea. What are you looking for?” Jet asked as he leaned forward to squint back out into the darkness.

“A flat space on the cliff face large and stable enough to take the weight of this thing and a way to land. We need to get as close as we can to that mountain in the middle.” Sokka moved without looking to dip the balloon lower yet.

“Why not just fly by and yell at them?”

“One, no way they will hear us. Two, not a chance they won’t take us down.”

“Alright. So, why don’t we land around here and walk?” Jet looked to a ledge that looked to be what was needed.

“Can’t. Zuko would never make a walk even if we carried him. Suki said he is getting better and I want to believe her, but I know shock when I see it. He is in shock with no fire and his body is fighting itself. He needs Katara, and the sooner the better.” Sokka sighed heavily.

He closed his eyes tight to block out the image of Zuko going flush with a wave of fever heat. His pale skin had looked so fragile and Sokka had felt his pulse go from slightly elevated to a frantic flutter. Suki knew what she was doing and she had not seemed worried. He truly believed that was only because they were almost to Katara, and a landing only to walk for who knew how long could be detrimental.

“Alright, so our hands are tied because of one person.” Jet paused and glanced around before he sighed.

“Hey, about earlier. Sorry for talking about you and Lee- I mean Zuko- uhm… Sorry for the things I said. I was just- no. It doesn’t matter, does it, how I felt. What matters is you are right and I have been saying things that really should not be said. So, sorry.” Jet rubbed the back of his neck and focused on the cliff side that was passing by at level height with the balloon.

“Uhm. Thank you?” Sokka glanced towards Jet before going back to trying to read the panel. “Look, I get you and Zuko have some weird thing. Could you please put it aside and leave him alone? You can talk to me all you want but he really doesn’t deserve the things you say when he is not even here.” Sokka sighed and glanced toward the back door. His father had something to do with this and in more ways than one Sokka was grateful.

“I can try. I might make a mistake or slip up but I will try. Though, if he comes at me I won’t sit quiet.”

“No, I didn’t think you would. Jet, thanks. I don’t forgive what you said before but I will accept you moving forward. We can talk more when we land this damn thing.”

“Well, my son. Looks like Tui is blessing your path.” Hakoda chuckled as he walked into the room.

“What?” Sokka popped up from where he had been working to watch the descent. It was true, the moon was glowing as bright as it could over the lands. They could see their path and even small flickers of light on the mountain side that could be the campsite of the others.

“That’s great! I don’t think a spirit has anything to do with it, but bless Yue if it does! Let’s swing around and try the sides-”

The balloon shuddered hard and groaned far louder than any other noises that it had ever made. The nose tipped up as if punched and the valve whistled with the pressure change. Once the nose dipped back down to level off those inside dragged themselves off of the floor to scan the rock face to locate just what had happened.

Sokka noticed the movement on the ledge, the shadows dancing in the moonlight, and swore so loud he thought Yue might just scold him. With no thoughts he was charging out of the room. Another rattle and twist to the ship had it listing heavily to the left and making it so that Sokka had to grab the door frame or he risked sliding down the hall. As it half rolled, he fought to drag himself towards the deck door.

He swung the door to the outside air open wide as the balloon began to roll back to level off. Jet, for all he was annoying, was damn good at steering this thing. Using the railing, Sokka dragged himself out into the night air and leaned heavily on the support bars.

He scanned desperately for the figures again only to pinpoint shards of ice as they whistled through the air and connected to the side of the balloon. The ice sliced deep and the balloon once more screamed as it twisted violently.

“KATARA!” Sokka screamed and he gripped the railing as tightly as possible. He kept screaming names while the balloon continued to rock as it dropped quickly.

Not good, the most not good of not good. Sokka couldn’t think, could not do anything but latch tight to the siding and scream. They were far too high up and the sounds of the balloon were drowning any noise he made. The rocking and tipping of the balloon was alarming beyond anything else. He could only hope that Suki had somehow strapped a certain someone down or Katara was making her next job so much harder.

Another shower of ice pulled him from his thought and he had to duck to not take a face full of bolder. Great, so Toph had joined and they were going down. Bad just got so much worse. He could only be thankful that they did not have a working firebender at the moment, seeing any fire would sign their death right away.

The next boulder was met with the balloon pulling itself into a near front roll to avoid it. The move almost dislodged Sokka, but did miss the rock. Hats off to Jet, he may be reckless but perhaps they would make it to the ground safely.

“Toph! Katara! Aaaaaaang!” Sokka screamed, it was all he could do and when he saw a flash of white he screamed harder. Anything. He needed anything that he could use to flag them down.

The flash dipped past the railing as the balloon bucked once more to roll away from another massive wave of rock and ice. Sokka could only scream as he gripped to the railing in hopes of not falling to his own death. Once the roll leveled off he heard a noise from the rail that had his head whipping around.

Sitting on the edge, chattering curiously, was a flying lemur.

“Momo!? Momo! Hey buddy! Oh, please. Make them stop! You need to stop them! Please, Momo! Get Aang!” Sokka yelled over the screams of the balloon as it turned. Momo chattered happily as he swooped away with all of Sokka’s hopes. Momo had never listened to him, but there was always a place to start.

He had only moments to wait and see if Momo had actually done what he had asked when a giant ice shard whipped past him, nearly taking his arm with it. The shard had been so close that Sokka had felt the cold of it as it flashed past and embedded itself into the balloon behind him.

The scream from the giant hole made by it told Sokka that it had gone straight to puncture the connector to the burner. This hole was going to be what brought them out of the sky faster than all others. Without thinking, he let go of the railing and grabbed onto the free flapping fabric to try and pull it together. The heat blasting from the hole burned his arm but the hand had already taken damage from heat a few hours ago so it felt little of it. He tried to pull the flap closed as the balloon tipped downward and screamed.

The last thing he had wanted to see as he felt like his skin was being steamed off was the massive boulder ripping through the air at them. They had gotten so close to safety, only to fall in friendly fire. Sokka squeezed his eyes shut tight, not wanting to see the impact, only to hear the rock shatter and felt no change to his current situation.

A roar had Sokka slipping from his hold as he fell back into the railing with a pained scream. His body felt far to hot and everything hurt to move. The balloon was about the roll and he knew he would not be able to hold on this time around. Only, it did not roll, it leveled off and started a slow descent.

Sokka pulled himself up to look over the ledge and saw Appa supporting them into a slower and safe landing. He sighed in relief and sagged into the railing.

“Appa! Please! We need to land.”

Appa growled low in answer and Sokka felt like he could cry. The balloon shook as it fully settled onto the bison and relied on Appa to direct it.

Another flash of color had Sokka looking up despite the painful pull to the red hot skin on his side. The flash dipped low and he realized that it was Aang as he dropped to fly parallel with his bison.

“Hey there buddy, what are you doing- Sokka!?” Aang shrieked and dipped out of sight only to turn back around and fly straight up. He landed on the rail much like Momo had only to look panicked as he took in the sight.

“Oh no… Sokka- We didn’t- H-Hold on!” Just as fast as he had appeared, Aang dropped from the rail and disappeared into the night.

Within a moment, the ice turned to water only to help guide the balloon down. Once the balloon had settled to the ground Sokka rolled from the ledge only to drop a few feet to the ground and laid on the earth groaning. They had made it. He hurt like hell but they had landed and were alive.

Fuck war balloons. He did not care how rare of an opportunity he had with having access to this specific one, or that he had helped create them. The last two times he had been in one it had crash landed. Those were odds he was not about to test again and he groaned even louder as he laid on the ground bathed in the light of the moon. He must have hit his head in the fall because it almost looked like the moon was watching him with concern, but that was crazy. The night was far too cold on his steam cooked skin and all he could do was curse the damn balloon for using hot air.

He only had a moment to lay in agony before voices cut through the balloons continued screams as air escaped. He could not make them out but knew who was coming so it did not matter. He would lay there and wait.

“Sokka!?” His sisters voice reached him only moments before he saw her drop to her knees beside him.

“Sokka, what happened!?” She was muttering in her haste but the relief that came from cold water over his raw skin drowned out most of it. He sighed and smiled as he felt all the angry burns begin to fade into nothing. Thank the spirits for magic water and magic sisters.

“Who did this? Where is Zuko? If Zuko had anything to do with this I’ll-”

“Katara, he didn’t do this. It was the blimp. You ripped it and I tried to stop it from falling by holding it together. It was my stupidity and the hot air that did this.” He hissed as the cool water reached his hand.

“Then why is this hand covered in old-”

“Stuck my hand in the burner.”

“What? Why would you do that? Just use the firebender since you took him along on your brain dead trip-”

“Katara. Leave Zuko alone, he has done nothing wrong.” He glared at her and was met with an equal look. He had forgotten in the two days away just how ready she was to tack every bad thing to Zuko.

“Hey, welcome to the ‘firebenders are not half bad’ club. Took ya long enough.” Toph snorted as she marched up the side of the cliff like she was walking up simple stairs. “Quite an entrance you made there.”

“Hey Toph, yeah, sorry I am late to the party.”

“Whoa, this thing is massive! Who did you steal from, royalty?” Toph broke out into a wide grin as she reached the flat of the cliff side and took in the size of the airship. She stomped a bit harder than necessary as she made her way to them to really look at it.

“Actually…”

Toph paused, glanced down towards Sokka and laughed. “No way! You did, didn’t you? What kind of fishing trip did you two go on?”

Katara moved back to help Sokka sit so she could check his back. “Nothing good. Don’t encourage him.”

“Oh come on! It was good. Well, no, it sucked and still does but it was worth it!”

“Really, Sokka. How is getting shot out of the sky and covered in burns ever worth anything?” Katara huffed as she leaned back, satisfied with her work.

“Not my fault you all decided to finally grow back bones while I was away. Besides, I caught friends and family!” Sokka exclaimed loudly. He motioned towards the pathetic looking airship as it creaked and settled fully into the ground.

Katara gave him a look that spoke of deep disagreement and stood to dust herself off. She looked back to the balloon with doubt.

“You left us, we had to make sure we were safe somehow and you were the one who usually had the ideas on just how to do that. Sokka, how were we supposed to know you were going to come back in something like this? And what do you mean you found ‘friends and family?’ If you say you and Zuko are-” Katara started to really get into a rant and Sokka was ready to take it. He sat pouting only to grin when the door to the balloon opened and Toph twisted to take in the new person.

“She does have a point, son.” Hakoda chuckled as he slowly stepped out of the balloon and down the few steps to the ground. Once his feet hit the ground Toph grinned.

“Way to go, Sokka. That was not what I was expecting.”

Katara stood frozen. She looked over Sokka, clearly confused before she spun to see their father. He held his arms open for her and it took only a moment before she was charging forward. She threw herself into the hold with a sob and clung tight.

“Told you we caught family.” Sokka chuckled as he took Toph’s hand as she helped him up.

“So,” Toph started with a grin. “Where is Sparky, anyway?”

Sokka felt the moment his insides changed to worry. Everything inside twisted as he looked between his sister and the balloon. He knew that he needed her help. She was the only one who had healing but he had not thought about just how he would get her to agree. He had also just forced her to waist energy healing him and hoped that it would not be too much for her to do it once more. He had to find a way to get her to agree to heal the one person she hated the most on this planet.

Toph froze, her grin dropped into a deep frown as she stood holding Sokka’s hand. The grip tightened to an almost painful one and her unseeing eyes widen.

“Sokka. Where is he?”

“What?” Sokka was pulled from his trying to make a plan to take in the fact that Toph was very aware that something was wrong. He swallowed hard, not sure how to tell her and felt as her body tightened up as if ready to fight.

“What happened?”

“Toph, he’s alright-”

“Flying Hogshit!” Toph yelled out, startling everyone who had appeared and gaining all the attention Sokka did not want. She dropped his hand and began to stomp her way towards the war balloon.

“T-Toph, wait!” Sokka hurried to catch her side only to be tripped by a rock that was definitely not there a moment ago.

“Either you bring Sparky out right now or I will go in and find him! What did you do, where is he?” Toph spun to glare at Sokka who was once again on the ground.

“Toph, what has gotten into you?” Katara demanded as she rushed to help her brother.

“Zuko. Where is he?”

“What? Who cares where he is? Probably ran off again-”

“Katara, he is here. I don’t think he can run, actually.” Sokka cut in and regretted it instantly. The moment the words were out Toph was climbing up the ladder to the outer deck of the airship and pulling the metal door away like it was paper. She was terrifying, and was about to see everything.

With one deafening stomp to the deck Sokka hoped she would be enough to get the help they needed. He also feared her reaction the most to the extra cargo they had brought. Toph froze, listening, and slowly turned a disbelieving look towards the group.

“How? It’s… no it is not possible.” She stomped once more, bending the metal under her foot with just the sheer force.

“I’ll be dealing with the zombie later. Sugar Queen, feels like I am gonna need you to follow.” With the final statement she spun on her heel and disappeared into the airship.

“Did she just…” Hakoda began to ask but lost the words needed to finish his question. He was staring at the discarded and crumpled metal door.

“Metal bend? Yeah, that is a Toph specialty actually. Right now I think she is on her way to our makeshift injury ward. Don’t get in her way, but also we should follow her. Katara, she is right. We need your help.” Sokka looked towards his sister and saw her sigh in defeat.

He must have looked awful for her to give in so quickly but he did not care. All that mattered was getting to Zuko, especially after the landing they just had. Suki may also be needing a bit of help if he thought about it. That was a rough landing and they had no way of knowing it was over.

“Just who else do you have?”

“You will see soon enough, not important. What is important is that someone is hurt really bad and feverish because of it all. Please, Katara.” He pulled himself off the ground and reached an expectant hand towards her.

“If it is that bad then we should hurry. I’ll see what I can do.” Katara nodded and let him pull her up to lead towards the balloon.

With the help of his father Katara was up and into the damaged main navigation room. Sokka had expected to see Jet, but he seemed to have thought it best to hide for now. Leave it to him to actually have a good idea for once. If Katara saw him, then getting her to Zuko was going to be next to impossible with how fast she would turn on them all.

Sokka pulled away from his sister to run ahead. His worry over just how those two had faired in the crash landing had begun to bubble over to the point that waiting was going to make him sick. He rounded the corner and saw Toph standing in front of the rooms door with a confused furrow to her brow.

“Who is that with Sparky?” She tilted her head towards Sokka.

“Oh, yeah. That is an old friend of mine actually. Her name is Suki. She is really great and has been caring for Zuko.” Sokka laid a reassuring hand on her shoulder and squeezed.

“Alright.” She made no move to open the door and Sokka felt his nerves go from a buzz to extreme. Toph never hesitated. She was always so sure of herself and she rarely waited for others. She had made it all the way to the room but something inside had her frozen and he needed to know. Needed to be sure that those inside were alright.

He ripped open the door without a word and took in the sight. The room was in shambles. The nightstand was on its side against the opposite side, water spilled everywhere. Pillows and blankets were mixed in with the water as the bed had been tipped up onto its side, spilling everything it had onto the hard metal floor. The main problem was the lack of people as he frantically scanned the area.

“S-Suki? Zuko?” His voice had popped embarrassingly high but he cared little.

“Were alright, just a bit rattled is all.” A voice called from behind the bed frame. Sokka rushed forward and looked over the edge to see Suki sitting up in the corner of the room. She had Zuko in a tight embrace, having him sat in her lap as she wrapped herself around him and used a free hand to grip the edge of the bed. His face was buried into the crux of her neck as a protective measure as she had most likely used the bed frame as a stable point to counter the harsh rolls.

Sokka sighed in relief when Suki relaxed and moved to tilt Zuko so that the others could see that, beyond a nasty little cut to the head, he was the same. That first roll must have taken them by surprise like it had everyone else and if all that was done was that one cut then they had made it out pretty easily.

“Thank the spirits.” He breathed out, relief making his knees shake with the effort of standing. “Katara is on her way, let’s get you guys out from your little makeshift hiding spot.”

Toph had moved into the room after him and helped reach out. Her eyes had blown wide once they made contact with the hot skin of the momentarily dead weight of a firebender as she helped drag him over the tipped bed. Once he was on the ground and easily accessible she stayed by his side, hands traveling the bandages to get an idea of just what was happening. Sokka left her to it as he turned to help Suki up.

“Suki!? Are you alright? You… look alright…?” Katara rushed into the room, missing the people on the floor in her shock at seeing a familiar face appear suddenly.

Suki straightened her clothes and smiled wide. She bumped Sokka’s shoulder in thanks and rushed forward to pull Katara into a tight hug with a delighted squeak.

“Katara! Thank goodness. I was starting to think we would never make it.”

Katara pulled her into an equally tight hug, laughing freely at the sudden attack.

“I am just fine. Bit shaken by that rough landing but nothing major. You lot have gotten a lot stronger. That is good, but it is not me who needs your help. Zuko-”

Katara pulled back to look towards the ground at the figure laying unresponsive to Toph as she started poking for him to move. “No. Suki, I don’t know what he did or told you but-”

“Katara, you said you would help! Look at him! He needs you and is like this because he saved our lives! What in Yue’s blessed moon do you mean, no!?” Sokka exploded. He had gone through everything with the fact that if they could just get to Katara everything would be alright and she had the nerve to look at the man on the ground and say no.

Toph punched a fist so hard into the metal floor that the echoing pop made all in the room jump.

“I can’t even see and I can tell Zuko is really hurt. Stuff whatever shit you are on somewhere else for a fucking minute and help, Sugar Queen!” She snarled.

“Watch your language. And just because I am the only one here who can heal does not mean I have to do anything-”

“He saved my life, Katara. All our lives. Repeatedly. He risked himself to help save our dad! Are you really just going to let him suffer as a thank you for that?” Sokka was seeing red and felt his heart rip as he fought his own family to just treat his maybe boyfriend for injuries that Sokka believed to have played a key role in. He looked to the figure, noting that Zuko was trembling slightly but had yet to wake.

“Katara? Sokka is right, you know. Zuko really did put his life on the line to protect us all. I am not saying you have to forgive anything, but do it for me. Please? His shoulder is really bad and his body is in shock. He needs help.” Suki’s voice was soft and pleading. She turned a worried glance towards the subject of all the attention before squeezing Katara’s elbow to gently tug her forward.

Katara glared at the group before slowly allowing Suki to guide her into the room. She turned her glare to the motionless bender with a snooty huff and Sokka felt his knees finally buckle as she bent to kneel.

“Fine. I’ll take a look. Don’t expect it to be anything, he is just being dramatic.” She huffed as she pulled the water from the floor to hover just above Zuko’s chest.

“He is not! Zuko wouldn’t even tell us he was hurt why would you-” Sokka was kneeling on the benders uninjured side. He had taken his hand without much thought but hearing the words against him had struck a nerve yet again.

“I said I will check him! If you don’t like that answer then leave.” She rolled her eyes and turned to slowly place the glowing hand to the place Suki had pointed.

“Besides, I doubt it is-oh! Oh…” She had moved the water to be more securely attached and froze. Every muscle in her body tightened as Katara put her full attention to what she was working with. She quickly moved to be more stable as she began to work the water around the area. Her other hand moved to start to pull the bandages and Sokka released the hand he had been holding to help. The coloring under made Katara gasp as she pulled more water to start healing the blackened chest.

“Okay, I was wrong. Very wrong. This is not good. The muscles are all a mess. Some are popped, others torn. His shoulder is just barely in the socket and the joint is a mess. I need- I don’t… this is really bad. His blood pressure is far too unstable with all the bruising to really get this figured out. Sokka, get dad. I need help.” Katara had moved to expand a large amount of glowing water over the entirety of Zuko’s chest, easing the swelling but the worry in her face was clear.

Sokka swallowed hard and looked from her to Zuko who had grunted in discomfort and gasped as the cool water dipped around to his back. He shuddered hard and the pitiful whine that escaped had Sokka grabbing his hand once more to comfort as he could.

“What? Why?” He was scared she would stop but knew most of that push back was just a show of anger and she would relent to help eventually.

“I need someone strong enough to keep him stationary without getting in my way. Some of his chest muscles are also close to tearing. What happened? Was he dragged up a mountain side?” Katara asked and Toph shot up at her words.

Without a word she stomped out of the room past everyone on a mission for who knows what. Sokka could not spare her much thought as he watched Zuko shudder once more before he sighed in relief as the water popped something.

“Something like that. He was caught from a free fall at a pretty high speed on a shoulder that was already injured.” Suki informed. She was keeping his head stationary and watching for any signs of pain. For once, it was a hard thing to find as the water began to ease things into place.

“That could do it. Tui and La, this is a mess. We need to get the circulation working first to bring down the fever. This is going to take a few times of healing just to get everything straightened out…”

Zuko jerked suddenly as his eyes fluttered open. Suki was quick to throw her elbow onto his chest to prevent much movement and was met with a moan of pain.

“S-Sokka?” Zuko croaked out, his voice sounded painful as he looked towards the person on his right.

“Not even close. Stay still.” Katara retorted as she continued to work the water.

Zuko instantly froze. “K-Ka…ta…”

“I said stay still! Shut up and just let me work, would you?” She set a glare on him that put an end to his unconscious and frantic pull away from her.

He swallowed hard and fell back onto the floor. Sokka rubbed a gentle thumb over the hand he still had clutched tight. He slowly gained the benders attention and smiled.

“Hey buddy. We made it. Just stay still and let my sister work, yeah?”

Zuko winced and groaned as the water focused once more on the shoulder. He nodded once, panting hard with exertion.

“S-So-rry” He stuttered through a shudder.

“Shut it. If you were truly sorry you would have just-”

“Katara.” Hakoda chided from the doorway. He followed Toph who had a no doubt vise grip on his hand as she pulled him into the room.

“Dad, you don’t understand! He is always hiding behind fake apologies-” Her water had lost the glow and slid off of Zuko with a splash.

“I know enough. I also know that when it comes to injured teammates, it is best to practice forgetting grudges in favor of healing. Healing is always priority, while anger can come later.”

Katara huffed and crossed her arms tight.

“I thought he had taken Sokka to somewhere and I’d never see him again. They had left the dumbest note about fishing and I thought he had taken my brother…” Hakoda moved to her side to grip her shoulder. He squeezed before pulling her into a tight hug.

“I understand. You are so strong, my little waterbender. I would not ask this of you if it was not necessary. Can you maybe put that fear and anger aside for a moment to continue? Or, shall we step away from the situation and come back in a little bit? Best to work with a clear mind, after all.” Hakoda asked, he was providing the space for her to choose and Sokka felt a small amount of guilt for demanding over making sure his sister was alright. She had just healed her burnt to a crisp brother, after all, and had good reasons to fear Zuko.

Katara sighed into the hug and slowly sagged with little energy left to argue. “Okay. He is a body in need. I can work with that. Okay, I’m sorry I was so resistant. I was just so scared and worried and angry for two days. I was up just worrying about things I couldn’t change.”

“Katara… I am sorry. It was all my doing, not Zu-” Sokka started.

“Later. I need to heal him as much as I can and take a nap. We can talk later.” She glanced towards him and accepted the answering nod.

“Alright. Everyone but dad, out. Too many people in here as it is.” She ordered as she stretched once before pulling the water back to her hands.

Suki was the first to move, trading places with Hakoda as she popped up to take Toph’s hand. With a tug she was able to lead her out of the room as Sokka lingered. He was held in place by the pleading eyes Zuko had locked onto him as he debated just what to do.

“Sokka, out. I need to have the room and you will just get in the way.”

“I-… yeah. I know. Just, can I stay to support him?”

Katara turned a searching and confused look at him before rolling her eyes while she pointed towards the door.

“No. He is not a child, he will be fine. Out.”

Sokka swallowed hard and sighed. He turned to look at Zuko who only nodded in understanding and squeezed the hand holding his, causing Sokka to chuckle.

“I’m supposed to be the one doing the comforting, you jerk.” Sokka chuckled and leaned forward to press his forehead to Zuko’s overly hot one. The smirk he received was one he wanted to burn into his memory forever. It was so smug, playful and open that it took his breath away. The small laugh that had followed the smile made his heart flutter.

“I know. You can do that after your sister puts me back together.” The flash of teeth before the small smirk was solidified once more made Sokka laugh openly. He squeezed the hand once more before he sat back.

“Alright. I’m gonna hold you to that you know. You are going to have to deal with me and I will warn you I am an awful nurse.” With one last squeeze he stood and slowly left the room.

He pointedly ignore the openly shocked and overly confused looks his sister was sending the both of them as he slipped out of the room. His heart felt tight with worry and fluttered openly with hope. Zuko was still overly hot but he had been so responsive to Sokka just then. He had asked for him, held tight to him, and joked openly despite his sister being right there.

Once he was given the okay, he was going to be the most obnoxious nurse until that man could use his arm perfectly.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

Please let me know any thoughts with this, I live for comments &#60;3

## Chapter 22

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

Hello, Jet here.

#### Chapter Notes

Air Temple! Finally!

### Text

Leaving the room had somehow been the hardest thing he had done that day. Somehow, leaving him to his sisters care did not make it feel any better. If anything the only reason he did eventually give into her demand was because his father had promised to stay. With the most dramatic sigh he could muster for show, hoping it would lighten the mood a bit, he forced his legs to move against their will.

He shut the door and breathed out. This had been what they had hurried back for. To not only make it back safe, but to have Katara help them. Why did it feel just as bad as it had when he had seen Zuko freezing in the cooler? Why did the walk away from the room hurt just as bad? His feet dragged along the metal floor and a thought hit him suddenly.

Before he left the balloon he spun around to rush towards the engine room. Chit Sang was sitting against a far wall, well away from any of the lose objects and balloon debris that littered the floor, and hidden out of range of the burner. He looked alright, besides some black smudges along his arms and shirt, and was relatively relaxed when he spotted Sokka. Given that this room had been the most explosive and hazardous out of all the balloon, it was a major relief. With nothing more than a quick check in and a nod the two agreed to leave the conversations till they were fully out of the death trap.

Together they made the slow walk in the light of the moon towards the hidden stairs. These were located on the outer ledge and led to the air temple below. One wrong move could have one slipping into the canyon so it was another quiet decent. Once inside Chit Sang had whistled loudly and made comments about the massive upside down structures, much like all of them had the first time they had seen the place, but Sokka was far too deep in his own mind to hear. They had split off at the campsite after some attempts at an introduction and Sokka took that moment to lean heavily onto Suki as she sat talking with Toph. He felt like he could sleep for a week and still be tired, but knowing his sister and dad were still in the balloon was enough to keep him wide awake.

“Why hello there.” Suki chuckled and moved to allow Sokka to rest his head on her shoulder a bit more securely. The only response he gave was a grunt of thanks.

“Someone is being clingy. Glad it is not me he is latched to at least.” Toph snorted as she popped something into her mouth and chewed loudly.

“Yeah, well, you would be too if you had had the few days we did.” He retorted as he reached in front of Suki to attempt to steal whatever Toph was snacking on. The hand did not get close as she instinctively turned away.

“I’m not the one who screwed up a fishing trip so bad that I came back with people. Hands off, I still have questions.” She slapped Sokka’s hand down and set a glare onto the campfire.

“Yeah, alright, fire away.” He sighed and cuddled closer to Suki, abandoning his attempt at food.

“Zombie. I just- how?” She asked, her voice far quieter than before.

“Zombie?” Suki asked. “I’m sorry, who is that?”

“I have a pretty good guess that she is talking about Jet. Toph likes to tack nicknames to people, even if they are longer than their real name.”

“Oh, that makes a lot more sense. Guess we should trade notes sometime. Love me a good nickname.”

“Really? What you got so far?”

“Well, our resident can’t walk a straight line without hurting himself fire prince is ‘Sunshine.’” She grinned.

“Hm, not bad. I prefer ‘Sparky’ myself because he is all spark no blaze.”

Suki laughed so hard Sokka worried she would fall over with him still using her as a pillow. He had not found the nicknames all that amusing but to each their own he supposed. He was just too tired to really care.

“That one I get, kinda, still confused on Katara’s if I am being honest.” Sokka muttered.

“Sugar Queen? Easy. She is all ‘sweet and innocent’ but in reality she has wannabe Queen energy all the way.”

“Is there a problem with being kind but also wanting things fair?” Katara asked, making all but Toph jump. Toph, for her part, just popped another whatever she had into her mouth with a grin.

“Not a thing, Sugar Queen, how’d it go? He feels… stable.” Her grin slipped as she put more energy into listening to the people around.

Sokka sat up and whipped around to see his sister standing with her arms crossed tight over her chest. She rolled her eyes at Toph before she huffed and turned to watch her father approach carrying a sleeping figure. Before he could move to help his father he watched as Katara glanced towards Zuko’s things in a far corner and motioned towards them.

“You can just drop it over there dad. I’ll check things in the morning.”

“What the fuck, Katara!” Sokka barked out as he moved to stand. A glare from his father had him hesitate just enough.

“What? I healed what I could to help his fever break! That is all I can do until it does. You’re welcome.”

“Katara, I do not think it was your healing that was in question. Perhaps it was the fact that you called Prince Zuko an ‘it’ and talked about him like he was something to discard rather than a person requiring care.” Hakoda said softly, urging his children to not raise their voice.

“Well, sorry. Must have mistook my words. I am tired.” She huffed and moved to take a seat across from the others around the fire.

“Liar.”

Katara sent a glare towards Toph who pointedly glared back in her general direction in challenge. Sokka only gave her a disappointed look before he jogged over to help his father. Katara was tired and there was no point fighting that battle at this point anyway.

Hakoda was standing where he had been directed and taking in the general lack of anything with a furrowed brow. He was toeing the paper thin blanket and lack of sleeping roll as he turned to give his son space to join him.

“Forgive me, son. But just who’s corner is this?” He looked to the single pack tucked up against the wall and over his shoulder to note just how far it was from the group. His eyes traveled to the pile of sleeping rolls near each other and his look furrowed further.

Sokka glanced back towards the pile that looked far more like a group living together than this corner and flinched. He had never really given this set up a second thought but now felt just how cold it was being so far from the main huddle. He also realized that Zuko had put himself as far from the usual campfire as he could, whether that was some unconscious move or an effort to minimize threat he was unsure.

“Are you going to tell me this is the corner prince Zuko has been using? He is part of this group, is he not?”

“He is! Er- He is now. I won’t lie, we were not kind to his coming to us for awhile and he may have been forced out here. That will change, I promise.”

The tired and somewhat disappointed look in the answer he received stung. His father was still cradling the subject of conversation, his even and deep breathing the only thing cutting the silence. Sokka watched as his father looked around once more before a quizzical expression settled.

“Katara, I do wonder if there are going to be enough supplies to give to those of us who are joining without much notice.” He called back to the campfire.

Sokka looked around confused as to the sudden change to the conversation. He had been wondering about Zuko being tucked in a corner a moment ago, what was the connection to having extra stuff? With another curious look to the things in the corner he realized just what was missing. Zuko had been sleeping on the ground with nothing more than his pack and a blanket. That may have been fine before, but no way was Sokka allowing for it to continue. Not with the injuries he had sustained.

“I’m sure we can make something, dad. Don’t worry. If anything we can use Appa or stuff an old sack with his fur.” Katara responded. She was too busy poking the campfire back to life to look over.

Hakoda nodded and moved slowly against the corner wall to sit. Once he was seated he took care in shifting Zuko to be comfortably sat in his lap, head resting on his chest. The movements were so soft and gentle that they made Zuko look fragile as he continued to sleep through it all.
