Sokka could not help but be reminded that Zuko, like himself, was nothing more than a teenager and should have been protected from all of this in the first place.

He wondered while watching his father take such care in holding him if Zuko’s own father had ever done so for him. If Ozai had ever been that kind and caring to a child. To his own child. A look to the scar slightly hidden by his fathers prison shirt was all the answer he needed.

“Well, that would work. Sokka, do me a favor and go make me one of those fur beds for the prince, would you? Might be best to not put his shoulder on an uneven and hard surface.”

“If that is the problem then ask Toph to level it out. He has plenty-”

“Katara. What is wrong with you right now? It is not like giving something nice to Zuko is going to burn you, so quit.” Sokka cut in on his way towards the piles of supplies. If it came down to it he would just hand over his own roll. He was at the end of his admittedly short grace line towards this attitude.

Without a glance backwards he decided that Appa was probably a better and comfier option to the problem. He grabbed the nearest old and ripped up sleeping roll that was in the ‘burn’ pile and walked towards the sleeping bison. Appa, who woke when he heard someone coming, only groaned and moved once more to go back to sleep. He gave only happy grunts as Sokka began combing through his fur, tugging slightly at knots to get as much as he could. Appa was not shedding like he was before, but the task was still an easy one.

A shuffle to the side drew his attention away from the bag that he was fighting to keep the stuffing inside. As he peeked around Appa to see just what was shuffling forward he watched Toph slowly stand. She had not reacted beyond that and Sokka knew just what or who he would see. Suppose that little bombshell was bound to drop at some point, at least Zuko was out of the line of fire this time.

Jet saw that he had caught Sokka’s attention and waved, grin spreading over his features as if walking into a scorpion hornets nest was an average evening activity. It fit, when he thought about it. Out of all the people in the room, Jet was the poster child for delusional. Closely followed by Zuko but that was being worked on.

The seam to the make shift bedding popped once and drew his attention back to the task. The fabric was thin and warn and the zipper had long since jammed itself making keeping any of the newly added stuffing in tricky. Sokka huffed a dull thanks when he felt the edge of the bag be lifted from the ground in an angle meant to help.

“Looks like that is all you are gonna fit. Where to?” Jet asked as he twisted his hold on the bedding.

Instead of answering, Sokka only tugged to lead the way towards his father and away from the shocked faces behind him. He had felt the room grow cold and could only hope that Katara kept her ice to herself while they were in the corner. With the extra help they were able to lay out the tattered but fluffy new bed roll. Sokka flopped down onto it and wiggled slightly, testing the feel, and noted proudly that it was very much warm and cloud like.

He also realized it was not easy to get out of and had to take Jet’s hand to be pulled out or risk losing all of the padding out the sides. Jet snickered and helped Sokka dust off as Hakoda moved to gently place the sleeping bender into his new bed. The sight of it all made Sokka feel a flash of pride, Zuko had yet to wake and he was already proving to be a pretty good nurse.

“What. The. WHAT!?”

Right, so the extra cargo was out in the open now. Sokka sighed deeply and focused on the bender sleeping. With the lightest of touches Sokka brushed the hair from the benders face and noted that he no longer felt like he was cooking. Instead, he felt chilled and Sokka reached for the thin blanket to make sure all below his neck was covered. Once satisfied, he finally looked towards the shocked group only to catch Suki’s mild glare at his hand still resting on the bedroll close enough to touch Zuko’s left shoulder. Clearly, her ‘no touching’ rule was firmly in place.

He moved to put his hands up as he looked away from her and towards Katara. She looked two steps from either exploding or running away and had yet to make up her mind. Toph, who had known for a bit now, just looked ready for an explanation. It was Aang who looked so close to crying and conflicted that really shocked him.

“Jet?” Aang asked so quietly it could have been mistaken for the breeze if one did not see his mouth form the name. He had come out of a back room he had been hidden away in with his staff at the ready and a tired look to his posture. Most likely, Katara’s scream had brought him back into the group and he was just as stunned as the rest.

“‘Sup, been awhile.” Jet waved once and smirked, clearly not processing just how close he was to being punted over the edge or locked in an empty room in the temple.

Sokka could not help but snort a laugh. No matter how hard he thought about that little introduction he could not help but think on how weirdly similar it was to Zuko’s ridiculous ‘hello, Zuko here.’ Three words, each one as damning as the last.

“Explain.” Katara turned her glare towards her brother, the demand as cold as her ice.

“I will, just, give me a second? Everyone get comfy, we are in for a long night.” He sighed and double checked the stuffing of his makeshift bed once more. It had not changed but anything to buy time was better than what was about to happen. He stopped shifting the makeshift pillow when he saw golden eyes flutter open for a moment to look around confused. He froze in his movements, hoping they would just close once more.

“He’s not awake, if that’s what has you scared. As long as you don’t shake or talk to him he should sleep.” Toph whispered to the fire. As if her words were all that was needed, Zuko’s glassy look settled onto Sokka for a moment before closing once more.

Sokka sighed in relief, he knew full well Suki would have at least kicked him if he had woken their firebender before the sun rose. With that little moment of panic over he decided it would probably be best to just get this conversation over with. He had his own conversation he needed to address, after all, and that would have to come after everyone was on the same page.

The group sat around the campfire in silence. Suki, Toph, and Katara were sat on one side while Aang was hovering at the outer edge of the bend to the odd circle they were making. Jet had taken a seat across from them with a cocky grin but by the look of Toph he was possibly not as brave at the moment as he seemed. Sokka had taken a spot opposite Aang, on the edge between Toph and Jet so that he could get the most accurate information from her reading him.

“So, long time no see?” Jet offered in an awkward attempt at conversation.

“I’d say. Last time we saw you, you died. So, I suggest you spill it. How are you alive, or is Zombie more accurate than we think?” Toph quipped.

Jet sighed deeply and sagged in on himself. He shared a look with Suki who looked far more understanding than she should. She gave him an encouraging nod and a small smile that was to be for them alone and Sokka’s insides shifted uncomfortably. They had some kind of understanding, and he never trusted a bond with Jet.

With minimal help from Suki at times when Jet’s voice seemed to fail, the group learned of the brainwashing, the inner battle, the fight that they were there for, and the moments directly after. That they had not been wrong in believing him dead, for he had died.

He looked into the fire, avoiding the searching looks of the group around him as he talked about the Dai Lee. How they had found his dying or dead body. How he had known nothing and then suddenly all his world knew was pain. That coming back from death was the worst pain he knew and almost wished deep in his bones that he had stayed dead.

The flinch and knowing look from Aang made the group collectively swallow hard. Suki glanced between Sokka and Aang, searching for an answer and all he could do was mouth ‘later’ and hoped she would wait. He would tell her of Ba Sing Se in greater detail, but for now the pieces of a puzzle were being placed and the picture was not one any of them could begin to process.

If what he was saying was true, and by the horrified look on Toph’s face he was, then they had watched two people die that day only to be faced with the reality that they were alive. They had left one person behind. He had to physically remind himself the Jet was possibly only alive because of the Dai Lee and that they would have not been able to help even if they had wanted to.

As he told of his time in the hospital Suki had moved to his side and had begun to rub his back and he had let the tears fall freely without even the slightest change to his speaking pattern. He had spoken from a memory like reading a book, so detached from it all, yet his body had reacted without his knowing.

When the story finished with him in the prison the whole group sat in heavy silence. They let the weight of his words settle and accepted that they were the truth.

“Jet…” Aang had started only to find he had no further words to say. What could he say? They had known Jet had died, they were struggling to process his survival.

Jet’s body jerked as he sat up straight and stretched in a way to hide his wiping away the tears. It was not as smooth or subtle as his usual movements but no one commented on it. He sent a shy and grateful smile towards Suki who smiled in return and pulled him into a tight hug. Sokka watched as Jet latched onto her as if she were his tether to life and the two shared a whispered conversation.

Katara took the moment to nudge Toph, demanding to know if his story was true without saying a word. Her face went through a mix of emotions at Toph’s nod in confirmation and she settled on emotionally exhausted. The two clinging to each other finally broke apart, Jet chuckled lightly at some quip Suki had made before he turned towards them again.

“So, yeah, worst week of my… not life?” He attempted to joke, but only Toph snorted in response.

“Jet, I can’t begin to imagine everything that you went through.” Aang sighed deeply. “I’m just glad you found Sokka and are alright.”

“Me too. Never thought I would find him in a prison dressed as a Fire Nation guard of all things. Gotta say, that was a shock even for me!” Jet laughed and Sokka flushed at the disapproving look Katara sent him.

“Really? You went to a prison?” Toph asked.

“Of course he did. How else would he find all these prisoners?” Katara huffed only to freeze as she watched Toph wave her hand in her own face to prove a point.

“Blind, Sugar Queen, I don’t know what you see but I see they went fishing and came back with more mouths to feed instead.”

“Right, sorry.”

“Wait, what do you mean blind?” Suki asked as she looked over Toph from her new spot opposite the small earthbender.

“As a dog-bat! I was born blind. I see through the earth though so I know where things are and usually what. Just, clothing and looks mean nothing so sorry. You can get all dolled up and I will never know.”

“Oh, well. That is really cool. Sorry, didn’t mean to ask like that. Don’t mean to be rude or anything.”

Toph waved her off before turning her attention back towards Jet. “So, Zombie, what are your plans now?”

“Well, to be honest I don’t really have any. Can’t really go back to Ba Sing Se now can I?”

“You could-” Aang had started but snapped his mouth shut at the mix of looks his start to that train of thought received. Jet, however, laughed.

“Ah, yeah, I suppose I do have a lot of work to do to get a shred of trust back. Look, I am sorry for everything I put you all through if that helps things start.”

The group collectively turned to Toph, who nodded, and Suki looked towards them all in even more confusion than before. Right, Sokka had neglected to inform her that Toph was both blind and a walking lie detector. Better late than never, though that one was kind of a right of passage so he may just let her figure it out.

“Okay, so, you all met in the prison. Just what in the spirits good name happened in that place?” Katara asked as she looked between those still in the Fire Nation clothing.

“I suppose this is where my story starts.” Sokka sighed.

He was ready to just get this over with and admittedly gave a very abbreviated version of events. He told of how he got the information needed out of Zuko by playing to his need to be helpful, earning a look of disapproval from both Suki and his father who had joined around the fire. Yeah, he agreed with them now and felt those looks were well earned.

From there he told of Zuko forcing his own way into the foolish idea with far more logic than Sokka had put forward to his harebrained scheme. Of the crash that had been the reason Zuko had lost his war balloon and with it a few of his other packs, and how he had yet to even mention that little fact. Of the deep regret at his father not being there, only to be filled with joy at Suki. When he told of Zuko getting found out and captured, he left out his little moment with Suki and her flush and nod of approval to that abbreviation was only noticed by the groups lie detector.

From there it was just the plans of escape at their most basic form and how they were executed. He did talk about wanting to keep Zuko from the cursed cooler and of how Jet had worked and twisted the situation to land him in there despite Sokka’s wishes. Jet, for his part, gave a guilty grin and a quiet ‘well, it worked.’ Sokka glared at him and watched as Jet relented easily by holding up his hands in defeat.

He told them of the cooler boat and how they had abandoned the plan in favor of waiting a day. Of watching his father and finally of the final escape. The gasps when he mentioned Azula was all he needed to really hype up just how Zuko had saved all of them at the cost of his own well being. How they had escaped with their lives, and Azula’s war balloon, only because of him and that he was now doused because of it all.

“Sounds like you guys had a heck of an adventure. I call next time.” Toph grinned, trying to lighten the mood.

“Actually, there is one thing that I still very much need to clear up.” Sokka glared towards Jet and watched as Suki stiffened and shifted away from him slightly.

Jet sighed heavily and nodded. “Yeah, suppose you do. Are you really telling me you don’t see why I did what I did? Sokka, you are supposed to be the smart one here.”

“Wait, what else happened?” Katara asked as she looked between them both.

“Jet tried to kill Zuko.”

“What!?” Toph shrieked as Aang gasped.

“Sokka! That is not true and you know it!” Jet retorted and glared.

“Okay, okay, relax! Both of you. I’m confused. How can it be true and not at the same time?” Toph asked as she glared into the fire while she listened to them both.

“Fuck. Jet, do you honestly believe that you were not trying to kill him?” Sokka snarled.

“I believe that I was trying to save you! I’m sorry that in doing so it held the high risk of his falling.” Jet slumped back and crossed his arms tight.

“Hey guys. I think you are leaving out a lot of detail here. Mind cluing us in? Maybe we can sort through it and come to an understanding-” Aang tried to cut in, only to stop at the glare he received from Sokka.

“I hope we can, too.” Sokka huffed and watched Jet roll his eyes.

“Come off your high horse, would ya? Just because your new boyfriend got a bit torn up doesn’t mean every bad thing was intentionally aimed at him!”

“Boyfriend!?” Katara shrieked and sent an icy glare towards the far corner. If looks could kill, Zuko would have been in trouble. “Okay, Aang’s right. You two need to back this story up and start from the beginning. What happened?”

“I’ll tell you what-” Jet started only to glare as Sokka cut him off.

“No, you are going to twist this in too many ways.”

Jet threw his hands up in defeat and leaned back against his palm, he waved in Sokka’s direction in a passive matter and gave an aggravated huff. “Fine, go on then mister ‘knows everything.’ You tell it.”

“I will. When we all got to the gondola it had already begun to pull away from the platform. Zuko was left behind because he was our only fire power and the guards had cut off his retreat. By the time he had a chance to try and make it we were a significant distance away and he had charged the ledge and jumped for the gondola. I had thought him crazy, as I think everyone here would agree, but it would have worked if he had a stable point to grab. I had reached out with every intention of being that for him and was in perfect position to catch him. That was, until an arm pulled me in and away, leaving Zuko to fall to the boiling lake below.”

Jet matched Sokka’s glare and huffed. “Yeah, I pulled you in, and an adult took your place. Your little prince is safe. I saved you.”

“How the fuck was that saving anyone!?”

“Sokka, I get this is upsetting but stuff it or you will wake Zuko up.” Toph cut in.

“Look, Sokka, can I tell my side now?” Jet waited and did not move until he received a nod. He sat up slowly and turned to address the group rather than face off with someone far too upset to listen.

“The odds were stacked against Sokka on that stunt working in the first place. He was damn near half way out the window on a gondola moving at a slower rate than Zuko was. Zuko, who I shall remind everyone, is bigger than Sokka. Not only that, but that thing was wet and slippery as shit! The metal had basically just gotten polished with the constant pelting rain we were in. What would have happened if Zuko had made contact with Sokka like that?” He paused and looked around slowly to the mixed looks.

“I’ll tell you what. On a lucky chance, Sokka would have been able to withstand the force and slowed Zuko’s fall. Maybe he could even pull the probably heavier person up and into the gondola, but that came with risk of injury. From where I stood, however, that seemed extremely unlikely.

“What was more likely to happen was that Zuko would have grabbed onto Sokka and dragged him out and down with him. Zuko, without meaning to, would have been the reason we lost two people. From what I could see there was just no way Sokka would have been able to maintain his grip. He was basically dangling like a fish on a line. He couldn’t even manage to stay upright as he jumped from the three levels on wet metal only moments before all of this happened! I had to do something, seeing as no one else saw the problem. I’m sorry. I knew it would hurt you to lose him, but I was not willing to gamble Sokka’s life on an unlikely maybe.” Jet finished by turning his glare onto Sokka himself.

Toph sat still for a long moment before she sighed deeply. “He’s telling the truth, from what he believes.”

“Then why did you not say anything!? Help us make a plan? Why let one almost die over vocalizing the problem!?”

“Sokka, there was no time! It happened far too fast to say anything! It was a do or don’t and I was the only one fast enough to do something!” Jet retorted.

“You could have helped me get him!”

“And risk all of us going over?”

“Sokka, what was done is done and I agree with Jet.” Katara challenged. She continued to glare in her set resolve despite the shocked look she received from her brother.

“Look, Toph said you both are telling your truths. If Jet is really serious that he believed you were going to be hurt or worse then I agree with his action to protect you.”

“You would have let Zuko fall-”

“Yes. To save you.”

Sokka sat stunned. He had expected she may not like that Sokka had risked a few things but had never expected her to agree with Jet. When her look did not so much as waver he huffed a final ‘I’m tired’ and got up to grab his sleeping roll. He kept his back to the group as he grabbed his things and in a split second decision dragged them over to join Zuko in the far corner.

He ignored the hushed conversation from the fire at his actions, knowing his dad was probably talking to Katara about a few things but could not care less. He wanted nothing to do with them and threw himself into his bedroll like a child.

The roll was touching the outer edge of Zuko’s own and he found himself burying his face into the Appa fur leaking out from the side as he snaked an arm over the edge. He rested his hand on Zuko’s left shoulder and let the strong heart beat and steady breathing calm him. If he heard his name being called from the fireplace, no he did not.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

Thank you so much for reading along! No, this is not finished. I would do another chapter or two before cutting it and building the next part in a different story. The reason being that the 100K+ was not in my plan at all and I don't know if the story should continue from this point in one story or be broken into collections.

Any thoughts on this chapter and collection idea are welcome!
