## Chapter 23

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Warnings: Mild panic attack

As you can see, the chapters are now coming up to the final moment of this arc! I fully intend to continue this story but have decided to break it into sections that work together. The large number of words is scaring even me.

### Text

Everything.

Fucking.

Hurt!

The mere thought of twitching a single muscle was enough to trigger a wave of pain. From the minimal amount of light let in through his eyelids to the shuffle of something small on the ground. Everything was enough to send pounding waves from his head to his toes. If that wasn’t enough, the voices that echoed made his world feel like it was imploding.

He made all of this and more known when he opened his mouth, meaning to ask for some quiet, and was only able to groan out as moving even his tongue seemed impossible. His mouth was so dry.

Within a moment whatever he was laying on jostled around making him gasp out as another ripple of pain cut through the fog in his mind. Something cold touched his cheek for a moment. It had felt so nice against his hot skin but the feeling had not lasted long as it disappeared just as fast with a slap that sounded far too close to his ear.

Why was he so hot? Was it a fever? His own fire? No, a quick test to his core proved that he was still without fire and that his core temperature was lower. The heat was an external problem then. Great, just what he needed, a problem that demanded he try and move. That idea was aborted immediately as an attempt to roll his shoulders was met with such a shock wave that his eyes flew open, only to burn with the intensity of the morning light and be squeezed shut once more.

“Katara!” Someone had screamed, far too close to his face, and Zuko was extremely upset at his lack of fire. He would have blasted whoever that was instantly, so maybe it was good he was nothing more than a body.

The cold thing was back, this time in multiples. One large one had settled on his forehead, the other dug its way between the hot thing beneath him and his neck. Both causing a sense of relief that left him tipping his head as best he could to keep the contact. Another set of cold points were like ghosts dancing over his too hot skin until finally something was removed and the cool morning air washed over him.

“He was just overheating. That’s what you get for using your winter coat as a blanket for him.”

“What? Like it was my fault it got cold! He was shivering and how am I supposed to know when to take it off?”

“You’re an idiot, you know that?”

The voices were just so. Damn. Loud!

Zuko let out a slight growl to try and announce the he was one, awake. Two, in pain. And three, very much hearing this stupid argument between whoever they were.

The cold points pulled away at his growl, causing him to regret it instantly. He tried to hide this fact, but the whimper that slipped out was one he would be embarrassed about later. For now, he had to be thankful because at least the people talking had shut up.

A moment later he found his right side engulfed in a new cold. One that bled through his very bones and the relief found within it was mind numbing bliss. He sighed out, only to feel something rough and cold touch his lips. Water. Sweet Agni he was thirsty. His body had jerked as if to grab for the container that was letting only a teasing amount of water out at a time, only to be shoved roughly back down.

“Stay still, or you can fix your own body.” A woman growled. He knew that growl on an instinctual level. His body tensed and he held himself as still as stone to prevent from angering her further.

“Relax, Sunshine. Just drink and tell me when you are finished. There is plenty, don’t worry.” A different voice added. This one was far kinder and he felt a calm settle over his nerves despite the fear only a moment before. He knew this voice as well. It was safe. Wasn’t it?

“He is dehydrated. Probably gonna go right back under after this so don’t bother saying too much.”

“We can say all we like, Katara.” The male voice barked, still far too loud.

“You two wanna heal your own *boyfriend* then be my guest. Oh wait, you can’t.”

“We get it, spirits, Katara. Thank you for helping him.”

The cold left Zuko’s side and he almost sat up to follow it. The only thing keeping him on the hot cloud was the water skin held to his lips, letting in only the most aggravating trickle of water that was soaked up by his mouth before it reached his throat, and the threat found in the other voice. Katara, his mind slowly supplied, hated him with good reason.

“So what? That’s it?”

“What else you want us to say, Katara? Really, thank you for helping. I don’t know what else you need!” The male voice, Sokka his far less foggy mind supplied, sounded beyond frustrated.

“How about a ‘it’s not like that,’ huh!? How about anything that makes more sense than this! Suki, he burned your village. How can you just-” The words cut out and gave way to a frustrated sound that matched her brothers.

“What are you even talking about?”

“Last night! Jet said- You know what, forget it. Just, I want that water skin back later and I will check him again after lunch.”

Zuko lay drinking, finally feeling the cooling reach his throat, and let the water clear away the lingering fog. Suki had been the one to decide when to take away the skin, probably for the best as Zuko himself could not bring himself to listen to his own body. He had been so focused on his throat he had not thought that too much water would have been counterproductive.

“How you feeling?” She asked quietly.

“Like I was crushed by the volcano.” He croaked out, wincing at the sound of his own voice. It sounded like he had swallowed knives.

An answering chuckle from the distance was enough to make him crack an eye despite the brightness. It took a few blinks and a solid minute before things came into focus. On his right side, sitting on what he could only assume was a makeshift bed, and jostling him around was Sokka. Still holding his neck as a support, and smacking Sokka’s hand away whenever it so much as twitched in Zuko’s direction for some reason, was Suki.

Toph was sat against the wall a few feet away from them and digging through Zuko’s pack as if it was her own. She had been the one to snort out a laugh at his comment and was receiving a mix of looks ranging from confused to aggravated. Zuko could really care less, what was she going to find in his stuff? Most of his more sensitive items had been in his war balloon and were at the bottom of a boiling lake now, anyway. Out of everyone, Toph had full rights to his things and he had nothing to hide.

A clattering of metal on the floor reminded him that he did, in fact, have something to hide. She had found his swords and distracted the group fully.

“Huh. Jet was right, you did have a sword. Cool.” Toph grinned as she felt along the handle. “Oh, I think it’s broken. Sorry.”

“Swords.” Zuko corrected.

“Swords? So this split on the handle is not a break?” Toph twisted the swords slowly and grinned wider as they split into two. “Sweet! That is handy but the flat handle is super weird. Can you use them?”

“Yeah.” He was watching her from his bad side and could only really make out where she was sitting. Her movements were slow, so he was not as worried as he probably should have been with a blind person handling his sharp things.

“Toph, put those back! Just what are you even trying to get at?” Sokka snapped. He was seconds from launching at the girl with said sharp things and Zuko wondered just how any of them were still alive. Really, this all screamed accident waiting to happen. Must be luck.

“Just proving a point, relax. This morning Sugar Queen went on a rant, talking about how a fireless firebender was pointless and what not. Seems to me he has more to offer than just flames, is all.” Toph shrugged as she put the swords onto the ground near Zuko’s pack.

“Pointless…” Zuko started but found his voice failed. He was not sure why it stung to hear, but he supposed it was true. He was rather pointless being bedridden and without fire. He should at least get up and start to instruct Aang on his next exercise.

Before he could even begin to shift and sit up Toph frowned and stood. “Absolutely not, Sparky. Twinkle Toes is mine today so don’t you think about it. He has been slacking on earthbending anyway.”

Without another word she stomped off, screaming for her target in question to hurry up and meet her outside. The answering squeak and groan from a certain Avatar made Sokka laugh as he watched her go.

“Do you ever get used to… that?” Suki asked quietly.

“Get used to what? Toph?”

“No- well, yes. But what I meant was her ability to see, like, everything. Even things you can’t. How had she known about the swords?”

“No, and yes. You get used to it, of course. Sometimes little things will remind you just how unusual it is though. Others learning how to navigate her is always a good reminder.” Sokka sighed.

“Hm. Okay, I’ll take your word for it.” She frowned as she watched Toph all but catapult Aang out of the temple while laughing. She sighed and turned her attention downwards with a gentle smile as she thread her fingers through Zuko’s hair to get his attention.

“I think it is safe to say you are with us for the time being?” She asked.

Zuko nodded, giving her a questioning look in response. The fog clouding his mind had dissipated for the most part and whatever Katara had done had all but erased the pain. Maybe erased was not the word he needed, more just made it to a level just below what he would have considered workable. One more good healing and he would be more than able to ignore the rest of the aches in favor of getting back to work. They were short on time, and a lazy bender was a pointless one.

“Good. I need you to check in with me, okay? What’s your name, Sunshine?” She was still carding her fingers through his hair and he almost missed the question.

Name? That was such a weird question but it was familiar somehow. She had done this before, when? The balloon. Right, these were her check in questions to make sure he had not brained himself so hard he needed to be monitored. What were the other questions again?

“Hey buddy, you with us?” Sokka asked, inching forward on the side of the bed. He had reached out to touch but the hand had been slapped away just as fast by a stern faced Suki.

“I mean it, Sokka. Hands off. Talk, to a lot of people, before touch.”

“Okay, okay. But Suki, he hasn’t answered you.”

“He’s thinking, give him time.”

“About his name?”

Zuko looked back to Sokka, puzzled. Why would he be thinking about his own name? Shit. Suki’s check in. He had been so confused on how he had known about them that he had forgotten to answer. He really was awful at this.

“Zuko. Ex? Prince of the Fire Nation?” He said, unsure why he added the last bit but it seemed better than saying his parents names.

“See?” Suki chuckled. “You got lost in your thoughts, didn’t you?”

He nodded again, this time far more embarrassed than he would admit to being. Though, his face felt hot and the answering smile he got from both of them told him that maybe they already knew.

“Alright, how old are you?” Sokka asked.

That would depend on the months at this point. Last he had checked he had been 17, but that felt like a lifetime ago. If it was spring then the odds were he was actually 18, but that did not feel right. No, it had to still be summer, but that meant that the last few weeks had been so eventful that his body felt a year older or more.

“Buddy, you don’t have to riddle these out.”

“17. I-I think.”

“You think?” Sokka asked.

“I can’t remember if spring has gone by, could be 18.”

“Doesn’t really matter, that is close enough.” Sokka snorted.

“It does matter.” Zuko retorted. “Suki asks these and wants the correct answer-”

“Sunshine, you are doing just fine. Breathe. Last one. What is the last thing you remember?”

Huh, what was the last thing he remembered? He definitely remembered that cursed prison. That was not what she was asking, though. He remembered Azula. She had attacked them, tried to kill them. She had sent lightning, and he remembered her look of shock as he had sent it back moments before the world had faded.

He remembered the balloon. That had to be it, but what about it? It had been Azula’s. It had also needed fire to fly and he had been the only one who could give that. The burner room had been large, probably meant for many benders to rotate through feeding it fire. The moment his fire had gone cold was when he lost connection with the rest of the world.

The time frame after was a mix of fever dreams, moments of possibly being awake, and things that were simply impossible. It was just not possible that he would have woken to Sokka in a bed with himself. He was the one who had navigated them to the air temple, not slept. He felt as the heat from his face bled down his neck as he swallowed hard. He remembered waking to Sokka, words had been exchanged, and it had ended with the most improbable action. There was no chance that Sokka had-… No, it was a fever dream, that’s why he almost thought he knew the feeling of it all.

“The burner.” He had answered, it was the last thing he could trust to be a real memory. No way he would say a kiss only to be outed and rejected. He couldn’t run, but he would need to find a way to if he let that little fever dream out.

“The burners? Are… are you sure?” Sokka asked, he sounded hurt by this but Zuko could not understand why.

“Yeah?”

“Sokka, I told you it might get a bit mixed. Give it time, maybe things will clear up as he heals. If not, just add it to your talk you two will be having later.”

“Yeah, alright, okay. Do you know where you are at least?”

“The air temple?” Zuko glanced around and nodded, sure of that answer at the very least.

“I see princess is awake.” Jet snickered as he walked towards the small group.

“Jet, fuck off, would you.”

“You still mad about last night? Listen, I said I was sorry but what happened is done. What can I do to show to you guys that I am going to try to do better?”

“You could leave, for a start-”

“Sokka.” Suki huffed and rolled her eyes. “He is right that it is in the past and we heard his reasons.”

“Suki, you can’t be serious-”

“Let me finish.” She waited until she was sure he would sit at least somewhat quietly.

“He had said that he had feared you going over with Zuko and reacted on an instinct. While I do not agree with it, I understand that stressful situations can sometimes cause one to act without thought. I do not agree with how things were handled. I could name a number of different ways they could have gone if only words were used in the moment. However, I am willing to let him learn from this seeing as it all worked out in the end and I have both of my boys still here with me. I do want to see Jet do better, I do not trust him to do so without supervision.”

Sokka crossed his arms tight, shifting more into a pout than anything and it made Zuko want to reach out. He wanted to touch him, to get his attention, to show that he was alright. They were fighting over what Jet had done in the gondola and a part of Zuko had agreed with Jet’s actions. If it was ever a choice between himself and Sokka, by Agni he hoped they always chose Sokka.

The problem was that when he went to reach out, he felt the entire movement tug across his chest. His arm, he realized, was strapped down so tightly it was almost laughable. Had he really messed it up that bad? He had known it had dislocated, sure, maybe a pulled muscle or two. This was overkill and he huffed in annoyance and gave another tug.

Suki laughed and gently settled a hand onto his strapped down arm. “Would you stop pulling like that. These took forever to get tight. I know they must be uncomfortable but they are needed.”

“Really? Looks like you expect my arm to fall off.” Zuko huffed but relaxed back into the cloud.

“Yeah, we kinda did.” Sokka retorted.

His look of frustration and something more made Zuko freeze and swallow hard. That look, it was clear to Zuko that he had messed up. That his failure in the burner room was not one to be forgotten and that whatever he had built with Sokka was most likely ruined. Never show your weakness, the rule he had been trying so hard to follow, was about to cost him everything.

“Sokka, you are not helping. You and Jet go, I don’t know, fight this out with swords and bond like weird boys do. Leave Sunshine alone or he is going to get it in his head that things are wrong or his doing like the pessimistic blockhead he is.” Suki hissed.

She had clearly not meant for it to be loud enough for Zuko to hear, but he had and was now even more confused than before. His head was starting to hurt again with the effort to keep up with the things happening around him. It was- great, he couldn’t feel the suns location. Agni, he was a mess inside and out. That did not matter, what did matter was that the light shining from the opening to the temple ledge meant that the sun was up. That meant he should be as well.

Cool fingers once again brushed through his hair, making his thoughts quiet. He had not even noticed that he had closed his eyes until he felt the bed shake. Popping one eye open, he watched as Sokka got up to shuffle away. He did not get far, however, as he got hit with a rock and a hushed hiss from Suki for some reason.

Jet laughed as he too stood to follow out of the corner. He threw an arm over Sokka’s shoulders to pull him into his side as they walked away and something hot and angry stirred deep within Zuko. He had no flames, but this new feeling did not seem to care. It did demand that he hurry up and heal so that he could punch Jet.

“Hey, you should get some rest. These healing sessions take a lot out of both you and Katara, according to everyone.” Suki smiled her warm smile that made him feel so welcomed and confused all at once.

“Not tired.” He lied, eyes already slipping shut at the cool fingers taking care to graze his scalp.

The answering chuckle he got told him that what he had missed was the worlds biggest eye roll. He felt his own face twitch into an answering smirk but was unsure just how long anything had lasted. The warmth of the sunlight, cool air, and Suki’s gentle touch blurred the line between sleep and reality far faster than anything.

He had come close to breaking the vice grip sleep had on him only once when that familiar cold washed over his side. It had not lasted long enough, however, and he felt himself slip back down into the blackness.

“..ko?”

The black fog broke and light began to seep once more into focus. Something had changed close enough to him that he felt the need to wake. It was not easy, and he felt like he was swimming in a lagoon to try and actually get back to his body.

“..ko?”

That voice was still there. It was asking something, probably just his name but that was always the start of a need. He was needed, and so he needed to wake up.

“Hey buddy?”

Zuko cracked and eye open and winced at the light, not because it hurt, but because he had forgotten the feeling of it after so long. After a few blinks to get his vision to focus he was met with the face of Sokka leaning over him, far closer than he should be.

“Yeah?” He chroaked.

Sokka sighed in relief. He flashed a grin that made his face glow in the morning light and Zuko’s heart skipped. Shit. Shitshitshit no. No, not good. He was so, unbelievably, screwed.

“Oh good. I was getting worried. You were out for a day or so there and while others seemed not to think much on it, I thought that was not really normal is all.” He flushed lightly as he explained. He sat up slightly so he was no longer just mere inches away and Zuko felt cold suddenly.

“Hm, not sure. Only done it a few times, sleep a day away I mean.” Zuko looked to the wall, very much away from Sokka or he was in for a rough conversation.

“Sorry to wake you, just got really scared that you wouldn’t.” Sokka all but whispered.

Zuko startled at feeling fingers brush along his cheek. He had been so focused on watching the wall on the opposite side that he had not seen Sokka move. The touch was cool to his naturally warmer skin and so gentle it made his heart do funny things that really should not be healthy. He looked to find Sokka watching him, taking in every reaction. His brain shorted out and he really did not know what he was about to do, so he was just as shocked as Sokka to hear himself speak.

“Suki will be pissed if she sees you touching.”

Sokka snorted, tried to control himself, and failed. He crumpled into the side of the bedding, half laying on Zuko’s right side but low enough that he did not disturb his shoulder, and giggled into his bandaged chest.

Zuko’s whole world stopped. He was shocked by the touch, that his words had gained such an open reaction. He had thought Sokka was mad at him, maybe that had been wrong. Now he believed that just maybe he had come away from Boiling Rock with a friend. One that he was so undeniably head over heels for that Mai had seen it before he had, and she had not even seen them together!

The absurdity of it all, from Sokka laying on him while giggling like he was the funniest person alive, to the fact that he might actually really like him. It was all too much.

“Yeah, she would probably gut me.”

Zuko snorted in response as he imagined Suki attacking Sokka over simply a touch. The idea that it would be himself that caused it was the most confusing part of the puzzle, but it was still an amusing thought. He knew she would never actually hurt them, though.

Sokka beamed after he had heard the laugh he had gotten and shifted. He moved to once more be hovering just above him to keep their whispered conversation to themselves, resting his weight on one hand just past Zuko’s scarred ear. His forehead was a mere inch away from Zuko’s, demanding his attention.

“Be your fault if she did. I have two swords she could borrow.” Zuko stated, more just facts.

“Hey, not nice.” Sokka snorted and grinned, clearly not offended in the least.

“Who said I was nice?”

“Alright, fair point. Jerk.” Sokka’s grin changed to something far less recognizable and Zuko gave him a puzzled look in return. The fingers were back, this time they traced his jaw line and kept him from looking away.

“So, jerk, you gonna teach me how to work those duel swords?” Sokka asked, his look flicked from his own fingers touching Zuko’s neck, just above his pulse, to Zuko’s eyes and stayed.

Zuko was going to answer, had wanted to. All he could do was nod in response and swallow hard at the warm smile he got for it. He hoped Sokka would just think it was his being hurt causing all these problems.

Sokka’s grin slowly fell into a more serious look as he moved back slightly. His hand moved from his neck to brush a piece of hair Zuko had felt fall close to his eye. He would have ignored it, used to his hair being everywhere and just short enough to not fit any ties. When Sokka did it, however, it made his stomach flip and everything else seemed so far away.

“Hey, do you really not remember anything after the burners?” Sokka frowned in thought, fingers lingering at Zuko’s hair line.

Zuko swallowed again and looked towards his pack against the far wall. He remembered a great deal of things, that was not the problem. The problem was that he could not trust those memories to be real over twisted realities and fever dreams.

“Zuko?” Sokka sounded so quiet. So hopeful and searching. What could he be looking for? What was the truth in the mess of moments he had dancing through his dreams?

“I… no.” Zuko grit his teeth, not willing to look at the most likely hurt look again.

“You’re lying.” Sokka huffed.

“N-No I am not!” Zuko broke his own rule and looked.

He really should not have, but he always acted without thought. He froze, instead of the hurt or anger he had expected to see he saw only some kind of hope and amusement in the grin forming. Fuck this man, how did he have any right to be this handsome while literally laying on him like a lump. It was just not fair and he was so tired.

“Tell me what you remember? Please?” The smirk on Sokka’s face fell into the perfect pout and Zuko blinked to try and see if he was seeing things. No, that was definitely the pout he used to get seconds from Katara after a meal.

“I’m not serving food. That look won’t work, you know.” He huffed and rolled his eyes.

“Ah-ha! So you do remember something! You are just keeping it to yourself!”

“I-… What!?” Zuko blinked in surprise, trying to riddle out just what had happened. “You-That was cheating!”

“No such thing as cheating in a conversation, my prince.” Sokka smirked and winked, his words had rolled off his tongue like honey and Zuko felt as his face burned.

He wanted to say something to that. He was going to tell him off, imply he knew nothing. To demand he leave. Well, not leave, but know that that was uncalled for. He could not form words though as his brain echoed my prince in a way that made his spine tingle and his stomach do more of the weird twists it had before.

He found opening and closing his mouth like a fish was also just making Sokka feel more pride than he deserved so he clamped his mouth shut. Without answering, he settled on a glare that made Sokka chuckle.

“Flying Hogmonkeys!” Toph shrieked before she burst into giggles. “No fucking way!”

“Toph!” Sokka pulled away to sit up just as fast as he had appeared, leaving that cold feeling to return once more in his absence.

“Oh this is fantastic! I thought it was all pissy words and people being sour. Color me shocked to feel all this! You gonna tell him?” Toph chirped far happier than she had a right to being.

Zuko wished she would open the earth beneath him and end it all. He felt his whole upper body, from his chest to the face, burn with the knowing look behind her glassy stare. He could not, however, tell just who she was addressing. By her words though, it had to be himself for there was no way she had not read his traitorous heart. It had been doing all sorts of irrational things since he woke up and if anyone was to hear it, it was her.

“Toph, go away!” Sokka snarled, far less anger and more something else in his tone.

“I don’t think I will. I need a good show and this is solid gold.”

“You don’t even know what gold is!”

“If anyone here doesn’t know what gold is, it is you, Watertribe. Gold is earth, newsflash, earthbender.” Toph rolled her eyes and crossed her arms, both actions making Zuko wonder just how she knew how to do them.

“Seriously, Toph. Back off. This doesn’t involve you.”

“If it involves Sparky, it involves me. We both know he spirals into the worst before accepting the good. I’m here to prevent that.”

“You are ruining everything, is what you are doing!” Sokka barked out.

“If anyone is ruining it, it is you. Seriously, do you even know how to talk to Sparky? You have to be direct, probably multiple times, if you are ever going to get the answer you are clearly digging for. Playing games like this will win you nothing but a fight.” She waved a hand in their direction.

“We were getting to things!” Sokka retorted, face flushed with probable anger.

“Suuuuuure.” Toph once more rolled her eyes and it did nothing to settle Zuko’s raising nerves.

“Sokka!” A female voice echoed causing the three bickering to freeze. “Get off of him!”

Zuko lifted his head, using far more energy than he thought it should have taken, and was shocked to see Katara as she stormed forward. She had a furious glare set onto her brother. “What?”

“Hands off! Quit using him as a bed! If you undo all my hard work just because you left your brain at that prison then, so help me La, I will freeze you to a wall and let you fall once it melts!” She continued her advance and grabbed her stunned brother by the ear. A strong twist had him being peeled away from Zuko in a whimpering mess.

“Kataraaaa! Let go!”

“Promise me you will stay off of him.”

“I promise!” Sokka groaned in fake tears at his sisters pull.

Katara huffed and released him. She dropped to her knees on Zuko’s left so fast that he could not help but flinch, gaining a look of annoyance from the waterbender.

“Stay still. I am going to take a look and see if he messed anything up.” Her water followed her words and with it the mind numbing relief. He had forgotten he had been in such debilitating pain for a moment, taking in only the world between Sokka and himself.

“Toph, don’t you have an Avatar to harass?” Katara quipped without so much as a glance to said bender.

“Sure do, Sugar Queen. Be nice, I’ll know otherwise.” Toph retorted and pivoted to head in the direction of the campfire.

Zuko risked twisting slightly to look towards the campfire and saw that Aang was sitting with Suki, both clearly eating. Aang was waving a hand around as he talked, the excited energy for being able to tell a story to someone new overflowing from him. He was so into whatever he was talking about that he had yet to realize a certain lemur swiping food from him in small amounts. Suki was both listening closely and giving Aang the encouragements needed to keep him talking. Was not much, but Aang fed off of the attention like a starved tiger-bear.

Sokka had shuffled towards the group and slumped like a kicked polar-puppy by Suki’s side. He had muttered something that had Suki pull him into her side in both mock babying and shoot Zuko a concerned look. Her eyes locked with his and she raised a brow in question. A flick of her eyes in Katara’s direction was all the question he needed. She wanted to know if he was okay, and if he needed her.

Why would he need her? She could not heal and she would not be able to stop Katara. It was not like Katara was going to kill him. Sure, he was not comfortable with the idea of being alone with her but that was a him problem.

She had a lot of reason to hate him, and even more reason to despise his fire than he had. As much as she seemed to be just being unreasonable and aggressive. Zuko recognized the defense for what it was. He had spent years in the same explainable rage and had poured it all onto the Avatar.

He watched as Suki’s gaze became more of a glare and he could tell she was moments from doing something. Before she moved, he answered with a quick shake of his head and settled back down onto the bedding fully. He caught the annoyed look he had received from all the movements but ignored it in favor of just laying still and basking in the blissful relief.

“I don’t know what you are doing, but stay away from my family.” Katara stated in a flat tone that had Zuko jerk slightly. He had almost slipped into oblivion and that had not been what he had expected. He had no idea she wanted to talk, not like he could have known.

“What?” Great, now he sounded dumb. Her answering look of aggravated disapproval was one he was quite used to.

“Listen. Just because Aang and Toph forgave you does not mean you deserved it. Those two are an easy sell, and so is my brother and Suki it would seem. Whatever happened in that prison changed things and I do not like it.”

“Not my doing. I told them the same shit. You want to know why the others seem to like me you should talk to them. I don’t get it either.” He hissed as her water jabbed into his shoulder with far more force than he thought was necessary. He would never voice this and knew his speaking at all was going to be the problem.

“If you know that, then leave them alone. Leave my brother alone.” Her voice was cold as ice and her glare was just as sharp. He should have just let her get whatever was eating at her out, but of course he was never that smart in the moment.

“Tell that to him, then. I can’t really move around so I am not the one starting anything-” It had been the wrong time to start this kind of conversation. He grit his teeth at the cold wave that bit deep, too cold to even allow for his body to shiver.

“Listen here. I am healing you as the thanks you deserve for bringing my brother and father back. Do not think this is a kindness, however. It will do you good to remember that I can harm just as easily as heal.” Her words were quiet, yet every single one was burned into his mind as the freezing caused something deep in his chest to pop. The wave of lava hot pain that followed stole his breath for only seconds as the spot was soothed, healed, and pain was erased by the water that was once more a healing cool.

“You will stay away from my family.” It was not a question, yet she set a look that demanded an answer.

He lay with her soothing water easing his pain and injuries of the gondola away. His body felt somehow detachedfrom himself and he seemed unable to move. His heart was beating far too fast and he could feel the sweat that had started. He was on the brink of panicking and she still had him held down by her water.

The water was the reason for this, he realized. After that little display he had lost his trust in her not hurting him. She could kill him with nothing more that a twist of her finger and blame it on the injuries. He suddenly wished he had not told Suki to stay. Sokka had needed her comfort, and he was not sure he deserved to remove it from him then.

Now, he had to admit, he was scared and he could do nothing about it. Katara’s look had sharped as he lay staring at her. She had not stopped the healing and it had not changed from the soothing pressure, yet he could no longer find it to be something relaxing. He understood a part of her anger but how Sokka came into it was beyond him. It was not like he had control over him, but she wanted Sokka to not be around him anymore. That idea made his heart, which was already panicking, feel hollow.

He finally had a friend, someone who wanted to be near him. He should have known that was too much. If he knew anything, the stronger and younger bender of a family would always keep him away from something like that. It happened with Azula, and it was not a shock that it was happening again with Katara. She hated him, with good reasons. If he let his mind wonder, he found himself thinking that maybe she did not have that much of a reason for this but he stopped those thoughts quickly. They would not help now.

If it would fix things for everyone, he’d agree. He would leave Sokka alone. He would push him away and ignore how that thought ripped at his insides like a knife. It was better than this fear of Katara-

“Hey, Sugar Queen. I told you if you did something I’d feel it.” Toph was suddenly right behind Katara, arms crossed tight as she glared. Suki was walking up behind her with a concerned look.

Katara slowly pulled the water away and stood with a stretch. “Look, I don’t know what you are on about but you can stop acting all tough. It was just a rough healing session and I had a few things to try an understand.”

Toph looked unconvinced but said nothing. Her deep frown showed just how much she liked that idea.

“Sunshine, how you doing?” Suki inched forward, ignoring the glare and eye roll from Katara at the name.

Why. Could. He. Still. Not. Move!?

He tried to nod. Tried to shake his head. Tried to do anything and found that all he could do was start trembling as he clenched his teeth. He was trying so hard not to panic, even without that water, and his body was just not listening to him.

“Suki, his heart is racing. I think… I think he is panicking. Katara, what did you do!?”

“Right, you guys leave. I got this.” Suki cut in. “Take this outside, I will stay.”

Katara sent a glare towards Zuko but he paid her no mind. He was trying to just breathe and even that was a fight. His lungs felt like they were frozen and he squeezed his eyes shut tight. He did not hear what was said between them as he tried to focus on calming his heart. This was not his first panic attack, but it was the first caused by someone who was supposed to be a healer.

He felt as fingers worked through his hair. He knew that touch to be Suki and it broke something deep inside. He had almost cried out for her to help him. Had wanted her help and now that it was here and given he felt like such a child. He could feel her whispering as he leaned into her touch. Felt her lift him from the bed just enough to sit him up against her. His back was to her chest and his head rested on her shoulder where he could both feel and hear her take deep and even breaths. It was obvious that he was meant to copy.

“I’m sorry. My fault.” He croaked out.

“Shhh, Sunshine, I highly doubt any of that was your fault.” Suki stated quietly.

“It was. I knew better than to anger her. She only wants to protect her family.”

“Then she is going to have to get it through her thick ice that ‘family’ includes you. Either way, you two are not to be alone together. Even if it is for healing, you come and get one of us, okay?” She retorted and moved just enough to wrap her arms around him in a loose hug.

Zuko could not find an answer to that. Everything was so new and he had no frame of reference for something like this, minus his uncle but that thought stung. It was so foreign from how family was supposed to be. If anything, he found Katara’s reactions far more understandable and closer to the actual definition. He let this be known only by using his left arm to grab tight to one of Suki’s. It was the only way in this position he could hug back, but she had understood as her arms tightened in response.

Agni, he was a mess. At least he had people for support, and that thought made him realize just how alone he had been for all those years. How much he wanted them to like him. He was not going to turn Sokka or Suki away, even if Katara wanted it, because he needed them and maybe that was reason enough.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

PLEASE BE KIND: Note we love Katara in this house hold but also know she went through a LOT of stuff without really getting any help or needed support. Actions in this chapter may not be as they seem!

(Also, I love Suki being a great support for Zuko but have also always kinda liked the a Sokka/Suki/Zuko pairing. Thoughts?)

As always: reactions, thoughts, opinions, suggestions, or ideas are always welcome! &#60;3

## Chapter 24

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

Katara is angry.

#### Chapter Notes

This is a Katara side of the story. It is a lot.

Side note: I am going on vacation with my family for two weeks and so, I hate to do this, but the last chapter of this arc won't be until early December.

### Text

Katara had stood to watch for only a moment as Suki had gathered the trembling mass of a firebender into her arms. How Zuko had crumpled into the hold like a kicked polar puppy despite being absolutely fine. He had really turned up the dramatics this time and she rolled her eyes before leaving the group of them to sort whatever this was out. He was not hers to deal with when it came to theatrics, only that messed up shoulder and muscle groups.

She had chores to do, seeing as no one seemed to remember that a camp took work to keep running. With all the extra bodies, the work was piling up faster than anyone could keep up with. Sokka had decided to slack, not a shock, if given any chance to avoid chores he would jump on it. Toph was just not a fight she wanted to try at the moment. Aang would help, he would even feel bad for not helping, but she just could not bring herself to add to his already rising stress.

She sighed heavily and turned to follow the outer ridge to where they hung the laundry to dry. Zuko usually just heat blasted it for them as a safe way to use fire but that was out of the question at the moment. Once she collected all pieces dry enough to bring in she turned to head back. Next would be to fold it and look for holes that were at least fixable. The prison uniforms had to go, but for now they had nothing big enough for a few of the adults that had joined.

Her father smiled wide seeing her head into the common space on the opposite ledge. This one was through a hall that divided it from their sleeping area and was far more open for things like bending practice and overall hangouts.

Haru was with him as the two talked about something and Katara could only hope that it was how to shave. Really, Haru had been so much better looking without that weird pointy thing growing neatly trimmed on his upper lip. She knew that was unkind, but it was a gut reaction and one she herself would never say out loud, unlike Sokka who had laughed second one of seeing it.

With a smile in return, she took a seat next to her father and began sorting through the clothes. She piled the pieces needing mending away from those that were alright and began to fold them. Her father and Haru were talking about some old stories that she had come into at some halfway point but she did not mind. It was nice just to hear her fathers voice as he laughed and told a story once more. It was relaxing, and made the world feel somewhat okay.

“Hey, Sugar Queen, with no due respect what the actual fuck is wrong with you?” Toph stormed up towards the group, ground cracking under her feet in an irresponsible way for someone in an upside down air temple.

Haru was the first to react, moving in a way that fixed the breakage caused by Toph and into a defensive stance. Between the two earthbenders, it was no question that Toph would win in any fight thanks to her vast experience on the matter but he would try if needed.

“Really, is there a need for such language?” Hakoda sighed heavily, all but giving up on this argument.

Katara rested her hand on her fathers shoulder and shook her head lightly. “I’ve tried, they just don’t listen to the fact that language like that doesn’t help.”  
“The fuck it doesn’t. You wanna tell me why you decided physical torture was an okay thing to do?” Toph stomped one final time, digging her heel deep enough that Haru could not fix this one without her moving. She was just in front of the three now and gaining the attention of the others quickly.

“Toph, what has gotten into you?” Katara asked as she moved to set the half folded laundry to the side and out of the line of fire should Toph kick up any more dust. Really, did no one realize how hard it was to keep things clean and working around here?

“You, that’s what. You wanna tell me just exactly what happened between you and Zuko?” She crossed her arms tight, clearly holding back and Katara felt her temper rise rapidly.

“What are you on about?” She demanded as she stood to match the glare with one of her own.

“What happened to Zuko?” Sokka asked as he approached the group from the training grounds with Jet close behind him. They had been lazily sparring since Suki had shooed Sokka from the campfire. Now, he was looking between the three standing with a worried expression.

“How should I know?” Katara rolled her eyes.

“You should know because you did something to him!” Toph retorted.

“I have done nothing but laundry and cooking! What could I have possibly done?” She rolled her eyes again and waved in the direction of the half finished laundry.

“Not now, when you were supposed to be ‘healing’ him! You did something to him, I know it.” Toph’s glare somehow sharpened as she ground her heel into the earth deeper.

“Katara, what did you do?” Sokka turned on her and she felt a flash of anger for just how fast he could do that. She was his sister for spirits sake! Surely that meant her word meant something.

“After *you* used him like a bed I had to fix a few things. He acted out like the dramatic little prince he is and apparently has Toph convinced I did something.” Katara huffed and shifted her weight.

“WHAT!?” Toph shrieked loud enough to cause a few loose pebbles to break free and trickle down from the hanging buildings.

“Okay, back up. Katara, you and I both know Zuko is not dramatic when it comes to his own pain.” He paused to look over her doubtful face and sighed.

“Okay, maybe you are not familiar with that side of him but trust me when I say he would walk on a broken leg for miles before realizing. And almost double that before he’d consider asking for help. I don’t think he knows how to listen to his body when it comes to these things so I doubt he acted out over nothing. Now, I would really like to know what happened.” Sokka cut in before Toph could launch at Katara.

Something was very clearly wrong in how fast he defended the firebender. He had jumped right over what Katara had said and latched onto one spot without listening to her. He was also getting into his ‘I’m the leader’ mode which always aggravated Katara to no end when it was unneeded, and it was extremely unneeded now.

“What happened was that I healed him and we talked.” She stated with a glare towards her brother. “Whatever he did after was not my doing.”

“Not your- What the fuck?” Toph stomped. “Katara, he started panicking WHILE you were so called ‘healing’ and you cannot tell me that is not connected to what he was doing afterwards.”

“What was he doing?” Sokka asked quietly, his gaze lingering on the hallway leading towards the sleeping and night gathering rooms.

“He was all but sobbing into Suki as he battled his own body in a panic attack. Katara did something, and it hurt him bad. Like, really bad.”

“Katara!” Sokka spun to face his sister in shock. “How could you!?”

“Whoa, take it easy with all the yelling! Let her speak. I’m sure Katara would never hurt anyone without a reason.” Jet cut in.

“Unlike you.” Toph quipped with a glare in the direction of the boys. “Don’t think I haven’t forgotten that him being like this is in part your doing.”

“I did not hurt him! I have never hurt someone with healing outside of unavoidable moments.” Katara cut in and turned her glare onto all of them. They were all way out of line with this! How dare they accuse her of hurting that man when it was him hurting them. At least Jet seemed to be understanding, and that should say a lot about just how much that firebender had this group in a twisted mess.

“Katara, maybe it would be best to tell us what exactly you did.” Hakoda chimed in, his face was set in a neutral expression.

“You want to know? Fine. I reattached a major muscle group in his chest that he had severed. In a few days he should be alright with a simple sling if the healing took. I had not been looking forward to that bit of healing, for I have never worked on completely detached muscle groups before but the swelling and everything was perfect so I just went for it.” She stated and sent a glance to the growing pairs of watching eyes in the distance.

“Would that hurt him.” Sokka asked in a flat tone.

“No.”

“Liar. You can’t honestly think that would not hurt!” Toph pointed an accusing finger towards Katara who rolled her eyes.

“She said no, that she did not hurt him. What more is there to know? That sounded like a complicated amount of healing so be thankful she could do it at all!” Jet pushed Toph’s hand down only to be met with a snarl as she smacked his hand away.

“Look, I knew it had a chance to be uncomfortable, sure. I took the extra time while we talked to slowly freeze the target area to numb it so all he should have felt was an uncomfortable tug or a weird pop at most.” She shifted once more and looked towards her father for support. He was still sitting in the way GranGran would when children were in the middle of an argument and she did not have enough information to intervene.

“Did you tell him that? Did he know what you were doing? Did you even tell him why you froze him, or was he left thinking it a byproduct of whatever ‘conversation’ you two were having?” Sokka pushed as he clenched his fists in the way he did to keep himself from acting out.

“No, I guess I didn’t.” Katara fixed a piece of loose hair back into her braid as she ignored her brothers growing glare and her growing agitation. Since when had it mattered?

“I don’t tell you guys what I am doing while healing and it’s always been fine. If something hurts you tell me and we fix it.” She stated as if it was obvious. It should have been obvious. They have been healed by her water countless times by now and all were more than comfortable expressing discomfort, loudly usually.

“Katara, I think this situation is very different from healing those who know you well and who trust you. Forgive me for saying this, but there seems to be little trust between you and the prince. Do you think he would say if something hurt like the others would?” Hakoda asked, brow furrowed in thought.

She went to retort but stopped herself. It was true that Zuko and her did not see eye to eye about almost everything and that she still did not believe he deserved his place here. Not after everything he had done, and continued to do in her opinion. She did realize that this might be her first time actually healing him, however, so she was unsure if he would tell her if something was wrong. In the moment, perhaps she should have been more open about what she was doing but that still did not warrant this kind of reaction over a little jump scare. They were overreacting and there was a simple fix to this whole thing.

“I don’t know.” She finally sighed out and crossed her arms. “Maybe. Still, I don’t see why that is such a big deal. I’ll be more mindful about informing those I am healing about what I am doing in the future, okay?”

“Thank you.” Hakoda agreed.

Toph stomped once more. “Not good enough!”

Jet rolled his eyes and crossed his arms while taking in the raging earthbender. “Not good enough? What do you want her to do, undo her healing? Would that appease you all? Keep him bedridden so you can continue to have a token firebender to baby? She said she would do better communicating in the future, what’s your problem?”

“What my problem is, Zombie, is that whatever Katara did was not just a little uncomfortable. I felt the moment it happened and it felt like his whole body reacted in the way that is only caused by a lot of sudden pain.”

“What? That is not possible. I numbed the area!”

“Well not good enough! Add that to whatever weird shit you told him that had him clinging to Suki like she was going to be taken from him and I don’t see you getting any kindness over this, Sugar Queen.”

“Hey! She said she numbed it and that he did not say anything. If it hurt then he had a responsibility to communicate.” Jet retorted just as loud.

“Jet, drop it. This does not involve you so quit trying to make this a sides thing.” Sokka shot him a warning glare before turning to his sister as she processed the words being said.

“No- no that is not. He didn’t- Oh spirits.” Katara gasped and covered her mouth, her eyes widened with horror and a numb feeling crept up her spine.

She had felt the moment she had tugged the muscle back into place and forced it to stay. The moment it had popped and she had worked quickly to seal it and heal the area. From that very moment Zuko’s body had entered fight or flight and she had thought it was over her words. At her demand that he leave her brother alone and things were to go back to normal.

He had not made a sound to signal that anything of that healing had been painful minus the hiss as she froze the area to numb it. That had been expected, ice and fire do not mix well so she had thought that would have been the worst part but he seemed to have handled it just fine. She had thought- oh Tui and La had she been wrong?

Had her healing done that? No, no she had never hurt someone with it before. She had also never healed fully severed muscles before so what if she had done it wrong and caused more damage? No, she had felt the healing take and everything had felt as it should. That does not mean it had been painless despite her effort. What had she done?

The room felt cold as the realization of everything that had just happened settled in her stomach like a glacier. She had treated him while making sure he had known her stance on him had remained unchanged. How she wanted him to just leave her family alone like he had before and for things to go back to what they were.

“I didn’t- He didn’t say anything.” She stammered, lost in spiraling thoughts.

“No, he wouldn’t. Katara, he is already terrified of upsetting you! Why would he say anything against you?” Sokka’s frustration was growing.

“Then that sounds like a him problem.” Jet cut in.

“No, it really isn’t. It is a communication problem.” Sokka retorted with a warning glare.

“And communication works two ways!” Jet glared back.

“I listened to Suki and his conversation for a bit afterwards. Why did he say you had warned him before it happened in the form of a reminder of his place? That it was his fault for this?” Toph asked just loud enough to quiet any other comments before they could get off topic.

“Warned? Oh, we talked a bit during the session. Wait, he thought- Spirits, I would never! I had told him that I could hurt just as easily as heal, but I would never use healing to do the hurting! I meant fights, like the North Pole or moments when he was chasing us. I would never hurt someone who could not at least fight back!”

“Did you say that? That you would ‘fight him’ or did you say ‘hurt’ and then proceed to actually hurt him?” Toph asked, a grave expression set on her features.

“Does it matter?” Jet asked in a way that spoke of clear dismissal.

“Yes. It does and you of all people should know it does.” Sokka broke in with a glare towards Jet.

“Katara, I get that you don’t talk with Sparky so I don’t fault you on not knowing too much about how his mind works, but he is the most literal thinker I have ever met.” Toph twisted to look between them while speaking just to prove she knew exactly where they were.

“What do you mean?” Katara slowly asked, not seeing how that had much to do with what had happened.

“I mean, if you tell him you will kick his ass for, say, not doing the laundry or some shit that you usually say to Sokka, he will take you at face value. He will think you absolutely will fight him if he doesn’t dry every piece perfectly and will do it with that fear in mind. The reason he ‘fails’ or whatever he calls it is not because he does a bad job, but because he holds himself to the unattainable standard of perfection probably drilled into him by his monster of a family for the most basic of human mistakes.” Toph stated as she turned to look towards the hallway with an expression that was far more sympathetic than Katara could understand.

“So? Why does his thinking have anything to do with this?” Katara pushed.

“That is to say” Sokka joined in with a deep sigh. “If you told him you would fight him if he stepped out of line he would believe you. But, if you told him you could do him harm and heal at the same time that you were healing then proceeded to actually hurt him. He is going to take that as it looks, that it was intentional and that you meant it. Look, Katara, I don’t want to think you meant all of this but you will have to at least swallow whatever you have against him even breathing and apologize. Talk to him, with us there, and let him know exactly what has happened and what you had meant.”

“I didn’t mean to hurt him, and he did not tell me it had hurt! We were talking about his place in the group and he seemed to understand it a lot better than you all do at least. I just wanted to remind him that he is on thin ice with us, I am sorry if it was all mixed with his inability to understand a conversation-”

“Enough, Katara.” Hakoda cut in, voice echoing in the way only a father could do.

Katara recoiled and spun to look towards him shocked. The last thing she had expected was for him to join the conversation. She had especially not expected him to side against her on this.

“Dad-”

“No, Katara, you are not listening and it is clear something is keeping you from hearing this situation fully. While I see and understand that you did not mean harm, you must face the fact that harm has been done. If it was myself you had said and done that to, would you refuse to admit a wrong or apologize for it?”

“What- of course not!”

“Then, I fail to see why prince Zuko is not a person to be shown equal treatment to.”

“Dad, he has hurt every single person here at least once!”

“And he has apologized. He has put himself forward and done better. From the small amount I have seen he is growing, are you?”

“Sir, with no disrespect I don’t think you know just who you are talking about.” Jet tried to cut in only to stop at the look he got from Hakoda.

“I know enough. So I am very aware of his past and his work to do better.”

Katara swallowed hard. She had known that she would have to apologize the moment she knew she had actually caused pain with her healing. Would demand that he voice it in the future for her to know when things were wrong instead of having to guess, but a larger part of her wanted to let that be the only part to apologize for. She had meant her words, had meant that he was to stay away from her family because all he did was burn people.

That all being true, she could understand how the order of events had possibly played out to him believing she had meant that threat as one to be acted on while he was unable to fight back. That was just not true. Not only was he without his own fire, but he was also far too exhausted to even walk around. She would never act out on someone who could not even fight back. She knew that, but it was clear that no one else did and that both burned and saddened her.

“I would never harm someone with healing on purpose.” She whispered as she looked at the ground rather than those around her.

“Katara, I understand that. We all do. You don’t need to tell us that, the person who doesn’t understand is Zuko.” Sokka reached out towards his sister but let his hand fall back as she twisted away from him.

“Then that is a him problem. He should know me better if he wants to be a part of this group.” Katara quietly quipped, the anger in her words was gone but she still pushed against them.

“That goes two ways. If he needs to trust you, you need to give him reasons to do so and right now you just boiled that away like bad tea.” Sokka sighed deeply.

“Fine, I’ll go apologize and make sure everything is okay.” Katara threw her hands up in defeat. She was worried about her healing, if it had really hurt then she would work to make sure to fix that. She had never meant him harm, but she would not take back her words. If he misunderstood, that was on him.

“What I think Sokka is trying to say is that as far as miscommunications go you just took it to a whole new planet. Don’t think you can just waltz back and fix it, either. You sure as shit are not going anywhere close to Sparky for awhile. Feels like Suki has gotten him to relax a bit now and you going back in like this is only going to make the situation spiral.” Toph sighed as she twisted her heel to fix the small hole she had made.

“Then what do you want me to do, huh? First you come in screaming at me and now when I am about to apologize and admit you all might be right you say not to? What do you even want from me?” Katara threw her hands up, this was ridiculous!

“Katara, relax. I appreciate that you are willing to admit this might have been a wrong that can be fixed but you are only going to make it worse right now. How would you feel if someone hurt you and when they apologized for it they looked ready to fight?” Sokka stepped between her and the hallway, hand up and ready to grab her if needed.

She glared at the hand before turning towards her brother. This made no sense. She could admit that she had meant no harm. They were right, and if the healing had hurt she should check it out now in case something was wrong. Who cares if she was upset? Not like Zuko would ever accept an apology from her regardless.

“Really, Katara, you need to calm down like a lot before I let you talk to him. I kinda get the anger cause I went through it too, but he is not half bad. Just, give him a chance and you would see it.” Sokka smirked at some thought that had been brought up by his own words and she felt the anger once more twist in her gut.

“No, you don’t understand. None of you do!” She retorted with a snarl.

“I do.” Jet added quietly and she believed him. Out of everyone here, she could see he really did. That he was shockingly her only ally in this.

“Look, I recognize and accept that my healing may have been painful and poorly communicated. For this, I am deeply sorry and I will do better at voicing things. I will also tell this to him, if you would just let me.” Katara was growing beyond agitated. They were demanding her to fix this and then stopping her from doing so!

“You better take out the attitude when you do it, because right now this is really fucked up and he thinks you will kill him. You come at him with this hostile apology and he is not going to believe a word of it.” Toph rolled her eyes.

“I am not being hostile!” She exclaimed.

“Right, and I can see!” Toph retorted.

“Katara, perhaps it would be best to hold this conversation tomorrow. Give all parties an equal chance to relax.” Hakoda urged.

“Fine. But don’t blame me if he has a problem.” She huffed and flopped back to the ground to angrily continue to fold laundry.

“Katara-” Sokka started but she stopped him with a glare.

“Just who’s side are you on? I thought you of all people would remember all he has done and understand just what kind of person he is. He threatened GranGran! Or did you forget?” She huffed and bunched the shirt in her hands.

“I haven’t forgotten anything and I am willing to hear him out. He is a person, Katara, and if you would just listen to him you would understand a lot more of why he did things-”

“Like why he betrayed even his uncle? Why he turned against us and got Aang nearly killed? Or how he forced us around the world with the goal of dragging Aang to his father? How about all he did in the North Pole!? Sokka, if anyone here deserves forgiveness, it is not him. I agree to apologize for my poor healing, I will not take back my words but I will clarify that I meant that if he ever touches my family I will kick him out.” Katara huffed and pulled lingering water out of the shirt she held with a bit more force than needed.

“For touching- Katara, who talks to him or decides to be his friend is not your choice! If that is how you are going to be on the matter then you are going to have to suck it up. I like Zuko, and I am not going to leave him alone because your fragile ego can’t handle it!”

“What!?” Katara jumped up once more to glare at her brother. “You hated him just as much as I did not four days ago! What happened!?”

“I fucked up, a lot, that’s what! I made mistake after mistake and you want to know the one person who supported me? Zuko. He is the only reason father is here and okay! That I made it to and from the prison! Through it all I saw him for who he really is, and I-” His voice caught and he looked away from a knowing smirk on Toph.

“Look, Katara, if you would just stuff your hate for him for one second and look past his tough guy defense you might just see an actual person. Someone who has been through just as much as we have in different ways. Someone who is kind, and caring, and thoughtful, and who tries till it hurts him. He might be a bit socially awkward, but that is just part of the person he is. Sure, he is a jerk at times but really-” Sokka would have continued if it had not been for a cough from his father that cut off his rambles.

Katara was staring at him, eyes wide as she processed everything. “No. You- no. Sokka, you can’t. You don’t- How dare you!”

“What?” Sokka blinked, beyond confused.

“How dare you!” Katara fumed. She threw the shirt still balled in her hands at him as she screamed. “How dare you turn away from everything we are! You are just so- ahg! Typical! You are so typical! Sokka, you cannot be serious! He is Fire Nation!”

“You- Katara what are you saying?” Sokka took a step back as he tried to catch up to wherever the conversation had turned. He was searching for something and he found it. His face fell into a snarl and he took a step forward to square himself for whatever was about to come.

“Say it. Katara. I dare you.” He growled out and squared his stance.

Katara glared at his movements. This change, this attitude. Sokka never fought her like this, like he would actually stand in her way instead of with her. All of this was one persons fault. Why could no one remember everything that guy had done? Why was she left alone to remember the sleepless nights they lay awake over fear of that man? Of the times spent in terror over his appearance at times they had never expected? Of him throwing them to the side just as quickly as he had seemingly joined them?

This was his doing. All of it. Every day those around her fell further into his smoke and ash and no one seemed to remember just who they were dealing with! Fine, she would remind them.

“How dare you turn away from your family for the prince of the Fire Nation! For that ash-”

“Katara, don’t.” Sokka’s tone was harsh as he cut her words short and she growled. Her glare sharpened to ice but she did not try to repeat herself.

“News flash, Sokka, his family is the reason ours suffers! That the South Pole is secluded and barely surviving! That all our people went years without so much as a whisper of the outside world. That our village men and women suffer and die!”

“Because of his family, Katara! You want a news flash? He is only 17 and this war has been going on for generations! He wouldn’t have even known the South Pole existed! Let alone order attacks onto it.” Sokka retorted as the others shifted uncomfortably, unsure what to do.

“He attacked the South Pole! He burned down your watch towers and attacked GranGran!”

“Attacked is a very harsh word. His ship broke into our village in the search of the Avatar that you set free! He manhandled GranGran a bit, yeah, but he didn’t so much as burn a single hut and kept his word to leave when Aang asked it of him! He has never not kept his word!”

“Oh yeah? How about when he lied in Ba Sing Se!”

“Did he lie? Or did you fill words to his actions without him saying them? If he said he would join us, great, he changed his mind like the idiot we know him to be. If he didn’t actually say it then, he didn’t say it and therefore never lied!”

“Why are you so quick to defend him!? How could you forget all those attacks!? He chased us around the world! He hurt people! Sokka, I don’t know why you can’t remember this!” Katara yelled over her brother, trying in desperation to remind him.

“I never said I forgot.” Sokka had grown quiet and was looking at the ground with such intensity that Katara too went quiet. “Katara, I can’t forget all he has done. But, as an older brother, I get it a lot more than you seem to. If my little sister offered me a way home after years at sea, I might have taken it too. Even if it hurt people I didn’t really know. I’m sorry you were hurt. I haven’t forgotten anything, but I have forgiven. Can you?”

Katara watched as he slowly looked up towards her and saw red. “No. I can’t. I won’t. He hurt my family and is still hurting it. This, right now, is his doing. Sokka, you need to stay away from him. Why can’t you just stay with Suki and the two of you leave him alone? He is good at nothing but hurting others.”

“That is not true. If anything, he is only good at hurting himself. Suki took the time to see the real Zuko. She likes who she found, even if he can’t seem to understand it himself. I do, too. I’m not sorry for this Katara, but I really like him and I will not let you hurt and torment him any longer. That shit ends right here and now, do you understand me?” His stupid ‘I’m the leader’ voice was out again and no one seemed to be ready to admit she was right.

Katara was fuming, her insides felt like they could boil over at any moment. She could also see that she was outnumbered in this and that nothing she was saying had held any weight. Her own family was choosing the firebender over her and she needed space. She would not forgive, but she could see she would not be forgiven if she did not so much as relent at the moment.

“Fine. You take care of him. As of right now I will heal him and nothing more. I will apologize for the pain, but he is nothing more than fire to teach Aang and the moment he finishes that I want him gone.” Katara spun on her heel and marched away without so much as a glance back.

She needed space. Needed to be away from everyone for awhile. Needed to bend a lot of water to clear her head. She hurried towards the ledge and followed it to the waterfall that roared past one of the eroded walls, creating a window that only saw the water falling at high speeds.

The water was roaring just as loud as her own anger and emotions. It screamed with her as she pulled parts of it out and away. She shoved water against itself to create a wall and threw it out further over the edge in a shower. Punched through surrounding rocks with shards of ice, and froze the entire waterfall over for as long as she could before the rushing water at the top broke through.

“Whoa, will you teach me that?” Aang chirped as he approached almost silently.

Katara huffed. “Teach yourself. You don’t need me for waterbending anymore.”

“What? Of course I do! Katara, just because I can do a lot of things you can, doesn’t mean I don’t need your help continuing to learn. You will always be my teacher when it comes to new things you make. Like that ice shelf!” Aang nearly floated to be behind her as she turned to face him. He wore a guilty smile.

“Aang. I know why you are here so you can drop the attempt at conversation.”

“Oh, uhm, okay.” Aang fidgeted slightly before he looked between the raging waterfall behind them to where Katara stood. “I heard from the others, but wanted to hear from you too.”

“Then you know what happened.” Katara crossed her arms.

“I know what they think happened, yes.” Aang agreed.

“Then you know what happened. I hurt their poor little firebender and when I admitted that it was wrong they wouldn’t let me go and fix it. They wanted me to apologize but barred me from doing so. I was wrong, happy? I did not mean to hurt him but I won’t take back my words, either. I meant it when I told him to stay away from my family.” Katara spun on her heels to glare out through the falling water.

Aang was silent for a good while. She almost thought he had left her alone after that, almost. That was until he sighed.

“Katara, I’m proud of you for admitting a wrong. That can’t be easy, to take criticism against your bending hurts. Do you think they have a reason from preventing you from actually apologizing?”

“No. They told me to do so, I wanted to, and they told me to wait. I don’t know, Aang, you’d have to ask them.” Katara pushed forwards and split the falling water to peer out over the rolling mountains beyond.

“Do you think it has something to do with this?” Aang motioned towards the scattered rocks that had fallen to her ice.

“No.” Katara looked around once more and sighed. “Probably. I won’t apologize for not trusting him. Aang, you of all people here should understand.”

“I do. Well, I did. I don’t anymore. Zuko really is trying in his own way to be better. He had a whole three days to do something to me and the most he did was get us stuck in tar cause he couldn’t not touch a gold egg.” Aang snickered at the memory and shook his head. “He is trying really hard, Katara.”

“I want to believe you, Aang. But I just can’t forget everything the Fire Nation has done. Everything his family is a part of. Everything he himself has done to hurt us.” Katara sighed and closed the hole in the waterfall.

“He is not the Fire Nation, Katara.”

“What? I know that!”

“Do you?” Aang’s look was that of open question.

He had not sounded like he had been judging yet that was how her heart took it. Of course he was siding with the firebender. What was it about that man that turned everyone against her!?

“Of course I do! Just because he is not his people, doesn’t mean he didn’t turn his back on us to go back to them the second he got a chance! How come no one but me questions that? How come I am the only one who remembers that he made that choice, and that almost killed you!” The water behind her cracked and rippled as it froze and shattered with the natural power of the waterfall but she paid it no mind. Her focus was on Aang and how he had taken her outburst with no show of pain beyond a flinch.

“You can be angry. You have every right to be angry, more so than others. But don’t you think this is a bit much? Katara, if you really think about that moment it makes a lot of sense-”

“No! It doesn’t. How does any of that make sense!?”

“Don’t you want to go home? Want your brother and your father to go back to before everything?” Aang looked towards the ground.

“What? Of course I do. I would never give up everyone here, but of course I want my father here. I want my brother here, too. They mean the world to me but this makes no sense. How does that explain anything?” Katara had let go of her pull on the water as she watched Aang shift around.

“I want people I miss, too. Even some who I remember being better than they were. Katara, he is only a teenager. He wanted family and took a chance he saw to go home.” Aang let his gaze flick towards her. “Is that really so bad?”

“Of course it is! If anything that makes it worse! He has a family of monsters and if he wanted to go home then that makes him just as bad!” Katara yelled. Why could they just not see this?

“Toph wanted to see her mother. She wanted to go back to a person who saw her as nothing more than a doll and denounced her abilities based on a physical thing that she saw as a flaw. Does that make her weak?” Aang countered.

“What does that have to do with anything? Of course Toph would want to see her mom. That is her mom!”

“And a person who emotionally kept her at a distance. Yet she still wants that connection.”

“I don’t understand what this has to do with anything, Aang. Get to the point.” Katara huffed and crossed her arms tight.

“My point, is that Zuko might be similar. He might be chasing something he thought he had with the people who were supposed to be who taught him about love. What if they taught him something else? What if he now is trying to learn just what love really is? Katara, he is not a monster. He is a boy who made mistakes because he was never taught how to do anything but fight.” Aang held her glare with his own sorrowful eyes.

“Even still. His family is a bunch of monsters and I can’t forgive that. Aang, I know what you are trying to do. I get that you chose to see all that, even if it is not true, but I can’t. I see someone who has hurt everyone here and would do so again given a chance. I’m not sorry, but I will not take back my words. I will only apologize for my actions and that is final.” She turned back to the water rushing and focused once more on bending it to her will. She heard more than saw Aang as he left and felt a deep pang of something in her heart. She ignored it, choosing to focus on the water in her grasp.

All of the bending took her whole concentration as she worked through everything she knew and some things that she just let her body move through in the moment. By the time she had finished the sun had vanished from the sky and the sliver of the moon was glowing through the water. The light it cast danced along the tunnel walls. She stood to watch the light, only to hear a whistled tune from behind her.

She spun, water pulled to follow her movements and ready to strike out. She hesitated at seeing Jet, only in part because the light of the night made him look a bit ghostly and that brought an uncomfortable reminder that he had died once already to the front of her mind.

“Jet, what do you want.”

“To say that I agree with you. I think you are right, you know, about Zuko.” Jet twirled something in his hand before he popped it into his mouth. Wheat, where had he found wheat?

“Thanks, but I don’t need your support.” Katara huffed and let the water fall. She watched it flow down till it was out of sight over the dark edge, the feeling of the days betrayal still burning deep.

“I know. You don’t need me. I just want you to know you are not alone in this. I know exactly how you feel.” His voice was quiet yet it felt so loud to her.

“Like you possibly could ever know!” She spun to glare but lost her momentum at the open and pained look that had settled across Jet’s features.

“I do. When I first met Zuko, he had told me he was Lee. Had said he was a nonbender and was a refugee. I had believed him. We had joined forces, just like you had done in Ba Sing Se. He had opened up to me, only to get me to do the same just so he could burn me from the inside out.” Jet clenched his teeth and the wheat stem snapped audibly.

“He lied.” Katara said, she had not meant to speak but the words were so ready to bubble out that she could not stop them.

“Yeah, he is good at that. Feeding into the soft spots of people only to burrow deep and burn them from the inside out. I see it. I know it, personally. I tried to warn Sokka but that boy won’t listen to reason. He sees ‘attractive firebender needing a hug’ and is unwilling to heed warnings.”

“Wait, attractive? Zuko? You mean he really is-”

“Oh yeah. Head over heels for the walking disaster.” Jet snorted. “Trust me, it burns bright and strong while it lasts but it will burn you down from your core. I would know.”

“What? Jet, were you and Zuko-”

“For a bit, yeah. Though only with ‘Lee.’ Zuko did not show his true self till the prison actually. Lying, runs in that family it would seem. Point is, I support you, even if you don’t want or need it.”

“Jet.” Katara looked him over, searching for even the slightest hint that he was trying to fool her again. She couldn’t find it, nothing about him said this was false or a way to get anything. What could he gain from coming out here to agree with her? She could not see this giving him anything in return and he had just been so open with her.

“Look, Katara. I agree with your choice here. Apologize or whatever tomorrow to appease the mass still unable to see through his smoke, but I say keep your ground on the matter of just how far you will let him in. You are right, and eventually the others will realize it too.”

“Thanks, I guess?” She looked back over to the waterfall still roaring and thought of how her brother had squared up against her. How he had done that for Zuko. He had turned against family for that firebender. For the people who took everything from them. She thought of the pain Zuko always caused and she felt her resolve settle. “You are right.”

Jet smiled wide. “And you are strong.”

She watched as he disappeared back into the shadows. It was strange, feeling like she had someone who believed her. Who trusted her. And that person was not family.
