## Chapter 25

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

If you noticed the chapter numbers change, it is just my poor planning. Can't not write way too much and cause the delay of the next part.

It was either cut it up, or upload next week the longest thing I have ever written.

So: I cut it and you get two more chapters of this! Have fun!

### Text

A light tug deep inside made the act of dragging himself from the depths of sleep start far sooner than he would have liked. It was warm, just comfortably so, and his body was yet within his control. The tug grew every more demanding and with a reluctant moan Zuko cracked an eye to see the dusting of light from the early sun.

He let his eyes fall closed with a sigh as the realization that the sun was what had pulled him from sleep hit. That Agni could reach his core once more. That within a few hours to a day’s time he may just have his own fire back. It was a relief, but also meant he should probably get up to meditate.

A twitch towards his right was met with a chirp. The chirp quickly turned to an upset chatter as Zuko continued to try and turn that direction. He quickly had a face full of fluff and a small hand like paw tugged his cheek to turn his face away. The chattering mass shuffled around and refused to leave, making escape out of the right side of the bed impossible. Zuko chuckled lightly and blew a puff of warm air towards Momo who quickly settled back down with a purr.

With more effort than he thought should have been needed, even with one arm still annoyingly strapped down against his chest and a ball of fluff stealing his pillow, he twisted towards his left. A weight that had been so light around his middle had tightened and within a moment he found himself pulled forward into a solid and warm mass. His left arm was effectively trapped between the thing and the bed. Something heavy had been thrown over his legs, catching them. He was trapped, and the thing holding him captive had just snored.

It took a good amount of wiggling to loosen the arms holding him enough to look at the person, but he was not surprised to see the sleeping form of Sokka. What he was not expecting was just how the early sun rays seemed to kiss the highlights of his face. How his hair, which fell free of its usual wolf tail, perfectly framed said face. He was so close that he could feel every puff of air Sokka breathed against his own forehead. If he wanted to, all he would have to do was to scoot upwards and he could- no. Nope. Absolutely not.

Heat burst from his chest and bled its way up through his face till his whole upper body felt like it was boiling. Stupid sleep slowed brain, how dare he think such things! Sokka was a friend.

Despite having tried his best to stop a certain train of thought, the sun seemed to enjoy teasing him. One attempt to escape in particular had cause Sokka to shift and cling, the movement itself made Sokka’s face and especially his lips become highlighted by Agni’s golden rays. Agni was being a cruel piece of work at it was not even fully morning yet.

Zuko’s mind had taken that color and supplied what it would feel like to just move ever so slightly and to ever so gently meet them. Either with his finger to trace the color or his own lips to feel the warmth, both made his stomach flip. He wondered what it would feel like. What would he do if he could see Sokka wake and smile at him with those very lips Agni was kissing. To hear him try to whisper in the early hours for only Zuko and- NO.

With a rather harsh twist and a low growl that had Momo swatting at him before he flew off, Zuko attempted an escape. He shoved and rolled, narrowing his thoughts to only that of getting up, and was rewarded with being able to turn onto his right side. With a kick out his legs pulled away from their place under Sokka’s. He was not going to think about the sudden chill. About just how tangled they had been. He was so close to the edge, just needed to roll off the cloud of a bed and-

The flash of color that reached beyond himself was the only warning as to what was coming. That arm was back . Zuko dug into the bed side, trying to hold onto the fluff leaking out to give some kind of support as the hand latched tight to his wrist. With surprising ease and quickness, it dragged Zuko’s hold back and away. Before he could attempt to break free or do anything but grunt in surprise, Zuko found himself once more pulled back into the center of the cloud.

His arm was now held firmly to his chest and his back was flush against Sokka. The leg was thrown over his own once more as Sokka burrowed into him tightly. Sokka let out a grumbled warning when Zuko moved to pull against this new hold. The growl caused an involuntary shudder through the firebender as he felt it vibrate through his back as well as heard it. Zuko grumbled back lightly about wanting out and tried to wiggle free weakly, only to feel the hold tighten. He felt the huff of breath against his ear in frustration and flushed harder. His heart was beating out of control and the warmth of everything was threatening to cook him from the inside out. This was not the battle he expected to be having at dawn, or ever.

Zuko swallowed hard and tried to not let his heart beat loud enough to wake anyone. A specific heart reading person would be the death of him. He tried to steady his breathing and just lay with his accepted fate. It did not feel bad, being trapped against Sokka. If he relaxed a bit, he would say it felt pretty nice. Kind of a weird feeling, being trapped yet feeling safer than he had in a long time.

It felt very much opposite of everything bad that had been happening, but that was the problem. Sokka was unaware of what was happening. Of what he was doing to Zuko. Would he be upset when he woke? Would he stop sleeping next to him in fear of this happening again? Would he-

All thoughts ended in an abrupt change of position which had Zuko realizing one major thing. Sokka had just buried his face into Zuko’s neck, and pulled tight against his stomach, and Zuko very much needed to escape. He needed a bathroom, now, and any other rational thoughts fled as he started to try and claw away from his now prison.

“Sokka!” He hissed, only to be rewarded with a tighter squeeze which made him shudder harder. He kicked back to try and make contact to wake the lump.

“Ssstop mo’in’.” Sokka grumbled and shifted up higher, out of kicking range.

“Let. Me. Go!” Zuko snarled as he twisted harshly against the ever tightening hold and prayed to Agni for help. He was going to explode.

“Nooo.” Was the moaned response before snores once more took over.

Zuko was about to scream at the lump snoring and making his current situation a muddled mess of problems. If he could he would mix a bit of fire in, just to sell his point. He would have, if Chit Sangs amused chuckle had not wafted over from the fire in the center of the sleeping chamber.

“Chit Sang! I need-” He had shifted enough to look towards the other firebender, the one who was most likely also awake due to the suns pull, and stopped seeing that the man was already on the way.

“Bit stuck, are ya?” He chuckled as he crouched in front of the make shift bed housing the two very tangled teens.

“I need to- ah.” Zuko snapped his mouth shut and flushed deep realizing just what he needed and that this situation had just gone from frustrating to awful.

“Need to piss? Figured that was it. Alright, lets get ya out of this nest and stretchin’ them legs of yers, little dragon.” Without an ounce of embarrassed energy, Chit Sang worked quickly to untangle the two and scooped the small bender up before the water boy could latch tight again.

Zuko let out a shocked squawk as he felt himself lifted into the air, having fully expected to be helped to his feet. A frantic glance towards the man told him that his only job now was to hold things in until Chit Sang got them to a safe point. He quickly buried his burning face into his free hand and tried his best just to focus on breathing. He felt more than heard the chuckle from the beast of a man but ignored it.

A longer and more embarrassing than he ever wanted to talk about time in the bathroom chamber later, he and Chit Sang walked together silently through the twisting halls slowly. Zuko had convinced the man to never say a word of what happened to anyone despite Chit Sang stating a ‘little help’ was no big deal and that it was his honor to assist.

“Seems ya found yer legs again.” Chit Sang chuckled as Zuko realized he had not had a guiding hand on him for some time.

“Yeah, feels good. Uh- Thanks for this morning.” He flushed again and looked out of a low window in the hall at the sun just fully over the horizon.

“No problem at all, any time. We all need a bit o’ help after a riot. Nothin’ I ain’t seen, done, or needed before.” Chit Sang snorted and Zuko felt his face turn a deeper red as he continued to ‘not look’ at the man.

“Sure.”

“Say, I’m hungry. Fancy headin’ back?”

“No. I’m alright, thanks. Think I need to walk a bit more.” Zuko glanced back and caught the nod. He sighed and relaxed his tightened shoulders just a bit.

“I get it, ya need space. No worries. I’ll save ya some good stuff from breakfast. Have fun, and don’t fall off the edge.” He smirked as if there was more he was itching to say and the confused looked Zuko sent him seemed to be all the okay he needed.

“If ya need the bathroom again, ya know where to find me.”

Zuko kicked a rock from the hall at the man who only laughed in response as he took it as his queue to leave quickly. Zuko was fuming, embarrassed beyond the realms of what he had thought possible, and really just kinda wanted to fall back into oblivion. Stupid only one working arm. Stupid tight and weak muscles from inactivity for nearly three days. Stupid body.

Keeping true to his word, Zuko started to slowly walk the twisting halls of the air temple. He stuck to the ones that stayed as open to the morning light as possible. The sun felt warm, like a soft sweater after a stormy night, and he did not feel like leaving it any time soon. The slight burn in his legs from walking felt just as good as he let his feet mindlessly guide him.

He was not worried about getting lost or even out of range. A certain little earthbender always had him in her sights, and was always quick to block him. As if on queue he felt a wave through the ground and his foot was suddenly forced upwards an inch. The disruption, which usually was just a light warning to turn around, caused him to sway and slip. He tumbled backwards as his limited balance was tested and tensed for the rough rock impact awaiting him. It never came, but he did land on his back in a shifting pile of perfectly shaped to him stone.

He sat in his make shift pit and blinked in surprise before he snorted a laugh. Took a bit of wiggling, and one good twist, but he was able to stand and watched the floor smooth over once more.

“Thanks.” He sighed out and knocked a knuckle against the near wall just to be sure she felt his words.

It wasn’t long till he heard her response through the wall in coded pulses. Her message, ‘don’t fall off the edge Sparks,’ caused him to snort and knock once more before he continued back through the way he had come.

The time it took to make his way back should have been short, but the twists and extra turns he took extended it. He was just not ready to face the others. Sokka, Suki, and maybe Aang would smother him back into that damn bed. Katara was actively ready to break him at any moment she got and that sent a shudder through his core. He was up, walking, and his arm felt far better and the pain level overall was so low he would have thought it just a normal day after a hard training session. He did not need her healing, if he could stay away from them all.

Toph was a different problem. She would not force him back into that far too fluffy pile. She would not treat him like some broken doll like the others were. No, far worse, she would rib him for how his heart gives itself away any time Sokka did anything. The fact that she knew and had picked a fight with Sokka over it right away was enough for him to not ever want to be near them all again. He knew he was hopeless, and that as emotions go, his heart was never one to lie.

He hated to even think it, but it had become true at some point over the last few days. Sokka was a guy who was always two steps ahead and processing things far faster than anyone. The guy who could make admittedly so bad they were funny jokes no matter the situation. Deadly accurate with nearly every weapon he got his hands on, even if he had a weird obsession with a specific boomerang. He was always learning, even when it looked otherwise. Always attentive to the people around him. He was so damn perfect, even when he wasn’t and Zuko had let himself fall into that pool just as easily as he had dove head first into the frozen waters of the north.

He let out a groan as the weight of the acknowledgment settled deep within his core. He really, really liked Sokka. He loved how he threw himself into learning. How he was always so many thoughts ahead. How he smiled, laughed loudly, and was just so comfortably himself. He was brave, strong, kind, loving, and everything Zuko could never be.

A shove to his foot as he stepped told him that he was broadcasting his internal hatred again, and a certain bender disagreed. It was hard not to huff and stomp back. To not tell Toph to just mind her spirits own business. He had spent days trapped with people, he was allowed to wallow for a bit if he wanted to. Not that he really did want to, nor did he actually mind her telling him off for it. Just, thinking kindly of himself was hard. Toph had told him before that she was determined to break the habit out of him, whatever that terrifying thought meant, and had been keeping true to her word.

Instead, he sighed heavily and tried his thought again. This time he turned to actual events rather than general opinions. The Boiling Rock, admittedly up there on his top ten, no, five list of the worst events of his life. The only good thing that had come from that damn place was friends. Maybe? That was still unclear.

Suki, he thought, might actually like him and be somewhat a friend now, despite everything. Kind of like how Toph was. Jet could stuff everything where the sun never shown and then some for all he cared. Chit Sang was a weird situation, but not a bad man. Really, more like one of his old crew. Could joke back and forth but the balance of status was off. Zuko was younger, but Chit Sang still referred to him as a prince and that offset things. Hakoda, well, he was a whole different problem.

Hakoda was a Water Tribe Chief, and a leader, and a father. Every part of his being screamed threatening to Zuko, despite him never once raising more than a quick hand to catch him. He had yelled at him before, on the gondola, but everyone else had also been yelling so it should probably not count. The man had done nothing wrong, at least that Zuko could pin down at the moment.

Yet, his body seemed to fall back into his old patterns from home whenever he so much as heard him. Those old freeze or flight responses he had thought were well trained out of him until recently. That years on a boat with uncle had broken those habits, yet only a few weeks at home had rekindled them and one man was enough to drag them out even in this place. He sighed deep, tried to relax the growing tension in his shoulders, and lifted his head to look forward only to freeze.

There, walking towards him with a speed that was just within alarm bells of danger range, was the very person he had been pondering. Hakoda had his stern eyes locked on Zuko, every step closing the distance with a determined speed. Zuko’s own inner self screamed for escape and he became hyper aware of just where they were. Of the space around them, from the walls to the window, and took in every possible path away. He then locked down that feeling in case he angered the man by turning his back in such a clear dismissal.

“What are you doing here?” Hakoda barked out, face falling into that of a far more threatening one as he took in the prince. He sounded untrusting, angry, and ready to defend.

With a glance around them Zuko felt his panic rise. His heart started to pound hard in his ears and his chest felt tight. They were alone, close to a large window open to the cliff face, and everything screamed danger. Hakoda looked furious to find Zuko where he was not supposed to be. His eyes were themselves the same angry blue of Katara, and his stance that of Sokka’s when he was surprised. Zuko was unsure of what he had done to earn the fury of this man, but one thing was certain. If he let the man get any closer he would be defenseless to whatever style of teaching he used.

Zuko let himself fall into autopilot. Everything around them fell into a tunneled sort of view as he scanned for anything to help. He had no answer for the man, and he knew that that meant he was automatically wrong. He also did not know how Hakoda fixed problems, and even if he did he could not fight back against him. This was Sokka’s father. A Chief, and the figure of authority now. Instead, Zuko let old habits take over.

With shocking speed, if Hakoda’s remark of surprise was accurate, Zuko pivoted towards the wall closest to himself. It was one he knew well, as it had a crack between the rock ceiling and one of the air temples hanging. It was just large enough that a small person could wiggle into, and Zuko had done so before. First time had been out of curiosity mostly. Others had been to get away when needed. This time, it was with the practiced speed and need for safety that had him up the wall and twisting through the tight space at the top with a bit more force than was usually needed given his useless arm.

Once he pulled himself fully into the space he laid back on the floor of one of the upside down buildings. His breathing was heavy as the panic and exhilaration of sudden exercise slowly left his body. He listened hard through his pounding heart as the man yelled for him to come out. The voice had gone from shock to that of booming anger and Zuko grit his teeth to stay as still as possible. To quiet everything from his heart to his breath. Before long, he heard the man curse followed by quick footsteps that echoed loudly in the empty space of the temple.

Most likely, he was going to tell the others. Which meant Zuko was now in deeper trouble than before just because he could not just sit and take whatever punishment he was about to receive. They would all probably be really upset but he could not bring himself to leave. They had all gotten to have space or time to think and be left alone. Was it just so wrong that he wanted that too?

A flash of new upset pulsed through his core at being herded around like some animal. Treated like some glass pottery. Like one wrong move, such as walking alone, was going to shatter him. Well, screw their weird rules. He had told Chit Sang he needed space and that man had given it. Just because Hakoda was mad at him, did not mean he was done being alone. The others, he decided, could deal with it.

—

“Ew, Sokka, did you just eat off of your boomerang?” Suki remarked as she scrunched her nose.

“What-no!” Sokka quickly wiped off the edge of the boomerang he had been using as a knife for the fruit he was eating and stuffed it back into its holster. “Why would I do that?”

“Uh-huh.” She rolled her eyes and looked back towards her own rice and fruit. Not the greatest breakfast, but it was better than others they had had before.

Sokka yawned loudly and let his gaze slip back towards the empty bed in the corner. He had been so warm this morning. Had a spotty memory of cuddling into a certain firebender. Of feeling him both relax into him and playfully fight him but sleep stole most of the context. When he actually woke, he had expected to still be holding said bender. Had wanted to use it to have a certain conversation. Had wanted his first kiss good morning under the warm summer sun.

He sighed deeply. He had woken to a cold bed, no Zuko, and no morning kisses. It was silly, but it really did make his heart hurt a bit at the loss. He had really been looking forward to finally smoothing things out and that cuddle time would have been picture perfect! Another plan gone to waste.

“What’s got you all sighs and frowns this early?” Suki snorted as Sokka let out another long breath.

“What? I’m not! Just, Zuko’s not back yet.” Sokka glanced towards the hall expecting to see him and was meant with nothing.

“He’s fine, Sokka.” Suki sighed and rolled her eyes. “Chit Sang said he wanted to stretch his legs and Katara said that if he was able to walk on his own then he was well enough to be up and about. Relax, he probably just wanted some space after everything.”

Sokka sighed even louder, earning a rough nudge and a chuckle from Suki. He had to admit, it was a nice morning. The weather was looking to be perfect, people were settling, and Zuko seemed much better. It was almost back to normal, with a few adjustments to the group. He took another large bite of the rice and nearly choked at hearing his father call for him.

He looked back towards the empty hall and instantly forgot the food still in his hand. His father was approaching with his worried frown set deep in his features. Quickly, Sokka set what was in his hands onto the ground and scurried up to meet his father halfway. Hakoda had slowed seeing his sons approach, but that worried look had not lessened in the slightest.

“Dad?” Sokka slowed to a stop and let his father choose just how close or loud this conversation was going to be. Apparently, it was a fairly open one as he too stopped and looked around.

“Son, I am not quite sure how to ask this.” Hakoda paused and his brow furrowed further as if trying to riddle something out.

“Maybe I could help?” He glanced to the empty bed again. Actually, he’d rather enjoy a puzzle. Could help take his thoughts off of Zuko.

“Since when could prince Zuko climb walls?” Hakoda asked, less like an actual question and more just a piece to the main problem.

Scratch the thought that this would help.

“Dad. I’m sorry, I don’t think I heard you right.”

“Prince Zuko, did you know he could climb walls?”

Sokka raised his hand to stop his father from speaking further.

“Okay, so I did hear you. Was really hoping I didn’t. I just, what in Tui’s blessed night makes you think he could do that?” Sokka was really hoping this was all just some weird joke.

“I just saw him do it.” Hakoda motioned towards the empty hall and Sokka swore he was going to go back to bed and try this day again. First he woke up with no cuddle time, now his father was delusional.

“Dad, respectfully, I highly doubt that. Tell me what happened.” Sokka sighed heavily and rubbed his face with his palm.

“Sure. I was mapping out the halls, this place really is massive, and I came across the prince walking on his own. He looked pale and confused at first so I tried to hurry and help him. He really should not be up and on his own for long.” Hakoda looked back towards the hall is if he hoped this conversation would summon the firebender.

“Yeah, okay. You found him and he looked rough. Then what?”

“Right, I asked him why he was there-”

“How.” Sokka cut in.

“Pardon?”

“How did you ask him? Dad, remember yesterday. Words are very important to riddling out Zuko’s reactions to things.” Sokka crossed his arms.

“Of course. I asked him ‘what are you doing here’ in that very tone.”

Sokka thought about the situation. For some reason, whenever his father was near Zuko was already jumpy. His father said that they were alone, which could be a point of anxiety for Zuko. Then he had come towards him, presumably with a bit more speed than normal. Point two for a rough situation. The question his father had asked, however, should have been a factor to at least neutralize that. He had sounded worried. Concerned even.

“Okay, so, what happened?” Sokka was trying to see just what reaction would lead to his father thinking Zuko could climb anything.

“He had gone stone white, looked like he was gonna pass out actually. I feared he would fall without my being close enough to catch the kid. Instead, he scurried up the back wall like some-” Hakoda looked around the room for anything to describe what he had seen. “Like some lemur! He wedged himself through a crack in the ceiling and vanished. I tried to ask him to come out before he hurt himself further but no use. I don’t know where he is.”

Sokka rubbed his face hard with his palms and groaned. So he had not misheard, his father really did believe Zuko could scale a wall. He had hoped things would finally be going back to some kind of relaxing normal after everything that had happened. No where on any plane of existence did he ever think he would be faced with this specific situation. The mystery of a one armed climbing firebender.

“Forgive me, dad, but I just don’t think Zuko could climb a wall on a normal day. With one arm? No way.”

“You wanna take that bet?” A voice called over.

Sokka groaned. “No, Jet. I don’t.”

“Oh, why not? You sounded so confident. I bet your father saw just what he says he did. Wanna wager otherwise?” Jet smirked as he walked up to nudge Sokka with his elbow.

“And just how do you know if he can?” Sokka asked. Tone stating that he very much did not believe him.

“Trust me, I know.” Jet winked.

“I kinda want to hear how, too.” Suki added from her spot by the fire. She flashed Sokka a wicked grin as he glanced towards her.

“Sure, nothing interesting really. We got into a bit of a disagreement once during a raid that was poorly planned. The main building turned out to be locked and the only open space was on the side of the wall. He was being a pain and I said that unless he could somehow scale a building and get us inside then he could just stuff his ideas elsewhere. Ate my words, I did.” Jet snorted at his own thoughts and Sokka tried to picture Zuko climbing anything.

“I don’t believe you. I have never seen that guy do anything that didn’t have him firmly on the ground.” Sokka crossed his arms. He had seen him jump for things, run after people, and on one occasion run through a door only to fall straight through a nonexistent floor. But climb?

“Actually, Jet and Hakoda are right about this one. Zuko can be very tricky to catch and light on his feet when he wants to be.” Aang added with a knowing smirk as he walked to join the growing group. Something Sokka had learned about large groups of people, nothing stayed small or quiet for long.

“Right, and I am supposed to think the hot headed, loud, and impulsive firebender can just vanish like smoke at the drop of a hat. Look, I’ve never seen him do this before so it is really hard to believe.” Sokka groaned loudly.

“Maybe we should go and look at where the supposed wall climbing took place?” Suki asked as she too set her breakfast down and stood.

Sokka sighed but nodded. That should probably have been his first thought, but a climbing Zuko had stunted everything. How had so many people collectively lost their minds!? He stayed silent as they all followed closely behind Hakoda to the supposed place where it had happened. There was a good sized hallway, a large open window, and a wall that extended several stories above before it connected with a hanging temple. He looked with the others for any signs of said wall being recently climbed or burned. They found none.

“There, see. Nothing. Not a chance he climbed up this wall, and even if he did there would be no way he could go that high.” Sokka crossed his arms tight and nodded at the wall.

“Hate to disagree, Sokka. I have seen him climb something this size before.” Aang smirked as he ran a hand over the wall in question and looked up.

“What!? No way. When!?” Sokka whipped around to glare at the Avatar.

“When you and Katara were sick. I ran into Zuko a bit then and he scaled a wall much the same height. Pretty quickly, too.”

“I thought you only fought Zhao then!”

“Oh, right. Yeah, I did only fight Zhao. Zuko was kinda just, also there? Did I not mention that? My bad.” Aang flashed his caught and guilty grin and Sokka groaned as he smacked his head into the wall in question.

“Don’t tell that to Katara, please, and I still don’t think I can imagine this.” Sokka sighed.

“Of course, it is always a shock when you first see it, but he really is good. Probably the best hide and seek player ever!”

“That’s great Aang, but this is not a game. He is not supposed to even be up and about, let alone climbing! Besides, he only has one working arm!” Sokka sighed heavily as he pulled away from the wall and waved his hands in some random mock of climbing. This man was going to be the death of him at this rate.

“He can’t have gotten far, just, help me look for him?” He asked before he turned to stare up the smooth wall at absolutely nothing big enough to fit a person bigger than himself.

“Sure thing!” Aang chirped.

He turned his attention to the crack at the top of the wall where the supposed disappearance had taken place. He kicked off the ground and used the air around to hold himself at the top while he tried to get a grip on the crack. It took a few tries of poking himself into different points of the long seam for Aang to squeeze into the space and vanish. For a moment Sokka wondered if he had found Zuko, just maybe, but just as soon as he had gone into the temple he popped out with another shake of his head.

“Not here, but something was up here recently. Fresh leaves and stuff are everywhere but no tree.” He said as he slowly lowered himself back down to the group.

“Probably just an animal making a nest. Alright, lets look around the room.” Sokka nodded to the space and tried to think of just where he would hide if he were scared of something.

Aang bowed in acceptance and popped up to glance around as the others moved in seemingly random ways. He ignored them all as he hurried towards a small corner blocked by a few large stones that had most likely fallen years ago. The space between the wall and the stones was possibly enough for one kid to squeeze into, no way an adult would fit, and not the first place Sokka would have thought to even glance at.

Sokka watched out of the corner of his vision as Aang popped over the rock and looked puzzled as he shook his head. Obviously he was not going to find anything. Had he thought otherwise? Had Zuko somehow squeezed into that spot before? When? How? Sure, he was skinny, but not that small.

Sokka continued to watch as Aang flitted towards every small, out of the way, place and ignored every place Sokka had thought to look. As they moved their search from the hall to the rooms nearby he kept Aang in his sights at all times. Never able to guess where he would deem worthy of searching.

Where Sokka would have just opened a rooms door, Aang flitted inside to check for small spaces. Where Sokka walked past large cracks in the walls of the halls, Aang whispered into them. With every new spot Aang checked without a hint of making fun of the idea of looking for Zuko, Sokka’s heart sank lower.

At first he had thought it was some joke, that Aang was not taking this seriously. Jet had just been following and kicking rocks after all. He quickly realized that not only was Aang taking this very seriously, he had done this kind of thing before. That or at some point he had found Zuko in some of the places he was now checking. Which meant that Zuko was very good at hiding in small, tight, dark spaces. Possibly for hours at a time.

“You won’t find him.” Jet huffed after they had spent a good hour searching.

“What makes you say that?” Hakoda glanced towards the teen who had just kicked a rather large rock against the wall.

“Experience, mostly. If he hasn’t come out or made his hiding spot easier to find by now then it’s pointless. Trust me. Let him be dramatic and he will come out when he is ready.” Jet crossed his arms as if it proved his point.

“He has to be somewhere close by.” Sokka blatantly ignored Jet and dusted his hands off. Where could Zuko be? If he had climbed up that wall then there was no telling what he had slipped into and that worried Sokka far more. He had to be close still, possibly within hearing range.

Wait, if he was close then they had all just been wasting time. That realization alone made him groan loudly as he swung his hand up to slap into his own forehead. The speed of the slap forced his head back and he sighed as he looked up at the roofs above them.

“Where’s Toph?” Sokka asked as he slowly lowered his head, forehead stinging with the reminder of just how stupid he had been this whole time.

“Great idea!” Aang hopped up and without a word created a blast of wind to propel himself out of the hall.

“Forgive me for asking, but why do you need the blind child to help search for a hidden firebender?” Hakoda asked as he chuckled watching Sokka rub the growing red mark on his face.

“Toph is more than just blind, dad. She has this really amazing way of seeing that lets her see through the earth or something. Point is she can hear and see things we can’t even begin to understand. She can even feel your heart. It’s how she knows when someone is lying, actually.” Sokka explained as he watched the hall where Aang had rocketed down.

“That sounds incredible and terrifying.” Hakoda agreed. “Though it also sounds like it has limitations.”

“It does?” Sokka turned towards his father. He always forgot that Toph had any kind of flaw, besides personality, at times.

“If she needs earth, then on a ship or in the air must be awful.” Hakoda stated his way that spoke of thinking out loud rather than answering a question.

“It is, thanks for noticing.” Toph grinned as she followed Aang towards the small gathering. “You looking for Sparky?”

“Yeah. Any idea where he could be?” Sokka asked, hopeful.

“That depends.”

“Depends?” Sokka turned to face her.

“How bad do you need to find him?” Toph asked as she rocked back onto her heels.

“I would really like to find him, does that help?” Sokka was not in the right mindset to play Toph’s little mind games. Zuko had been ‘missing’ for over an hour and he really hoped that he had not passed out from a dizzy spell of some kind somewhere bad. His luck, he’d go right over the edge before the others even noticed.

“Not at all. It is not up to you if you can find him. It is up to how bad does he not want to be found.” Toph glanced up as if she was taking in the rooftops of the hanging buildings.

“What’s that supposed to mean?” Sokka rolled his eyes as Jet snorted.

“Means, he knows my little tricks, and I know his. He’s gotten really good at figuring out ways to get around my line of sight when he really doesn’t want to be perceived.” She twisted her foot and focused. “And he really doesn’t want to be around people right now I think.”

“Wait, does that mean he is not in the temple?” Sokka’s heart rate sped up as he let himself relive the fear of Zuko fainting right over the edge. The amused look he got from Toph was the only reason he did not ditch the group to start looking along the cliffs.

“No, he’s here. I know that for sure. I keep a pretty accurate eye on everyone here, generally speaking. If someone goes out of range without a reason I alert the group. He is here, just can’t pinpoint him right now. I have a few good guesses though, so the question remains. How bad do YOU need to find him right now, or can you lot just wait him out?” Her eyes narrowed as she looked back towards the ground, both listening for their answer and something more.

“He shouldn’t be alone right now!” Sokka stated, this had done absolutely zero to relieve his worry.

“Says who?” Toph asked.

“Says everyone who thinks someone with injuries like he had should still be resting!” Sokka was growing far too frustrated with this game as it was.

“He is resting.”

Sokka growled. “How do you know that yet you don’t know where he is!?”

“Never said I don’t know where he is. Said he is hard to pinpoint.” Toph crossed her arms and stomped her foot, sending the lightest of tremors through the floors. She held out a hand to keep everyone silent for a moment before she grinned wide. “There, I found him.”

“Great! Where?” Sokka sighed in relief.

“None of your business, that’s where.” Her grin only grew as she felt Sokka nearly lose his mind.

“What is that supposed to mean?” Jet huffed.

“Just what it means, Zombie. I just asked him to come out or let me in. He told me no. I respect his no unless he gives me reasons not to.” Her face fell into something more serious before she suddenly burst into a loud laugh and shook her head.

“Yeah, he really isn’t coming out any time soon. He did give a message, though.” She snorted and somehow rolled her eyes in a perfect way that spoke of practice.

“A message?” Sokka asked.

“Yeah. He said ‘leave me alone. I’m fine.’ See? He’s fine! Said so himself!” Toph laughed.

“How do you know he said that?” Aang looked towards the walls like they were the things speaking.

“Easy, same way he knew I was asking him to come out or let me up.” Toph shrugged.

“And HOW exactly did you do that?” Jet rolled his eyes. His face showed just how un-amusing he found this little game.

“I nudged him, he answered.” Toph stomped once more.

This time Sokka really focused on the tremors. The waves were not what one would expect from an earthquake. They were random in strength and length but over in seconds. It took a moment for him to realize why they felt so irregular. They were intentionally that way. She was using a mini earthquake and somehow talking with Zuko through it. Which meant she knew some kind of code, and Zuko knew the same one.

“If you can talk to him, then he is near.” Sokka once more looked around the space they stood and groaned out in frustration. He really had no idea how far her sight went and was learning just how bad that little fact was needed now.

“Listen here, loverboy. You keep this up and you are only going to chase him further into the walls or holes or wherever he can fit. What I suggest you do is actually listen to him, for once, and fuck off till he is ready to come out.” Her finger was shockingly fast as it was shoved under Sokka’s nose to make sure she had his attention.

“Wha- I- That’s not-” Sokka stammered as Toph’s sharp gaze slowly morphed into a triumphant grin.

“Is too. You want a crash course on Sparky? He likes his space and has a hard time vocalizing when he needs time away from others. You lot get your chance to walk around and be alone or with people when you choose, but he hasn’t had that choice in days and I think some alone time is very much earned.” She finished her little rant by crossing her arms and settling a glare onto the general area of people in the group, rather than the ground.

“But he is still recovering-” Hakoda tried to cut in, only to snap his mouth shut at the snort Toph gave.

“When has that stopped Sparky before? Really, it is like you guys don’t know him at all. If you really want to impress him I suggest you all let him have his alone sulk and go do something, like, I don’t know, make tea so he can at least have something hot when he comes down.” She once again rolled her eyes in the way that made Sokka very uncomfortable.

“So all I am to do is leave him to hide in a cold, probably tight place. Somewhere he could get stuck, or hurt himself, or get lost… and make tea!?” Sokka was not following the logic of this day at all. First Zuko had wandered off, next he vanished like smoke apparently up a wall one handed, and now he was to just do nothing?

“Yup!” Toph popped the 'p' for emphasis and nodded.

“Not happening.” Sokka groaned.

“It is that, or you KEEP looking and only piss him off further to where he hides longer just to prove some nonexistent point. Your choice.” She shrugged and turned to leave.

“Wait! You said before that you have a general idea on his ‘usual spots.’ What does that mean?” Sokka wondered now that he was more calm about Zuko being at least okay.

“Just what it sounds like. You lot might be “people” people, but he is not and usually hides if he needs space. I don’t think he trusts his own space not to be invaded, no offense.” She shrugged as if that all made perfect sense. It did not.

“We would never-” Aang looked horrified at the idea of invading someones space unwanted.

Toph snorted. “Sure. Maybe not intentionally, but that doesn’t mean you haven’t done so accidentally. Remember, Sparks is not some random Fire Nation guy here to help. He is Prince Zuko. Even casually touching could be counted as an invasion for all we know. At least in the Earth Kingdom noble scene, touch was not given unless family. Fire? Probably less than that.”

Aang’s horrified expression only deepened as he listened.

“Hey, Twinkle Toes, relax. If Zuko was unwilling to do something or upset he’d let you know. He means what he says. If he tells you to back off, then listen. Easy as that.” Toph huffed and waved in Aang’s direction.

“But-”

“If you are going to bring up the situation now, this is just him being peopled out and needing space in a place that has zero private areas. You lot are always buzzin’ around loudly. Always needin’ someone to bother. He just can’t be bothered to provide that kind of energy right now so drop it.” Toph huffed and rocked back onto her heels.

While they continued to argue, Sokka tuned them out. He was starting to try and riddle out just what he was learning. This situation had worked to split Zuko into two different people. One was this loud, troubled, explosive teen. The one that stopped at nothing when he had a goal to achieve. The one that spit fire first and asked questions never. He had not even stopped to reason that things were possibly dangerous for himself in multiple situations! The brash, action over thought, in your face firebender was the image of the Prince of the Fire Nation.

Now, this new Zuko seemed to flip that whole image on its head. The Zuko in this picture was small. One that was light on his feet and quick to run or hide. He was quiet and timid in the face of something new or threatening. Constantly aware of ways to escape or of places to hide. This new Zuko was quiet, constantly on alert, and just a terrified teen who saw danger in every situation.

These two Zuko’s were at odds in Sokka’s mind, but both made so much sense if he gave them equal time. He had seen Zuko shrink away, any time someone around him remotely raised their voice unexpectedly. Had seen him buckle under a simple glare when he thought he was in the wrong. Had also seen him become loud, angry, and defensive when he thought he was right or was cornered. That somehow this new Zuko, was not so new after all, and it hurt his heart to think about.

“I agree with Toph, tea does sound great.” Suki added lightly, taking Sokka’s arm with a slight tug towards the main room.

“Yeah, alright. I’m tired anyway. Let’s just, make tea and wait or whatever.” Sokka sighed for the hundredth time it felt like and let Suki all but drag him down the hall. He very much ignored the snickers and comments, mainly from Jet and Toph, as he all but gave up the hunt.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes
