## Chapter 26

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Happy holidays to everyone, no matter what you celebrate!

As a gift, I thought it would be a good time to finish my first ever fic and part one of my series "Boiling Over".

This is very easily the most I have ever written, but it all felt needed before this part could end. Let me know if you agree!

### Text

He had not intended to hide long. Just until his temper could level out and let him think clearly. Perhaps until lunch to prove a point, what that point was he had no idea but the others seemed pretty set on testing it. If he was lucky, he could have merged into the group unnoticed and avoid making any more of a scene.

That plan was gone now as he slowly peeled himself from the wall he had fallen asleep leaning against. His whole being ached with the unnatural angles he had been locked in for however many hours as he slowly twisted to stretch. He had been so against them all herding him into that weird cloud of a bedroll and here he was proving them right without the actual argument. Deep down he knew he was still healing, that building ones fire from nothing ate energy like a starved fire eats through anything burnable. Did not mean he accepted that as a reason to sleep yet another day away.

He had ignored his already growing exhaustion as he pushed to fully sit away from the wall. Testing his inner clock proved that he had been right, it was nearly dinner. The reach out towards the sun felt fairly easy. Like it did during a cloudy week. Despite the exhaustion that still reached deep into his core he pushed at any heat he found. He used his left hand, brought it up and snapped. Sparks. The flash of them in the darkness of the room was startling.

Another, faster and far more forced snap and the scattering sparks dusted the stones and leaves on the temple floor. The bright lights danced and died just as quickly as his own energy. They were far more than he probably should be trying for, but they made him smile in relief regardless. In a few more hours, his fire should ignite. He would still need to be careful of just how much fire he used after that, but it was a good start. He was not going to be a ‘pointless firebender’ for much longer.

His stomach decided that the fire show was enough to warrant a reminder that he had skipped meals, unintentionally. It was not happy feeding a fire on nothing. The angry rumble echoed around him through the empty rooms and he curled into himself to will it to stop. He’d dealt with this feeling before.

The thought of heading back to the main room made him hesitate. The others were bound to be upset. He had run like some scared child. Had known they would check the room he had first entered and moved through the hanging buildings to one of his padded rooms. Knew that they had gotten smart in their search when Toph had asked him to come out. He had told her no and she usually respected his need for space. This time had been no different, thankfully. Still, he had moved from that room to one further in and now had a long way to weave till he reached one of the hidden stairwells that led to the ground floor with everyone.

With a deep sigh and a slightly unsteady shove from the wall, he stood and waited for the dancing spots to fade. Perhaps forcing the sparks had not been such a good idea. Once the wave of dizziness passed and his breathing came easier he quickly turned to follow his usual path out. He hopped through windows connecting rooms, used his arm to support any longer drops as the path got narrower. The maze was easy, mostly muscle memory now, but the one arm thing really was a pain. It slowed everything down and made the few climbing sections tricky.

Zuko let out a large sigh of relief once his feet touched the main floor. He sat for a few minutes, just letting his heart settle and the crackling in his ears to ease. His head felt like it was being squeezed and all he really wanted was something to eat, drink, and to sleep for days.

With a deep and controlled breath in for four, hold for four, and out for four, Zuko found his legs and pushed to stand. This time the spots did not invade his vision and his legs felt only somewhat floaty. Better.

‘Okay,’ he mouthed. ‘I can do this.’

Zuko turned toward the main room and swallowed hard. His first idea was to just try to skip meals altogether and sneak into into bed, avoid everyone. That was bound to just get him cornered so that was out. The second idea was run, but that was also out. He could just walk in and announce himself. Cause that always went so well… Why was this so hard?

“I could just… go… and…” He was trying to get his brain to help. Thinking out loud sometimes made it easier, but to do so meant he needed a thought. Clearly he had none.

His stomach made it for him. Another loud grumble rolled and with that he gained the attention of a certain airbender. Aangs face appeared in the main halls archway, just a few feet away, and the smile that broke out had Zuko frozen.

“Thought I heard shuffling! Sifu Hotman is here!” He chirped before his form completely disappeared.

Zuko sighed, counted to ten, and when no other sounds came from the room he slowly let his feet drag him forward. He was in for a world of hurt, and he might as well just get it over with.

The room was warmly lit with two different fires, the main one had Katara and Hakoda working over it. The smells and sounds from the many different pots and flames made his empty stomach shift loudly once more. Zuko quickly settled a hand over it, hoping to just will that kind of noise away.

The second, far smaller fire over by his things, was being shared by Suki and Sokka. Both were looking right at him and he froze. He glanced between the two as he tried to determine just how angry they were. The fact that neither looked remotely upset helped to loosen the tension in his shoulders as he followed Suki’s wave to join them.

“Hey Sunshine, how you feeling?” Suki asked in a quiet and calm voice.

Zuko felt a familiar wave of panic and shame burn hot in his gut. He almost wanted to snap, he was not some scared animal! That flash of anger, however, gave him reason to clench his jaw tight. It was the exact reaction a scared thing would give, was it not? Instead of letting that first instinct win, he took a calming breath and nodded.

“Sit with us at least?” She asked and motioned to the open spot in their triangle around the small fire. Zuko hesitated for a moment but let himself slowly settle into the spot as he glanced between the two.

“Hey buddy, I uh, made tea. Well, make is a strong word. I kinda just, uh, boiled water and leaves? I don’t even know what kind actually. Uhm, anyway, would you like some?” Sokka asked as he waved a hand over the whistling pot in the fire.

It took Zuko a moment to riddle out the question but when he did he could not hide his surprise. He would not admit it, but tea sounded really, really nice. Something familiar. The excited smile that spread over Sokka’s face at his acceptance made his heart skip a beat as he watched the boy quickly poor a cup. Suki chuckled as he accidentally poured some of the hot water over his own hand and Zuko could not help the smirk at the poor form. His uncle would have had a fit.

The cup warmed his hand as he sat with it. The smell helped to ease the rest of his spring coiled tightness till he was able to just be there. Once he was ready, he brought the cup to his lips and let the admittedly burnt tea warm his core. It tasted awful, and despite his trying to hide this fact, his face had scrunched as he swallowed hard. When he opened his eyes it was Sokka’s puppy dog pout that he was met with.

“You don’t like it?” Sokka sniffed. Zuko was fairly sure that he was faking his upset, but there was a chance that he was being serious.

“Wha- no, it’s not- I mean, it’s, uhm- it’s a good first try!” Zuko stuttered, gaining only a snicker from Suki who was being no help. He shot her a glare to showcase this point and she burst into laughter.

“You can just say you don’t like it.” Sokka reached out, clearly intending to take the cup.

Zuko panicked and pulled the cup back towards his chest. He twisted just enough to keep it out of reach and leaned back. Sokka huffed and rolled his eyes slowly.

“Dude, you don’t like it so just toss it! I get it, I can’t make hot leaf juice.” Sokka scooted around the fire to be next to Zuko and reached around for the hidden cup.

Zuko did his usual. He acted without thought and chugged the rest of the hot, burnt tea. Felt it singe his throat a bit as it burned down the path to his stomach. He ignored it all, shuddered slightly, shoved the cup into Sokka’s outstretched hand and met his shocked look with a glare.

“Another.” Zuko stated, ignoring the choppy rasp of his slightly upset throat and realized that maybe he had been too rough with that statement. “Please?”

Sokka slowly took the cup and blinked a few times. He shared a look with Suki for a moment before the two of them shared a laugh. This was not the reaction he had expected.

“You are a wonder, you know that?” Sokka chuckled as he did what was asked and poured another cup. “But really, you don’t have to drink this stuff. I have not the faintest idea what I did and Suki said it tasted like licking dirt.”

“So? Tea is really hard to get right. Point is you made it, and you offered it to me.” Zuko took the cup once more but did not move to drink. This time he only wanted to sit with its warmth in his hand. A reminder that he was welcomed here, if only in this corner.

“Better?” Suki asked, she kept her voice low.

“Yeah, thanks.” Zuko muttered back, his eyes locked on the steaming liquid in his hand.

“I’m glad. You had us a bit worried.” Sokka nudged his elbow into Zuko’s just enough to gain the benders attention.

“Sorry.” He muttered in answer, refusing to look up. He did tilt his head just enough to the right to better hear them over the echos of the room. Why were they both being so quiet?

“Nothing to apologize for, Sunshine. As long as you are alright, everything is fine.” Suki moved the kettle back over the dying fire.

Zuko hummed in response but made no motion to actually accept her words. Instead, he let his thoughts crackle in all different directions. He was trying to think over all the events that had taken place over the last few days but most things were just a foggy dream. When he got tired of just feeling the emotions of the last few hours he glanced around and noted that neither Sokka, nor Suki, were paying attention to him.

They were playing some kind of game drawn on the floor between them with charcoal from the fire quietly. Whatever it was, Suki had the upper hand if her smug look and Sokka’s increasing hunch was anything to go by. Suki caught his stare and winked as she tossed a piece of wood into the small fire pit.

“Hey Sunshine, finally out of your head?”

“Huh, oh. What?” Zuko scrunched his brows. How could one be outside of their own head, wasn’t that impossible?

“She means, you done thinking so hard you stop noticing what’s around you?” Sokka clarified as he circled some place on the ground. Suki took only a second to counter leaving Sokka stunned as he hunched for his next move. “No fair!”

“Ah, sorry. Yeah. I’m here.” Zuko flushed and looked back to his cold cup. He really had gone into his thoughts for awhile.

“No problem. We all need that time to think and a lot has happened.” Suki waved him off and marked a quick spot on the ground which left Sokka growling.

“I can see it in your eyes, however, and I am going to stop those thoughts now. No one here is mad at you for needing space, Sunshine. Worried, yes. You spooked a few people but that was not a bad thing and you have done nothing wrong.”

“I-” Zuko started but had no answer for her. He snapped his mouth shut and focused instead on heating the cold tea. Once the steam started to rise he looked back up and caught her smile.

“Ah-ha!” Sokka laughed as he dramatically crossed something on the ground.

Suki glanced down, drew a line, and laughed hard as Sokka processed his loss.

“You cheated!” He squawked. He had contorted himself into the weirdest position, sticking his backside skyward as he all but shoved his own face into the ground.

“You were hovering so close the whole time, no way I could cheat. You just are bad at this.” Suki snorted as Zuko finally leaned over to look at the game they had been playing.

“Wait, is that…” He could not help the snort of a laugh as he glanced towards Sokka who had moved to sit normal while still pouting at the floor. “Was that three in a row? You know there is a way to force a tie no matter what, right?”

“Wait, for real?” Sokka looked up and grinned. “Teach me!”

“You really don’t know?” Zuko turned to sit to be facing Sokka as the other drew them a new game.

“Nope, you will have to show me! There were no other kids my age in the Southern Water Tribe, never got to really play these games.”

“Oh, that’s- I’m sorry.” Zuko paused in his drawing of his first move and glanced up to watch Sokka shrug.

“‘S’okay, not your fault or doing. Now, show me how to force a tie so that Suki will stop kicking my ass.” Sokka eagerly leaned forward and Zuko put his full attention into showing all the ways he knew on how to force a win or at the very least a tie. It was fun, in a simple way, and the laughter that followed Sokka’s first win against Suki made his heart flutter.

“Well, this has been lovely, but I should probably go help get things ready for dinner. You two should actually talk.” She sent a pointed glare to Sokka that had Zuko cocking his head in confusion. Why was she upset with him? It was only a kids game, had he done something wrong with teaching Sokka how to win?

“Right, yeah, okay.” Sokka cleared his throat as he set to poking the near dead fire with his drawing stick, his face had started to turn a similar shade to that of the embers.

Zuko looked between them before he set his glare onto the flames themselves. If he could, he would will the fire to explain just what had happened. They had all been so peaceful a moment ago and now Sokka was in trouble and it had something to do with him.

Sokka cleared his throat as he tossed his stick into the fire and watched it pop. Zuko glanced at him, waiting. He had no idea what had changed, but clearly something had. He had missed some kind of shift in the mood and it was up to Sokka to fill in that blank. He didn’t.

Instead, Sokka sighed heavily and slumped forward. “Why is this so hard?”

“I have no idea what ‘this’ is.” Zuko shrugged.

“Right, okay. Yeah, that is a good point. Alright.”

Sokka sat up straighter and turned to face him again. Zuko matched his energy and gave his full attention to the teen struggling to maintain any semblance of eye contact. When they did meet gazes, Zuko raised his only eyebrow in question. Instead of getting an answer, however, this got a quick glance away while Sokka looked to be going for a whole new shade of red. Really, it looked like he was going to pop and Zuko was becoming very worried for his health.

“So, ah, yeah. Change of tactic, I think.”

“O-kay?” Zuko glanced at him before turning his attention back to the dying fire with the hopes that it would help Sokka relax a bit. He snapped a few sparks towards it to help the few embers it had grow. This got a strange sort of strangled noise from the other teen and Zuko glanced towards him concerned. Last thing they needed was for him to pass out.

“See, I was kinda just gonna wait. You know, for you to remember. I don’t think that is going to ever happen, however.”

Zuko froze and slowly dragged his eyes from the fire to meet Sokka’s. Confusion felt like it was etched in his very bones. What was he waiting for him to do? Had he forgotten to do something he said he would?

“Yeah, that right there. You don’t know what this is about, so waiting is probably just hurting everything. So maybe it would be best to just say it all and see what happens, ya know?”

Zuko shook his head slowly. He had no idea how he fit into whatever the problem was, but he would fix it.

Sokka took a few deep breaths and shook his hands out as if that would help him find his words. He looked around the room, looking everywhere but at Zuko. They had said they were not upset, but had that been a lie?

“Ever since, well, I really couldn’t tell you a start time. That doesn’t matter, what matters is, uh, well. I think you are really great.”

Toph snorted a laugh as she walked by. Sokka glared at her as she passed which was only rewarded with the smaller teen sending him a rude hand gesture she very much should not know. Sokka was going to follow that temptation out of the current conversation but halted.

“Uhm, thanks?” Zuko muttered, extremely puzzled. That was not how one started a ‘you did this wrong’ statement, that he was fairly sure about.

“Look, I am really bad at this. Like, first time I did this she turned into the moon!” Sokka groaned loud and Zuko shot him a far more perplexed.

“First time you did what? Who turned into the moon? Wait, do you mean your girlfriend from the North? What does she have to do with this? I didn’t even know her.” Zuko’s head was spinning. Nothing about this conversation was making any sense.

“Snoozles, get to the point.” Toph snorted.

“The point, right. Forget about the moon, not the point.”

“Forget about the moon? Sokka, I don’t-”

“I mean, forget it for this conversation. Not important.”

“You were the one who brought it up in the first place.”

“I know I did! Just, forget it, doesn’t matter now.”

“Okay, so what does matter?” Zuko huffed and wrapped his free arm over his chest. He leaned back and closed his eyes tight, trying to follow the lines of conversation.

“You. I mean, of course you matter, just, your feelings. That is what I am, uhm, trying to ask about?”

Jet let out a loud wheezing laugh from his place at the far fire. He looked as if he had been trying to keep that laugh quiet and lost that battle which left him seconds from toppling off of his stump. Zuko sent a glare over his shoulder at the other teen and watched as he lost said battle and fell backwards. He was laughing so hard he was struggling to breathe and the wheezes sounded painful, at least Zuko hoped they were. It was then that he noticed that everyone was watching this absolute train wreck.

Toph was snickering from the corner she had moved to to be closer. That was not a surprise, she loved this kind of situation where she was privy to information not everyone had. Suki was chuckling from where she was dishing up bowls of rice. When she noticed his stare she flashed a large smile and sent a quick thumbs up that was mimicked by Aang to her right.

Katara was staring at her brother in horror, like she was seeing the worst case scenario play out before her. That might just be the case, if only Zuko had the faintest idea what that could be. He knew she hated him, and Sokka actively choosing him over her was against her threat from the night before. That was probably what was upsetting the waterbender. Whatever the meaning behind that look was, it meant at least one person was acting predictable.

“Sokka.” Hakoda’s voice echoed in the open space.

Zuko froze, everything in him turned to ice. Hakoda had yelled at Sokka, why? Because he was talking to him? If that was a problem then it was Zuko’s doing, he would not allow Sokka to face that. When he looked to see Sokka, however, he noticed that the teen was merely looking towards his father. He had not a sliver of concern in his frame and actually looked curious. Zuko internally scolded himself for his initial reactions.

“I find that when my thoughts and emotions are twisting my words, that following traditional guidelines can be a great way to overcome this.” Hakoda winked at his son before he turned his back to the boys in favor of focusing on the nights stew.

“Oh, right! Okay, yeah, follow tradition.” Sokka thought for a moment before he turned himself to truly face Zuko. This time, he made sure to make eye contact in a way that had Zuko forgetting about Hakoda. “Here goes nothing. Zuko. Prince of the Fire Nation-”

“I’m not-”

“Would you just let me do this?” Sokka snorted and rolled his eyes. “After I say it all, then you can ask your questions.”

“Do what?”

“Confess, you idiot! At least I have been trying to but you keep cutting me off!”

“What are you confessing? If you went through my stuff, I don’t care.”

Toph’s snort of a laugh echoed as Sokka stared at him. He watched the other teen slowly blink a few times as if processing the worlds most confusing puzzle. Good, now he knew how Zuko felt with this whole conversation.

“What? No, Zuko, that’s not what ‘confess’ means.” Sokka stated slowly.

“It’s not?” Zuko blinked in surprise. What else could he be confessing if not an invasion into personal things?

“Oh buddy, can I just say what I have been trying to and we can talk out any points you are still confused about?”

Zuko wanted to object but realized he had no idea just what he would be pushing against. He had no fight and this whole thing was making his head spin uncomfortably. He sat for a second trying to riddle something out and when nothing came he nodded.

“Right. Well, uhm.” Sokka cleared his throat. “Zuko, prince of the Fire Nation. I, Sokka, son of the Southern Water Tribe Chief, hereby announce my feelings of admiration and affection. If accepted, I’d like to formally-uh… date? Go on a date? Not court, obviously, it would be way too soon even if this speech is usually for that but like, get to know one another? Kiss and stuff, too, if you’d be okay with it. Boyfriends?” Sokka finished his ramble with a swallow of nerves and sat quiet, clearly waiting.

Zuko felt the moment his brain had clunked to a grinding halt. When Sokka’s words started to feel oceans away instead of inches. His whole world was shifting and melting into something he could barely even comprehend. There were so many watching them as Sokka all but declared something that was so far beyond comprehension that it took a whole minute before his last word penetrated the thick smoke between his ears.

In his wildest dreams he thought he might be able to enjoy hearing that kind of thing directed towards himself. That Sokka, ever the plan man and far more deserving of Agni’s light, would look to him as maybe a little more than a friend. If he was lucky, best friend, but he was never that lucky. But maybe he could be?

He could be. If he let himself actually take in the words that were now rattling around. Sokka, man of words and swords of equal strength, had just asked if they could be… what? Had asked for more than friends. Had said many confusing things but the end result was that he was not upset at something that had or had not been done. He was asking to be together, with Zuko, in a way he had only ever dreamed possible.

The Fire Nation would burn them where they stood for this. The second those loaded words left Sokka’s mouth they would have had their fates sealed. Banishment would have been a kindness, though it wasn’t a given. Zuko grew up learning this, believing it, and fearing it beyond any other fault in his being. That first moment he saw a theater production with his mother and felt a new pull towards the masked figure terrified him. From there he worked hard to stuff that part of him down, far under the surface with the rest of his flaws and cracks, and worked to be the prince he was supposed to be. It had worked for years, and had been working well into his banishment, right up until Ba Sing Se.

Jet had done what he seems to do best. He had burst through walls, rather unwelcome, and ignited a fire ready to rage deep within his core. He had given him a taste of something forbidden and all too exciting. The cracks, flaws, and faults that he had shoved so far deep into his core fed that fire like kindle until it was all consuming. It had burned away the facade of a prince he had so perfectly crafted until all that remained was the broken pieces of a broken person. Even still, Jet had fed that flame. He had played with fire, and for that they were both burned.

It had been unplanned, unexpected, and all consuming. Whatever he and Jet had had, it had been fast and passionate. It was all take, on both sides, and they gave nothing in return. They both used the other to feed separate fires until it was too much for either of them to contain. Jet wanted Zuko to give him a world, and Zuko wanted him to build a new one in return. It was only behind closed doors, away from the eyes of everyone, that they ever dared to share the fractured world they actually had.

Sokka, however, had just opened his world to Zuko. The one where his friends and family were all around as he openly asked for Zuko to consider something more with him. He was now watching every muscle twitch and thought with growing concern at the possible rejection, yet he held his tongue despite that growing anxiety so visible on his frame. He was honest, supported by those around if the reactions and muffled words of the others were anything to go off of.

It was then that his past relationships, or lack there of, came to him in flashes. Mai had been arranged the moment he showed a lacking of fire as a safe means of securing the future. Her family had always been in high standings with previous Fire Lords and it had seemed like a smart match. It had been, he loved her, but she was like a sister and had a heart that could never love him the way they were supposed to. There had been other girls, mostly uncles doing, and they were all gentle and kind but they left no spark within his heart. Then Jet had lit an inferno that none could control.

Sokka, he felt like a campfire to his skin. He was warmth, strength enough to weather any storm yet calm enough to keep all around safe. He was providing, both in light and in giving his warmth to better those around him. Zuko had wanted to sit just close enough to feel that warmth on his skin, but never touch. He had feared that his touch would extinguish the beautiful warmth. That he was not worthy of such a fire. The closer they got, the more he felt and the harder it was becoming to turn away.

Maybe, just maybe, he did not have to. He did not have to ignore this pull towards the other person. Maybe, he could try to feed the low burning flames with his own and things would not hurt. He could give into Sokka, and the other could do the same with him. He could take what was being offered, just because he wanted to. Agni, he wanted to so bad. Maybe, he could be enough, just being Zuko.

“Okay.” Zuko muttered, far quieter than he meant to be.

“I’m sorry?”

“Okay. Sokka, I-Yes, we can- try. If you, if you are sure then-” He bit his lower lip to stop himself from speaking further. He looked towards their low burning fire, the warmth not strong enough to reach further than the near empty kettle settled in the coals. Despite this, he could feel heat crawling its way up his face as Sokka sat far too quiet.

“Wait, really!? No questions? Just a yes?” Sokka exploded and jumped up with an excited flail and a shout that echoed throughout the temples. Suki’s laughter joined in the echo.

“Sokka, sit down.” Zuko hissed as the heat in his body mixed with his panic.

“Sorry, sorry. Just, excited. Thought you would drill me with questions first.”

“I have questions, lots of questions. Just, hard to think right now.” Zuko scrunched his brows to try and focus. “So, uh, what now?”

Zuko took the moment of silence to glance around at the others again. It was like nothing had happened. No world changing moment had just taken place. Jet was snickering still but his attention was on those around the main fire. One small boy, from what the others had said he used to be a part of Jet’s old gang, held most of the teens attention. Aang and Suki had all the dinner spots ready and waiting on the main course. Katara, however, had gone from frozen to glaring ice daggers. He ignored her by turning his attention back towards Sokka.

“Not sure. Never asked someone out and gotten a for sure yes before without it being confusing.”

“This is confusing, Sokka.” Zuko huffed.

“Is it? Sorry, didn’t mean for it to be. Here, lets try something.” Sokka scooted over to close the distance between them and leaned against Zuko’s left. The involuntary reaction that ripped through his body was instantaneous as Sokka all but became only a weight on his side. The other must have felt it, for he immediately pulled away and looked Zuko over worried.

“Sorry! I just-. That- that didn’t hurt, did it?” His eyes landed on Zuko’s right arm, still strapped down and hidden under his shirt.

“No. Just, I can’t-” Zuko shook his head and let out a frustrated growl.

How was he supposed to just say that he can’t hear Sokka or see him speaking if he is on the left like that. That he has a permanent weakness that was always going to be a point of problems for him, and if Sokka wanted to be close to him, he would have to just accept this as fact. That because of his fathers lessons, he was still learning those were wrong, he would forever have a weakness that needed to be accounted for.

Instead of speaking, he decided it would be easier to just move. Zuko stood and took the three steps needed to switch sides, Sokka watching his every movement with his calculating stare. Once he was seated again he nudged Sokka with his right shoulder, offering it in place of his left. Sokka slowly accepted, leaning somewhat awkwardly into him.

“Better?” Sokka asked quietly, still very much not putting any weight into his lean.

“Yeah, I just- I can hear you even if you whisper this way.”

“You really can’t hear out of the left?” Sokka moved his head up just enough to look at Zuko who had tensed.

“I can. Just, not well.”

“Hm, okay. What about your left eye? Can you see?”

Zuko huffed and rolled his shoulder to dislodge Sokka fully. “Getting right to it, huh. Not well. Mostly just shapes and color.”

“Sorry, just, kinda always wondered. How big do things have to be for you to see them?” Sokka moved to sit up.

“Pretty big.” Zuko sighed and tucked into himself slightly.

He flinched when a hand came into his line of sight and he looked towards Sokka. The other flashed a challenging grin that had something stir deep inside Zuko. “Up for a trust exercise?”

“What? No. Why would I be?”

“Do you trust me?”

Of course he trusted him. How dare this man, after everything they had been through and would potentially get into, doubt this fact. Zuko huffed a thin stream of smoke, ignoring just how the heat tugged at his lungs. He was never one to back down from a challenge, and that was not about to change. He straightened up and rolled his neck to try and relax. He shrugged as if the very idea did not cause his insides to melt with nerves.

“Sure, fine. Do whatever.”

Sokka hesitated a moment more before he moved to slowly press his left side into Zuko’s. He wrapped his arms around him once and squeezed as if that made this whole thing any better. The hand that had been so threateningly close a moment ago reappeared and slowly moved to cup his right cheek. Zuko’s heart was pounding as he sat as still as possible. Sokka seemed to notice the tension because he did not make a move until he felt Zuko relax just enough into his touch to be sure he would not panic.

“Ready?”

“No.”

“Would you like a safe-word?”

“A what?”

“You know, a word that we share that signals for an end to anything. No questions. It can’t be a common word though. Of course, we can also always change it later.” Sokka moved to rest his chin onto Zuko’s shoulder to look at him, still keeping his hold from behind firmly in place.

“Yeah, okay, what kind of word?” Zuko could feel his heart rate slowing as he relaxed slightly into the body behind him. If he had something that was an out, no questions asked, then maybe that would not count against his trust.

“Hm, it needs to be one shared by the people. Easy enough to remember but nothing that would come up in usual conversation.”

“What about Agni? You never say their name.” Zuko offered as he tilted his face to better look towards him.

“No, that won’t work. Good start, but you say his- I-I mean their- you say Agni all the time. Fire Flakes?”

“That’s two words. Besides, we are in the Fire Nation. Bound to find them. Turtleduck?”

Sokka snorted and squeezed Zuko’s shoulder slightly. “That’s cute. Yeah, alright. For now that will be our safe-word. Well, until something better fits or we can’t use it for some reason.”

Sokka shifted to sit back up straight and moved to place his hand back over Zuko’s good eye. He froze feeling the change in tension and put his left hand around Zuko’s shoulder to hug him from the back and squeezed.

“Remember, anytime. No questions asked. Got that, Turtleduck?” He whispered and Zuko nodded.

The moment Sokka’s hand had settled over his right eye his line of sight had gone from alright to nothing but fuzzy lumps of color. It was disorienting to have something cause this come from so close and he blinked a few times to try and will things back into focus. He was used to compensating for the half fuzzed vision, had been doing so for four years now, but to be fully out of focus was always a head rush.

A flash of color whipped by close to his face, maybe, and he flinched hard. He trusted Sokka to not let anything actually get that close to him or pose a threat. Once the thing flashed by again, this time far slower, he trailed it with his bad eye. It was a darker color than everything close to him so that ruled out water or fire at least.

“How many fingers do I have up?”

“I didn’t even know that was a hand.” Zuko huffed and twisted lightly to pull the right eye free. This was ridiculous!

“Hey now, no cheating. Okay, so close things are out. That is actually really good to know. How about-” Zuko could feel Sokka moving around as he looked for something to target and crossed his arm tight over his chest to wait.

“My dad.”

“That doesn’t count.” Zuko snorted and rolled his eyes, hoping Sokka could feel the action under his hand to prove his own point. He jerked to point towards Hakoda.

“Whoa, how’d you do that?” Sokka sounded amazed. At least one of them was enjoying this stupid ‘trust’ thing.

“I know where your dad is, just, not a good question.”

“Alright. How about… Momo! Where is he?”

Zuko sighed and scanned the area for any sign of that all too familiar flash of white, and found nothing. Either Momo was not in the room, doubtful, or he was not moving. Momo was also small which was a point against him being able to pick that specific fuzz ball out of just a pile of bedding or Appa shed. This was why he hated this kind of thing, how was he supposed to prove he was worth anything when Sokka was listing these tiny things! Agitation flashed through him and he whistled. The movement of ears told him exactly where that lemur was and Sokka snorted.

“Smart, okay so movement makes it easier. Good to know.”

Zuko smirked and settled back into Sokka’s make shift hug. He had found Momo, there was no rule that said he could not call for the named thing. When Sokka’s only movement was that twisting again he sighed. The man was clearly not going to be giving this up any time soon.

“Next, where is Appa?”

Appa? The massive beast of a bison was- surprisingly not easy to find. The sun was setting and the shadows it made distorted the size of things. This meant that while Appa was the biggest thing here, everything had an equally massive shadow. The color also did nothing to help. Appa was the same shade, if not just a little lighter, as the bleached walls that liked to reflect the sun. It took a lot of focus, moving from one fuzzy white thing to the next, till one finally moved.

“There. That’s Appa.” Zuko pointed in the direction of the only moving white and brown he could see.

“Dude, that’s a pile of rocks.”

“What? No way! That’s definitely Appa, don’t lie to me.” Zuko growled as he twisted to try and see with both eyes.

He was sure that was Appa, no way he had been wrong. Sokka only gripped a bit tighter and snickered like a fool. So he was lying. What point did he think he was going to prove by that?

“You ass. That has to be Appa. I know it, I tracked him all around the world. I think I would know what he looked like better than anyone here!” With a rough twist of his head he freed his right eye just enough to offset the hazy vision.

In the far corner, behind a pile of rocks, was Appa. He was currently staring at the two, mouth lazily chewing something though his attention was clearly on them after having said his name so many times. Okay, so it had not been a full lie, but there had been no reason to deny him when he had been kind of right. Zuko smacked down hard onto Sokka’s thigh and puffed out smoke, feeling a flash of embarrassment and anger.

“Why be a jerk about that?” He turned slightly to level a glare onto Sokka to showcase just how much he had not cared for that little stunt.

“Okay, you got me, sorry. I got carried away. You were right, that was *mostly* Appa.” Sokka flashed a grin, clearly he was in a ‘test his luck’ mood.

Zuko narrowed his glare, very much not amused. “Jerk.”

Sokka mocked being stabbed in his chest, for reasons Zuko could not understand. A chest wound was often fatal and nothing here had been that much of a pain. He knew his uncle would do the same motion when Zuko disrespected tea, but that also never made much sense. He watched as Sokka, seemingly recovered from his fake wounds, settled back with his arms open slightly in invitation. Zuko did not move.

“Sorry. Done, I promise. No more teasing. Turtleducks. Just, let me hold you again?” Sokka pouted.

“No.” Zuko deadpanned.

Despite the rejection he turned and let himself flop back with more force than was needed into the open arms. He felt a satisfied smirk break his otherwise angry mask at hearing Sokka’s surprised ‘oof’ as he took the sudden weight. It was nice, just slowly relaxing into the other teen without expectations.

“One more okay?” Sokka broke the silence and asked after a few minutes.

“Fine.” Zuko huffed and crossed his arm over his chest.

He felt as the other moved slowly to once more cover his right eye. This time, however, he did not ask a question. Instead, he had moved to force Zuko to sit up on his own as he shuffled around, the lack of body heat causing a slight shiver to roll through the tired bender.

It took a moment for him to realize that things had gone dark, and a moment more to understand why. Sokka was sitting right in front of him, far enough that he would have to reach out to touch him, but close enough that the action would be welcomed. Zuko blinked in surprise and felt as a familiar heat traveled up his neck.

That exact reaction seemed to be what Sokka had been waiting for. Before Zuko could let his brain catch up with the movement his eye told him had occurred he felt rough, wind chapped lips brush his. The touch was so gentle that if he wanted to, he could believe it had never happened. He didn’t want to. He let his left eye close and chased after the feeling.

This chase was rewarded with a low chuckle moments before those teasing lips were back. Zuko refused to let them escape this time and pushed forward. He felt rather than saw the hand that had been covering his right eye slide to his cheek. Finger tips hooked around his jaw just under his ear to pin him to the other, not that he had had any intentions on breaking away.

Zuko reached up to grab Sokka’s shirt with his free hand for support. The hold gave him just enough leverage as he pulled the other closer. Everything else faded away until all that remained was Sokka. His chapped yet warm lips, his hand on his face, the thumb rubbing under his right eye. That faint smell of burnt tea. The hand that had grabbed his shoulder to hold them together. It was enough to make him dizzy and it was all his for the taking.

A kettles gurgling and sudden whistle caused both of them to snap back into reality. They shoved away from each other, fists still clutching the others shirt. They sat back just enough to glare at the spirits damned kettle before glancing back to the other in an embarrassed silence.

Sokka was the first to break the new silence. He had taken in how both of them were out of breath and most likely red in the face and smiled wide. Without warning, he fell into Zuko’s right side and began to giggle like a fool. Zuko could find no humor in the moment, but noticed the eyes of some of the others watching them and his world spun as heat burned his entire being.

“Hey love bugs, dinner.” Suki snorted as she appeared seemingly out of nowhere and cause the two boys to jump apart. Both flushed equal shades and avoided eye contact.

“Here, take it. These bowls are hot so be careful.” She elbowed Sokka fully off of the bender and shoved a bowl into his hands to keep him at a distance. She turned her sharp stare onto Zuko as she gently placed the second bowl into his hand.

“Sunshine, I need you to eat this. I want you to also eat some rice in a bit. After that we can see about getting some sleep.”

“What, but that’s-”

“Because you skipped two meals today and the reason we have extra anyway? You are right. Good job riddling that out!” She beamed towards him in a way that made further arguments impossible.

She was right, did not mean he liked that fact. Zuko grumbled as he took a bite of the steaming stew in his lap and realized just how hungry he was. His stomach loudly agreed with Suki as it rolled the second the warm mix reached it. The flicker of fire in his core sent a wave of heat through his fire network and he nearly moaned in relief. His fire was back, and the food would help him keep it burning.

“Oh yeah, if that was the reaction one bite got then I agree with Suki. Eat up.” Sokka snickered and brought his own bowl to his face. Ever the food driven man, finished his statement by proceeding to shovel nearly half of his bowl into his mouth in one go.

Zuko rolled his eyes and pointedly turned and looked away. There were some things that were a no, and watching someone eat like some animal was pretty high on that list. Unfortunately, when he turned he got a glimpse of Hakoda and paused. That man, the Chief of the Water Tribe, father of a warrior and master waterbender, was eating in the same barbaric way. Apparently that had been a learned behavior. He turned his look downwards to his own food. There were some things that were just not right.

As soon as his stew was finished, it had been replaced with a massive bowl of rice. Suki, true to her word, had sat in waiting. She had not relented to his glares or empty remarks. Instead, she ignored him or countered with things that had no reason being correct. Fine, she could win this round, he would eat the damn rice.

“You finished yet?” Katara’s voice sliced through his core like an icy whip as she approached the small group.

“Just about, yeah.” Sokka said, his attention was on getting a single piece of rice that seemed to be evading him. Zuko was mildly surprised he had not just used his hand- nope, scratch that.

“I was talking to Zuko.”

That seemed to gain the attention of the others as most of the room fell silent. Even those by the main fire had cut their conversations short and were trying to hide their glances. It felt like a blanket of ice had just been thrown over the mountain, despite the summers heat.

“I- yeah.” Zuko slowly set his empty bowl to the side and turned to face her. He hated just how hard his heart was pounding. It was Katara, there were people all over, he was safe. Wasn’t he?

“Good.” Katara quipped and dropped to her knees in front of the group. She kept her icy stare locked onto Zuko. “We need to talk.”

Zuko swallowed hard and glanced towards the others. Suki looked every bit relaxed, eating as if the interaction in front of her was miles away. If one did not know where to look, they would think she did not care. Zuko knew better. She had moved to sit in a way that would take only a second to launch at them if needed.

Sokka did not hide his nerves as well as she did. He really did not hide them at all. While Katara had moved to be in front of the other bender, Sokka had moved to his side and pressed himself close. If he thought it needed, it would only take a harsh twist and he could force himself between the two and that was terrifying in a whole new way. Sokka was lining himself up to potentially fight his own sister over a firebender and the picture that painted was hard to process.

Katara seemed to agree with Zuko’s last thought as she took in her brothers new spot. Her eyes narrowed and her lips thinned but she did not make any further movement to oppose. The silent conversation the siblings were having was one Zuko would never understand, and for that he was grateful.

“So.” Katara broke the tension filled silence and leveled a new glare onto her target. “It has been brought to my attention that we… might have had a misunderstanding.”

Zuko blinked, looked between the people around, and blinked again. What kind of misunderstanding could they have had. In his mind, they had been very clear on their stance of each other. That she was going to hurt him if he so much as-

Ice flowed through his core. She had threatened him over just simply being close with her brother. He had all but forgotten just how quickly she had acted on that threat and had jumped into something far more than friendship with that very person. Had ignored the threat in her glare as he had accepted. No taking it back, not that he would even if he could. It was time to face the consequences of that choice.

“Relax, Tui and La. I am not going to hurt you. Look, yesterday, I need you to tell me the truth. Did you feel anything when I healed your chest?” Her gaze slid to his right side and he swallowed again.

His first instinct told him to lie. Say no and this would all go away. When he went to speak, however, he found that his voice felt squeezed and no sounds were coming. Her glare only sharpened as he prolonged his answer. A squeeze to his knee had him jumping slightly as he followed the feeling to see Sokka’s hand. The hold, while strange feeling, did help give himself something to focus on as he nodded instead of vocalizing.

“So you felt it?” She asked, he nodded again.

“What did you feel, exactly.” If her eyes were all she needed to freeze, he would have been long dead.

“I-… It felt-” He cleared his throat, the choked sound had startled even him.

“What did you feel.” She pushed.

“It felt like… a snap? Something just kinda popped. Then the area felt like lava for a moment before everything cooled. It’s fine, I get why-”

“No, you don’t. When I said I would hurt you I had meant in a fight. When you could fight back. I never meant to hurt with healing. If you really felt that, then I am sorry.” Her look softened as she took in his side once more, only to harden again. “I won’t take back my words. I mean it. You hurt my family, I hurt you, but I would never use healing to do so.”

“I-” Zuko sat stunned. He had thought, felt with his very being that she had meant those two things as both proof and warning. Had she really not meant him harm while healing?

“Look, Zuko. I need to check and make sure something was not done wrong to cause that pain. Before I do that, I think we need to establish some ground rules.” She huffed and crossed her arms tight.

“Okay.” Zuko nodded slowly and leaned backwards, further into the warm body behind him.

The arm that he felt snake around his back to hold him helped sooth the raising panic in his gut. Her glare landed on her brothers hand but she made no further mention of it. Instead she took a deep breath and seemed to count in her head before she continued.

“I will tell you what I am doing, before I do it. I will vocalize my healing. You will tell me what it feels like, and the moment it feels remotely painful. If I sense you panicking or you say it hurts we stop and try again. If I say I am done, we are done. Agreed?”

Zuko sat stunned for a moment before he felt a squeeze to his shoulder once more. This time it shook him just enough to gain his attention and he twisted to look up at Sokka.

“Turtleducks. Got it?”

“Yeah, okay.” Zuko nodded and turned back to face the waterbender who was looking at them like they had grown extra heads.

“Turtleducks? Do I want to know?” She settled a questioning look on her brother who shrugged. “Whatever. Shirt off, I need at your arm.”

Zuko flushed deep. He had forgotten that little detail, and while it was usually no big deal, it somehow felt so much more problematic with Sokka so close. It was not like he had not seen him without a shirt before, but that was before they were- something. Now was probably not the time to suddenly feel embarrassed by his pale, skinny frame. The longer he sat, the worse this whole thing would be.

“Well?” Katara scoffed.

“Alright, I’m doing it.” Zuko snipped back as he moved to struggle to remove his prison shirt. Why was he still in that gross thing, anyway? He twisted, pulled, and very much ignored the extra hands that had appeared to help him out of the offending shirt. Said shirt was then tossed into the small fire.

“Oops.” Sokka said in a way that stated that the burning had been very intentional.

“Alright. I am going to check the chest first. It should be only cold. If it freezes or burns say so.” Katara pulled her water to her hands. She waited for his answering nod before she moved forward and settled the water to his chest, letting it seep deep into his side.

He shivered at the sudden cold and shook his head at the questions. It did not hurt, much the opposite, it was like a soothing balm. Just because it did not hurt, did not mean he was okay with the feeling. He knew she would not hurt him. Not with people so close and she had kept to her word about stating any action. That did not mean his body believed this to be fact. His heart was pounding and when she dug deeper he could not stop himself from gasping at the sudden shift.

The water pulled away as fast as it had appeared and she leveled him with a glare. “What? Did it hurt?”

“N-no, sorry.”

“Then relax. I’m almost done.” She rolled her eyes and brought the water back up quickly, only to stare at the hand that had appeared between them to stop her.

“Katara, you also need to relax. Just, take a second.” Sokka huffed and brought the outstretched hand around to pull Zuko backwards slightly.

The action put himself between the two, and allowed him to steal a hug which Zuko was not complaining about. He settled back into the hold and felt the heat that Sokka continually gave off seep into his back. He could feel Sokka’s heartbeat, steady and strong, and focused on that to help calm his panicked system. When he was once more able to breathe without feeling constricted he nodded and felt the arm pull away.

Katara rolled her eyes once more before she brought her water up. It was only a few minutes at most but the whole process had felt like it had taken hours. The cold seeped into his bones and he had very nearly called for it to stop when she had turned her attention towards his shoulder. It had felt like ice was cutting into it, but she had said that might happen. She had also said that if he could deal with it then this might be enough to no longer need the extreme treatment.

Once she pulled the water away she drilled him on every feeling. Every spot that was even slightly cold, the spots that were still tight. She had him rotate and raise his arm until it pulled, tightened, or hurt and watched it all with a calculating glare. Once she was done making him do the weirdest stretches she deemed it healed enough to do with a simple sling. That he was not to do crazy things, like climb, but simple things like eating were fine as long as they were brief.

Zuko had agreed, readily. He was more than ready to not have an arm cemented to his chest like some lost appendage. It had been weird and off balancing to have his right arm, the one that compensated for his left side most of the time, basically nonexistent. With some help from Sokka he was able to quickly put on one of his actual shirts, already feeling so much better, and let Suki use an old sheet as a make shift sling.

“Okay,” Sokka started as he helped to adjust the sling to sit at the proper height, “that was rough.”

“She didn’t have to do that, you know.”

“Nope, she did. She absolutely did.” Sokka rolled his shoulder and looked around the small space. Others had already started to drag out their bed rolls and were settling down for the night.

“Alright! Enough of all the tough stuff.” Sokka stood and moved towards the bedroll Zuko had been using for the last few nights. With a quick dip he latched onto the top and began to drag it to wards the center of the room.

“Wh-what are you doing?” Zuko leaned forward to better watch Sokka take the makeshift bed away. Was it because he no longer needed to be on bed rest that he was losing it? Perhaps he was healed enough to go back to using his own stuff, or lack there of. The thought of doing so sent a pang of something painful through his core.

“As the leader of the group, I say the group sleeps close together. You being close to the edge ends now.” Sokka huffed as he kicked the bed into place nearest Suki’s own roll.

“Wha- Sokka, I am fine here. Nothing has happened.”

“That is a statement begging for a ‘yet.’ The group sleeps together, end of question. You are a part of the group and so you are moving closer. Besides, I’m sick of being cold.” Sokka marched back to begin gathering his own roll and worked to stuff it back into its worn holder.

“You- what does your being cold have to do with this?” Zuko huffed, agitation boiling as he stood.

“If we are gonna share the Appa bed I want to be by the fire. You are warm, but not that warm.” Sokka hefted Zuko’s pack onto his back and moved to throw it towards the mentioned bed.

“I- what!? Sokka, you can’t just toss my stuff around!” Panic flashed through Zuko as he watched Sokka move to do just that and he stammered. “T-Turtleducks!”

“Sorry, force of habit.” Sokka lowered the pack and Zuko sighed in relief.

Sokka might not have thrown his pack, but he had proceeded to toss his own sleeping bag in the general direction with much less care than one should have with a limited supply item. He was making quick work of the area and had hefted Zuko’s pack onto his back.

“You could have just, I don’t know, moved? If it was such a hassle to be all the way over here why not just leave?” Zuko watched as Sokka turned to kick out their small fire, snuffing out the only thing heating them from the chill of the mountain.

“Because, prince of pessimism, I was waiting for you. Either we do this together or I move the group out here.”

“Wha- but, why!?”

Sokka stopped his kicking to put out the fire and sent a tired look over his shoulder. He sighed deeply and moved to dust off his pants before he turned to face him. He held out his fist, only to raise his pointer finger before continuing.

“One, it is safer to have the group together. A person on the outer edge is far more likely to be attacked without the rest realizing a problem.” Sokka said, locking Zuko into one of his ‘I know better’ looks that made his blood boil for an argument.

“Okay, then I could just move into the outer edge here and-”

“Two,” Sokka cut in while raising another finger. “One sleeping this far away from any warmth of others and the fire, on a mountain, is at a higher risk for illness given the changing temperatures.”

“Null point, firebenders-”

“Are still human.” Sokka stated while lifting yet another finger. Zuko felt like static was prickling just under his skin at every point. “Three, in the case of an emergency you would be the straggler to make it to Appa in time. I refuse to let petty feelings be a reason that you may be left behind.”

“I’m fast.”

“Four, I’m sick of childish feuds. You are in our group and have been proving yourself far more than some here who are allowed into the night circle. That is reason enough.”

Zuko made motion to counter but closed his mouth tight. All his previous counters had either been blatantly ignored or shut down.

“Five, you just agreed to being my maybe boyfriend and I would really like to finally have my spirits damned cuddle that is not forgotten or over before morning.” Sokka huffed and turned to finish putting out the coals.

“I- what!? That’s not- You can’t just say that stuff!” Zuko felt as the prickling anger from a moment ago exploded into a new kind of heat.

“He can, Sunshine, and will. You said yes to the koala-sloth, he gets this way.” Suki snorted at the desperate look Zuko sent her way.

“Hey, buddy.” Sokka walked closer, still holding Zuko’s pack.

Zuko spun to face him, still feeling that new flushed feeling that was becoming far too familiar.

“If you ever don’t want to do something, even a little, all you have to do is say so. You don’t have to even use a safe-word. If it is something that can be done, it will be done. You only have to say no and I will respect that.” Sokka stopped only when he was standing close enough to touch. He used that closeness to reach out and grab Zuko’s right wrist to squeeze lightly.

Zuko snapped his mouth shut and let what he was hearing sink in. Sokka was giving him an any time out, no matter what, that did not require a silly word agreed upon for some weird games. Of course there were things that would not stop, like Sokka all but forcing him into the group, but that made sense. He looked back towards the growing group of bed rolls and noted that Jet was watching the interaction closely and that sent a flash of boiling anger through him.

“Yeah, okay.” Zuko nodded and turned his attention back towards Sokka. He watched as the other boy studied him. He seemed to have taken Zuko’s silence to mean something deep and the look he wore showed just how unsure he was about it.

“Hey, can I hug you?” Sokka asked and tugged at the wrist still caught in his hand.

“What? Oh, uhm, sure?” Zuko stammered, caught off guard by the change.

“Buddy, that sounded way too unsure to be a ‘yes’ and I don’t know about you but an unsure anything is a ‘no.’” Sokka smirked as he watched Zuko riddle through his words.

“I-” Zuko paused. He was not the best at thinking before he spoke, but maybe this was a chance to practice. “I don’t mind. Just, don’t know why you would want to.”  
Sokka nodded and seemed to accept that as answer enough.

“I want to because this is a hard thing for you and I personally take comfort in closeness. No matter, let’s focus on the main point. Why are you so against moving to the group?”

“I’m not-”

“You super are. This whole argument has been against moving. So, what’s up?” Sokka shifted the pack on his shoulder higher.

Zuko stared at the movement as if it could end this whole conversation. Why was he so upset at Sokka moving them from the far corner to the main group? It was not as if he really feared or distrusted them. If anything, this brought him closer to those who had wiggled into his personal space. Those who had proven to have his back and be somewhat of a support, oddly enough. Still, the action felt like a finality that scared him. Why?

“I don’t-”

“Zuko, you are with us, right? You are here to stay?” Sokka asked, he let his voice fall to be just for them.

All Zuko could do was nod in agreement. He was there with them. He was here, willing to give his life for the cause of finally ridding the world of the wrongness that was his families control. Nothing in this world or the spirit world would get in the way of his putting a stop to this hundred year war and giving his all to the Avatar and his group.

That was it, wasn’t it. No matter how hard he pictured the Avatar, Aang his thoughts countered, it never included himself. It was always them, and he was there in the distance, righting a great wrongness. The idea that he would break that bubble and walk into the group like he meant something felt… wrong. Like he was still lacking something. That line was still a wall for him to climb, yet Sokka was just opening a secret hatch and dragging him through.

“I haven’t-”

“You have. We trust you, and you belong. Let me prove this like you have proven yourself?” Sokka took Zuko’s stunned silence as agreement and tugged the hand still caught in his own. When he found no resistance he grinned as he led the silent firebender towards the main fire. With far more care than he had shown with anything he handled he placed the pack he had been holding hostage next to the bedroll.

“I never-” Zuko started, slightly tugging to weakly free his hand.

“Relax. Just, lay down for a bit? You don’t have to sleep but I am tired and would like to turn in.” Sokka released the hand and flopped onto the Appa fluff as if he owned it. With a lazy motion he patted the space beside himself. Zuko stared at it as if it would swallow him whole before he even laid there.

“This is okay, right? Sharing, I mean. I know I moved you suddenly, but if you don’t wanna share then I’ll unroll my own. No questions. Say the word and I’ll-”

“Sokka, it’s fine. Just, give me a second.” Zuko threw up a hand to stop the others rambling.

It was hard to think. His head was starting to pound again and his fire was demanding that he stop fighting for once. His body wanted nothing more than to fall into the warm pile of fluff, but Sokka was there and everything was just so new. Sokka wanted him to just lay there, in the same bed, in front of everyone. No thought or care. His father was two people away and he was acting as if this was the most normal thing in the world! The fact that they had been sharing a bed roll in some way for a few days now seemed to matter very little at the moment.

Sokka looked from the outstretched hand, arm, and settled onto Zuko’s shoulders. He slowly looked over the other teen and took in just how tight he had become. With little more than a sigh the second bed roll was ripped out of it’s holster and spread neatly against the Appa roll. Sokka sat up with a fake stretch and rolled to be on his own things.

“Wha- What are you-”

“Take the bed, one new thing at a time. Today has been too much as it is.”

Zuko let his knees buckle, the pull of the open bed far too strong to resist for much longer. From the sparks, steam, and overall exercise of the day he was more than ready to drop off into oblivion. The fact that a portion of the bed was still warm helped pull him down fully until his face was half buried. The instant relief from the world fading into muffled nothingness lasted all of two seconds. His instincts, still very unsure of the majority, sent off alarm bells which forced him to roll onto his left.

He cracked his right eye open at feeling a tug on his leg and realized Suki was shoving him over to pull a blanket up. The fabric was light weight, perfect for the summer nights. He had wanted to stay awake for a bit longer, prove some point about something he could not remember, but his bone deep exhaustion laid over him like the blanket and pushed his consciousness under.

One moment he had been awake, hearing muttered words and had felt the chill of the mountain. The next he was waking to that all too familiar pull deep in his core. The one that told him Agni was awake, and with it all firebenders were to rise. As he moved to follow the pull upwards he felt the weight of a body pressed firmly to his back and froze.

A glance forward showed that Suki was still sleeping soundly in her own roll. She looked peaceful in the beauty of the morning light. Her hand was buried under her own pillow and Zuko had little doubt that she had a weapon of some kind tucked under there. The thought of her sleeping with a weapon so close was oddly comforting. He had blacked out, not even stirred while the others were still moving, and that made something uneasy shift in his gut. If Suki was prepared, at least he was not defenseless.

That left the mysterious weight to be that of Sokka, who seemed to have a problem not clinging to the closest firebender when he slept. A small wiggle was all it took for the lazy lump to curl further into his back and grumble. An arm was thrown over his side to catch him. Unlike the first morning like this, however, Zuko felt no need to attempt an escape. By the look of Aang still lazing with Appa, a sleep in day would not be unwelcome.

With a sigh and a shift to move the lump behind him into a far more comfortable position, Sokka had some bony joints, Zuko moaned lightly and soaked in the warmth. The sun was feeding his small flames, Sokka was thawing the morning air, and everything felt so right for once. Of course that meant it was never meant to last.

A rumble and boom shook the mountain. Flashes of red and white could be seen coming closer at alarming speeds over the cliff sides. Aang, having felt the first wave of red hot blasts crash into the mountainside, was the first to react. Gathering as much wind as he could in such short time he blasted another wave of smoldering bombs backwards as he slammed the air temples defense panels closed tight.

The crashes against the mountainside continued as the rest of the group struggled to get up fast enough to avoid any falling debris. Zuko, having been awake with the sun, was one of the first up. As he was working hard to throw supplies towards Aang he all but missed a large bolder as it crashed down just to the side of him. With a glance towards the ever shaking ceiling he noticed a particularly large stone break loose and acted without thought. He dove towards the person it was on a direct path with and pulled Katara out of harms way. The pull held her under him as he took the few smaller stones and shielded their only healer.

The second the falling rocks settled he felt the waterbender elbow him just under his ribs, causing him to cough hard and glare.

“What do you think you are doing?” She glared back, ice in her words.

“Saving you? Making sure you are not crushed to death?” He retorted, making sure the questions sounded every bit ridiculous as her own had. What else would he be doing when their entire camp was literally falling apart!?

“Well, you are crushing me. All safe, so get off!” She shoved her elbow back once more and used the space it created to scramble up and away.

“Why thank you, Zuko, for putting yourself in harms way to save me. How kind.” He groaned as his gut throbbed once and mocked Katara’s voice without an ounce of care for the accuracy. A hand grabbed his shirt collar and he found himself dragged to his feet just as suddenly.

“Come on Sunshine, now’s not the time.” Suki shoved him forward roughly as she moved to snatch up any lingering camp items.

Another rumble ripped through the camp, rocks and dust mixed as they fell towards the ground in a deafening thunder. One of the panels twisted on its axis in an odd angle, allowing Agni’s light to seep through for a moment until the all too familiar red and black colors passed before the sun. There was only one person he knew who could have found them and was firing with such force against their stronghold.

Azula.
